# CLAUDE.md — agent grounding

This file is for Claude agents working on SwiftNodalong. **Read it before doing anything.** Sessions get long; product context drifts; this file is the fixed point.

When you finish a meaningful piece of work, append a note under **Decisions log** at the bottom so the next agent inherits your context.

For setup and the file map, see `README.md`. For the Apple Music feature roadmap, see `MUSICKIT_PLAN.md`. For Firestore schema, see `../nodalong/ERD.md` (in the sibling React Native repo).

---

## 1. What Nodalong is

**A social hub for Apple Music users.** A friend-driven music discovery app where:

- **Home** is the "for you" hub — distinct from Feed (which is strictly about people you follow). Sections: Jump Back In (recent listens), Friends' Spotlights (a rail of what people you follow have spotlit today), Your Playlists, and Apple Music's own personalized recommendations (subscriber-only). Added 2026-07-24 as the new first tab, replacing the app's prior reliance on Search-as-discovery.
- **Feed** is what people you follow are listening to — tap to play in-app via Apple Music, with attribution showing whose listen surfaced the track and a running **nod** count.
- **Spotlight** is a deliberate "this is the one I want you to hear today" recommendation, capped at **3 per day per user**. The cap exists to make spotlighting feel significant — it's a friend's pick, not algorithmic noise.
- **Shelf** is the user's personal collection — tracks and (eventually) albums they've saved. Modelled on a vinyl collector's shelf: a personal, curated library separate from their Apple Music likes.
- **NearMe** is a Waze-style radar of nearby users on an animated map. Tap an avatar → see their recent + spotlit tracks, follow them, view their profile.
- **Profile** shows the user's spotlit 3 of the day, a swipeable historic spotlight carousel, and recent listens.
- **Search** has two top-level modes (People / Music). Music mode has a sub-toggle (All / Artists / Albums / Songs) with carousels for artists and albums. Briefly (2026-07-24, Tab Navigation 2.0) moved out of the tab bar into a screen-triggered overlay; reverted the same day to a real tab (`Tab(role: .search)`) matching Apple Music's own separated-search-tab pattern — see the decision log. Shelf keeps its own local library `.searchable` filter regardless.
- **Profile** is likewise no longer its own tab — it's a persistent entry point (avatar button, top-right) on every tab, pushed onto that tab's own navigation stack.

The differentiator is **real people, not algorithms**. Apple Music is the music backend; the *product* is the texture between users.

---

## 2. Hard-and-fast rules

These come up repeatedly. Don't violate them without an explicit user decision.

| Rule | Why |
|---|---|
| **All playback stays in-app.** Never open Music.app via deep link. | Nodalong IS the player. `ApplicationMusicPlayer.shared` routes audio through our session; lock-screen + Control Center show Nodalong's brand. |
| **No Spotify code.** Spotify is gone; don't propose adding it back. | The port is done. The `MusicService` protocol is the seam — Spotify can be re-added as a parallel conforming service later if needed. |
| **Don't add MusicKit-specific types to view models.** | They go behind `MusicService` (in `AppleMusicService` only). Views and view models speak `NodalongKit.Song` / `ArtistResult` / `AlbumResult`. |
| **Don't store MusicKit objects in Firestore.** | Always convert to `NodalongKit` models first. Firestore knows nothing about MusicKit. |
| **Feed is Following-only.** No "Your Music" toggle. | The user's own recents live on their Profile. Feed = others. |
| **Spotlight cap is 3 per day, today-only un-spotlight.** | This is product, not a perf limit. Don't increase or relax it. |
| **Preview playback is the fallback for non-subscribers.** | Subscriber → full track via `ApplicationMusicPlayer`. Non-subscriber → 30-second preview via `AVPlayer` from `Song.previewAssets`. Only `trackUnavailable = true` if BOTH fail. |

---

## 3. Architecture seam — `MusicService`

The crucial abstraction.

```swift
// NodalongKit/Sources/NodalongKit/Services/MusicService.swift
public protocol MusicService: AnyObject, Sendable {
    var serviceType: MusicServiceType { get }
    var isAuthenticated: Bool { get }

    func authorize() async throws
    func logout() async throws

    func getSavedTracks() async throws -> [Song]
    func getRecentlyPlayed() async throws -> [Song]

    func play(_ song: Song) async throws
    func resume() async throws
    func pause() async throws

    func searchTracks(_ query: String, maxResults: Int) async throws -> [Song]
    func searchArtists(_ query: String, maxResults: Int) async throws -> [ArtistResult]
    func searchAlbums(_ query: String, maxResults: Int) async throws -> [AlbumResult]
    func searchByIsrc(_ isrc: String) async throws -> SearchResult?
    func searchByTitleArtist(title: String, artist: String) async throws -> SearchResult?
    func searchAll(_ query: String, maxPerType: Int) async throws -> (artists: [ArtistResult], albums: [AlbumResult], tracks: [Song])

    func getArtistAlbums(artistId: String) async throws -> [AlbumResult]
    func getAlbumTracks(albumId: String, albumName: String, artwork: String) async throws -> [Song]
}
```

**Today there is exactly one conformer: `AppleMusicService`.** It uses MusicKit — every `MusicCatalogSearchRequest`, `ApplicationMusicPlayer.shared.play()`, `MusicSubscription.subscriptionUpdates` call lives in that file.

**To extend later** (e.g. YouTube Music): add a new class `YouTubeMusicService` conforming to `MusicService`, with its own underlying SDK calls. The protocol's `Sendable` + `AnyObject` requirements give you `@MainActor` class flexibility. Views inject `any MusicService` via `@EnvironmentObject` and don't care which backend is wired.

`MusicServiceType` enum keeps a `.spotify` case for forward-compat with a possible second backend — don't remove it.

## 4. Product status — what's built

Updated after the audit on 2026-06-06 (when sessions started running long enough that drift became a real risk).

| Pillar | % to vision | Key gaps |
|---|---|---|
| **Home** | ~85% (was ~75% before the 2026-09-07 pass) | Stations/Radio deferred (needs a new continuous-playback model, doesn't fit the fixed-queue player); on-device visual/spacing pass still needed |
| **Feed** | ~85% (was 75% before dropping Your Music) | Pagination (currently loads all in one shot) |
| **Shelf** | ~70% | First-class album saves; chronological-by-release-date sort within artist |
| **NearMe** | ~95% | (Follow button + multi-track sheet done 2026-06-06.) Minor: could surface follow-graph hints. |
| **Profile** | ~95% | (Historic spotlight carousel on other-user profiles done 2026-06-06.) |
| **Search** | ~95% (was 40% before overhaul on 2026-06-06) | Apple-Music-style artist detail (top songs / EPs / singles sections) |
| **Player** | ~95% (was 90% before the 2026-10-31 queue manager) | Queue / Up Next / skip ⏭⏮ done 2026-06-10; auto-queue-the-list on tap 2026-06-27; single queue manager + gapless natural/Control Center advances + reorder/remove/play-next without a blip done 2026-10-31 (see decisions log). Gaps: in-app ⏭/jump still reload the track (a short gap), no Play Next outside the "…" menu |

**Apple Music integration is functional** — auth, subscription state surfacing, preview fallback, search, library sync. See `MUSICKIT_PLAN.md` for the phased plan; Phases A1 and A2 are complete.

## 6. Conventions

**Preview-safety guards.** Any VM whose `init()` would touch Firebase, MusicKit, or system audio must guard like this:
```swift
init() {
    if ProcessInfo.processInfo.environment["XCODE_RUNNING_FOR_PREVIEWS"] != "1" {
        startFirebaseListener()   // or AVAudioSession, or subscription observer
    }
}
```

**Mock factories.** Every `ObservableObject` view model has a `static var mockPreview` in a `#if DEBUG` extension. New ones must too. They're injected by `View.withPreviewEnvironment()` in `PreviewFixtures.swift` — add your new VM there.

**`#Preview` blocks.** Every view has one at the bottom, wrapped in `#if DEBUG`. One-liner: `#Preview { YourView().withPreviewEnvironment() }`. If the view takes nav params (`UserProfileView(user:)`), pass a `PreviewFixtures.*` value.

**Doc comments.** Every exported type / function / property gets a `///` comment. Apple-style: what, when, why. The existing files set the standard — match their voice.

**Conversion helpers.** `Post → Song`, `LibraryTrackDoc → Song`, etc. live in `Nodalong/Shared/SongConversions.swift`. If you need a new one, add it there with a doc comment.

**Firestore collection name** is `tracks` (not `catalog`). The code and ERD agree.

## 7. Recent build status

`xcodebuild` succeeds with **two known warnings** that are pre-existing and unrelated to our work:

- `FirebaseFirestoreInternal` Sendable warnings in `Follows.swift` + `Posts.swift`. Resolvable by adding `@preconcurrency import` if it ever annoys someone, but it's just a warning.

Anything else failing is yours to fix.

---

## 8. Things to ALWAYS do

- **Run `xcodebuild`** after any non-trivial change. Build green = ship-ready baseline.
- **Run `xcodegen generate`** if you add a new file. The `.xcodeproj` is generated, not committed; new files won't be picked up otherwise.
- **Read this file** at the start of every session. It updates as decisions land.

## 9. Things to ALWAYS check before suggesting work

- Is this **already on `MUSICKIT_PLAN.md`**? If yes, defer to that prioritisation.
- Does this **conflict with a rule in Section 2**? If yes, push back, don't just do it.
- Does this **add MusicKit types outside `AppleMusicService`**? If yes, route through `MusicService` instead.

- **2026-10-31 — Playback queue rebuilt around one queue manager; every reported desync between "what's shown", Up Next and what actually plays (incl. Control Center) fixed and confirmed on device, phase by phase. Build-green, 400/400 NodalongKit tests. Full write-up, device logs and per-phase plans in the side-session notes folder (`queue-manager-plan.md`, `phase0-findings.md`, `phase3/4/5-plan.md`).**
  **Architecture.** (1) `QueueState` + `reduceQueue(_:_:)` in NodalongKit (`Utils/QueueState.swift`, 42 tests) is the ONLY place queue order/membership/current can change; current is held by `QueueItem.id`, never an index. `PlayerViewModel.send(_:)` runs the reducer, publishes `queue`/`currentIndex` (now mirrors), persists, and for pure edits runs the native mirror + prefetch; `applyQueueEffect` carries out `.load/.adopt/.restart/.stop`. Tap-in-album/playlist still replaces the whole queue (`.replace`). `QueueView` edits by id from the same `upNext` snapshot it renders. (2) ONE serial native job chain (`enqueueNative`, `nativeJobs`) for every load AND every mirror sync — they used to be serialised by two unrelated guards, so a mirror `insert` could overlap a load's `player.queue =` (the 2026-08-07 freeze class). `requestPlayCurrent` drops a superseded target and collapses duplicates. Never call `enqueueNative` from inside a job (deadlock) — inside one, call `performNativeQueueSync()`/`playCurrent()` directly. (3) MusicKit's own moves (natural end, Control Center next/previous, multi-track jumps while locked) are only acted on while `isSettled` (`nativeJobs == 0 && loadedItemID == currentID`).
  **🔴 The finding that reshaped the design: MusicKit re-issues queue-entry ids on its own, 0.2–3 s after they're inserted** (device capture). The 2026-08 id-keyed `mirroredEntries` therefore matched almost nothing: every advance fell back to title/artist matching, which can't see a removed track or a backward move. Replaced by POSITIONAL tracking: `nativeLayout: [UUID]?` mirrors the native queue's order (we only ever replace it with one track, or insert at `.tail`/`.afterCurrentEntry`, so we always know it), double-checked against metadata, nil = lost → metadata fallback until the next load. **Lesson: the id design looked right on paper and was dead on device — a 4-line log at the decision point would have shown it in one capture.**
  **Write-ahead** (`performNativeQueueSync`): when the very NEXT native entry isn't `upNext.first` (after a reorder, removal or Play Next), insert the correct 3-track window with `.afterCurrentEntry` (anchors on the playing, never-transient entry; verified to land right after current). Only for the next slot — rewriting whenever anything deeper differed re-inserts on every track change (the displaced tail always differs) and grows the queue unbounded; deeper mismatches are caught by `nativeHopIsConsistent()` when MusicKit actually moves. Capped at `maxNativeEntries = 40`; every load resets the native queue. Tail top-up only when what's ahead is a prefix of the window. All inserts go through `insertIntoNativeQueue` — still never mutate `player.queue.entries`. Side effect: calling `ApplicationMusicPlayer.shared.queue.insert` directly removed a pre-existing Sendable warning (PlayerViewModel now at 3).
  **Bugs fixed, each confirmed in a device log:** reorder/remove/Play Next then song end played the OLD next song for 0.4–0.7 s (now gapless, 12:39–12:41 2026-10-31); Clear then end let the cleared song play in FULL under the previous title, uncorrected (now detected → stop); Control Center previous was never followed (now adopted backwards); a load finishing after the user had skipped on played under the newer title (re-check after `play()`; pauses if dismissed mid-load); "skipped song comes back" path closed by `isSettled`; double-advance and ▶/⏸ flicker from the end-of-track confirm (now checks the stopped item is still current, re-reads MusicKit's live status, ignores edges within 1 s of our own load and 1.5 s of a pause/play tap — `lastToggleAt`, which a resume doesn't clear); in-app pause flicker (the "something resumed it" reconcile now waits 1 s after our own pause); party reconcile threw away history so Control Center previous went FORWARD (history kept); Up Next edits by offset could hit the wrong row if the track advanced mid-drag (ids now); a count-based "stale" verdict after stepping back made a fast double next land one track short (stale = entries ahead aren't a prefix of Up Next).
  **Removed:** `isAdvancing`, `pendingReconcile`, `loadTask`/`loadGeneration`, `isSyncingNativeQueue`/`pendingNativeQueueSync*`, `mirroredEntries`, `handleNativeEntryChange`, the timer's ~150-line metadata-hop branch, `classifyNativeAdvance` and the rest of `QueueMath.swift` (+ tests). Phase-0 debug tooling (`queueLog`/`queue.log`, the 🧪 Queue-sheet button) removed; `logQueue` kept as field-log-only `[queue]` lines (ids/indices only, privacy-safe) for future "Share diagnostics" reads.
  **Still open:** in-app ⏭/jump still reload a single entry (short gap) — native advances are gapless; the exact "Control Center previous on track 3+, then next twice fast" repro was never exercised on device (the fix's logic was, via a two-track jump). `nativeQueueMirrorStale` now stays true after any write-ahead (the displaced tail) by design; only the layout-lost fallback reads it.

- **2026-10-30 (later) — Three follow-ups to the prefetch pass, all found on device; two were mine. Build-green, installed; the profile fix is NOT yet confirmed by eye.**
  **🔴 Regression 1 (mine): seeding a screen in `init` skipped its reveal animation.** `AlbumDetailSearchView` rows render at `opacity(rowsRevealed ? 1 : 0)` and `rowsRevealed` only flips on a `tracks.count` CHANGE; a list seeded in `init` never changes, so every cached album showed a correctly sized, completely invisible tracklist. Fixed by seeding `rowsRevealed = true` too. **Before seeding any view's state in `init`, grep it for state that is only set by an `onChange`/`onAppear` reacting to that data arriving.**
  **🔴 Regression 2 (mine): a paging `TabView` that already has its pages when first built inside a scroll view draws BLANK** until something rebuilds it. Hit by the artist page's Top Songs when seeded in `init` (that seeding was reverted — `loadData` reads the sync peek instead), and it was the real cause of Profile's "empty, then appears": Spotlight and Recent Tracks carousels are seeded from the snapshot. New `.remountOnAppear()` (`Shared/UI/RemountOnAppear.swift`) rebuilds the pager once after it appears; applied to both carousels. Browse-time hero-image prefetching was also pulled back to high-intent spots only (player current/next, "…" menu) — warming 600px art for every settled tile competed with real taps.
  **🔴 Pre-existing, root-caused from a device log: two caches shared one snapshot file.** `FirebaseAuthViewModel` saves the signed-in `UserProfile` under `SnapshotStore` key `"profile"`, and `ProfileView` saved its screen snapshot under `"profile"` too, so each overwrote the other. Log on open: `ProfileDiag init: snap=nil peek=nil` → refresh 300ms later. Consequences: your profile opened blank after every launch; after any profile visit the next launch couldn't restore the user, so the instant-launch splash skip failed AND `ContentView`'s one-shot own-profile warm-up ran with a nil uid and skipped for the whole session (the `peek=nil`). Fixes: `ProfileView.snapshotKey = "profileScreen"`; the warm-up is now `.task(id: authVM.currentUser?.uid)`; the Tracks stat reads `libraryVM` (Shelf's VM isn't loaded until Shelf is visited, so it read 0). **Lesson: `SnapshotStore` keys are a flat namespace per uid with no type check, and a decode mismatch fails silently via `try?` — grep every existing key before adding one.** The first launch after this still opens Profile cold (the new file doesn't exist yet).
  **Method:** three rounds of code-reading said the profile path was correct; a pulled device file decoded fine through the real models in a throwaway test; one 4-line diagnostic capture settled it. `devicectl … --console` needs the phone unlocked at launch, and macOS has no `timeout` — `perl -e 'alarm N; exec @ARGV' …` bounds the capture.

- **2026-10-30 — Prefetch pass: a warm cache now actually produces an instant screen, plus 9 new prefetch sites. Build-green, no new warnings in touched files, 370/370 NodalongKit tests. NOT verified on a device.**
  **The finding that mattered most was not a missing prefetch.** Most tap sites were already warmed, but a cache hit still painted a loading state: (1) `AlbumDetailSearchView`/`ArtistDetailSearchView` read `MusicDetailCache` through an actor `await` in `.task`, so even a 100% hit drew one skeleton frame and cross-faded out; (2) `ResolvingAlbumView`/`ResolvingArtistView`/`ResolvingArtistsView` started with `resolved == nil` and only filled it from `.task` — a guaranteed placeholder plus a 0.25s fade on EVERY track-anchored tap (player, "…" menu, Jump Back In, Friends Listening, Shelf header), which `CatalogPrefetch.artistByName`'s own comment had already noted no prefetch could remove. **Fix: synchronous peek mirrors.** The `PlaylistPeek` pattern was generalised to `Peek<V>` and every dictionary a screen reads at init (`albumTracks`, `albumPending`, `artistCore`, `artistByName`, `resolvedAlbums`, new `resolvedArtistsByKey`) mirrors itself via `didSet`, so it can't drift; `didSet` doesn't fire in `init`, so snapshot restore mirrors by hand. The detail screens seed their state in `init`, and the resolving views take a `cached:` value read at push time. The actor `peekCore` is gone (replaced by `peekCoreSync`). No `Snapshot` change, so no schema bump. A pre-release album whose refresh is due is deliberately NOT served by `peekAlbumTracks`, so release-day refresh still runs.
  **Track-anchored resolves now share one cache keyed by uri** (`pendingResolveKey`). Before, only the currently playing track was ever warm, and every other resolve result was thrown away after use. The player's own prefetch seeds the same keys.
  **🔴 Real bug found on the way:** `PendingAlbumResolve` carried no track title, so `resolvePendingAlbum` → `resolveAlbum` fell back to searching for a SONG named after the ALBUM — the exact 2026-08-19 bug, fixed for Home then but still live for cold album taps from the player and the "…" menu. It now carries `trackTitle`.
  **Existing paths tuned:** the player's 2s debounce is skipped (and a still-sleeping one restarted) once the full player is open; the chained artist prefetch no longer adds a second 0.4s settle; the player's album/artist taps push the real `AlbumResult`/`ArtistResult` when already prefetched; Home's private 1s `prefetchAlbumResolve` became the shared `CatalogPrefetch.albumResolve` on the standard 0.4s settle; `resolveDetailedSong`'s single-entry memo became an 8-entry one (prefetching other tracks would otherwise evict the playing track's).
  **New prefetch sites:** "…" menu open → album + artist + artist page (`CatalogPrefetch.pending`, no settle — opening the menu is the intent); New Releases + New Music Daily (`releaseRail` never had one despite its comment claiming `AlbumsCarousel`'s); Jump Back In's Apple recently-played albums (prefetch lives on `AlbumsCarousel`, not `AlbumCard`); every Home See All grid; Recommended See All (10-group request warmed 3s after Home loads, passed in via `init`); playlist tracks for your own unopened manual playlists and for other people's (`PlaylistTracksCache.prefetch` — first 6, once per session, version-checked; others' go to a memory-only session map and an opened one skips its refetch); Friends' Spotlights profiles; a party remote's hero artist. **Images:** `ArtworkMemoryCache.prefetch`/`prefetchHero` decode the destination's exact rendition (album hero, artist hero, full-player hero for the current AND next track); `prefetchHero` warms both the square and vinyl-label size, since those can land in different buckets.
  **Costs to watch:** C6 is billed Firestore reads (capped); image warming adds decoded bitmaps to the 64MB cache (bounded per track change/tile).
  **NOT verified on device.** Check: the full player's album/artist tap and the "…" menu's Album/Artist land with no placeholder after a second's pause; New Releases/See All tiles open populated; another user's playlist shows tracks on frame 1; ⏭ in the full player shows artwork immediately; a pre-release album still refreshes; lock-screen skips still resolve nothing (debounce intact while collapsed). `MusicDetailCache … cache hit` diag lines are the evidence.

- **2026-10-06 (field log findings) — The first real field log (~5 days) explained the "mysterious stops", found one genuine app bug, and led to three changes. Build-green, installed on the device; none of the three has fired in the field yet.**
  **What the log showed.** (1) **Bluetooth dropping is the dominant cause**: ~8 mid-track stops line up with `routeChange oldDeviceUnavailable BluetoothA2DPOutput → Speaker` (2 Oct 19:08; 3 Oct 11:11, 12:37, 16:32; 5 Oct 19:55, 22:13; 6 Oct 08:03, 08:11). iOS pauses when the active output vanishes — standard behaviour, not an app bug — and the pause often lands 1–5s BEFORE the route notification, consistent with the link dropping. Deliberately NOT "fixed": auto-resuming when a device reconnects is against Apple's convention and risks sound coming out of a speaker unexpectedly. (2) **Two interruptions** (5 Oct 10:06, 6 Oct 08:31): `playing → interrupted`, then `paused` 40–50s later and stayed paused. **Our audio-session interruption observer NEVER fired for these — `ApplicationMusicPlayer` plays in a separate process, so this app's `AVAudioSession` never sees them.** That is why the cause was invisible. (3) **`MEDIA SERVICES LOST/RESET`** on 6 Oct 08:03 — iOS's media daemon died (the phone is on iOS 27.0, apparently a beta); not ours. (4) **Not memory**: 60–130 MB while playing, ~3 GB headroom. MetricKit's "killed for MEMORY" counts are suspended-app reclaim, and the "ended in background while playing" verdicts on 2 Oct were **my own installs killing the app** — ignore them. (5) MusicKit pauses/resumes itself via its own remote-command handling; `[remote] pauseCommand` never logs.
  **The one app-side bug (3 Oct 13:44):** a play tapped as the app went to the background timed out (8s), fell back to the 30s preview, audio-session activation failed with `!int` (`cannotInterruptOthers` — iOS refuses `setActive(true)` from the background), then MetricKit reported `CPU EXCEPTION totalCPU=48s over 49s` and the app was killed. A ~50s hot loop in the background. **Root cause NOT found**: the exported call stack is mostly system frames (one library, one address repeated 8× — a deep recursion), and the build's symbols were gone, so it could not be symbolicated.
  **Changes.** (1) **Sharper diagnostics**: `FieldLog.audioSnapshot()` (route port types, `isOtherAudioPlaying`, `secondaryAudioShouldBeSilencedHint`) is attached to every non-playing native status line and to the "stopped mid-track" warning; a 10s CPU sampler (`rusage`, % of one core) logs `[cpu] ⚠️ HIGH CPU` at ≥70% — first sample, then every third — with phase/playing/memory, plus "back to normal"; the heartbeat now carries `cpuMax`. (2) **No preview fallback from the background**: if `ApplicationMusicPlayer.play` fails while `applicationState != .active` and the audio session was never activated, it now fails cleanly (`isPlaying=false`, `playerLoaded=false` so the next tap reloads) instead of starting a preview that cannot get a session. This is a mitigation of a suspected trigger, not a confirmed fix for the spike. (3) **Resume after an interruption**: on `interrupted → paused`, resume via a bounded `play()` only if we believed we were playing, no pause of ours landed during it (`lastExplicitPauseAt > interruptedSince`), nothing is loading/advancing, and it lasted <10 min (`maxAutoResumeAfterInterruption`). Every decision, resumed or not, is logged.
  **Risks to watch:** the resume could fight another app that legitimately took over (it only fires once that interruption has ended, but `NOT resuming` lines are the evidence to check); and if the CPU spike recurs the `[cpu]` lines should now give a timeline, which is the whole point.
  **NOT verified in the field:** all three. Next step is to read the next export for `[cpu]`, `interruption ended`, and `in the BACKGROUND` lines.

- **2026-10-02 (queue QOL) — "Clear" on the queue screen, and the LP setup sheet now shows what is playing and the queue it will share. Build-green, zero warnings in the touched files, installed on the device; not yet seen.**
  **Clear:** `PlayerViewModel.clearUpNext()` removes everything after `currentIndex` (the playing track keeps playing), then does the same follow-ups `removeUpNext` does — `persistQueue`, `syncSpotifyNativeQueue`, `syncNativeQueueNeighbors` — so mirrored neighbours never point at removed entries. The button sits opposite the "Up Next" title and only shows when something is queued. **No confirmation, deliberately, by the user's request** — the action is not undoable. No party handling needed: `NowPlayingView`'s queue button opens the LP screen instead of this one while a party is active.
  **LP setup sheet:** Now Playing section (below the name, above "Who can add songs"; a quiet line when nothing is playing) and a read-only "Up Next · N" section below the permission picker. It is read-only because it is a preview of exactly what `createAndEnter` seeds — the playing track followed by `upNext` — so reordering belongs on the queue screen beforehand. `QueueRow` went from private to internal so both screens share it.
  **Not verified on device:** the Clear button's alignment in the section header against `.insetGrouped` list chrome (uppercase header styling was overridden with `.textCase(nil)`), and that the setup sheet reads well with a long queue.

- **2026-10-02 (spotlight) — A missing `catalogId` no longer blocks spotlighting. Only two things can: the track is already one of today's picks (then the control removes it), or the daily limit is reached. Build-green, installed on the device; not yet confirmed by the user.**
  **Root cause:** `FirebaseAuthViewModel.spotlightDisabled` was `catalogId.isEmpty || (atSpotlightLimit && !todaySpotlitIds.contains(catalogId))`. `catalogId` is a Nodalong/Firestore concept — a track started from Search, an album page or Top Songs comes straight from Apple Music with none (`toSong` never sets it) — so the Now Playing star and the "…" menu's Spotlight row were disabled for most tracks a user starts themselves. The empty-id clause was there for a real reason (a spotlight keyed by `""` can never be matched back to its track), but disabling was the wrong fix.
  **Knock-on bug in the same clause:** an already-spotlit track with no id showed as un-spotlit, so it couldn't be removed from that control, and at the cap it was disabled outright.
  **Fix:** (1) `todaySpotlight(matching: Song)` decides "already spotlit" LOCALLY with no network — by `catalogId` when present, else by the spotlight's stored `uri`, else by normalised title + `primaryArtist` (each spotlight already stores all three). (2) `canSpotlight(_:)` = already-a-pick OR under the limit. (3) `toggleSpotlight(_:)` removes if already a pick, otherwise gets a `catalogId` at TAP time via `ensureCatalogId(for:)` — the same thing the Shelf heart does — then calls `addToSpotlight`. A re-entry guard keyed on `uri` stops a double-tap during the catalog round trip from adding it twice. Cheap on repeat: `ensureCatalogId` consults `TrackCache` before writing. (4) `spotlightDisabled(catalogId:)` is deleted; `NowPlayingView` and `TrackActionsSheet` both use `canSpotlight`/`toggleSpotlight`. Now Playing builds its `Song` from the queue's own item when the uri matches (it carries ISRC/genres/track number), else rebuilds one from `NowPlayingTrack` — e.g. while tuned in to a broadcast with no local queue. This also fixes `NowPlayingView`'s old doc builder, which dropped ISRC and genres.
  **Audited:** these two are the only spotlight-adding controls. Feed/Profile/Shelf/carousels only DISPLAY spotlit state and use posts/library docs that already carry a `catalogId`.
  **Known cost:** the first spotlight of a never-seen track waits on one catalog transaction (probably a few hundred ms); there is no optimistic star for it, only for the add that follows. **Not verified:** that a track started from Search can now be spotlit from Now Playing, that an already-spotlit one shows gold and can be removed at the cap, and that the title+artist fallback never matches a genuinely different recording (a live version vs the studio one could, since `stripSearchNoise` removes "(Live)").

- **2026-10-02 (tabs) — Re-tapping the tab you are already on: back first, then scroll to top (Home/Feed/Shelf/Search), and Search also focuses its field. Build-green, installed on the device; behaviour NOT yet confirmed by the user.**
  **Step 0 was a device experiment, not an assumption, and it paid for itself.** The risk was whether iOS 26/27's `Tab` API calls the `TabView` selection setter at all when the tab is already selected. A build that only LOGGED the setter showed it does, with a value equal to the current one — `reTap=true` — and the log also showed something nobody had written: after a Home re-tap at depth 1, the next line read `depth=0`. **iOS pops a `NavigationStack` to root on a re-tap by itself**, which is where the pre-existing "tapping Home goes back" behaviour came from. (Device was iOS 27.0.)
  **Design:** the `TabView` selection is a wrapper `Binding` whose setter calls `AppRouter.tabSelectionSet`; an equal value routes to `tabReselected`. That checks the stack depth BEFORE doing anything: depth > 0 → `removeLast(depth)` to the root and stop (the tap that takes you back never also scrolls); depth 0 → bump `scrollToTopRequests[tab]`, a per-tab counter (a counter, not a Bool, so two consecutive requests are distinct values). **The pop is explicit even though iOS does it for Home**, because that was observed on one tab only; the double pop (ours, then the system's) is harmless since both land on an empty path. Pop is to ROOT, per the user.
  Each scrolling tab watches its own counter: Home and Feed use `ScrollPosition(edge: .top)` + `.scrollPosition($…)` (no anchor needed in a lazy stack); Shelf and Search reuse their existing `ScrollViewReader` + `.id("top")`. **Search additionally sets a `@FocusState` on the query field**, so a second tap means "I want to search". Near Me has no scroll view, so for it a re-tap only pops (its counter bumps with no observer).
  **Not verified:** that the scroll actually reaches the top on each tab (Home is a lazy stack under a `ScrollPosition`, the least certain), that the keyboard comes up on Search without fighting the system's own search-tab behaviour, and that a re-tap while a `fullScreenCover` is up does nothing odd. Also: the step-0 `[tabs]` log line was removed once it had answered the question.

- **2026-10-02 (last) — Artist page is first-page-then-APPEND, reversing the "load complete, reveal once" trade-off recorded in the entry below, at the user's request. Build-green, no new warnings; not verified on a device.**
  **Why reversed:** the cold-page wait (~0.5–1s) was judged worse than the alternative. The earlier entry's "complete then reveal" is therefore SUPERSEDED for the artist page (Home's batched commit is unaffected).
  **How it works now:** warm (a `MusicDetailCache.peekCore` hit — visited or prefetched): the complete lists show immediately. Cold: new `AppleMusicService.getArtistFirstPage` (ONE request, first relationship page of each — the old `getArtistCore` body) paints at once, while `getArtistCore` (the complete version, still what the cache, prefetch and `PlaylistGenerator` use) runs CONCURRENTLY, not queued behind it. Awaiting the first-page task before the append task is what guarantees the append can never land first and be overwritten.
  **The rule that fixes the original complaint is append-only:** what the first page showed never moves; additions go after it (`appendingNew` for songs, `appendingNewAlbums` for albums). The old bug was REPLACING the lists, which re-sorted Discography and grew the Top Songs pager.
  **Consequences to know:** (1) **Discography is no longer strictly newest-first** once the rest lands — the first page's albums come first in their own order, then the remainder newest-first. That is the price of never moving what's on screen; a purely sorted list would need a re-sort. (2) Appended albums skip any whose `canonicalAlbumTitle` matches one already shown, because the first page was edition-deduped on its own and the full list can pick a different edition as primary — otherwise the same record would appear twice. The same rule also hides a single and an album sharing a base title, which `dedupeAlbumEditions` already did within one list. (3) Top Songs arrows exist from the start for any artist with more than 5 first-page songs (virtually all), so the pager gaining pages causes no layout shift; the `topSongsComplete` gate stays deleted. (4) Similar Artists / Features On still assign when they land; Features On feeds the Discovery tab, so that tab can grow at its end.
  **Not covered by tests:** the two append helpers live in the app target (`ArtistDetailSearchView.swift`), so they have no unit tests — worth moving into NodalongKit if this logic changes again.
  **NOT verified:** that nothing visibly shifts on a cold artist, and that appended albums don't surprise anyone by landing out of date order.

- **2026-10-02 (later) — Home/artist/Shelf pass: Home is one long lazy scroll (cap removed), the pop-in is gone by design on Home and the artist page, New Releases actually refreshes, and a New Music Daily rail was added. Build-green, zero new warnings, 281/281 tests, launches on a simulator. NOT verified on a real signed-in device — none of the visible behaviour has been seen.**
  **New Releases was stale for three independent reasons.** (1) `hasLoaded` meant Home loaded ONCE per process, and iOS keeps a suspended app alive for days — Feed refreshes on foreground-return, Home never did. Now `scenePhase → .active` after >6h (`lastLoadedAt`) reloads. (2) It only asked about the same 30 artists (top by track count — nearly static). Now those ∪ the 30 most recently saved ∪ recent plays, deduped by `primaryArtist`, capped at 60, requested in concurrent chunks of 25. (3) 🔴 `guard !releases.isEmpty else { return }` meant an empty result never cleared a restored snapshot, so a stale rail could never go away. `getNewReleases` now returns `[AlbumResult]?` — **nil = the request failed (keep what's showing), [] = genuinely nothing new (clear it)**; nil only when EVERY chunk fails. Window 120→60 days. **Lesson: a rail whose "no data" branch is an early `return` can never become empty — check what a fresh-but-empty answer does, not just a failed one.**
  **There is no new-releases feed to scrape, verified rather than assumed.** MusicKit has no such request (checked in the SDK earlier); Apple's public RSS generator (`rss.marketingtools.apple.com/api/v2/<cc>/music/<type>/<n>/albums.json`) returned 200 only for `most-played` — `new-releases`/`new-music`/`hot-tracks` 404, `coming-soon` timed out; scraping music.apple.com was rejected (terms, fragility, review risk). So the non-personal rail is Apple's editorial **New Music Daily** playlist: `getNewMusicDaily` tries the global id `pl.2b0e6e332fdf4b7a91164da3162127b5` (confirmed to be Apple Music's "New Music Daily", ~84 songs), falls back to a name+curator search, takes the first 60 songs, and resolves their albums with ONE batched `MusicCatalogResourceRequest<Song>` using `properties = [.albums]` (a playlist track carries no album id), restoring playlist order since `memberOf` doesn't promise it. New `HomeSectionID.newMusicDaily`, base rank 12 (ranks must stay unique — a test enforces it), no see-all. **Unverified against a real storefront: the id lookup and the fallback search compile (typechecked against the iOS 26.1 SDK) but have never run.**
  **Section cap removed; the container is a `LazyVStack`.** The cap was also what kept Home's open cost down — the outer container was a plain `VStack`, so every section's tiles were built at open. Removing the cap without making it lazy would have made the stutter worse. The rails inside were already `LazyHStack`.
  **🔴 The pop-in has one cause, applied twice: showing partial data and then replacing it.** Home: six fetches each applied the instant they landed, over a snapshot already on screen — Jump Back In gained its second half, rails swapped contents, and sections re-sorted between fresh/not-fresh so a section could JUMP POSITION. Now `HomeViewModel.beginBatch/commitBatch/stage`: fetches stage a commit closure, everything applies in one MainActor turn (one render, order decided once), with a 4s deadline so one slow source can't hold the rest — later arrivals apply directly. `HomeView.load` owns the window so new-releases / genre-chart / New Music Daily are inside it too, and the snapshot is persisted AFTER the commit (persisting in `load()`'s defer would have saved pre-commit data). `isBatchOpen` keeps first-launch skeletons up until the commit. **Not covered: rails derived from `feedVM`/`libraryVM` (friends listening, most nodded, recently added) still update when those VMs do** — they usually return identical data, but a genuine change still shows.
  Artist page: `getArtistCore` fetched only the FIRST relationship page and the view topped it up in the background, so the Top Songs pager grew 2→6 pages and Discography was re-sorted when the full album list landed. **`getArtistCore` now fetches the complete top-30 and full discography concurrently (two requests, wall time = the slower one) and returns once**, `loadData` awaits core AND extras and assigns everything in one turn, and the loading state is a same-size shimmer (5×56+28pt card, ~380pt grid) so nothing below it moves. **The deliberate trade: a COLD artist page appears ~0.5–1s later than before, once; warm/prefetched pages are unaffected.** This reverses an earlier measured optimisation (pagination was 55–74% of the old call) — chosen for stability over first paint, and recorded so it isn't "optimised" back. Consequences: the view's top-up tasks, `topSongsComplete`, and `MusicDetailCache.updateTopTracks/updateAlbums` are gone; `PlaylistGenerator`'s own top-up block was redundant and removed; **`MusicDetailCache.schemaVersion` 2→3**, because on-disk `artistCore` entries held only a first page and would have been served forever as if complete (the versionless-cache trap this log already documents). `alternates(for:)` also calls `core()`, so opening an album now warms the artist's full detail — heavier, but cached and it warms the artist page.
  **Artist prefetch is now capped at 3 in flight** (`PrefetchGate`, an actor handing slots straight to the next waiter) because an artist fetch is heavier and a screen of artist tiles could start them all on the one MusicKit connection playback shares. A real tap never waits on it.
  **Shelf Artists/Genres were centred** because moving them off `List` to `LazyVStack` lost `List`'s free leading alignment; rows hug their content and a `LazyVStack` centres anything narrower. `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)` + `.contentShape(Rectangle())` on both rows.
  **Stutter is NOT measured.** Suspects addressed structurally (eager sections, per-change re-renders from nine observed objects, prefetch bursts), but no hang-detector trace was taken before or after. Next step is a signed-in device run with `MainThreadHangDetector` on Home and an artist page, not more guessing.
  **NOT verified on device:** that Home no longer rearranges after load; that the artist page reveals once with no shift and how long a cold open really takes; that New Releases now changes; that New Music Daily loads (and for a non-US storefront); that scroll feels smoother with no cap.

- **2026-10-02 — Field diagnostics: an always-on, privacy-safe flight recorder + MetricKit, so "playback mysteriously stopped while I was out" leaves evidence. Build-green, zero new warnings, verified writing/reading its log on a simulator; NOT yet exercised on a real playback stop.**
  **What existed before:** nothing for the field. `DiagLog.swift` is debug-only by design (it compiles to nothing in Release so testers' devices don't log track titles/uids) and `devicectl --console` needs the phone plugged in and unlocked, so a stop that happens in a pocket was invisible. No Crashlytics/MetricKit/exception handler anywhere.
  **Why not just a crash reporter:** the stops are probably not crashes. The likely causes leave different traces — iOS killing a backgrounded app for memory (NO crash report; the app just starts fresh), audio interruptions/route changes, MusicKit going `.paused`/`.stopped` behind our back, play timeouts, skipped tracks. A crash SDK catches none of the first four, which is also why Crashlytics was deliberately left out (extra package, dSYM upload, off-device data, and it wouldn't explain a silent stop).
  **Shipped (`Nodalong/Services/FieldDiagnostics.swift`):** (1) `FieldLog.event(category, message, withMemory:)` — thread-safe, size-capped (200 KB, rotates to `field.old.log`) file in `Library/Application Support/Diagnostics/`, ALWAYS on. **Privacy contract, enforced by convention: only fixed strings and numbers — never titles, artists, search text, uids, usernames, locations or party ids.** Errors go through `FieldLog.tag(error)` (`type/domain#code`), never `localizedDescription`, which can embed a title. Audio route logs port TYPES only, never device names. (2) **Previous-session analysis at launch:** each phase/playing/memory change and a 60s heartbeat write a snapshot to `UserDefaults`; the next launch compares it and writes a verdict — "ENDED IN BACKGROUND WHILE PLAYING" (iOS memory kill or force-quit, not a crash) vs "ENDED IN FOREGROUND" (crash/watchdog/force-quit) vs "clean terminate". **Honest limit: iOS gives no callback for a kill or force-quit, so those two are indistinguishable from the snapshot alone** — MetricKit's exit counts are what separate them. (3) **Observers:** audio interruption (began/ended, reason, shouldResume), route change (reason + port types), media services lost/reset, memory warning, scene phase, thermal, Low Power Mode, network path, plus a 60s heartbeat that logs only while playing or backgrounded (the last heartbeat before a gap is effectively the time of death; the mem/headroom trend across them shows a leak vs a spike). Memory lines carry `phys_footprint` and `os_proc_available_memory()` headroom (reads 0 on the simulator, so it's omitted there). (4) **Player hooks in `PlayerViewModel`** — `isPlaying` `didSet` mirrors into the log; native `MusicPlayer` status transitions with elapsed/duration/believedPlaying/loading/advancing (this is the line that says "ended naturally" vs "stopped mid-track by something else"); load start, resolve failure, play failure → preview fallback, preview failure, skip-unplayable, queue exhausted, track-end advance/finish, the stalled-state reconcile branches, toggle, and remote pause/play/next. (5) **MetricKit subscriber** (`MetricKitSubscriber`): daily metrics incl. `applicationExitMetrics` — `memoryResourceLimit`/`memoryPressure` in the BACKGROUND bucket are exactly the jetsam kills — plus crash/hang/CPU-exception diagnostics, summarised in the log with the full JSON saved (newest 5) and included in the export. **MetricKit only delivers for real installs (TestFlight/App Store/ad-hoc), at most daily, usually the launch AFTER the event — absence of a report from an Xcode run proves nothing.** (6) **Profile → Diagnostics → "Share diagnostics"** (`ShareLink` over a `Transferable` that builds the file lazily at share time): one text file with device/OS/app version, earlier + current log, and the MetricKit JSON.
  **Two bugs caught while building it, both worth knowing:** `event()` formatted its timestamp via a helper that did `queue.sync` — called from inside `queue.async` on the same serial queue, a guaranteed deadlock; it would have hung the first log line. And `applicationState` in `didFinishLaunching` reads `.background` on a normal scene-based launch, so a "background launch" label was wrong on every run — removed rather than trusted.
  **How to use it:** after a mystery stop, Profile → Share diagnostics, and read the lines marked ⚠️ first. Look for, in order: a `[launch]` verdict, `MEMORY WARNING` or falling `headroom` in the last heartbeats, `audio interruption BEGAN` / `routeChange oldDeviceUnavailable`, `native status playing→paused` with `believedPlaying=true` and time remaining, and `play failed` / `MEDIA SERVICES LOST`.
  **NOT verified:** that a real stop produces a readable trail — the simulator proves the file is written, the launch verdict works and the build is clean, but none of the audio-interruption, route-change, MetricKit or player-status paths have fired on a device. Needs a TestFlight build and a few days of ordinary use. Not done: an in-app viewer (export only), Crashlytics, and anything for the Spotify branch beyond the shared `isPlaying` mirror.

- **2026-09-15 (last) — 🔴 TWO BUGS I SHIPPED IN THE PREVIOUS ROUND, both reported immediately: unreleased tracks were still tappable (my own tolerant default defeated my own fix, via a versionless disk cache), and PROFILE WAS UNREACHABLE (I deleted every avatar button and never re-added it). Both fixed; plus a pre-existing dead avatar tap on Search found while auditing. Build-green, 281/281, installed.**
  **🔴 Bug 1 — the unreleased-track fix silently did nothing on exactly the albums the user had already opened.** `getAlbumTracks` correctly reads `playParameters`, so the fetch path was right. The failure was one layer up: `MusicDetailCache` **disk-persists `albumTracks: [String: [Song]]` with no schema version** (~300 albums on the device), and `Song.isPlayable` decodes tolerantly as `?? true`. So every cached album restored as fully playable. The tolerant default is still correct in isolation — assuming an unknown track is unplayable would dim real music — but **a tolerant default plus a versionless cache produce a fix that appears not to work**, which is a sharper trap than either alone.
  Fixed twice over, deliberately: (a) `MusicDetailCache.schemaVersion` (now 2) with an older snapshot **discarded rather than restored** — its rows decode "successfully" with defaults for any field added since, which is worse than a cache miss because the defaults are silently wrong, and everything in there is re-fetchable catalog data; (b) the album screen's new `isUnreleased(_:)` checks the release **date** as well as `playParameters`. The date is what actually carries this: `albumReleaseDate` has always been populated, so it is correct on any vintage of stored row, and it self-corrects the moment the date passes — which no stored flag does. Same reasoning Shelf's album screen already used, now shared by both.
  **Lesson: when adding a field to a model that is persisted in a versionless cache, the cache is the first place to check — not the fetch path.** A "fix that does nothing" on previously-viewed data is the signature.
  **🔴 Bug 2 — Profile became unreachable, and this one was pure carelessness.** The approved plan said the avatar would float top-right on the tab roots via `screenControls`. Migrating the five tab roots I deleted the `ScreenHeader(...) { ProfileAvatarButton() }` line and **never added the replacement**. `.profileEntryPoint()` survived on four of them, but that modifier only registers the push DESTINATION — `ProfileAvatarButton` is the trigger, and every trigger was gone. The app shipped with no route into Profile at all, and the build was green because nothing references a deleted view.
  Restored on all five roots as `.screenControls(showsBack: false) { ProfileAvatarButton() }`. **Worth noting the plan named this exact risk** ("Profile is still reachable from all five tabs via the floating avatar") in its own verification list — so the gap was one an on-device walk would have caught, which is precisely why that list exists and why not walking it before reporting was the real mistake.
  **Found while auditing every avatar site: `SearchView` hosted a `ProfileAvatarButton` but never applied `.profileEntryPoint()`** — so tapping the avatar on the Search tab pushed an `OwnProfileRoute` no destination was registered for and did nothing. Pre-existing, unrelated to this session, invisible because a `NavigationLink` to an unregistered value fails silently. Registered now, so that tap works for the first time.
  **Open question left with the user, deliberately not decided unilaterally:** whether the avatar belongs floating at the top of every tab root (restored, matches the chevron's treatment) or in the tab bar (already-present chrome, zero content cost, and arguably the honest home for a top-level "you" destination now that the top band is gone). Restored the planned behaviour first so the app isn't broken while that's decided.

- **2026-09-15 (later) — Unreleased-album handling (the "Track 1 / Track 3" screenshot), album release dates, and 🔴 THE HEADER BAND REMOVED FROM EVERY SCREEN — which retired the collapsing-header system and with it this session's entire scroll-stutter saga. Build-green, zero new warnings, 281/281 tests, installed.**
  **Prompted by a screenshot** of Fontaines D.C.'s *Dopamine Chamber*: tracks listed as "Track 1", "Track 3", "Track 4" with only the released single ("Marianne", 3:45) carrying a real name — Apple's placeholders for unreleased tracks, with nothing on screen saying the record isn't out and every placeholder row tappable. Plus two questions: how often the Home rails refresh, and whether the top header could go.
  **Refresh cadence, answered from the code:** snapshot paints instantly on launch; the full 5-task load runs **once per app session** (`hasLoaded` guards on both `HomeView` and `HomeViewModel`); pull-to-refresh re-runs everything; a completed library sync refreshes **Jump Back In only**; generated playlists rebuild the single most-stale lens past 7 days. **The gap: Feed refreshes on foreground-return and Home does not**, so a backgrounded-but-not-killed app shows yesterday's charts until pulled. Offered and **declined** — pull-to-refresh stays.
  **API verified, not assumed.** MusicKit's `.swiftinterface` is a known partial stub (the `MeshGradient` trap), so a `swiftc -typecheck` probe at `arm64-apple-ios26.1` confirmed `Song.playParameters`, `Song.releaseDate`, `Album.playParameters`, `Album.releaseDate` — clean. **`playParameters == nil` is Apple's authoritative "not playable yet" signal**; inferring it from a missing duration would have been a guess.
  **🔴 The queue bug behind the feature.** The album row tap loaded the WHOLE track list, so tapping the one released single on a pre-release album queued ten unplayable tracks behind it — each failing to resolve and burning through `skipUnplayableAndAdvance`, one "Skipped (unavailable)" toast at a time. Now only playable tracks enter the queue, with the start index remapped into that filtered list.
  **🔴 Shelf's album screen deviates from the plan, deliberately.** The plan said "the same three changes", but `LibraryTrackDoc` is a Firestore document: a stored `isPlayable` would be **frozen at save time**, so an album pre-saved before release would stay marked unplayable forever unless something rewrote every row. It derives from `albumReleaseDate > now` instead — self-correcting the moment the date passes, and incapable of being stale in the direction that blocks real playback. A heuristic beating an authoritative-but-staleable flag is the right trade for saved data specifically.
  **🔴 THE HEADER: `ScreenHeader` is gone from all 23 call sites and deleted.** It reserved ~52pt at the top of every screen, frequently for nothing but a back chevron. New `.screenControls(...)` floats the chevron and any trailing control as an `.overlay(alignment: .top)`, consuming no layout space — so content starts at the top and artwork bleeds under the status bar. Titles that were a screen's ONLY identity (follow list — Followers vs Following is otherwise unanswerable; See All; genre; Recommended for You; the discography grid; "Choose Artist") moved into the scroll content as `ScreenTitle` or a native `Section` header. Profile's "Edit" moved next to the avatar it edits; **Feed's toolbar refresh button was dropped** — `.refreshable` already covered it.
  **The payoff worth recording: `CollapsingHeader` is deleted.** It existed solely to animate that band out of the way, and its insert/remove transition was measured from a device recording as the direct cause of ten 50–100ms scroll hitches. With no band there is nothing to collapse, so **that class of stutter is now structurally impossible rather than fixed** — along with the four failed geometry-feedback mechanisms, the self-feeding measurement, and the `[Nodalong.Header]` diagnostics. An in-content title scrolls away for free, which is exactly what the collapsing header spent four rounds faking.
  **Three load-bearing details that would each have broken silently:**
  - **`.enablesInteractiveSwipeBack()` lived on `ScreenHeader`.** Hiding the nav bar app-wide disabled UIKit's edge-swipe, and `SwipeBackGesture.swift` records that `PlaylistDetailView` depends on an ancestor's install. `screenControls` carries it forward; installing twice is idempotent.
  - **`ArtistDetailSearchView`'s chevron drives `popArtistRun`** and is its only trigger — dropping the `onBack` would have made artist-hopping cost n back presses with no error anywhere.
  - **The three auth screens pass `tint: textOnAccent`** because they sit on `bgDeep`, where the default dark ink measures ~2:1 in light mode — an on-device contrast bug this codebase has already had once.
  **Two self-inflicted slips, both caught by the compiler rather than shipped:** `@ViewBuilder` on a *stored property* makes the memberwise init demand a closure (it belongs only on the function parameter); and inserting helpers anchored on a function's signature line landed them **between an orphaned `@ViewBuilder` attribute and the function it decorated**, silently re-pointing it at a `String`-returning helper. **General lesson: anchor a scripted insertion above a declaration's attributes and doc comment, not on its signature.**
  **Also fixed in passing:** `Song.from(searchItem:)` was silently dropping `isExplicit`.
  **NOT verified on device — the header touches every screen, so this needs a real walk.** Specifically: edge-swipe-back from `PlaylistDetailView`; the artist page's back still collapsing a whole artist run; Profile reachable from all five tabs via the floating avatar; the four identity-carrying screens still saying what they are; the auth chevrons legible on `bgDeep`; floating controls not obscuring Home's greeting or an album hero; and a real pre-release album showing dimmed, dated, untappable rows with "11 tracks · Releases <date>" and no toast burst on tapping the released single.

- **2026-09-15 — Home review: 🔴 found and fixed an ORDERING INVERSION that put Apple's charts above the user's own listening on every quiet day, retired 3 duplicate sections (15 → 12), and killed a live dead-end. Build-green, zero new warnings, 279/279 tests, installed.**
  Prompted by the user asking why Top Charts and Top in Your City "look the same", whether there were other duplicates, and why a Continue Listening section exists when the mini player already shows the last played track. All three concerns were correct; checking them turned up something worse.
  **🔴 The inversion — the real find.** `HomeView.freshSections` marked both chart rails fresh on `!isEmpty`, which is always true. Every other freshness test is real (a spotlight *today*, a post within *24h*, a release within a *week*); this one was a tautology. Fresh sections sort above ALL non-fresh ones regardless of `baseRank`, so **on any day the user's follows were quiet, Home opened with two Apple chart rails above Live Now and Jump Back In** — the exact inversion `baseRank`'s doc comment exists to prevent, and the doc comment immediately above the offending lines *stated the rule the code broke*. Fixed with `HomeViewModel.freshCharts`: `previousChartIds` is captured at snapshot restore (what the user saw last launch) and `noteChartFreshness` compares the incoming result by `uri` membership — so a reshuffle of the same ten tracks is correctly not news, and a cold launch with no snapshot reads as NOT fresh (a new account shouldn't have charts pushed above its own listening).
  **Lesson worth keeping: an availability check wearing a freshness check's name.** `!isEmpty` looks like a guard and reads like one in review. The tell is that it can never be false once the feature works at all. Any freshness/staleness predicate should be checked against the question "when is this false?" — if the answer is "only when the feature is broken", it isn't testing what its name claims.
  **Top Charts dropped.** `.dailyGlobalTop` (storefront Daily Top 100) vs `.cityTop` (Top 25: city) are genuinely different Apple endpoints, but for a storefront whose capital is a quarter of the population they overlap almost entirely — and **nothing deduped them**, unlike Recently Played which does dedup against Jump Back In. The honest reason they both existed, recorded when they shipped: *"two genuinely new chart slices from the SAME request type, no new plumbing"* — added because they were cheap, not because they were distinct. City survives (narrower, genuinely varies per user, and now has to prove it changed). Deliberately did NOT re-add a `.mostPlayed` fallback to the city chart: a storefront with no city chart now hides the section, which is honest, where falling back to the national chart would recreate the duplicate.
  **🔴 Retiring an enum case needed a decode fix FIRST.** `HomeLayoutPreferences.load` decoded `[HomeSectionID]` in one go with `try?` under a comment calling it "tolerant by design" — it wasn't. `Decodable` fails an array if a SINGLE element fails, so removing one section would have silently reset every pin **and un-hidden everything the user had hidden**. New `decodeSectionIDs` goes via `[String]` and `compactMap`s, so a retired id costs only itself. Valid because `HomeSectionID` is a `String` raw enum. **General trap: "tolerant" decoding of a collection is only tolerant if it decodes ELEMENT-WISE — a whole-array `try?` is all-or-nothing, and the failure is silent.**
  **Continue Listening deleted — it was redundant in every state it appeared.** The hero showed when `!isPlaying` with a loaded queue; the mini player shows when `nowPlaying != nil`; `restoreQueue()` populates `nowPlaying`. So after a pause AND after a cold launch, both were on screen showing the same track — there was no state where the hero appeared alone, despite its own doc comment claiming it avoided "one track in two places at once". Its only delta was album name plus queue position, so the position moved to `QueueView`'s "Now Playing" header ("Now Playing · 4 of 13") — where someone actually looks for it, and a screen with no layout history. Deliberately not the mini player (no room at 44pt) or `NowPlayingView` (documented fragile pinned layout).
  **Made For You merged into Recommended for You.** `getPersonalRecommendations` and `getMadeForYouPlaylists` read the SAME `MusicPersonalRecommendationsRequest` response and share the in-flight request via `recommendationsMemo` — one Apple payload presented as two top-level sections, the second titled four words from "Made for you, by real people" (the section that actually matters here). Now one more titled sub-shelf inside `recommendedSection`. `availableSections` had to widen to `shelves || playlists`, or the playlists vanish whenever Apple returns no album shelves. **This also retired a live dead end found while planning:** `hasSeeAll` returns false only for `madeByPeople / foundForYou / liveNow / recommended`, so `.madeForYou` fell to `default: true` and offered a "See All ›" — while `HomeSeeAllView` grouped it with the unreachable cases and rendered *"Nothing more to show here."*
  **Recently Played folded into Jump Back In.** Both rails were structurally identical (same `ScrollView`/`LazyHStack`/padding/`bgCard`/clip/marquee-gate) and `recentlyPlayedUnseen` already deduped against `recentAlbums`, so this was moving two `ForEach`s. What lands there is exactly the listening that never went through Nodalong — the device log shows ~5 albums + 1 playlist, which was a whole section. Leaves two history rails: Jump Back In (what you listened to, anywhere) and Recently Added (what you saved).
  **Tests: 280 → 279, and the delta is accounted for rather than absorbed.** Two tests were ABOUT retired sections and were retired WITH them, with the reasoning left in place rather than deleted quietly: `testPersonalChartsOutrankTheGlobalChart` (no global chart exists now) and `testRecentlyPlayedSitsWithJumpBackIn` (the adjacency it pinned is now a single rail, a view-layer concern this pure function can't see). One replacement added for the property that now matters — `testAChartOnlyOutranksPeopleContentWhenActuallyFresh`, which would have caught the inversion. Every other use of the three retired cases was as an arbitrary sample and was substituted with a surviving section.
  **NOT verified on device.** The inversion is the thing to check and it needs a quiet day: with no friend activity in 24h, Home must open with Live Now / Jump Back In on top and Top in Your City well down. Also worth checking that **pins and hides survive a force-quit** (that's the `decodeSectionIDs` fix, and the thing that would have silently broken), that Jump Back In shows no duplicates across its two halves, and that Recommended carries the "Playlists for You" sub-rail with no separate Made For You section.

- **2026-09-13 — `/simplify` pass over the uncommitted body (7.5k-line diff + 27 new files). All four review agents died on a session rate limit, so it was done inline; 3 fixes applied, 280/280 tests, build-green, installed.**
  **Second time the review agents have died mid-`/simplify` on this repo** (cf. 2026-08-26). Both times the inline pass still found real things — the agents are a convenience, not the substance — but an inline sweep is mechanical (grep-driven) where they read broadly, so **this pass is partial: dead code, write-only state, view-body work, normalisation drift and MusicKit layering were checked; deeper reuse/altitude angles were not.**
  **🔴 Fix 1 — `friendsListeningAlbums` / `mostNoddedThisWeek` were aggregating the whole feed from `HomeView`'s body.** Both are dictionary-building sorts over up to 100 posts, and each was read **twice per render** — once by `availableSections` for the `.isEmpty` check, once by the section body — plus a third uncapped recomputation on the see-all screen. `HomeView` observes nine app-wide objects, so an `isPlaying` toggle re-aggregated the entire feed four times. Moved to a `didSet` on `FeedViewModel.posts`, matching `LibraryViewModel.recomputeDerived()`'s existing convention and for the same stated reason: `posts` is assigned or mutated in **six** places (snapshot restore, load, refresh, the optimistic nod bump and its two rollbacks), so a `didSet` is the only form none of them can forget. Published uncapped; Home applies `.prefix(10)`. Accepted nuance: the 7-day cutoff is now evaluated on assignment rather than per render — `posts` is reassigned on load, pull-to-refresh and foreground-return, so the window stays current.
  **🔴 Fix 2 — album identity was defined three times independently.** `stripSearchNoise(x).lowercased().trimmingCharacters(in: .whitespaces)` appeared inline in `groupByAlbum`'s own grouping key, in `HomeViewModel.shelfAlbumKey` (whose doc comment said outright that it "mirrors `groupByAlbum`'s own keying exactly" — the drift risk stated aloud), and again in `HomeView.albumMatchKey`. New public `albumMatchKey(_:)` beside `stripSearchNoise`; all three now route through it. **Deliberately left alone:** `canonicalAlbumTitle` answers a different question (edition wording), and the post-grouping keys in `GroupPostsByAlbum`/`FriendsActivity` omit the trim and are pinned by their own tests — adding one is a behaviour change, not a cleanup.
  **Fix 3 — `HomeSeeAllView.allShelfAlbumsByRecency` was a byte-for-byte duplicate of a sort `LibraryViewModel` already performs**, re-derived in a view body over every album group on every render. `LibraryViewModel` now publishes `albumGroupsByRecency` and `recentAlbumGroups` is a `prefix(10)` of it.
  **Checked and clean, worth recording so it isn't re-swept:** 609 declared symbols across the new/changed code have callers — every zero-reference hit was an XCTest method (reflection-invoked) or a protocol conformance. No write-only `@Published` state, which is the shape that hid `emptyReason` for weeks. `topShelfArtistNames` looked like a per-render sort over the artist list but is only called from the async `load()` — false positive. The three `import MusicKit` outside `AppleMusicService` (`PlayerViewModel`, `PlaybackErrorAlert`, `ProfileView`) are all long-standing and documented, not introduced here.
  **Process note: a doc-comment rewrite silently deleted a property declaration** (`recentAlbumGroups`) because the matched block spanned comment *and* declaration and the replacement only restored the comment. The compiler caught it immediately — but when a scripted replacement's `old` text spans a doc comment and the thing it documents, the `new` text must restate the declaration.

- **2026-09-12 — 🔴 SCROLL STUTTER ROOT-CAUSED FROM A DEVICE RECORDING, after four rounds of wrong theories: the header's INSERT/REMOVE was the stall. All ten measured hitches land on a HIDE. Fixed by keeping the header mounted and collapsing its height. Build-green, zero new warnings, installed.**
  **Method — this is the round that earns the "measure, don't theorise" rule its place.** Four mechanisms had been reasoned about and shipped; a 47s screen recording settled it in one pass. Frames extracted at 60fps as 48×96 grayscale, consecutive-frame mean-abs-diff computed, then — crucially — **freezes were separated from pauses** by requiring fast motion on BOTH sides (a pause between gestures is indistinguishable from a hitch otherwise). That reduced 50 candidate stalls to **10 genuine dropped-frame hitches of 50–100ms**, each with post-freeze speed 3–4× pre-freeze, i.e. the scroll accumulated while blocked and then snapped.
  **The correlation, which is what actually proved it.** The recording's filename gives an absolute start time and the app writes its own timestamped `[Nodalong.Header]` lines to `nav.log`, so the two could be aligned: **all 10 hitches fell within 0.35s of a header toggle, and every single one was a HIDE, never a SHOW.**
  **🔴 That asymmetry names the mechanism.** `if !hidden { header }` doesn't hide the header, it **unmounts** it — tearing down the whole subtree on hide and rebuilding it on show. That subtree is expensive: `HeaderGlyph`'s `.ultraThinMaterial`, and on Shelf a `.pickerStyle(.segmented)` Picker (a UIKit `UISegmentedControl`) plus a `TextField`. Destroying UIKit-backed views mid-momentum costs exactly 50–100ms of main thread. **Insert/remove was the cause the entire time — the scroll-observation mechanism (geometry vs. phase) was never the stutter, only the earlier oscillation.**
  **Fix:** new `collapsingHeader(hidden:)` keeps the view mounted and animates `frame(height:)` to 0 with `clipped()` + `opacity` + `allowsHitTesting(!hidden)`. Same reclaimed space, zero teardown. `naturalHeight` is recorded via `.onGeometryChange` **only while visible** — measuring while collapsed would latch it at zero and the header could never return — and starts `nil` so the first render uses the natural height; once measured both ends are concrete, which is what permits the tween at all (SwiftUI cannot animate to or from a `nil` frame height).
  **Two suspects eliminated by evidence rather than argument, both of which I had argued for:**
  - **`onScrollPhaseChange` on `List`.** I claimed this was the likeliest fault since three of Shelf's five attach points are `List`s and this codebase had only ever used the modifier on `ScrollView`. The log shows `Shelf.liked` (a `List`) firing 32 times and making 3 HIDE + 3 SHOW decisions. **It works on `List`. The hypothesis was wrong.**
  - **The animated mesh.** Frozen (`paused: true`) as a deliberate A/B, and the correlation then put every hitch on a header toggle instead. Drift restored.
  All six attach points fire and decide correctly: Home 317 events, Feed 216, Shelf.albums 109, Profile 75, Shelf.liked 32, Search 12. **Detection was never broken — only the transition.**
  **Tooling note for next time.** `~/Downloads` is unreadable from this shell (`ffprobe` AND `cp` both return "Operation not permitted" — metadata via `ls` works, contents don't), which had already been recorded once and cost a round again. **Ask for media inside the repo from the start.** `ffmpeg`/`ffprobe` are installed at `/opt/homebrew/bin`. `mpdecimate,metadata=print` did NOT yield usable duplicate counts here; raw grayscale frames piped to Python and diffed by hand did, with no dependencies.
  **Follow-up in the same round, from the next device log: the mounted collapse had a self-feeding measurement, and that is what "Feed and Shelf still aren't quite right" was.** Every decision in the log was correct (Home 1639pt scrolls, Feed and Shelf both toggling on the right gesture direction), but two `Geometry action is cycling between duplicate values` warnings fired **immediately after `Shelf.albums -> HIDE`**.
  🔴 Cause: `onGeometryChange` sat INSIDE the animating `.frame`, so on the way back up — with `!hidden` already true — it measured each intermediate height (10, 20, 30…) and wrote it into `naturalHeight`, **which is the animation's own target**. The animation retargeted against its own output and settled squashed. **This also explains why Home looked right and the other two didn't:** `ScreenHeader` alone is rigid and reports its ideal height whatever is proposed, so Home self-corrected, while Shelf's `TextField` and segmented `Picker` genuinely compress. Correctness resting on whether a subview happens to resist compression is not a design.
  Fixed with `.fixedSize(horizontal: false, vertical: true)` above the measurement, pinning the content to its ideal height so the reported value is the true natural height even at a proposal of zero. The `!hidden` guard then becomes unnecessary and was removed — the measurement can no longer latch at zero, which was the only thing it protected against.
  **Worth noting the shape of this bug, because it is the fourth time this session:** a value derived from layout being fed back into the thing that changes that layout. The `onScrollGeometryChange` loop was the same shape (collapse moves `contentOffset`, which was the observed signal). **When a collapse misbehaves, check whether the measurement can see its own effect before proposing a new mechanism.**

  **NOT verified:** that the stutter is actually gone. The cause is now measured rather than guessed, and the fix removes that exact cost — but the same 60fps analysis should be re-run on a fresh recording to confirm, which is now a cheap and repeatable check rather than an argument. The `[Nodalong.Header]` diagnostics are deliberately still in the tree for that; remove them once confirmed.

- **2026-09-11 (last, cont. 3) — 🔴 The collapsing header is now driven by scroll PHASE, not scroll geometry. Three geometry-based attempts all failed the same way; the mechanism was wrong, not the tuning. Build-green, zero warnings in every touched file, installed.**
  **Three failures, one cause.** Every previous version observed `onScrollGeometryChange` continuously, and collapsing the header resizes the scroll view — which moves `contentOffset`, the very signal being observed. The action's own effect fed its input, so a self-inflicted jump was indistinguishable from a real scroll the other way and the state flipped forever. Device warnings every time: `<OnScrollGeometryChange Modifier> tried to update multiple times per frame` / `Geometry action is cycling between duplicate values`. The three remedies and why each only changed the loop's *period*: (1) dropping `contentInsets.top` — necessary, since the inset is exactly what the header changes, but `contentOffset` moves too; (2) a 0.35s settle lock longer than the 0.22s animation — the loop resumed the instant the lock lifted; (3) anchoring with a 140pt threshold larger than the collapsing block — reduced it, did not remove it.
  **🔴 The actual fix is a different mechanism. `ScrollPhase` transitions are caused by touch and momentum, never by layout**, so resizing the scroll view cannot generate one. There is no loop left to damp — which is why the new version needs no dead zone, no lock, and no oversized threshold (24pt now, down from 140pt; the large value existed purely to outweigh the code's own layout jump). Direction is measured once per gesture: offset recorded at `.interacting`, compared at `.decelerating`/`.idle`.
  **Verifiable rather than asserted: `onScrollGeometryChange` no longer appears anywhere in the app** (grep returns only this file's own doc comment), so the first warning is impossible by construction rather than by tuning.
  **Accepted trade:** the header changes state when the finger lifts rather than tracking the drag continuously. That is the price of correctness here, and it matches how most apps behave.
  **Worth knowing if `Geometry action is cycling between duplicate values` still appears alone:** that is a DIFFERENT modifier. `MarqueeText.swift:270` uses `.onGeometryChange(for: CGRect.self) { $0.frame(in: .global) }` for its on-screen visibility gate, and every marquee's global frame shifts when the header collapses. That is pre-existing and unrelated to the scroll modifier; do not assume the two warnings share a cause.
  **Method note, and the honest one: this codebase's own rule was available and ignored for three rounds.** The decisions log already says, twice, that when a fix does not land you instrument the decision point and read what it actually decided rather than theorising again. Three mechanisms were chosen by reasoning instead, each plausible and each incomplete. The thing that finally settled it was asking what class of signal *cannot* be perturbed by the effect — not a better guess at the same class.
  **NOT verified.** Whether it now feels right on all six screens (Home, Feed, Search, Shelf ×5 segments, Profile), and whether 24pt per gesture is the right sensitivity. If it is still wrong, the next step is diagnostics at the phase transition, NOT a fourth mechanism.

- **2026-09-11 (last, cont. 2) — 🔴 The collapsing-header jitter was a feedback loop that FRAME-TO-FRAME DELTAS CANNOT FIX, and two rounds of damping proved it. Replaced with offset ANCHORING plus hysteresis larger than the collapsing block. Profile added. Build-green, zero new warnings, installed.**
  **The loop, stated properly.** Collapsing the header resizes the scroll view, which moves `contentOffset` by roughly the block's height **in one step**. Measured as a delta, that self-inflicted jump is indistinguishable from a real scroll in the opposite direction:
  `show → offset jumps +52 → "scrolled down" → hide → offset jumps −52 → "scrolled up" → show → …`
  SwiftUI names it on device: `Geometry action is cycling between duplicate values` / `<OnScrollGeometryChange Modifier> tried to update multiple times per frame`.
  **Two damping attempts failed, and the failures are the useful part.** (1) Dropping `contentInsets.top` from the observed value — necessary (the inset is exactly what the header changes, so including it wired output to input) but not sufficient; `contentOffset` moves too. (2) A 0.35s settle lock outlasting the 0.22s animation — **only delays the loop**, which restarts the moment the lock lifts; the warnings were still in the next device log, unchanged. **A time lock cannot fix a loop whose trigger is a position change rather than a timing race.**
  **The fix is anchoring, not damping.** Direction is now measured against the offset at which the current state was *decided*, never against the previous callback — and the flip threshold (140pt) is deliberately **larger than the tallest collapsing block** (Shelf's header + segmented control + filter field). A self-inflicted jump therefore cannot reach the threshold on its own, which makes the loop structurally impossible instead of merely slow. The anchor also tracks the furthest point travelled in the current direction, so the reveal distance is always measured from where you actually are rather than from where you were a screen ago.
  **Lesson worth keeping: when an action's own effect perturbs the signal it reads, the guard has to exceed the size of the perturbation.** Damping the rate, or ignoring a time window, leaves the loop intact — it just changes its period.
  **Profile added**, at the user's request. It is the **only collapsing header on a pushed screen**, so it is the only one that takes the back chevron with it; `.enablesInteractiveSwipeBack()` is hoisted onto its container so edge-swipe survives, and scrolling up returns the chevron — but a gesture is briefly the only way out, which is exactly why every other pushed screen (and specifically the artist page, whose chevron drives `popArtistRun` and has no other trigger) was deliberately left alone.
  **Verification.** Clean device build, **zero warnings in `CollapsingHeader`, `ProfileView`, `SearchView`** (the two in `ShelfView` are the known pre-existing main-actor pair). Installed, mtime checked.
