  **NOT verified, and this is the third attempt at this specific problem — treat it as unproven until scrolled.** Whether the jitter is actually gone; whether 140pt feels right or too far to travel before the header returns (it is the one number to tune, and it may differ per screen since the blocks differ in height); and whether Profile's chevron disappearing is acceptable in practice or should be reverted.

- **2026-09-11 (last, cont.) — "Why is the animation smoother on Home?" answered structurally: Home is smooth because NOTHING sits between its header and its scroll view. Search and Shelf now collapse their chrome as one block so the scroll view is the only thing that moves. Build-green, zero new warnings, installed.**
  **The diagnosis, and it's a layout fact rather than a tuning one.** The collapse reflows everything between the header and the scroll content, so the cost scales with what's in that gap: Home has **nothing** there (header → `ScrollView`), Feed has a `safeAreaInset` party banner, Search has a `TextField` plus the filter row, and Shelf has a sync banner, a **`.pickerStyle(.segmented)` Picker** and a second text field. Shelf being the worst is not a coincidence — a segmented Picker is a UIKit `UISegmentedControl`, and UIKit-backed views being relaid out inside a SwiftUI animation is a classic source of exactly that stutter.
  **Fix: collapse the header and the chrome beneath it as one block**, so each screen reduces to Home's shape — one thing moves, the scroll view. Search's query field and filter row now scroll away with the title; Shelf's segmented control and filter field likewise. Confirmed with the user first, since it changes what disappears (you scroll up to reach the segment picker or the search field, the way Safari's address bar behaves). Checked before doing it that `SearchView` has **no `@FocusState`**, so removing the field while focused only resigns the keyboard — which is what scrolling should do anyway — rather than stranding focus state.
  **🔴 A second cause I claimed and then had to walk back on closer analysis, recorded because the retraction is the useful part.** `headerHidden` was put on `ShelfViewModel` as `@Published` (to avoid threading a `Binding` through five segment-view signatures), and `@EnvironmentObject` invalidation is coarse — any `@Published` change invalidates every observer. That sounds like the `PlaybackClock`-off-`PlayerViewModel` problem this log already documents, and it was presented that way. **On re-examination the gain from fixing it is small here:** only the *active* segment view exists in the hierarchy at a time, and it would be invalidated either way — via `@Published` now, or via the `Binding` it would have to receive instead, since `@Binding` is a `DynamicProperty` that invalidates on change too. The only view that genuinely stops re-rendering is `filterField`. **So the five signature changes were not done**, and the structural fix above is the one that matters. Worth revisiting only if the block collapse doesn't land it.
  **Lesson: "shared `ObservableObject` therefore over-invalidation" is a good instinct and not automatically true** — check which observers are actually instantiated, and whether the alternative plumbing creates the same dependency anyway.
  **Feed deliberately left alone** — its party banner is a `safeAreaInset` on the scroll container rather than a sibling in the `VStack`, so it travels with the scroll view and Feed is already close to Home's shape.
  **Verification.** Clean device build, **zero warnings in `SearchView`, `ShelfView`* and `CollapsingHeader`** (*the two in `ShelfView` are the known pre-existing main-actor pair, now at 545/547). Installed, mtime checked.
  **NOT verified:** whether Search and Shelf now actually match Home by feel — that's the entire point of the change and only scrolling them can say. If Shelf is still behind the other two, the remaining suspect is the `UISegmentedControl` itself being expensive to insert/remove rather than to move, in which case keeping the Picker mounted and collapsing only its height would be the next thing to try.

- **2026-09-11 (last) — 🔴 TWO BUGS OF MY OWN, both found in one device console dump the user pasted, both confirmed fixed by a fresh capture. One of them proves an earlier "this is correct, not a bug" explanation in this log WRONG.**
  **Bug 1 — the collapsing header fed back into itself.** Reported as "it breaks on feed and shelf"; the console named it exactly: `<OnScrollGeometryChange Modifier> tried to update multiple times per frame` and `Geometry action is cycling between duplicate values`. Toggling `hidden` removes the header from the `VStack`, which resizes the scroll view, which changes its geometry, which fires the action again. **I made it worse by tracking `contentOffset.y + contentInsets.top`** — the top inset is *precisely* what collapsing the header changes, so that expression wired the output straight back into the input. That normalisation was written to make "at the top" read as 0 regardless of safe-area insets, which sounded careful and was the other half of the loop. Fixed two ways: track `contentOffset.y` alone (the `alwaysVisibleWithin` threshold absorbs the inset instead), plus a 0.35s settle lock after each toggle that outlasts the 0.22s collapse animation, so geometry churn caused by our own layout change can never be read as a scroll. **Lesson: when an action's effect changes the thing the action observes, the derived value must exclude whatever the effect touches — and a "normalisation" that folds in a layout-dependent term is the easiest way to close that loop by accident.**
  **🔴 Bug 2 — `getRecentlyPlayedContainers` never worked, and I had already explained its failure away.** The console showed a flat 400 on every launch:
  `Failed to perform … /v1/me/recent/played?…&limit=20 … code: 40005 "Invalid Parameter Value" … "Value must be an integer less than or equal to 10, but was: 20" … source: .parameter("limit")`
  Apple caps that endpoint at 10 and **fails the whole request rather than clamping**. It shipped at 20, so it returned nothing, ever. When the measured result was `0 albums / 0 playlists` it was written up in this log as structurally correct — "Nodalong sets `player.queue = [singleSong]` on every track, so Apple never observes a container being played here." That reasoning is still true in itself, but **it was not why the count was zero, and it was accepted without checking the request had even succeeded.** `try?` swallowed the 400; only the device console ever showed it. Fixed by clamping to 10 and replacing `try?` with a `do/catch` that logs — a swallowed error is what let a wrong explanation stand. **Fresh capture: `recentlyPlayedContainers 5 albums / 1 playlists in 405ms`.**
  **Lesson worth more than either fix: a plausible structural explanation for an empty result is not evidence.** The check that would have caught this — does the request succeed — takes seconds, and `try?` is exactly what hides it. Any "this is correct, not a bug" conclusion about a zero/empty result should be backed by the call having demonstrably succeeded first.
  **Verification, by evidence not assertion.** Fresh `devicectl --console` capture after the fix: **0** occurrences of `cycling between duplicate` / `tried to update multiple times` (previously firing repeatedly), and the recently-played call returning real data. Clean device build, zero warnings in both touched files (the two in `AppleMusicService` are the pre-existing line-18 `MusicService`/`@MainActor` conformance pair). Installed, binary mtime checked.
  **Still unconfirmed by eye:** that Feed and Shelf now *feel* right while scrolling — the structural loop is gone from the logs, but whether `deadZone`/`settleLock` are tuned well is a judgement only scrolling can make. Also unrelated but visible in the same dump and NOT investigated: a `FirebaseApp.configure()` warning logged before `NodalongApp.init()` runs, three main-thread hangs during launch (2126/1974/1745ms), and the long-standing `applicationQueuePlayer _establishConnectionIfNeeded timeout` — none new to this round.

- **2026-09-11 (later still) — Tab-root headers now scroll away and come back on an upward flick. Build-green, zero new warnings, 280/280, installed.**
  **Why.** Every tab root pinned a `ScreenHeader` (title + avatar, ~52pt) above its content permanently. On a long scroll it holds nothing you're using. Scoped to **tab roots only** (`showsBack: false`) and the content takes the space back, both confirmed with the user before building. `onScrollGeometryChange` verified with a `swiftc -typecheck` probe at the 26.1 target first.
  **🔴 Finding 1 — the header cannot simply be removed from the hierarchy, and this is a real regression trap.** `ScreenHeader` ends with `.enablesInteractiveSwipeBack()`, which mounts a `SwipeBackInstaller` that re-asserts the pop-gesture delegate on **every update**. `SwipeBackGesture.swift`'s own doc comment says `PlaylistDetailView` draws its own header and **depends on the tab root's install** to get edge-swipe at all. `SwipeBackGestureDelegate.shared` is a strong singleton and nothing un-installs it, so an already-set delegate survives — but the re-assertion stops. Fixed by hoisting `.enablesInteractiveSwipeBack()` onto each tab root's own container, where it stays mounted regardless of header visibility; it stays on `ScreenHeader` too, and installing twice is idempotent (`install()` just re-assigns a delegate and sets `isEnabled`). **Lesson: before making a view conditional, check what it was installing as a side effect.**
  **🔴 Finding 2 — `safeAreaInset` was the obvious mechanism and is wrong for half the screens.** It works where the header sits directly on a `ScrollView` (Home, Feed), but Search has `queryField` + `searchFilterRow` beneath its header and Shelf has a sync banner + segmented `Picker`, so an inset on the scroll view renders the header BELOW that chrome. Collapsing in place inside the existing `VStack` — a conditional plus `.transition(.move(edge: .top).combined(with: .opacity))` — works uniformly for all four and needs no height measurement, which also sidesteps the fixed-height-estimate class of bug this repo already hit with the discography grid at large Dynamic Type.
  **Direction-driven, not offset-driven.** Hiding past a fixed offset means the only way back to the avatar from deep in a list is scrolling all the way to the top. `CollapsingHeaderScroll` hides on downward scroll, shows on ANY upward scroll, and always shows within 12pt of the top; a 6pt dead zone suppresses the rubber-band flicker on momentum settle. Offset is read as `contentOffset.y + contentInsets.top` so "at the top" is 0 regardless of safe-area insets.
  **Attached to each scroll view directly, never to a container.** Whether SwiftUI's scroll modifiers propagate to scroll views deeper in a subtree can't be confirmed at compile time and the failure mode is a **silent no-op**, so there is no propagation assumption anywhere: Home 1, Feed 1, Search 1 (`resultsScroll`), Shelf **5** — one per segment view. The three other `.scrollContentBackground(.hidden)` sites in `ShelfView.swift` (578/757/1184) are the pushed album/artist/genre detail screens and were deliberately excluded.
  **Shelf's five segments share state through the view model, not five new `Binding` parameters.** All five segment views already hold `@EnvironmentObject shelfVM`, and `ShelfViewModel` already owns presentation state (`segment`, `searchQuery`), so `headerHidden` went there — threading a binding through five separate view signatures was the alternative. The reset lives in `segment`'s own `didSet` rather than an `.onChange` at the view, so no future caller that sets `segment` can forget it; Search resets on `mode`/`scope` change for the same reason (results are replaced wholesale and the scroll returns to the top, so a hidden header would be stranded over an unscrolled list).
  **NearMe deliberately excluded** — its content is the procedural park canvas, which doesn't scroll, so there is no gesture to drive this. Its `ScreenHeader` call site is otherwise identical to the other four, which is exactly why it's named rather than silently skipped.
  **Verification.** `xcodegen generate` + clean `xcodebuild` for the device, **zero warnings in all six touched files**; the two in `ShelfView` are the known pre-existing main-actor pair, now at 531/533 — shifted by exactly the 10 lines inserted above them (3 header wrap + 4 segment attaches + 3 swipe-back hoist), which is how they were confirmed unchanged rather than merely assumed. 280/280 NodalongKit tests. Installed, binary mtime checked.
  **NOT verified on device.** Whether `deadZone`/`alwaysVisibleWithin` are tuned right — flicker on momentum settle or at the rubber-band bounce is the likeliest thing to need adjusting; that Shelf works in **all five** segments and that switching segment restores the header; that Search's query field and filter row stay pinned while only the title row goes; and — **the regression finding 1 exists to prevent** — that edge-swipe-back still works from `PlaylistDetailView` with the tab root's header scrolled away.

- **2026-09-11 (later) — Mesh now drifts (scene-phase gated), and the track-list containers went translucent so the mesh reads through them. Build-green, zero warnings in any touched file, 280/280, installed.**
  **Animation — asked for after I'd argued against it, so the risk got addressed rather than waved through.** The original static call was on real grounds (jetsam-kill history from sustained background work + `UIBackgroundModes: audio` means this keeps running while locked). Rather than just capitulating, the drift runs through `TimelineView(.animation(minimumInterval: 1/20, paused: scenePhase != .active))` — **the same proven-safe mechanism `MiniPlayerBar` uses for its spinning disc**, which exists in this codebase precisely because a naive `.repeatForever` there wedged the render server and produced the purple-screen-on-reopen bug. Backgrounded or locked, it stops dead. 20 fps not 60: points move at most 0.05 unit over ~20s, so most frames at 60 would be identical.
  **The pinning rules constrain the animation, not just the resting layout.** Corners (0/2/6/8) stay locked on both axes; edge midpoints (1/3/5/7) slide ALONG their edge only — point 1 is on the top edge so `x` is free and `y` must stay 0; only the centre (4) moves in both. Move an edge point off its edge and that edge bows inward, exposing whatever sits behind the mesh at the corners. Periods are long and mutually non-divisible (17/19/20/21/23/25s) so the axes never sync into a visible pulse — it should read as drift, not a heartbeat. Every drifting value stays inside 0...1 at full amplitude (tightest is 0.40 +/- 0.05), so no clamping is needed.
  **🔴 The container took THREE wrong answers before landing on "no fill at all", and all three are worth recording because each fixed the previous one's symptom while restating its mistake.** (1) `bgBase` — the original; the same colour as the flat page, so invisible. Correct then, but against a mesh it becomes an opaque flat patch punched out of the gradient. (2) `bgCard`, applied earlier the same day — fixed "invisible" by making it a *visible* opaque patch, i.e. the same problem stated louder. (3) `.ultraThinMaterial` — a distinct frosted layer with its own edge, so the cards sat on a surface that sat on the mesh; the user's words were "mesh in a mesh". **The answer was that it should not be a surface at all.** The track cards already carry their own background, corner radius and shadow; anything behind them is a second surface nobody asked for. `trackListContainer()` is now just `padding(6)` and the mesh reads straight through.
  **Lesson: when a container keeps needing a different fill, check whether it should have one.** Three rounds were spent choosing a colour for something whose job was spacing.
  **`padding(6)` is load-bearing and must stay** — verified before writing the comment that claims it: `SpotlightCarousel` has `containerPadding: CGFloat = 12` (6 top + 6 bottom) baked into both `pageHeight` and `maxPageHeight`, so dropping the padding would leave that carousel over-sized by 12pt.
  **Extracted rather than repeated:** all four sites (`FeedView` x2, `RecentTracksCarousel`, `SpotlightCarousel`) were byte-identical `.padding(6)` + fill + `clipShape`, so they collapsed into one `trackListContainer()` — which also deleted the four copies of the explanatory comment added this morning. Swapped with a regex asserted to match exactly 4 times, not a loose find-and-replace.
  **Known cost, flagged not hidden:** `.screenBackground()` is on ~31 screens, so a pushed navigation stack has more than one `TimelineView` alive and ticking even though only the top one is visible. At 20 fps that is small, but it is real. **If battery or scroll shows any impact, the fix is hoisting a single mesh behind `MainTabView` rather than per-screen** — deliberately not done pre-emptively, because that trades the current "every screen owns its background" simplicity for a global layer, and there is no measurement yet saying it is needed.
  **NOT verified.** The drift itself (needs watching for ~20s on device — if it reads as a pulse rather than drift, the periods are too close); that it genuinely stops when backgrounded; that the frosted containers read as surfaces rather than grey smears, **especially in light mode**, where `.ultraThinMaterial` over a pale mesh is least predictable; and battery over a longer session.

- **2026-09-11 — App background is now a static `MeshGradient` instead of a flat fill. Six treatments previewed as phone mockups before any code; mesh chosen, app-wide. Build-green, zero new warnings, 280/280 tests, installed.**
  **Why.** Every screen painted one flat `Color.nodalong.bgBase`, so `bgCard` containers, the floating mini player and the tab bar all sat on the same undifferentiated purple. Asked for depth; previewed flat / vertical fade / top glow / mesh / artwork-reactive / grain as real phone mockups in the app's own hex values, then implemented the pick.
  **🔴 `MeshGradient` is NOT in the SDK's SwiftUI `.swiftinterface` — that file is a partial stub.** Grepping it returns zero hits and would have led to the wrong conclusion that the API is unavailable. Confirmed instead with a `swiftc -typecheck` probe piped over stdin at `-target arm64-apple-ios26.1` (clean, exit 0). **For SwiftUI, unlike MusicKit, the swiftinterface is not a reliable availability check — typecheck it.**
  **🔴 The two modes need genuinely different colours, not one ramp inverted — and the light palette has a trap.** Contrast-checked every candidate stop before writing the file. Dark mode carries white text so the LIGHTEST stop constrains: `purpleAccent` #926897 measures **4.52:1** — passes, and is the ceiling. Light mode carries dark ink #241542 so the DARKEST constrains, and light `purpleAccent` #6B4FA0 measures **2.58:1 — FAIL**, despite its dark-mode counterpart being fine. The light mesh is therefore built only from tokens that already bear text elsewhere (bgBase 9.02, bgElevated 7.26, bgCard 6.04). Both modes run lighter at top / darker at bottom, which also gives the mini player and tab bar a darker field.
  **🔴 46 call sites, and only ~31 of them were screens.** `.background(Color.nodalong.bgBase)` appears 46 times across 29 files and a find-and-replace would have put a mesh gradient **inside Feed's rounded card and inside every carousel container**. Classified by reading the two lines around each site: real screens are the ones followed by `.toolbar(.hidden, for: .navigationBar)` / `.navigationTitle` / a `maxHeight: .infinity` frame. 31 swapped to a new `.screenBackground()`; **15 deliberately left flat** — 4 card fills, 2 row fills (`PartyView:185`, `NearMeView:310`), 2 NearMe canvas fills (the park draws its own procedural scenery and a purple mesh underneath would fight it), and 7 `#Preview` blocks. Applied by explicit file:line list, each asserted to contain the expected text before substitution, rather than by pattern.
  **A consequence that had to be handled, not just noticed:** `FeedView:73`/`:262`, `RecentTracksCarousel:139` and `SpotlightCarousel:121` filled their card with **`bgBase` — the same colour as the page behind them**, so those containers were effectively invisible. Against a mesh they would have read as flat patches. Switched to `bgCard`, which is what Home's own rails already use for the identical job; flagged in the plan as a visible change to Feed rather than slipped in, and the reason is left as a comment at each site.
  **Deliberately static, not animated.** A drifting mesh is the obvious next thought and is wrong here: this app has a documented jetsam-kill history from sustained background CPU (the 60 fps NearMe timer, 2026-06-14) and declares `UIBackgroundModes: audio`, so it keeps running while locked — a continuously-animating full-screen GPU layer behind every tab is exactly that class of cost. Recorded in the component's own doc comment so it isn't "improved" later without that context.
  **Untouched on purpose:** `AppChrome.applyWindowBackground()` stays a flat `UIColor` — it is the white-flash-on-resume fix (iOS composites the app-switcher snapshot against the live view and any gap exposes the window) and must remain a plain colour; it sits under the mesh and is visible for at most a frame. `NowPlayingView` keeps its blurred-artwork background; its `ScreenMetrics.bounds`-pinned layout has a long regression history and nothing here needed to go near it.
  **Process note — a self-inflicted bug caught by an assertion rather than by a build.** The card-fill pass inserted explanatory comment lines above each edit, which shifted the line numbers of later sites in the same file; the second `FeedView` site then asserted against the wrong line and aborted **before writing**, so there was no partial state. Fixed by processing line numbers in descending order. Worth the assert: without it this would have silently edited a `clipShape` line.
  **Verification.** `xcodegen generate` (1 new file) + clean `xcodebuild` from fresh derived-data paths for a generic simulator target, the booted iOS 26.3 simulator, and the device. **Zero warnings in any touched file** — grepped per file; the two in `ShelfView` are the pre-existing main-actor pair still at lines **521/523**, i.e. unshifted, confirming the edits landed where intended. 280/280 NodalongKit tests. Installed to the device.
  **NOT verified visually, and the reason is structural: every screen using `.screenBackground()` is behind the auth wall.** The simulator has no Firebase session, so it lands on `SplashView` — which uses `bgDeep` and was deliberately left flat — proving the build launches and renders cleanly but showing none of the mesh. **Only a signed-in device can show this.** Worth checking there: that it reads as depth rather than visible blobs; that **light mode** is legible over every part of the mesh (the mode least likely to get looked at, and the one with the measured failure mode); that Feed's card and the two carousels now read as intentional surfaces; that NearMe's park is unchanged; and that Home still scrolls cleanly with a large library.

- **2026-09-07 (last, cont.) — Answered "what are the sources for these playlists?" from the code, and it exposed the LAST hole in the exclusion set: the user's own Apple Music library was not a source at all. Now seeds and excludes from it, read-only. Build-green, zero new warnings, 280/280 tests (2 new), installed.**
  **The sources, for the record** (asked directly; answered by reading rather than recalling). Seeds come from three Nodalong-side places in `buildTasteSeeds`: Shelf saves (Firestore `library`, weight 2.5), recent plays (Firestore `recent_tracks`, 1.0–2.0), and spotlights (3.0). Recent plays *are* Apple Music data — `LibrarySyncService` calls `getRecentlyPlayed()` on launch and foreground-return — but `MusicRecentlyPlayedRequest` is capped at **25 per sync** and only sees listening that happened while Nodalong was around to sync it, so it's a slowly-accumulating partial bridge, not a history. The expansion *target* is entirely Apple's catalog (`similarArtists` → top 30 songs + up to 2 albums), so the catalog side was never the limitation.
  **🔴 The gap: `getSavedTracks()` had ZERO callers.** It's a `MusicService` protocol requirement, implemented on both services, and `LibrarySyncService` excludes it with the reason stated in its own comment — "importing the user's Apple Music library would flood the shelf for every new user." That reasoning is correct **for the Shelf** and was silently doing something else as well: `allArtistKeys` is built from the same three sources, so **an artist the user owns on Apple Music but never saved to their Nodalong Shelf was still eligible to be returned as a "discovery."** Directly the complaint from the round above, one layer further out — fixing the seed-limit truncation closed most of that wall, this closed the rest. The flooding objection is about *importing*; it doesn't apply to reading the library live to seed and exclude, which writes nothing.
  **Shipped:** `buildTasteSeeds` gained `appleLibrary: [Song]`, contributing to seeds, genre weights, `heardTitleArtist`/`heardIsrcs` **and** `allArtistKeys`. New `TasteSignal.appleLibrary` at **1.5** — below `shelfSave` (2.5) because a Shelf save is a decision made in this app recently while an Apple Music library can be a decade of accumulated saves including plenty no longer listened to, and above `play` (1.0) because owning something is still a genuine keep. `getSavedTracks(limit:)` now **pages** (`MusicLibraryRequest` exposes both `limit` and `offset`) to 500 rather than taking one arbitrary 100-item slice off the top of a multi-thousand-track library; it advances by what Apple actually returned and stops on a short page. Tracks rather than artist names deliberately, chosen with the user: genre-scoped runs need `genres`, which an artist-name-only set could not supply.
  **Swift note worth keeping: a defaulted parameter does NOT witness a no-argument protocol requirement.** `func getSavedTracks(limit: Int = 500)` failed `MusicService` conformance outright — the requirement is `getSavedTracks()` and the candidate's signature is `getSavedTracks(limit:)`. Resolved by keeping both, with the no-arg one forwarding at the original limit of 100 so its documented behaviour is unchanged.
  **Best-effort throughout:** a failed library read leaves discovery exactly as it was, and a `diagLog` reports how many tracks came back so an empty result is diagnosable rather than invisible.
  **NOT verified on device.** That the library read actually returns a meaningful count on a real account (`runTaste: appleLibrary N tracks` in the console will say); that artists owned on Apple Music but absent from the Shelf have genuinely stopped appearing as discoveries; and that a 500-track read doesn't add noticeable latency to the first generation.

- **2026-09-07 (last) — 🔴 ROOT CAUSE of "it shows a lot of artists I already listen to": the discovery lens excluded only the top 8 artists, because SEEDING and EXCLUSION shared one truncated list. Plus real album deep cuts, and genre-specific discovery playlists. Build-green, zero warnings in any touched file, 278/278 tests (5 new incl. a regression test for the bug), installed.**
  **🔴 The bug, and it is a one-line conflation of two different jobs.** `buildTasteSeeds` ends with `artists: Array(ranked.prefix(artistLimit))` — `artistLimit` is `tasteSeedLimit = 8` — and `knownArtistKeys`, the set that decides what counts as a *discovery*, was `Set(artists.map(\.key))`, i.e. derived from that **truncated** list. `artistWeight` holds every artist across library + recents + spotlights, so in a 150-artist library **142 artists the user actively listens to were eligible to be handed back as suggestions.** Exactly the reported symptom. Truncation is correct for *seeding* (expanding Apple's similarity graph from an entire library would be enormous); it is wrong for *exclusion*, and the two must not share a cut. Fixed with a separate `allArtistKeys` populated before truncation; `knownArtistKeys` unions it with the seed keys so a directly-constructed `TasteSeeds` (tests, fixtures) still excludes its own seeds. **Key normalisation was checked on both sides first and was never the problem** — `note()` and `rankCandidateArtists` both use `primaryArtist(...).lowercased()`.
  **None of the 17 existing tests caught it**, because they all built seeds from libraries smaller than the seed limit — where truncation is a no-op and the bug is invisible. The new `testKnownArtistsBeyondTheSeedLimitAreStillExcluded` uses 20 artists against a limit of 8 and asserts `knownArtistKeys.count == 20`; it fails against the old code. **Lesson: a test fixture smaller than the constant it exercises tests nothing about that constant.**
  **Real deep cuts, not just wider top songs.** The previous round widened the pool 10 → 30 top songs, but 30 top songs are still an artist's 30 most *popular* recordings — "deep cut" and "top songs" are different things and no amount of widening one produces the other. Album tracks are the only genuine source, so `runTaste` now also pulls from `deepCutAlbums(from:)`: full-length albums only (a single/EP has no deep cuts by definition; compilations filtered with the same `containsCompilationKeyword` list `getNewReleases` and `isBetterRelease` use), **oldest first** (an artist's earlier records are what a newcomer is least likely to know, and their newest is what surfaced them in the first place), capped at 2 per artist. `core.albums` came free with `getArtistCore`, so the only new cost is one `getAlbumTracks` per sampled album, disk-cached per album thereafter. **Ordering is load-bearing: top songs are appended BEFORE album tracks**, so when an album track turns out to be the same recording as a hit, `selectTasteTracks`' identity dedup keeps the top-songs entry and with it that track's familiarity penalty. Reversed, hits would sneak back in at full weight through the album door.
  **Genre-specific discovery playlists.** `Playlist` gained `genre: String?` — **part of a generated playlist's IDENTITY, not a label**: `getGeneratedPlaylist` now matches on `(lens, genre)`, because matching on lens alone would make every genre run overwrite the same document. `buildTasteSeeds` gained `seedGenre:`, and the whole correctness of the feature is in what that does NOT narrow: it gates `note(...)` (which artists seed the expansion) and deliberately never `noteHeard(...)` or `allArtistKeys`. Narrowing those would let a hip-hop run suggest an artist the user plays constantly in another genre — **the exclusion bug above, reintroduced through a side door** — so it has its own test. `Post`/`SpotlightTrack` carry no genres (only `LibraryTrackDoc` does), so in a scoped run they are excluded from *seeding* rather than guessed at, while still counting fully as heard and known. Candidate tracks are then filtered to the genre too, because Apple's similarity graph is genre-agnostic and an expansion seeded from hip-hop can still surface an artist's rock side project: seeds narrow *where we look*, the filter narrows *what we keep*.
  **UI:** "Found for you" became a rail — the general tile plus one per the user's top 3 genres (`libraryVM.genreEntries`, capped at 3 because each is a full run and past three it stops being "your genres" and becomes a genre directory). The general tile is **kept**: a cross-genre suggestion is a different thing from "more of the hip-hop you already play", and dropping it would narrow discovery to whatever the user listens to most, which is the opposite of the point. `PlaylistGenerator` gained `tasteBusyKey` so only the tapped tile spins — `generating` is keyed by `PlaylistLens` and every genre shares `.taste`, so without it one generation would grey out every tile. Still one taste run at a time (they share `emptyReason`/`errorMessage`, and each is a real fan-out).
  **Verification.** Clean `xcodebuild` for the device with **zero warnings in all six touched files**. 278/278 NodalongKit tests (273 + 5). Installed, binary mtime checked.
  **NOT verified on device, and this is the round where that matters most.** That "Found for you" no longer returns artists already in the Shelf — **the whole point, and the one thing to check first**; that the results now read as deep cuts rather than hits; that the three genre tiles appear, build, and produce genuinely genre-appropriate results; and that the first genre generation isn't unacceptably slow, since it is a full similarity expansion plus up to 2 album fetches per candidate artist before any cache is warm.

- **2026-09-07 (later still) — "Can we build a discovery playlist from similar artists?" IT ALREADY EXISTED — and reading it found the reason it wasn't landing: the engine was scoring for PLAUSIBILITY when a discovery lens needs SURPRISE. Inverted the rank weighting, widened the pool 10 → 30, added a seeded reshuffle, and fixed TWO routing bugs that made `.taste` regeneration a silent no-op. Build-green, zero new warnings, 273/273 tests (6 new, 1 deliberately inverted), installed.**
  **First answer: the feature was already there.** The `.taste` lens behind Home's "Found for you" already did exactly the shape asked for — seeds from library + recents + spotlights → expand through Apple's `similarArtists` graph → rank candidate artists by corroboration → pull their songs → drop everything already heard → 25 tracks credited "Because you listen to X, Y and Z." **Worth saying plainly before building anything: the right first move on a feature request here is to check whether it exists, because this one did, fully tested, and the useful work turned out to be one line of scoring rather than a new system.**
  **🔴 The actual defect, and it is a design inversion rather than a bug.** `selectTasteTracks` scored `positionWeight = 1 / (1 + rank × tasteRankDecay)` — which **rewards rank 0**, each similar artist's biggest hit. For a lens whose entire job is surfacing music you haven't heard, that is backwards: if Apple's graph says "you like Fontaines D.C., try Shame", the single Shame track you have plausibly already heard is their most popular one. The engine was tuned for plausibility when it should have been tuned for surprise, and the output read as a greatest-hits reel of artists adjacent to your taste. Replaced with `discoveryPositionWeight(rank:)` — signature tracks (rank < 3) **penalised** to 0.6, everything past that **flat**. Flat deliberately, not inverted-steep: rank within top songs is a popularity signal, not a quality one, so past the "you have definitely heard this" band there's no reason to believe track 12 beats track 5.
  **The pool was also too narrow to choose from.** `getArtistCore` returns a single unpaginated relationship page (~10) — an artist's most-played handful, i.e. precisely the band now being penalised. Candidates now top up to **30** via the already-built `getArtistTopSongsFull` and are written back through `MusicDetailCache.updateTopTracks` (the existing top-up path, disk-persisted), so this is paid once per artist ever. Without the wider pool the new weighting would have had almost nothing left to pick.
  **Seeded reshuffle — resolving "random" against this codebase's determinism rule rather than overriding it.** Selection was fully deterministic, ties broken on ISRC then title, explicitly so "regenerating with unchanged inputs produces an unchanged playlist rather than churning the user's list." That rule is right, but it had quietly turned the ↻ button into a no-op: same seeds, same 25. The resolution is that the rule exists to stop a playlist changing **underneath** the user, not to make an **explicit** rebuild meaningless. `selectTasteTracks` gained `shuffleSeed: UInt64?` — `nil` reproduces the old behaviour byte-for-byte (which is what every pre-existing test pins, and what the weekly auto-refresh now uses), and a seed jitters each score by up to ±35% via a splitmix64 `TasteShuffleGenerator`. **Jitter, not a free shuffle**, so corroboration still wins: a test drives 50 seeds and asserts none of them overturns a 100× score gap. An explicit tap draws a random seed; the weekly auto-refresh uses an FNV hash of the uid, so a background rebuild only changes the list insofar as the *candidates* changed.
  **🔴 TWO routing bugs found while wiring this, both making `.taste` regeneration silently do nothing.** `generate(lens:)` → `candidatePool(lens:)` returns `[]` for `.taste` **correctly** (it has no pool of people; the case is explicit with a comment saying it "never enters this pipeline"). That comment was true of `generate`'s normal callers and **false of two others**: (1) `refreshStaleIfNeeded` passes whatever lens is stale straight into `generate`, so a stale taste playlist produced nothing AND burned `hasAutoRefreshedThisSession`, meaning a genuinely stale people-lens didn't get refreshed either; (2) `rebuildButton` called `runGeneration(lens)` for every lens, so ↻ on "Found for you" went down the people pipeline and could never have worked regardless of the determinism problem. Both now route `.taste` to `generateTaste`. **Lesson: a "this case is unreachable" comment is a claim about callers, and callers get added.**
  **Test note worth recording: one pre-existing test was deliberately inverted, not weakened.** `testMaxPerArtistCapKeepsTheirBestTracks` asserted the cap keeps `["T0","T1"]` — an artist's two biggest tracks. Under the new weighting it keeps `["T3","T4"]`. Renamed to `testMaxPerArtistCapPrefersDeeperCutsOverSignatureTracks` and re-asserted, with the cap itself still explicitly checked so the rename can't hide a regression in the thing it was originally testing. 5 further tests added: the weight curve's shape, no-seed determinism, same-seed reproducibility, different-seed divergence, and the bounded-jitter guarantee above.
  **Verification.** Clean `xcodebuild` for the device, **zero warnings in every touched file**; the two in `AppleMusicService` are the pre-existing line-18 `MusicService`/`@MainActor` conformance one. 273/273 NodalongKit tests. Installed with binary mtime checked.
  **NOT verified on device.** That "Found for you" now returns tracks that actually feel like discoveries rather than the hits of near-neighbours; that ↻ **visibly changes the list** (this is the single most direct check — it demonstrably did nothing before, for two independent reasons); and that the 30-song top-up doesn't make the first-ever generation noticeably slower, since it's one extra request per candidate artist before the cache warms.

- **2026-09-07 (later) — "Home is still bare" investigated with REAL DEVICE NUMBERS, not guesses: New Releases showed 1 tile because three filters compounded, and `Artist.latestRelease` turned out to conflate a new record with a REISSUE. Fixed the pool + guards; made Top Charts actually change daily; added city + per-genre charts and Apple's own recently-played containers. Build-green, zero new warnings, 269/269 tests (2 new), installed.**
  **The 1-tile mystery, answered from the device rather than reasoned about:** `getNewReleases(12 artists): 4 in 277ms` — 12 artists → 4 inside the 60-day window → the caller then dropped 3 already in the Shelf → **1**. The resolver was fine (277ms, cache warm); three individually-defensible filters compounded. Widened to **30 artists / 120 days** (measured, not guessed: most artists in a real library are catalogue acts who haven't released recently, so the pool has to be wide enough for the few who have). Re-measured at 12 artists after the window change: 4 → **6**.
  **🔴 The finding that matters more than the count: `Artist.latestRelease` does NOT distinguish a new record from a reissue.** The one tile on screen was Mac Miller's *The Divine Feminine* — a 2016 album — which passed a 60-day cutoff because **Apple dates an anniversary remaster by its catalog date**, and MusicKit exposes no original-release date to compare against. **Widening the window makes this worse, not better**, which is why it can't just be a dial change. Mitigated three ways, and honestly labelled as mitigation rather than a fix: obvious reissues dropped by keyword (reusing `containsCompilationKeyword` — the same list `isBetterRelease` already scores catalog rows with, so "what is a reissue" stays defined in one place); anything already in the Shelf **under any edition** dropped by checking both the raw title and `canonicalAlbumTitle` against `groupByAlbum`'s own key; and every tile now shows **how long ago the release is dated**, so a survivor explains itself instead of looking broken. A plain-titled reissue with no keyword still gets through — a real, stated gap.
  **Top Charts was static because we were passing a default.** `MusicCatalogChartsRequest` was constructed as `(types:)` only, which silently means `kinds: [.mostPlayed]` — a rolling popularity chart that barely moves week to week. That is the whole reason the rail looked identical every launch. Now `.dailyGlobalTop`, with a `.mostPlayed` fallback if a storefront publishes no daily chart.
  **Two genuinely new chart slices from the SAME request type, no new plumbing.** `.cityTop` → "Top in Your City" (**confirmed live: 10 songs in 753ms** — Apple does publish one for this storefront), and `genre:` → "Top in {their genre}", resolved from `libraryVM.genreEntries`. That last one is the first real consumer of the genre data captured at ingest since 2026-08-25, which until now fed only one Shelf grouping. `Genre` is `MusicCatalogTopLevelResourceRequesting`, so a bare `MusicCatalogResourceRequest<Genre>()` returns the whole (small, fixed) list; cached per session and matched by name. Both sit ABOVE the global chart in `baseRank` and both count as **fresh** — a chart that genuinely turns over daily has earned that, where the old sticky one hadn't.
  **🔴 `MusicRecentlyPlayedRequest<RecentlyPlayedMusicItem>` measured `0 albums / 0 playlists` — and that is CORRECT, not a bug.** Apple's recently-played *containers* only exist if a container was played. `playCurrent()` sets `player.queue = [singleSong]` on every single track **by design** (our queue is the source of truth; MusicKit's is deliberately held to one entry), so Apple never observes an album or playlist being played from this app and has none to report. What this surfaces is specifically listening done **in the Music app itself** — real added value, just structurally zero for a Nodalong-primary listener. Shipped anyway (the section hides when empty) with the reason recorded in the doc comment so nobody re-diagnoses it as broken. It's also deduped against Jump Back In by normalised album title, so the two rails can never show the same covers side by side — the leftover is exactly the off-app listening, which is the only part worth a second rail. Playlists come through too, which Jump Back In structurally cannot show (it infers albums from track metadata; a playlist has no album to infer).
  **What we still don't touch, catalogued while looking** (asked directly, answered from the SDK interface rather than memory): `MusicPersonalRecommendation.stations` — already in a response we fetch and **discard**, blocked not by data but because the player is a fixed `[QueueItem]` array and a station is continuous; Playlist charts (`types: [Playlist.self]`); `Album.relatedAlbums`/`.otherVersions`/`.appearsOn`; `Playlist.moreByCurator`/`.featuredArtists`/`.curator`; `Artist.featuredPlaylists`/`.playlists`/`.station`; `Genre` as a browsable type (Search's genre tiles are still hardcoded search strings); `MusicCatalogSearchSuggestionsRequest` for search autocomplete; `MusicLibrarySearchRequest`/`MusicLibrarySectionedRequest`.
  **Method note.** Every number above came from `xcrun devicectl device process launch --console` against the real device, which works without any interaction because Home is the landing tab and its `.task` fires on launch — the app's own `diagLog` lines did the rest. Three rounds of "why is it bare" were settled in minutes that way, and the reissue trap would not have been visible from code reading at all. **The device must be unlocked**; the final re-measure at 30 artists could not be captured because it had locked (`FBSOpenApplicationErrorDomain error 7`), so **that specific number is unconfirmed** — the build is installed and the next launch will log it.
  **Verification.** Clean `xcodebuild` for both a simulator target and the device, **zero warnings in every touched file** (grepped per file from the full log); the two in `AppleMusicService` are the pre-existing `MusicService`/`@MainActor` conformance one at line 18. 269/269 NodalongKit tests (2 new: personal charts outrank the global one; Recently Played sits directly under Jump Back In). Installed with binary mtime checked against the clock.
  **NOT verified.** That 30 artists yields materially more than 6; that "Top in Your City" names something sensible and its songs play; that the genre chart resolves the user's actual top genre rather than falling back to a fuzzy contains-match; and that the dated New Releases tiles make an occasional reissue read as deliberate rather than wrong.

- **2026-09-07 — Home pass: Continue Listening hero, sections turned into ORDERABLE VALUES (fresh-first + pin/hide + a density cap), see-all screens for every capped rail, and a real New Releases section built on `Artist.latestRelease`. Build-green with zero new warnings in any touched file, 267/267 NodalongKit tests (15 new), installed to the device.**
  **The problem, stated plainly:** Home rendered **eleven** sections in hardcoded source order, each shown iff its array was non-empty. Nothing could reorder, count or suppress them, so a busy account got the same wall as a brand-new one — with the people-driven content that is §1's whole differentiator sitting above a large block of Apple shelves that look identical every launch. Every rail also dead-ended at 10 with no way to see the rest (only Recommended for You had a "See All"), and although `restoreQueue()` already brings a paused queue back on cold launch, Home said nothing about it.
  **Three decisions locked with the user BEFORE writing anything, after previewing a before/after mockup** (the convention this repo already uses for palette/discography/vinyl work): ordering is **fresh-first over a fixed base order plus pin/hide**, not a full per-launch re-score; the hero shows **only when nothing is playing**; New Releases draws from **the user's own Shelf artists**, not the follow graph.
  **🔴 The hero's load-bearing constraint is what it must NOT observe.** `ContinueListeningHero` reads `queue`/`currentIndex`/`isPlaying` and deliberately never `playerVM.clock`. `PlaybackClock` was split off `PlayerViewModel` precisely so the 2–10 Hz progress tick stops firing `objectWillChange` on every observer — that was the documented Home scroll stutter — so drawing a live progress bar here would have put it straight back. The "only when nothing is playing" decision makes that free rather than a sacrifice: while paused there is no live progress to draw. Note the "always show it" option we did NOT take would have walked directly into this. It also answers the redundancy objection: the mini player already shows the current track on every tab, so the hero earns its place only by showing what the mini player never does — album context and **queue position** ("Bon Iver · i,i · 4 of 13") — and by collapsing away entirely once playback starts.
  **Sections are now values.** New `HomeSectionID` + pure `orderHomeSections(available:fresh:pinned:hidden:)` in NodalongKit (15 tests), following the `PlaylistOrder`/`GroupByAlbum`/`TasteAffinity` convention; `PlaylistLens.title`/`.tagline` is the precedent for a NodalongKit enum carrying user-facing strings. **Two freshness buckets, not a continuous score** — a fully-scored re-sort makes Home structurally different every launch, which defeats muscle memory and makes "where did that section go?" unanswerable. Within each bucket `baseRank` holds, so **identical inputs produce an identical order**; that is the property the design exists to protect and it has its own test (including one asserting input order can't leak into the result). Pins outrank freshness (an explicit choice beats an inferred one) and `jumpBackIn.isHideable == false` — it's the one section that keeps Home usable with an empty follow graph and no subscription, i.e. exactly the account most likely to hide things. `HomeView.body` collapsed from eleven inline `if` blocks to a `ForEach` over `visibleSections`, capped at 7 with a "Show N more sections" button.
  **🔴 The density cap introduced a real bug that had to be designed around, not discovered later:** `madeByPeopleSection` carried `.task { await loadAndRefreshGenerated() }`, and **nothing else on Home touches `playlistsVM`**. Once a section can sit below the cap and never render, that task never fires and the generated-playlist tiles stay permanently empty — silently reintroducing the exact bug that trigger was added to fix on 2026-08-25. Moved to `HomeView.body`. **General lesson: making a section conditional orphans any `.task` attached to it — check what a section's own body was responsible for loading before allowing it not to render.**
  **New Releases — verified against the SDK before building, per the standing rule.** MusicKit has no new-releases endpoint (which is why `getPopularAlbums` is deliberately labelled a popularity chart), but `Artist.latestRelease: Album?` is real and requestable — read straight out of `iPhoneOS26.2.sdk/…/MusicKit.swiftinterface`, along with `MusicCatalogResourceRequest(matching:memberOf:)`, before a line was written. So the shape is: resolve each Shelf artist NAME to a catalog id, then **ONE batched request** across all of them, not N. Name→id goes through `MusicDetailCache.resolveArtistByName`, already name-keyed, in-flight-shared and **disk-persisted**, so each artist is cold exactly once ever. The fetch closure is byte-identical to `CatalogPrefetch.artistByName`'s **deliberately** — that cache is keyed by name, so a differently-shaped fetch would mean whichever caller ran first silently won. Capturing the service in the `@Sendable` closure is legal because `MusicService: AnyObject, Sendable`, which is why `CatalogPrefetch` already compiles this way. Releases already saved to the Shelf are filtered out by rebuilding **`groupByAlbum`'s own key** (`stripSearchNoise` → lowercased → trimmed) rather than inventing a second normalisation rule that could drift from it. Renders through the shared `AlbumsCarousel`, which already pushes `AlbumResult` — a destination Home registers — so **zero new navigation wiring**, and the carousel's per-tile prefetch comes free.
  **`loadNewReleases` is deliberately NOT a sixth child of `HomeViewModel.load()`'s task group** — it needs the Shelf artist list, which belongs to `LibraryViewModel`, so folding it in would either block those deliberately-independent children on the library fetch or run against an empty list on a cold launch. Sequenced after instead, and added to `HomeSnapshot` as an **optional** field: `SnapshotStore.load` swallows a decode failure with `try?`, so a required new key would silently turn every launch back into a cold network load with no signal — the `CatalogTrack` trap from 2026-08-26, avoided by construction this time.
  **Smaller calls worth recording.** The long-press menu is a `confirmationDialog`, **not** `.contextMenu` — this repo already migrated off `.contextMenu` once (2026-06-27) because its UIKit lift effect floods the console with `_UIGravityWellEffectAnchorView`/`_UIReparentingView` warnings. `HomeLayoutPreferences` is `UserDefaults`, keyed per-uid, not Firestore: it's a device-local presentation preference like `AppearanceMode`/`AlbumArtworkStyle`, and its decode is tolerant so a renamed section falls back to the default order rather than to nothing. Hiding from a header would otherwise be a one-way door (the header you'd long-press to undo it is the thing that's gone), hence the `HomeLayoutSheet` behind a control by the greeting. `recommendedSection` builds its own header so it had to opt into the menu by hand — worth doing rather than skipping, since five Apple shelves make it the tallest thing on Home and the likeliest thing a user wants gone. `HomeSeeAllView` deliberately declares **no** `.navigationDestination` (it's always pushed onto Home's stack, which registers every type it pushes) and reuses each rail's already-loaded array minus the cap; Top Charts is the one exception — its rail is capped at the FETCH (`limit: 10`), so there is genuinely nothing more in memory and it re-fetches at 50, mirroring what `RecommendedForYouView` already does with `maxGroups`.
  **Process note, second occurrence of a documented trap:** `xcodebuild -destination id=` and `devicectl --device` take **different identifiers for the same phone** (`00008140-…` vs `D82C32BF-…`); the first device build failed with "Unable to find a device matching the provided destination specifier" until that was noticed. Binary mtime was checked against the wall clock before each install, since a successful install line does not mean a fresh build was installed.
  **Verification.** `xcodegen generate` (5 new files) + clean `xcodebuild` from a fresh derived-data path for both a simulator target and the device, with **zero warnings in any of the nine touched files** — grepped per file from the full log, not inferred from a green build. The single warning in `AppleMusicService` is the pre-existing `MusicService`/`@MainActor` conformance one at line 18 (its class declaration), identical to the one `SpotifyMusicService:20` carries and already catalogued in §7. 267/267 NodalongKit tests (252 + 15 new `HomeSectionOrderTests`). Fresh binary installed to the physical device.
  **NOT verified — needs the device, and the negative cases matter as much as the positive ones.** That the hero appears paused on cold launch with the right queue position AND **collapses away once playback starts** (the entire point of the decision); that Home opens at ≤7 sections and "Show N more" reveals the rest; that spotlighting something floats Friends' Spotlights up on the next launch AND — **the failure mode this design exists to prevent** — that relaunching again with nothing new leaves the order unchanged; that pin/hide survive a relaunch and a hidden section is recoverable from the sheet; that each see-all shows genuinely more than its rail and Top Charts really re-fetches 50; and that New Releases populates for a real Shelf, excludes already-saved albums, and is near-instant on the **second** launch (the name→id resolves should be disk-cache hits by then — if it's still slow, the cache isn't doing its job).

- **2026-08-31 (later) — 🔴 SCREEN STACKING ROOT-CAUSED FROM A REAL DEVICE LOG, after three rounds of wrong theories. Value-equality dedup was structurally incapable of the job: THREE DIFFERENT TYPES push the same artist page. Replaced with destination-IDENTITY dedup + pop-back-to-existing. Build-green, 235/235 tests, installed.**
  **The evidence, and it disproved my own fix in two separate ways.** The log showed the dedup *working* — a genuine `nav.push DEDUPED PendingAlbumResolve tab=home depth=1` for the same album tapped twice in a row. But then:
  `nav.push PUSHED PendingArtistResolve tab=home depth=1 recordedDepth=1 valueEqual=false`
  `nav.push PUSHED PendingAlbumResolve  tab=home depth=2 recordedDepth=2 valueEqual=false`
  **`lastPushed` only remembered the LAST push, so any alternation defeated it** — album → artist → album each differ from their immediate predecessor, and the user's actual behaviour ("I can just keep tapping album/artist name") is exactly that ping-pong. Worse: **six `ArtistDetailSearchView.onAppear` lines fired against ONE router artist push** — the rest came from `NavigationLink(value:)`, invisible to the router. SwiftUI corroborated, warning three times in a row that a `PendingArtistLookup` destination "was declared earlier on the stack".
  **🔴 The finding that made the previous approach unfixable rather than merely incomplete: `PendingArtistResolve` (player), `PendingArtistLookup` (album screen) and `ArtistResult` (Similar Artists) are three different types that all land on the SAME artist page.** No amount of stack bookkeeping makes value equality recognise that. **Dedup has to key on the destination screen, not the pushed value.**
  **Shipped:** new `NavIdentity` (`.artist`/`.album`) built from the app's existing `primaryArtist(_:)` / `stripSearchNoise(_:)`, so "same artist" means here exactly what it means in `groupByAlbum`/`groupByArtist` — "¥$, Kanye West & Ty Dolla $ign" collapses to "kanye west" for free. `AppRouter` replaced `lastPushed` with a real per-tab **shadow stack** `[NavIdentity?]`, reconciled against `path.count` before every operation (pops truncate; a link push we never saw pads with `nil` rather than being guessed at). `push` now **pops back** to an already-open screen instead of stacking a copy — confirmed behaviour with the user, who chose it over a silent no-op: you asked for that page, you land on it. A push with no identity behaves exactly as before, so nothing already working changed.
  **Converted the invisible pushes the log implicated** — `AlbumDetailSearchView`'s artist name (the site the log named directly), Similar Artists, both discography grids, and `AlternateVersionsSection` — from `NavigationLink(value:)` to `router.push(_:identity:)`. **Deliberately did NOT convert all 41 link sites**: Search results and Home tiles push from a visible list at depth 0–1 and are not part of the pile-up, and `artist → album → artist` is legitimate browsing that *should* stack. Narrow first, widen only on evidence.
  **Method note — this is the round that justifies the previous one's file-based logging.** Three rounds of code-reading produced three plausible theories, all wrong; one real device log settled it in minutes AND showed the previous fix half-working, which no amount of re-reading would have revealed. **When a navigation fix doesn't land, instrument the decision point and read what it actually decided** — `DEDUPED` vs `PUSHED … valueEqual=false` is the difference between "not called" and "called and declined", and those need opposite fixes.
  **Diagnostics deliberately KEPT** (now with a `POPPED-TO` line) until this is confirmed on device; removing them is a follow-up.
  **NOT verified on device.** The ping-pong that produced the bug (player → album → artist name → reopen player → artist → reopen player → album) should now never grow the stack. **Also worth checking the opposite failure, which pop-back makes newly possible: Similar Artists → a DIFFERENT artist must still push, and Search → artist → one of their albums → back must still work.** Pop-back discards whatever sat above the screen returned to — that is the agreed behaviour but will be noticeable from a deep stack.

- **2026-08-31 — Nav diagnostics now persist to a FILE in the app container, because `devicectl --console` requires the operator and the user to be present at the same instant and kept failing. Pull path proven end-to-end. Build-green, 235/235 tests, installed.**
  **Why.** The screen-stacking repro (player → artist → reopen player → same artist) needs a device log, and `print()` from a device is only visible through `xcrun devicectl … --console`, which needs the device unlocked **with someone watching at the moment of the repro**. Three consecutive attempts died three different ways — force-quit (`signal 9`), tunnel drop (`RemoteXPCPeerConnection … state = canceled`), lock screen (`FBSOpenApplicationErrorDomain error 7`). **`--console` is fine for a repro you drive yourself and unsuitable for one that waits on a human.**
  **🔴 The enabling fact, verified BEFORE writing any code: the app data container is fully readable over `devicectl`, with the device locked and no coordination.** `xcrun devicectl device info files --domain-type appDataContainer --domain-identifier com.bigdeccyh.nodalong` listed 4,123 files, and `devicectl device copy from … --source "Library/Application Support/Snapshots/<uid>/profile.json"` pulled a real one ("File received from Device"). Proving the pull path first is what made the whole approach worth building — the alternative was another round discovering it didn't work.
  **Shipped:** `navLog(_:)` in `DiagLog.swift` — same `#if DEBUG` + `@autoclosure` discipline as `diagLog`, so Release writes nothing and the interpolation never runs. Prints AND appends to `Library/Application Support/nav.log` via a private `NavLogFile` on its own utility queue (never file I/O on the main thread — `AppRouter.push` runs there), capped at 256 KB and truncated rather than rotated, since it is scratch for one investigation. `AppRouter`'s three nav lines route through it. Added `ArtistDetailSearchView.onAppear` logging id+name — **the router logs what it DECIDED; this logs what actually happened**, so two lines for one artist in a run *is* the bug as a counted fact rather than a report. Pull with `devicectl device copy from --source "Library/Application Support/nav.log"`.
  **Could NOT self-verify the sink**, stated plainly: the approved plan's verification step was "navigate somewhere that pushes, then pull the file", and driving a tap on a physical device isn't something this environment can do. `nav.log` doesn't exist until something logs, and nothing logs at launch. Deliberately did **not** add a launch marker just to make it self-checking — that would be a code change beyond the approved plan, and the user's own repro exercises both log sites anyway, so a broken sink surfaces at the same cost either way.
  **Process note, second occurrence: a `cd` inside a compound Bash command persisted into the NEXT call**, so a device build again ran from `NodalongKit/` and failed with "workspace NodalongKit does not contain a scheme named Nodalong". Caught by checking the binary's mtime against the wall clock rather than trusting the build output — worth doing on every device install, since last time the stale binary got installed silently.

- **2026-08-30 (last, cont.) — 🔴 `.presentationBackground(.clear)` does NOT remove iOS 26's Liquid Glass sheet container. The track menu moved to a bare transparent `fullScreenCover` that draws its own scrim, card and transition. Build-green, zero warnings, 235/235 tests, installed. Stacking still unresolved — three console captures dropped before the repro could be run.**
  **Report:** "why is the 3 dot menu in a liquid glass container? can we not just make it its own thing?"
  **🔴 The finding, worth knowing before reaching for `.sheet` again on iOS 26: `presentationBackground(.clear)` clears the *background*, not the sheet's own container.** On iOS 26 that container is Liquid Glass and is drawn **outside** what `presentationBackground` reaches, so a custom card presented in a sheet ends up nested inside a system material no matter what background you set. There is no modifier that removes it. **The only way to own the surface is to stop being a sheet** — a `fullScreenCover` has no container of its own, so a transparent one is a bare layer you draw everything into.
  **What that bought, beyond the look.** The detent went away with the sheet, and with it `TrackActionsSheet.detentHeight` and all its `@ScaledMetric` plumbing — **the card now sizes to its content like any normal view**, which removes the "computed estimate drifts from the real layout" risk flagged when this shipped (and which the crash the round before had *also* been rooted in). Net: the fix for a cosmetic complaint deleted the feature's most fragile piece.
  **Hand-rolling the transition is required, not optional.** A `fullScreenCover` slides its whole layer up; since our layer includes the dimming scrim, the *scrim* would slide too, which reads as a sheet again. So the cover is presented with the system transition suppressed — `withTransaction { t.disablesAnimations = true }` around the one state change, deliberately **not** a `.transaction` modifier, which would flatten animations for everything else in the view — and `TrackActionsSheet` animates its own scrim fade and card slide via `@State shown`. Dismissal has to animate out *before* reporting, or the card vanishes with the cover: `close(_:)` animates, then hands off after 0.18s. **Accepted limit:** drag-to-dismiss triggers on release past a threshold rather than tracking the finger.
  **Reused the cover-geometry pattern rather than rediscovering it.** The card pins to `ScreenMetrics.bounds` + `.ignoresSafeArea()` and re-applies the **key-window** insets — the remedy `NowPlayingView` arrived at over several rounds after a `safeAreaInset` leaked a phantom inset into its own cover. That key-window read moved from a private var on `NowPlayingView` into `ScreenMetrics.safeAreaInsets` so the two share it instead of copying.
  **🔴 The stacking is STILL unresolved, and this round produced no evidence — worth recording why, because the method failed rather than the theory.** `xcrun devicectl --console` is the only way to capture `print()` from a device (`log stream` does not see it), but it requires the device unlocked at launch AND the operator watching at the same moment the user reproduces. **Three consecutive attempts died three different ways:** the app was force-quit (`App terminated due to signal 9`, ~65s in), the tunnel dropped (`RemoteXPCPeerConnection … state = canceled`, ~7s in — the same instability recorded on 2026-08-09), and the device locked (`FBSOpenApplicationErrorDomain error 7 … BSErrorCodeDescription = Locked`). **Lesson: `--console` is fine for a repro you can drive yourself, and unsuitable for one that waits on a human.** If this recurs, write the diagnostics to a file in the app container and pull it afterwards, so capture and repro don't have to coincide.
  **What the one partial capture did establish:** `nav.push PUSHED FriendsListeningAlbum tab=home depth=0` proves the logging works and router pushes reach it; and ~60s of subsequent browsing (album screen, `resolveArtistByName`, artist detail loading) produced **zero** `nav.push` lines — consistent with the album screen's artist name being a `NavigationLink` that never touches the router. **No `nav.dismissThenPush` line was ever captured**, so the full-screen-player path — the actual reported repro — remains completely unobserved. The `[Nodalong.Nav]` diagnostics stay in the tree until it is.

- **2026-08-30 (last) — 🔴 CRASH I SHIPPED, ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED: reading a computed property off a View struct that is NOT INSTALLED in a hierarchy. The new "…" menu trapped on every open. Plus `[Nodalong.Nav]` diagnostics added, because the screen-stacking fix did NOT land and the device log could not discriminate why.**
  **🔴 The crash — a general SwiftUI trap worth internalising.** `TrackActionsMenuButton` built the sheet in its `.sheet` closure and then read `sheet.detentHeight` off that instance to feed `.presentationDetents`. **A View struct constructed in a closure is not installed in a view hierarchy, so it has no environment.** `detentHeight` touched `@ScaledMetric` (backed by `Environment<DynamicTypeSize>` + `Environment<CGFloat>`) and, via `showsQueue` → `routeToParty`, `@EnvironmentObject partyVM`. `@Environment` silently returns a default in that state; **`@EnvironmentObject` traps outright**. The device log printed the mechanism verbatim before dying — two `Accessing Environment<…>'s value outside of being installed on a View` lines, then `Fatal error: No ObservableObject of type ListeningPartyViewModel found`. It compiled clean, produced zero warnings, and crashed 100% of the time on the one interaction the whole feature exists for.
  **The general rule:** anything read off a View struct **outside `body`** — to feed a modifier argument like `presentationDetents`, `frame`, `navigationTitle` — cannot use `@Environment`, `@EnvironmentObject`, `@ScaledMetric`, `@State` or `@StateObject`. There is no compile-time signal whatsoever. **Fix:** `TrackActionsSheet.detentHeight(...)` became a **pure static function** (no environment, no `self`) taking the row heights and visibility flags as parameters; the *presenter* owns `@EnvironmentObject partyVM` and the three `@ScaledMetric` values and computes the height while it genuinely is installed. Shared `static let` bases (`headerBase`/`actionRowBase`/`navRowBase`) seed both the sheet's own `@ScaledMetric` properties and the presenter's, so the laid-out rows and the detent can't drift apart.
  **🔴 The screen-stacking fix did NOT land, and I deliberately did not guess a third time.** The user's log shows `ArtistDetailSearchView` genuinely constructed three times plus SwiftUI's duplicate-destination warnings for `PendingArtistLookup` and `DiscographyTab`. But the log is from an **album-detail** screen (`resolveArtistByName('Bon Iver')` is `CatalogPrefetch.artistByName`, used only by `AlbumDetailSearchView`) whose artist name is a plain **`NavigationLink(value:)`** — one of **41 in the app** — which never touches `AppRouter` and so was never covered by the dedup at all. So the log cannot distinguish "the router dedup is broken" from "this tap never reached the router". Given two of the last three navigation fixes here were plausible theories that turned out wrong, the honest move was instrumentation, not a fourth theory.
  **Diagnostics added (TEMPORARY, tagged `[Nodalong.Nav]`, to be removed once root-caused):** `AppRouter.push` now logs `DEDUPED` or `PUSHED` with `depth`, `recordedDepth` and `valueEqual`; `dismissThenPush` logs when it fires. One repro then discriminates every possibility: **no line at all** = the tap went through a `NavigationLink` and never reached the router; **`valueEqual=false`** = the two taps built different values; **depth mismatch** = something pushed or popped in between; **`DEDUPED` yet a duplicate still appears** = the dedup works and something else creates the second instance. Same instrumenting-over-theorising approach that cracked the split-album bug.
  **Deliberately NOT done:** converting the 41 `NavigationLink(value:)` sites to `router.push`. It is the obvious move *if* the link path is the culprit, but it is a wide change to working navigation and **artist → album → artist is legitimate browsing, not a bug** — a link landing on a genuinely different screen *should* stack. Which links need dedup depends entirely on the real repro.
  **Process note worth keeping: a `cd` inside a compound Bash command persisted into the next call and silently built from `NodalongKit/`**, which failed with "workspace NodalongKit does not contain a scheme named Nodalong" — while the `devicectl install` in the same chain **succeeded, installing the STALE previous binary**. Caught by reading the output rather than trusting the "App installed" line; rebuilt from the repo root and verified the binary's mtime before reinstalling. **A successful install line does not mean a fresh build was installed.**
  **Verification.** Clean `xcodebuild` for both a generic simulator target and the device, zero warnings in all three touched files; 235/235 NodalongKit tests; fresh binary installed to the device.
  **NOT verified — needs the device.** That the menu now opens rather than trapping (this reproduced 100% of the time, so it is a one-tap check), and that the sheet height still looks right now that the presenter computes it. **And the stacking needs one repro with the console attached, plus which tap was used** — full-screen player → artist → reopen player → same artist, versus album screen → artist name → back → again. The `[Nodalong.Nav]` lines around that moment are the evidence.

- **2026-08-30 (later still) — The "…" track menu is now our own bottom sheet instead of the system `confirmationDialog`: icons + words, and album/artist rows that navigate. Made the menu SELF-SUFFICIENT, which deleted an inline `LibraryTrackDoc` duplicated across four screens. Build-green, zero new warnings, 235/235 tests, installed to device.**
  **Ask:** replace the native action sheet with our own menu — words plus icons (spotlight star, add-to-queue, like heart) — and add album and artist rows that navigate. Previewed two presentations against the real palette before writing code; **user chose a bottom sheet over an anchored dropdown, and "always show all rows" over today's adaptive set.**
  **🔴 The second choice is what shaped the implementation, and the obvious approach would have been wrong.** The old menu was passive: every call site handed it state and closures, and **four screens hand-rolled an identical 12-line `LibraryTrackDoc` inline** just to pass `onToggleLike`. Only `TrackCard` ever passed `onSpotlight`; `TrackCard` never passed `onToggleLike` at all. Showing every row everywhere by wiring all 10 call sites would have multiplied that boilerplate by six. **So the menu derives its own state instead** — give `TrackActionsSheet` a `Song` and it reads liked state from `LibraryViewModel.isInLibrary` and spotlight state from `FirebaseAuthViewModel.todaySpotlitIds`, both already app-wide env objects. Net effect: the feature grew and the call sites **shrank** — five of them lost their inline doc entirely, and one new `LibraryTrackDoc.from(song:)` in `SongConversions.swift` (the file's first *downward* conversion) replaced all four copies.
  **`song` became non-optional.** The menu can't show shelf/spotlight/album/artist without one. Verified first that all four real `TrackCard` call sites (`FeedView`, `HomeView`, `RecentTracksCarousel`, `SpotlightCarousel`) already pass `addToPlaylistSong`, so `TrackCard`'s guard simplified from `onAddToQueue != nil || addToPlaylistSong != nil` to `if let addToPlaylistSong` with nothing losing its menu.
  **Navigation needed no new plumbing** — grep confirmed `PendingArtistResolve`/`PendingAlbumResolve` are **already registered on all five tab stacks**, so the album/artist rows just build the marker from the `Song` and call `AppRouter.dismissThenPush`. Passing `dismiss: {}` because the sheet is already closed: the helper is reused purely for its captured-tab + 0.35s delayed push, which is required here for the same reason it is from the player — a push has to leave the modal stack before it can reach a tab's navigation. **This also composes with the router dedup shipped earlier the same day**: opening the menu twice and tapping "Artist" twice can't stack two copies.
  **Spotlight is disabled rather than alerting at the cap.** `spotlightLimitReached`'s alert is hosted only by Feed/Profile/Shelf, and this sheet also appears on Search and album/playlist detail, which host none — so an alert would be silently swallowed there. Disabling under exactly the condition that would set the flag makes it structurally unreachable, the same reasoning `NowPlayingView`'s own spotlight button already documents.
  **Height is computed, not measured.** `.presentationDetents([.height(sheet.detentHeight)])` sums only the rows actually shown, from **`@ScaledMetric`** constants — deliberately, because the discography grid shipped a real clipping bug (2026-08-14) from a *fixed* row-height estimate at larger Dynamic Type. A measured detent via `.onGeometryChange` is the fallback if the estimate drifts; not done first because it visibly resizes on the first frame.
  **`.presentationBackground(.clear)`** is what makes the card ours rather than something drawn inside a system surface. Worth knowing: **in dark mode `bgElevated` and `bgCard` are the same hex**, so a menu opened over a card has no colour difference at its edge — the sheet's own dimming scrim does the separating, with a 0.5pt hairline border as the backstop.
  **Verification.** Clean `xcodebuild` for both a generic simulator target and the device after `xcodegen generate` (one new file); grepped the log per touched file — **zero warnings in all eleven**. The two remaining in `ShelfView` are the pre-existing main-actor-isolation pair at lines 521/523, confirmed unchanged against this session's earlier build log (there were three; the `authVM.currentUser` one went away because this change deleted that reference). Three genuinely-new `unused isLiked` warnings were introduced and fixed, not shipped. Also swept for env objects left with no reader after their closures were deleted — a per-struct scan found five dead ones (`libraryVM`/`authVM` in `PlaylistDetailSearchView`, `ArtistDetailSearchView`, `PlaylistDetailView`) and confirmed `HomeView`/`ShelfView` had none despite file-level counts suggesting otherwise. 235/235 NodalongKit tests. Installed to the device.
  **NOT verified on-device.** Everything visual and behavioural: that the card renders with our corners/colours rather than the system sheet, on all four surface types (Shelf row, Search result, album track row, Feed card); that shelf/spotlight toggle and their icons change state; that spotlight is dimmed at 3-for-the-day; that the queue row says "Add to LP Queue" during a live party; that the playlist picker opens *after* the menu closes; and — **the most likely thing to be wrong** — that `detentHeight` matches reality, checked specifically on a track with no album (album row hidden) and at a large Dynamic Type size. Dead space at the bottom or a clipped last row means the estimate is out.

- **2026-08-30 (later) — Fixed the same screen stacking on itself when pushed from the full-screen player. `AppRouter.push` now no-ops when the destination is already the top of that tab's stack; all 17 direct `path.append` call sites routed through it. Build-green, zero new warnings, 235/235 tests, installed to device.**
  **Report:** tap the artist (or album) name in the full-screen player → land on that page; reopen the player and tap the same name → a **second identical page on top of the first**; repeat and you back out through three copies. Framed by the user as general QOL: "we seem to allow stacking of the same screen over and over."
  **Root cause.** `AppRouter.push(_:onto:)` was an unconditional `NavigationPath.append` with no idea what was already on the stack — **`NavigationPath` is opaque: you can read `.count` but never inspect its elements**, so there was nothing to compare against. This bites hardest for pushes originating **from a modal**: the player cover completely hides the navigation stack, so the user cannot see that the destination is already sitting underneath. `dismissThenPush`'s `0.35s` dismiss wait compounds it — a quick double-tap schedules *two* pushes before either has run.
  **Fix — track the last push and validate it against DEPTH, not identity alone.** New `lastPushed: [Tab: (value: AnyHashable, depth: Int)]`. Both halves are load-bearing: since pops only ever remove from the top, a recorded value is still the top **iff** the path's `.count` still equals the depth it reached when we pushed it. `push` no-ops when depth matches AND the value is equal; otherwise it appends and re-records. **Deliberately fails open** — any depth mismatch (the user backed out, or a `NavigationLink(value:)` pushed something the router never saw) falls through to today's plain append. A missed dedup is cosmetic; a wrongly-swallowed push is a dead tap, so the asymmetry is intentional.
  **Why the player case is caught exactly.** `ResolvingArtistsView`/`ResolvingAlbumView` swap themselves for the real detail view **in place** rather than pushing (see the 2026-08-07 round), so the depth stays 1 across the resolve and the record stays valid.
