The second tap builds an identical `PendingArtistResolve` (a `Hashable` struct over its stored fields) from the same `nowPlaying`, so value equality holds → no-op. Skip to a different track first and the struct differs → it pushes, correctly.
  **Coverage: 17 call sites reached past `push` straight into the published path**, so a fix confined to `push` would only have covered `dismissThenPush`. All rewritten mechanically to `router.push(x, onto: .tab)` — **keeping the explicit tab each site already hardcoded** rather than switching to `selectedTab`, so nothing changes but the dedup (several fire after an `await`, where the user may have changed tabs — `HomeView.runGeneration` is the clearest case). `AlbumDetailView` (`ShelfView.swift`) needed `@EnvironmentObject var router` added; its `@Binding var path` is **kept**, because it also *pops* (`path.removeLast()` after removing an album from the shelf) and only pushes route through the router. Grep confirms **zero `path.append` outside `AppRouter.swift`**. `NavigationLink(value:)` sites deliberately untouched — they need a tap on a row that is visibly on screen, so they structurally cannot produce the invisible duplicate this fixes.
  **Verification.** Clean `xcodebuild` for both a generic simulator target and the device; grepped the full log per touched file — zero warnings in `AppRouter`/`HomeView`/`SearchView`/`FeedView`, and the three in `ShelfView` proven **pre-existing** by diffing against this same session's earlier build log (byte-identical, same lines 521–525, unrelated main-actor isolation in a different part of the file). 235/235 NodalongKit tests (no NodalongKit changes — run as a regression check). Installed to the device.
  **NOT verified on-device.** The repro itself, and — more importantly — that the dedup does not **over**-fire. Worth checking specifically: artist → back → reopen player → tap the same artist (must push, depth changed); tap album then artist (both push); Home's Jump Back In two different albums in a row; Shelf album → artist name; and that removing an album from the Shelf still pops (that path is `removeLast`, untouched).
  **Known limit, stated plainly:** dedup is **top-of-stack only**. Artist X → album Y → tap X again still pushes a second X, because X is no longer the top. Popping back to an existing entry instead would silently discard Y, which is a bigger behavioural change than what was reported.

- **2026-08-30 — 🔴 ROOT CAUSE: all four generated-playlist lenses produced BYTE-IDENTICAL playlists, because a `PlaylistLens` only ever chose *which people*, never *which of their tracks*. Fixed by giving each lens its own signal weighting; plus a stage-1 ranking bug that made everyone without spotlights invisible. Build-green, zero warnings in every touched file, 235/235 tests (16 new), installed to device.**
  **Report:** "all 4 of my playlists are identical (for what it's worth I only follow one person)."
  **🔴 The mechanism, confirmed by reading rather than guessing.** A lens changes exactly one thing — the candidate pool. Everything downstream (`rankPeople` → `SocialCache.bundle` → `contributions` → `selectTracks`) is shared and **deterministic by design** (ties break on `catalogId`, deliberately, so regeneration doesn't churn the user's list). With one follow P: `circle` → `[P]`; `nearby` → includes P if they're nearby; `admirers` → `[P]` if P nodded you; `blend` → the union, so also `[P]`. Same person ⇒ same `[TrackContribution]` ⇒ four identical 25-track lists. **`skew` cannot rescue this**: it multiplies one person's contributions *uniformly*, so with a single contributor it is mathematically incapable of reordering anything. This is not an edge case — it is the normal outcome for any small follow graph, and `blend` is redundant with `circle` whenever the pools nest, which is most of the time under `deepFetchLimit = 8`.
  **🔴 Second bug, found while reading, arguably worse.** `PlaylistGenerator.personTaste(from:)` builds stage-1 taste from **`profile.spotlightTracks` only**. Most people have never spotlit anything, so their `TasteProfile` is empty → `tasteAffinity` returns 0 → `rankPeople` dropped them at the `minimumTasteAffinity` floor. So the whole feature only ever worked for people who spotlight, and the `nearby`/`admirers` pools structurally collapsed back onto whoever you already follow — which is a large part of *why* the four lenses converged. **Fix: distinguish "no data" from "bad match."** New `unknownTasteAffinity = 0.01` (strictly below the 0.02 floor, so an unknown can never outrank a real match at equal skew); an *empty* profile is kept at that baseline, while a *non-empty* profile with genuine non-overlap is still dropped. `deepFetchLimit` is untouched, so this changes *who* fills the 8 slots, never how many are fetched — zero read-cost change.
  **The main fix: a lens now has a character, not just a source.** New `SignalWeights` (NodalongKit) + `PlaylistLens.signalWeights`, threaded into `selectTracks` as a **defaulted** parameter (`.balanced`, derived from `TrackSignal.weight` so there's one source of truth) — every existing call site and all 15 pre-existing tests keep working untouched. `circle` leans hard on spotlights (5.0/1.5/0.4 — a friend's deliberate pick, capped at 3/day, costs the giver something); `nearby` on recent plays (1.5/1.0/**2.5** — what's actually playing around you *now*); `admirers` on replays (2.0/**3.5**/0.8 — the deep cuts people keep coming back to); `blend` stays balanced. The four lenses now sort the *same* candidate set by four different functions, and because `maxPerArtist: 2` is applied greedily **after** sorting, membership diverges too, not just order.
  **`blend` now refuses to be a solo act.** The one case that was actively dishonest: "Everyone whose taste matches yours" built from one person is just `circle` under a misleading name. Guarded on the *selected* contributor count (not the pool), so it needs no new schema and no extra fetch; `HomeView.generationNotice` already renders `emptyReason`, so it surfaces with no new UI.
  **Two UX gaps closed that would have hidden the fix entirely.** (1) The tile said "25 tracks" — what an algorithm's shelf says. `describe(lens:contributors:)` now **names people** ("From Aidooo, who you follow" / "From Aidooo, Sam and 3 others"), rendered via a new `tileSubtitle(...)` that falls back to the count for playlists generated before descriptions named anyone. (2) **Nothing could rebuild a playlist**: tapping a tile that already has one *navigates*, and `refreshStaleIfNeeded` only fires after 7 days — so without a manual trigger the user's four identical playlists would have sat there for a week and none of this would have reached them. Added an explicit ↻ button on the tile artwork, as a **nested `Button`** (not `.onTapGesture`) per this log's own 2026-08-14 finding that a nested Button reliably claims priority over its ancestor Button's action while a plain tap gesture does not.
  **Verification.** 235/235 NodalongKit tests (219 + 16 new). The load-bearing one is `testSignalWeightsChangeWhichTracksRankFirst` — same two contributions, `circle` ranks the spotlight first and `admirers` ranks the replay first — plus `testLensesDoNotAllProduceTheSameOrdering`, which asserts the exact property that was broken, and `testDefaultSignalWeightsAreBalanced`, which proves omitting the new parameter is byte-for-byte the old behaviour. Clean `xcodebuild` from a fresh derived-data path for both a generic simulator target and the physical device, with **zero warnings in any of the five touched files** (grepped the full log per file, not inferred from a green build). Installed to the device.
  **NOT verified — needs the user's own signed-in device.** That the four lenses now visibly differ; that `Your Blend` shows the honest refusal rather than a fourth clone; that the tiles read "From <name>"; and — **the opposite failure, which caching-style fixes are prone to** — that tapping ↻ twice on the same lens does **not** churn the list, i.e. determinism survived the weighting change.
  **Honest limit, stated plainly.** With **one** contributor, all four lenses draw from one small candidate pool (that person's spotlight history + recents, minus everything you've already heard). Different weights guarantee different orderings and meaningfully different membership, but if that person has barely more than 25 unheard tracks the lists will still overlap heavily. That is bounded by having one follow, not by the code. Also note change (2) means a nearby stranger with *no* shared taste can now contribute where they were previously discarded — the `maxPerArtist` cap and the excluded-`heard` set are the only quality guards on that path.

- **2026-08-26 (later) — `/simplify` cleanup pass over the generated-playlists + genre + visibility work. 7 fixes applied, 2 dismissed. Build-green, zero code warnings, 219/219 tests (6 new).**
  **All four review subagents died** (two on session limits, one stalled, one when the machine slept), so all four angles were done inline instead. Worth noting the review still found real things — the agents were a convenience, not the substance.
  **🔴 The most valuable finding was a missing feature masquerading as dead state.** `PlaylistGenerator.emptyReason` was written in four places with carefully-worded user-facing text ("Nobody has nodded your tracks yet", "Follow a few people to build this one") and **read by nothing**; `errorMessage` likewise. So tapping a lens with an empty pool spun and silently reverted, with no way to tell whether it failed or there was genuinely nothing to build — the honest empty states the plan called for existed only as unreachable strings. Now surfaced as a notice on the Home section. **Lesson: `@Published` state with no reader isn't just dead code — when it holds user-facing copy, the feature is missing and nothing fails loudly enough to notice.**
  **Efficiency.** Stage 2 awaited `SocialCache.bundle` **sequentially** over up to 8 people — 8× network latency for no reason. Now a `withTaskGroup` fan-out (safe: `SocialCache` is an actor with in-flight sharing). Required marking `contributions(from:person:)` `nonisolated`, which the compiler caught — it's pure, so that's correct rather than a workaround. Separately, `GenreDetailView.tracks` was a computed property filtering the **whole 744-track library from the view body**, re-running on every render — the exact shape of the documented scroll stutter, and inconsistent with every sibling segment, which reads a precomputed array. Now `@State`, derived on `.task(id:)` and on library change.
  **Reuse — and this one was a user-facing regression, not just duplication.** `GenreDetailView` hand-rolled a track row when `RecentlyLikedListView.likedRow` already rendered exactly this type. The copy was **strictly worse**: no duration, no `TrackActionsMenuButton` (so genre tracks couldn't be queued, added to a playlist, or unliked, while liked tracks could), and circular artwork where its sibling on the same screen used square. Extracted a shared `libraryTrackRow` used by both. Also extracted `decodePlaylists(_:)` — `getVisiblePlaylists` carried a verbatim 6-line copy of `getUserPlaylists`' decode-and-sort block, two places to keep in sync.
  **Altitude.** `normalizedGenres` (drops Apple's umbrella "Music" entry) was pure string logic with no MusicKit dependency sitting in `AppleMusicService`, where it couldn't be unit-tested. Moved to `NodalongKit/Utils/GenreNormalization.swift` with 6 tests. **Simplification:** `didAutoRefresh` was a `@Published` property whose only job was signalling one caller — now a return value, so a one-shot signal no longer invalidates every observing view.
  **Dismissed, with reasons rather than silently:** `getGeneratedPlaylist` does one extra full-collection query per generation — real, but one query against a ~1,000-read operation, so restructuring isn't worth it. `availableLenses` / `generatedPlaylist(for:)` run per render but are a filter over 4 enum cases and a `.first` over a handful of playlists — flagged and measured as trivially cheap, not worth `@State`.

- **2026-08-26 — 🔴 REGRESSION I SHIPPED AND FIXED: adding a non-optional `genres` to `CatalogTrack` (synthesized `Codable`) blanked the title/artist/album of EVERY track for the user, across Shelf, Feed and Spotlight. One-line class of cause, whole-app blast radius. Fixed with a tolerant decoder + regression tests; 213/213, installed to device.**
  **Symptom, from the user's own screenshot:** Shelf's Albums tab showed a single tile — **"Singles / Unknown Artist / 744 tracks"** — i.e. the entire library collapsed into one bucket, with Feed and Spotlight blank too. `groupByAlbum`/`groupByArtist` bucket empty-album→"Singles" and empty-artist→"Unknown Artist", so that tile is the signature of *every track having empty metadata*.
  **🔴 Root cause — the synthesized-decoder trap, on the one type where it is catastrophic.** `CatalogTrack` had no custom `init(from:)`. **Swift's synthesized decoder emits `decode(_:forKey:)` — not `decodeIfPresent` — for a non-optional property, and it IGNORES the property's default value.** So adding `public let genres: [String]` made decoding **throw on every `tracks/{catalogId}` document ever written**, none of which have a `genres` key. And `tracks/{catalogId}` is the single source of truth for all track metadata, joined on read by `enrichLibrary` / `overlayCatalog` / `enrichSpotlights` — every one of which swallows failures with `try?`. So the throw never surfaced as an error: it surfaced as `merged(withCatalog: nil)`, a silent no-op that leaves the slim per-user docs with their empty `""` metadata. Hence the whole app going blank at once.
  **What makes this worth recording: every guardrail passed.** Clean `xcodebuild`, zero warnings in touched files, 210/210 tests, clean launch on a simulator. None of it could catch this, because the simulator had no Firebase session and the tests only exercised models with fixtures that *included* the new field. **The bug lives exactly in the gap between "compiles + tests pass" and "decodes documents that already exist in production."**
  **Worse, I had already applied the correct fix to the wrong models.** In the very same change I gave `Song`, `LibraryTrackDoc` and `Playlist` tolerant decoders precisely for this reason — and missed `CatalogTrack`, the only one of the four whose failure takes down everything. The lesson isn't "remember the trap" (this log already documents it three times over, for `Song`, `Post` and `AlbumResult`); it's **check the type's decoder before adding a field, because the trap only bites types using the SYNTHESIZED one, and that is invisible at the call site.**
  **Fix:** a tolerant `public init(from:)` on `CatalogTrack` — every field falls back rather than throwing, with `titleLower`/`artistLower`/`albumLower` recomputed from their source when absent. Doc comment on it spells out the mechanism and the blast radius so the next person adding a field here understands why it is hand-written. 3 new `DecodeToleranceTests` cases, the key one decoding a row shaped exactly like a pre-genre document (no `genres` key at all) — which would have caught this before it shipped.
  **Confirmed NO data damage, by reading the write path rather than assuming.** Two things could have corrupted Firestore and neither did: (1) `upsertCatalogTrack` dedupes via the `isrc_index` collection using a **raw dictionary read** (`indexSnap.data()?["catalogId"]`), not a `CatalogTrack` decode — so the transaction kept finding existing rows correctly and **no duplicate catalog rows were created**; (2) `CatalogCache.metadata` `compactMap`s decode failures away and only ever caches successes, so the disk cache was **never poisoned** — no reinstall or cache clear needed, the fixed build simply refetches and decodes. The only casualty was the genre backfill branch silently no-op'ing, which is inert.
  **Second, unrelated bug found while auditing the write path:** both catalog-row **creation** paths (`createCatalogRow` and the transaction's inline `CatalogTrack(...)`) omitted `genres:` entirely, so a newly created row never carried genre — it could only ever have arrived via the backfill branch on a *later* touch. Fixed both.
  **Process note.** `/simplify` was running when this was reported; all four review agents died on a session limit, so **that review never completed and should be re-run.** The regression was found by reading the symptom (one "Singles/Unknown Artist" tile = empty metadata everywhere) straight back to its cause rather than by bisecting — the screenshot named the mechanism precisely for anyone who knows what those two placeholder strings mean.
  **Not yet confirmed by the user:** that the installed build actually restores their 744 tracks. Everything above says it will (the failure was read-side only, and the cache holds no bad entries), but that is reasoning, not observation.

- **2026-08-25 (later still) — Generated-playlist follow-ups: `visibility` now genuinely enforced (a real security gap closed), weekly auto-refresh, and genre captured at ingest with a Shelf Genres segment. Build-green, 210/210 tests (11 new), launches clean. Plan: `~/.claude/plans/mellow-exploring-raven.md`.**
  Follow-up to the generated-playlist feature shipped earlier the same day. **Dropped on discussion: blending Apple's own recommendations into generated playlists** — we'd already rejected catalog expansion for competing with Apple on less data, and padding a people-sourced playlist with algorithm picks silently destroys the one property that makes it defensible (every track traceable to a named person). Apple's recommendations already have their own Home shelves. **"Sharing" was narrowed** to making `visibility` mean something; link-sharing, in-app sends and public discoverability are all out.
  **🔴 `visibility` was almost entirely decorative, and that was a live security hole.** `firestore.rules` had `allow read: if signedIn()` on both `playlists` and their `tracks`, so **any signed-in user could read any playlist — `.private` included — given its id**. The only enforcement anywhere was one client-side `.filter { $0.visibility != .private }` in `SocialCache.swift:81`; `.friends` was never checked at all, anywhere.
  **Why the previous attempt at this was reverted, and the actual fix.** The log recorded a "v1 relaxation… because the visibility-gated reads break the `contributorUids array-contains` queries." That's real, and the mechanism is worth writing down properly: **Firestore evaluates read rules PER RETURNED DOCUMENT on a list query, and if one document fails the WHOLE query fails rather than filtering it out.** `getUserPlaylists` queries `contributorUids arrayContains uid` — pointed at *yourself* every returned doc lists you, so a contributor clause is satisfied and the query survives; pointed at *someone else* the docs list them, not you, so the clause fails and the query dies outright. **The fix therefore isn't in the rules, it's in the query shape**: new `getVisiblePlaylists(ownerUid:viewerUid:)` constrains by `ownerUid` + an explicit `visibility` set (chosen from whether the viewer follows the owner, via `FollowingCache`), so every document it can return independently satisfies the rule. `getUserPlaylists` is kept, unchanged, for your own playlists only — where the contributor query is both correct and still what surfaces party playlists you took part in but don't own. New `canReadPlaylist(data)` rules helper covers owner / contributor / public / friends-via-`exists()` on the follower doc; track reads inherit it through the existing `playlist(plid)` helper. The client-side `.private` filter is gone, so visibility is decided in exactly one place. **Accepted consequence: a `.friends` playlist now shows on a profile rail only to someone who actually follows the owner** — which is the point; today it shows to everyone. **NOT deployed** — `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` replaces the entire ruleset and is the user's call.
  **Weekly auto-refresh — no new field needed**, `Playlist.updatedAt` already existed and `upsertGeneratedPlaylist` already maintained it. Triggered from Home's section `.task`, deliberately **not** app launch: a lens costs ~1,000 reads and this codebase has repeatedly had to defend the launch path. Rebuilds **only the single most stale** lens past 7 days (four at once would be a ~4,000-read burst — precisely what the per-lens on-demand design exists to avoid), guarded by a once-per-session flag so a failing lens can't retry on every Home appearance and compound cost, and skipped entirely while a manual generation is in flight. **Silent by construction** — it calls `generate` directly rather than `runGeneration`, which is the only path that pushes onto `router.homePath`; an automatic rebuild must never yank the user into a playlist they didn't open.
  **🔴 That work surfaced a real bug in what shipped hours earlier: nothing on Home ever loaded `playlistsVM`.** `load(uid:)` is only called from Profile/Party/AddToPlaylist/PlaylistDetail — so on a fresh launch where Home is the landing tab, all four lens tiles rendered as "not generated yet" even when the playlists existed. The same `.task` fixes it (`load` is `loadedForUid`-guarded, so it's cheap).
  **Genre — verified against the real SDK, not memory**, per this repo's convention: read `iPhoneOS26.2.sdk/…/MusicKit.swiftinterface` directly and confirmed `Song.genreNames: [String]`, `Album.genreNames: [String]`, `Artist.genreNames: [String]?`, plus `MusicCatalogChartsRequest(genre:kinds:types:)` — which means `SearchView.genreTiles` (today hardcoded *search shortcuts*, easy to mistake for a genre system) could become real later. `genres: [String]` added to `Song`, `CatalogTrack`, `CatalogTrackInput` and `LibraryTrackDoc`, captured in `toSong` and threaded through the ingest sites. **`Song` and `LibraryTrackDoc` got the tolerant-decoder treatment, not a plain property** — both are persisted (`Song` via `SnapshotStore`'s `topChartSongs`), and a required new key would make every existing on-disk snapshot fail to decode, which the read path swallows with `try?`, silently turning each launch back into a cold network load with no signal. **Apple's umbrella genres are filtered** — `genreNames` routinely includes "Music", which is on nearly everything and would swamp any grouping. **Backfill is incremental, not a repair sweep**: `upsertCatalogTrack`'s existing backfill branch now fills `genres` when empty (never overwrites, same one-directional principle as the release repair), so the ~4k existing rows heal as normal syncing touches them — no `CatalogArtworkRepair`-style service needed.
  **Shipped one consumer deliberately** — a **Genres segment in Shelf** — because storing data nothing reads means nobody notices for months that it's empty or wrong. Pure client-side `groupByGenre` over `libraryVM.tracks`, **zero new queries**, computed in `LibraryViewModel.recomputeDerived()`'s `didSet` alongside the others rather than in a view body (the scroll-stutter lesson). Two deliberate semantics, both tested: a multi-genre track counts in **every** bucket (so totals exceed the library size — it's a bucketing, not a partition), and tracks with **no** genre are skipped rather than shown as "Unknown", because empty means *not captured yet*, and surfacing that as a bucket would present missing data as a fact about the music.
  **Deliberately NOT done: a genre cap in `selectTracks`.** Most people's taste spans few genres, so capping per-genre would gut a generated playlist rather than diversify it. Mood/vibe lenses remain the real payoff and need genre to have *accumulated* first — which is the actual argument for storing it now.
  **Verification.** 210/210 NodalongKit tests (199 + 9 `GroupByGenreTests` + 2 `Song` genre decode-tolerance cases). Clean `xcodebuild` for both a generic simulator target and iOS 26.3, **zero warnings in any touched file** (grepped the full build log per file, not inferred from a green build). Installed and launched on iOS 26.3: no crash, no exceptions, process alive.
  **NOT verified — needs a signed-in device, and two accounts for the visibility work.** The simulator has no Firebase session (`authed=false`), so none of this has been exercised against real data. Specifically unconfirmed: that a `.private` playlist is genuinely unreadable by another account; that a `.friends` one appears only while following; and — **the single most important check, because it is exactly what broke the previous attempt** — that **your own Playlists rail and Shelf still load at all** under the new rules. Also unconfirmed: that newly-played tracks land with sensible genres and older rows heal on re-upsert; and that setting a generated playlist's `updatedAt` back >7 days produces exactly one silent rebuild on opening Home, with no navigation. **Rules are written but NOT deployed** — deploying replaces the whole ruleset, so test against the emulator first.

- **2026-08-25 (later) — Generated playlists shipped: one engine, four social lenses ("From Your Circle" / "Around You" / "They Liked Yours" / "Your Blend"). Build-green, 199/199 NodalongKit tests (34 new), launches clean. Planned first — plan file `~/.claude/plans/mellow-exploring-raven.md`.**
  User asked "how do we craft playlists based on their taste — where would we even begin". Deliberately **not** a recommender competing with Apple Music: §1's differentiator is real people, not algorithms, and a weaker copy of Apple's own recommender using strictly less data is a losing game. Instead each playlist has a premise sayable in one sentence ("people near you are playing this") and every track is credited to a named person — which is the part Apple and Spotify structurally cannot copy, since they don't have the follow graph.
  **Locked with the user before any code**: friend-sourced framing (rejected own-history retrospectives and similar-artist catalog expansion); real persisted `Playlist` docs; **client-only, no Cloud Functions**; generation **one lens at a time, user-initiated**; nearby tile hidden outright without a location fix.
  **🔴 The finding that shaped everything: there is NO genre, mood, tempo or audio-feature data anywhere in the app.** `CatalogTrack` is title/artist/album/isrc only, and `SearchView.genreTiles` is hardcoded *search shortcuts*, not metadata — easy to mistake for a genre system. So vibe-clustering is impossible without new ingest; affinity is computed from **overlap of real tracks and artists** instead, which is arguably better here (grounded in listening, and explainable to the user).
  **The cost findings that shaped the architecture.** `firestore.rules` already lets any signed-in user read anyone's `library`/`recent_tracks`/`spotlight_history`, so this is legal client-side — but a naive fan-out over every follow is thousands of reads. What's cheap: followed users' today-spotlights ride free on the profile bundle `UserProfileCache` fetches at launch, **`getNearbyUsers` returns full `UserProfile`s with `spotlightTracks` embedded** (so the nearby pool's deliberate picks are also free), and friends' recents are already in `FeedViewModel.posts`. What's expensive and therefore never a source: full libraries. Hence **two stages** — rank the pool on free data, then deep-fetch only the top 8 via `SocialCache.bundle` (~1,000 reads, paid only on an explicit tap). Reusing `bundle` rather than a leaner taste-only fetch is deliberate: it shares the cache with profile browsing, and **the blend lens then rides on whatever the single-lens runs already warmed** — which is the whole reason generation is per-lens rather than one "refresh all" burst.
  **Pure logic + tests are where the real guard is** — `NodalongKit/Utils/TasteAffinity.swift` and `PlaylistGeneration.swift`, 31 new tests. Affinity weights **artists 0.7 / tracks 0.3** because exact-`catalogId` overlap is genuinely sparse (a 705-track library may share almost no identical recordings with a real taste-match) while artist overlap is dense; a pure track-Jaccard ranking would score nearly everyone at ~0 and rank noise. Normalized by **their** set size, not Jaccard — Jaccard punishes someone with a small library for being small, when the question that matters is "how much of their taste do I already share". Artist keys go through the existing `primaryArtist(_:)` so featured credits can't split an artist *or* sneak past the per-artist cap. **Corroboration is emergent, not special-cased**: two people contributing sum two terms, so a track several matches independently love outranks one outlier — that's the collaborative-filtering core falling out of the sum. Every ordering tie-breaks on a stable id, tested, so regenerating with unchanged inputs produces an unchanged playlist rather than churning the user's list.
  **🔴 Deviated from the approved plan on one point, deliberately: `contributorUids` is the OWNER ONLY.** The plan said to list the sourced people there "exactly like a frozen party playlist" — implementing it, that's wrong. `getUserPlaylists` is an `arrayContains` query on that field, so listing them would make your generated playlist appear on **their** profiles unbidden, and for the `nearby`/`admirers` lenses those are strangers who'd effectively be told their listening had been mined. A party is consensual co-presence; this isn't. Per-track credit is unaffected — it rides on `addedByUid`/`addedByUsername`, which is what actually delivers the feel.
  **`PlaylistTrack.addedByUsername` has been stored since parties shipped but was never rendered anywhere** — now shown per row in `PlaylistDetailView` (hidden for your own tracks, where "added by you" on every row is noise). For a generated playlist that single change is the whole point; it also improves party playlists for free.
  **`nod_records` gets its first-ever reader.** Written since nods shipped, never read back — new `getNodders(sourceUid:)` powers the "They Liked Yours" lens, skewed by nod count so someone who reacted to twelve of your tracks outranks a one-off. Single-field equality query, so Firestore's automatic index covers it — nothing to deploy.
  **Enum-decode trap handled up front** (the one this log already documents): `Playlist`'s decoder used `decodeIfPresent(PlaylistSource.self) ?? .manual`, and `decodeIfPresent` **throws** on a present-but-unrecognized raw value rather than returning nil — so the moment `.generated` shipped, any build lacking the case would have silently dropped those playlists via the read path's `try?`. Both `source` and the new `lens` now decode through `try?`; 3 new tests in `DecodeToleranceTests` cover unknown raw values, the new values, and a pre-lens doc.
  **Two real bugs caught during implementation, both by verification rather than by the build** (every broken version compiled): (1) `madeByPeopleSection` was accidentally nested *inside* the `friendsListeningAlbums.isEmpty` conditional, so it would only have appeared when an unrelated section had data; (2) a `sourceService` ternary whose branches were identical. Also hit the Swift **type-checker timeout** on `rankPeople`'s chained map/filter/sorted/prefix — split into explicit steps with a comment, since it fails the build outright rather than compiling slowly.
  **Verification.** 199/199 NodalongKit tests (165 existing + 34 new). `xcodegen generate` + clean `xcodebuild` green for both a generic simulator target and iOS 26.3, with **zero warnings in any touched file** (checked by grepping the full build log per file, not by trusting a green build). Installed and launched on an iOS 26.3 simulator: no crash, no exceptions, process still alive. Registered `PlaylistGenerator` in `PreviewFixtures.withPreviewEnvironment()` per convention — and fixed a **pre-existing** gap while there: `AppRouter` had never been registered, so `HomeView`'s preview couldn't render at all.
  **NOT verified — needs a signed-in device with a real social graph.** The simulator has no Firebase session (`authed=false`), so Home was unreachable and **no lens has ever actually been generated**. Worth checking specifically: that a generated playlist contains 25 tracks, none already in Shelf/recents, max 2 per artist, each row credited to a real person; that regenerating replaces in place rather than creating a second playlist; that the Around You tile is genuinely absent with location denied; and — since it's a load-bearing assumption of this design, not a guess to trust — **measure in the Firestore console that the blend costs far fewer reads than a cold lens.** The empty states (no follows / nothing new / nobody matching) are each written but none has been seen.
  **Deliberately out of scope**: weekly auto-refresh (needs a staleness timestamp and would put a background write on the launch path); genre enrichment on `CatalogTrack` at ingest via MusicKit's `genreNames` — cheap, and the single highest-leverage unlock for mood/vibe lenses later, but nothing here needs it.

- **2026-08-25 — App icon regenerated from a new spec PDF (`docs/Nodalong_Application.pdf`): mark grows 60% → 83% of the canvas, both brand purples shift slightly, all three appearance variants rebuilt. Build-green, verified via `assetutil`.**
  User supplied a 2-page spec (page 1 light, page 2 dark) and asked to match it, noting "the size is different." **Measured rather than eyeballed:** rendered both pages via **PDFKit** (NOT poppler — the 2026-08-18 entry established poppler mis-renders this brand's colour-managed PDFs, producing two different wrong palettes before a PDFKit render matched Preview), then measured the icon square and the mark's ink bbox inside it. Findings: mark ink is **82.95% of the icon width**, horizontally centred (left/right insets identical at 0.0852), vertical centre 0.4945. Current shipped icons measured **59.96%** — so the mark grows ~38% linearly, which is the size difference the user saw. Colours also shifted: light ground `#B092B4 → #B590B6`, mark `#7F4E89 → #874B8D` (dark is the exact inverse). Kept the tinted variant's black-ground/white-mark scheme, resized to match.
  **🔴 Three real bugs hit while generating, all caught by verification rather than by the build (a green build proves nothing here — every failed attempt also built fine):** (1) **The mark PDF paints its own fill colour**, so pre-filling a grayscale mask context black and then drawing the mark (which paints black) produced a solid, mark-less image. Fix: render the mark into an **RGBA context on a transparent ground and derive the mask from the ALPHA channel** — never from the drawn colour. (2) **`CGColor(red:green:blue:alpha:)` creates a generic-RGB colour that is converted when drawn into an sRGB context**, lightening it — `#B590B6` came out `#C3A3C3`. Fix: build every colour with `CGColor(colorSpace: sRGB, components:)`. (3) **`nodalongMark.pdf`'s mediaBox has padding** — its ink is only **0.8555** of the mediaBox width, so scaling the mediaBox to 83% put the ink at 70.7%. Compounding this, `inkBounds` assumed buffer row 0 was the bottom; **an empirical probe (fill a known CG-bottom strip, see which buffer rows light up) proved row 0 is the TOP**, and the inverted mapping was also pushing the mark off-canvas and clipping it (height 616 vs the expected 689).
  **The fix that made it exact, and the technique worth reusing:** a **three-pass measure-and-correct** placement instead of computing a transform analytically — pass 1 probes at a known scale to learn the ink-to-mediaBox ratio and solve for the final scale; pass 2 renders at that scale untranslated and measures where the ink *actually* lands; pass 3 translates by the exact measured delta. This needs no reasoning about scaling anchors or coordinate flips, so it is immune to all of (3). Landed at ratio 0.8262 / centre (0.4995, 0.4941) against targets 0.8295 / (0.4995, 0.4945).
  **Verification.** Independent re-measurement of the written PNGs (colours exact, geometry within 0.005), `hasAlpha: no` on all three (iOS requires opaque icons), a rendered side-by-side against the spec pages, clean `xcodebuild`, and `xcrun assetutil --info` on the built `Assets.car` confirming **all three entries — default/light, `UIAppearanceDark`, `ISAppearanceTintable` — at 1024×1024**. Deliberately did NOT trust a simulator home-screen screenshot: the springboard icon cache doesn't reliably show freshly-installed icons (already recorded in the 2026-08-18 entry).
  **Not done / worth a look.** The generated mark's strokes read slightly heavier than the spec PDF's (visible in the side-by-side: marginally thicker smile, slightly larger ear cups). Cause: the icons are composited from the repo's existing `nodalongMark.pdf`, which a prior session **hand-wrote** by tracing paths off the brand-guide cover, rather than from the spec PDF's own artwork. Overall shape/size/colour match well, so this was left alone — but if exact stroke fidelity matters, extract the mark vector from `docs/Nodalong_Application.pdf` and replace `nodalongMark.imageset/nodalongMark.pdf`, which would also update all 11 in-app `NodalongMark` call sites. **Scope note:** only `AppIcon.appiconset` was touched — the in-app mark, splash, and `LaunchScreen.storyboard` are unchanged.

- **2026-08-21 (later, cont. 2) — Spotlit track cards redesigned: the whole card is now gold FOIL, replacing the gold border + soft wash. New `SpotlightFoil` theme type; applied to both `TrackCard` layouts. Build-green, 165/165 tests, installed to device.**
  **Why.** The previous treatment (2pt gold border, breathing glow, soft top wash, gold pill badge) was judged "not sufficient — needs to look more pronounced". A spotlight is a friend's deliberate one-a-day pick and should be unmistakable mid-scroll. Four directions were previewed (stage beam / gold bezel / top ribbon / corner fold), then a second round on the user's steer to colour the ENTIRE card in something that "more resembles gold than the basically yellow we have seen". **"H · Foil sheen" chosen.**
  **The tone finding, worth keeping.** The app's `gold` token `#F6BC0C` is high-saturation amber and reads as **yellow**, not metal. What actually sells gold is the light→dark→light banding of a reflective surface, so the new treatment is a **7-stop ramp** (`#8E6C1F → #D4AF37 → #F9F0C8 → #D4AF37 → #8E6C1F → #C9A227 → #F0DFA0`), not a flat colour. **Deliberately NOT an asset-catalog colorset** — a colorset holds one colour per appearance, and this only means anything as an ordered sequence. It's also deliberately appearance-independent: it sits on album artwork (arbitrary user content), not on the app's own background, so like `TrackCard`'s black legibility scrim it must not shift with light/dark mode.
  **New `Nodalong/Shared/Theme/SpotlightFoil.swift`** — `sheen` (the ramp, angled ≈CSS `115deg` via `UnitPoint(0, 0.30) → (1, 0.70)`), `artworkOpacity` (0.42), `ink` (`#3B2E08`, for anything drawn ON the foil), `badgeBacking` (frosted white), `glow`.
  **`.fullBleed`** — the foil is now the SURFACE and the artwork is composited into it with `.blendMode(.multiply)` at 0.42, so the cover reads as pressed into the metal rather than sitting on it. Needs `.compositingGroup()`, or the multiply composites against whatever is behind the card too. The old top gold wash was deleted (redundant — foil covers the whole card) and the 2pt gold ring dropped to a 1pt hairline in the ramp's DARKEST stop, since a bright ring around a bright card adds nothing. `artworkOpacity` is the knob to tune: higher = more recognisable cover, lower = more metal.
  **`.row` extended for consistency** (it's used by Profile's Recent Tracks carousel and does render spotlit). No artwork multiply there — the row shows its cover as a separate sleeve/disc cluster, so the foil is unmodulated.
  **🔴 The non-obvious cost, and where the real work was: a gold surface breaks every colour that assumed a dark backdrop.** Three classes of breakage, all fixed: (1) **gold-on-gold** — the "Spotlight" badge was a `gold` pill and the spotlight star was `gold`; both would have been invisible. Badge → frosted-white backing + `ink`; star → `ink`. (2) **mode-dependent tokens on a mode-independent surface** — `.row`'s five text labels use `textPrimary`/`textSecondary`, which are near-WHITE in dark mode and would vanish on gold. New `rowPrimaryInk`/`rowSecondaryInk` helpers flip them together. `.fullBleed` needs no equivalent: its text sits on the black scrim. (3) **shared child components** — `AddToQueueButton` and `TrackActionsMenuButton` set their own mode-aware colours, which SwiftUI foreground inheritance cannot override; both gained an optional `tint:` (default `nil` = today's behaviour, so no other call site changes) which the spotlit row passes ink into. **Lesson for any future "colour the whole surface" change: grep the surface for every colour that assumed the old backdrop — a token that's correct on dark artwork can be invisible on a light one, and mode-aware tokens are especially dangerous on a surface that is deliberately mode-INdependent.**
  **Verification.** Clean build, zero warnings in any touched file, 165/165 NodalongKit tests, installed to the physical device. Confirmed by grep that **no `Color.nodalong.gold` reference remains in `TrackCard`** — every gold now routes through `SpotlightFoil`.
  **Not verified from here / worth a look.** How the foil reads against BOTH app backgrounds (it's a bright card on the light mauve `bgBase` as well as on dark purple); whether `artworkOpacity: 0.42` leaves covers recognisable enough in Feed, which is the main way a track is identified at a glance; and whether a full-foil `.row` is too heavy in a dense list (up to 3 spotlit tracks a day can appear in Profile's Recent Tracks) — if so, that block is a contained revert without touching `.fullBleed`.

- **2026-08-21 (later, cont.) — Stale artwork on track change ROOT-CAUSED: `@State` survives a view update, so `CachedArtworkImage`'s `image != nil` short-circuit kept serving the PREVIOUS track's bitmap. One fix covers every artwork surface in the app. Build-green, 165/165 tests, installed to device.**
  **Report:** queue several songs, open the full-screen player, press next (locked OR unlocked) — title/artist/album update correctly but the **artwork stays on the previous track**. Screenshot confirmed: "…ys on Mopeds / HELP(2)" with the prior song's cover still in the vinyl label.
  **🔴 Root cause — the classic SwiftUI `@State` identity trap, in code whose own comment claimed it was handled.** `CachedArtworkImage` reads the decoded-image cache **synchronously in `init`** (`_image = State(initialValue:)`) — deliberately, so a cache hit renders with zero placeholder frames. It also carries `.task(id: urlString) { await load() }`, commented *"`id:` so a recycled cell repointed at a different URL reloads instead of keeping the previous track's artwork."* The `.task` genuinely does re-fire. **But `load()` opened with `if image != nil { return }`** — and **SwiftUI only uses `State(initialValue:)` when a view IDENTITY is first created, never on subsequent updates**. The full-screen player is one long-lived view whose `urlString` changes in place on every track change, so on the update `image` still held the previous track's `UIImage`, the guard short-circuited, and the new artwork was never loaded. The intent was right; the guard defeated it.
  **Fix:** new `@State loadedURLString` recording which URL `image` actually belongs to. `load()` now detects `loadedURLString != urlString` first: resets `didFail`, re-reads the low-res bucket, takes a cache hit for the NEW url if present, and otherwise **clears the stale bitmap** so the placeholder shows rather than the wrong cover. Set on every path that assigns `image`, and seeded in `init` only when the synchronous read actually hit.
  **One fix, every surface** — `CachedArtworkImage` is the single component behind the full-screen player's blurred background, the vinyl disc's label (via `VinylArtworkView` → `ArtworkThumbnail`), square-mode artwork, and all ~39 thumbnail call sites. Confirmed by grep it is the **only** place in the app holding a `UIImage` in `@State`, so there is no second instance of this bug. `VinylArtworkView`'s `flipAngle`/`isFlipping` were checked and ruled out — a tap-to-flip rotation, unrelated to artwork content.
  **Why it wasn't caught before:** list cells usually get fresh identity as they recycle, which masks it. A single view whose URL mutates in place — exactly the full-screen player — is the worst case, and the only place it reliably shows.
  **Verification.** Clean build; the one warning in a touched file (`NearMeCanvasView:348`, main-actor `tick()`) was proven **pre-existing** via `git show HEAD:` — byte-identical at line 314 in the committed baseline, merely shifted by 35 lines added elsewhere in the file. 165/165 tests, installed to the physical device. **Not confirmed on-device yet** — needs a real queue + next-track to verify the artwork now follows the track.

- **2026-08-21 (later again) — Prefetch pass: follow lists, profiles and NearMe now load invisibly. New `SocialCache` (profile bundles + follower/following lists), browse-time warming bounded to 6, and NearMe's park no longer gated behind the users fetch. Build-green, 165/165 tests, zero new warnings, installed to the physical device.**
  **What was actually wrong, in three parts.** (1) **Follow lists had no caching whatsoever** — `FollowListView.load()` ran on `.task` and did three round trips (the list, then `getUserProfiles` to enrich it, concurrently with your own following list) behind a `ProgressView`, despite the user *always* being one tap away: they got there by tapping a follower count that was already on screen. (2) **Other users' profiles were fully cold every single time** — `UserProfileView.loadData()` fired five fetches plus a spotlight enrich with zero caching. Already well-parallelised (`async let`), so this was never a concurrency problem, it was a caching one. Worth noting the identity half was already instant (every push site passes a complete `UserProfile`), so the pop-in was purely those five — which is exactly what the spotlight carousel and recents rail wait on. (3) **NearMe's wheel was self-inflicted** — `usersContent` replaced the **entire canvas, park included**, with a full-screen `ProgressView` while users loaded, even though `ParkBackgroundView` is procedural (seeded `Canvas`, no network) and never needed that data.
  **New `Nodalong/Services/SocialCache.swift`** — deliberately the same actor shape as `UserProfileCache` (TTL + in-flight sharing, so a real tap either lands on a completed warm entry or **joins** an in-flight fetch, never starting a duplicate). Holds `ProfileBundle` (recentTracks / spotlightHistory / playlists / followerCount / isFollowing) keyed by uid, and follower/following lists keyed `"<uid>:followers"|":following"`. Both expose a non-blocking `cached…` peek so a view can paint a warm result on its FIRST frame without awaiting a fetch. **Not a replacement for `FollowingCache`**, which holds a single uid's *following* list only because it exists to serve Home/Feed reading YOUR OWN graph — it structurally cannot answer "who follows this arbitrary person", which is the follow-list screen's whole job.
  **Prefetch wiring — every site routes through the same cache the detail screen reads.** Search (`.task(id:)` on the result uids), NearMe (after `nearMeVM.load`), and `FollowListView`'s own rows all warm `.prefix(6)`. `ProfileView` and `UserProfileView` warm the follow lists for the profile being viewed, since the counts are on screen and the list is one tap away. `ContentView` gained a launch task warming your own bundle + both follow lists — `ProfileView` already painted instantly from its disk `ProfileSnapshot`, but that only refreshed when Profile itself appeared, so the FIRST visit of a session showed last session's data until the network caught up.
  **The bound is load-bearing and the reason differs from the music prefetch.** Round 9.20's album/artist prefetch is bounded at 6 for politeness — it hits Apple Music's API, which is free. Here each bundle is **~5 billed Firestore reads**, so an uncapped warm of a 30-result search would be ~150 reads for profiles the user will mostly never open. `prefetch(uids:viewerUid:limit:)` caps at 6 and skips the viewer's own uid.
  **Invalidation lives at the API layer, not the call sites.** `followUser`/`unfollowUser` (`Follows.swift`) already invalidated `FollowingCache`; they now invalidate `SocialCache` for **both parties** — the target's follower count and `isFollowing` change, and both users' lists do. Doing it there means a follow toggle from anywhere in the app can't leave a stale count or button state. `FirebaseAuthViewModel.refreshProfile()` invalidates your own bundle, which covers every spotlight add/remove and profile edit since they all funnel through it.
  **NearMe — the park now renders unconditionally.** Both `ProgressView`s are gone. The canvas draws on the first frame with whatever users exist (empty at first) and avatars populate into it; loading/empty/error became **overlays on top of the park** rather than replacements for it, and each only shows while `nearbyUsers.isEmpty`, so a background refresh with content already on screen is silent. The `geohash == nil` branch renders the park too — location is only needed by the users fetch, not the map, and `LocationService.init()` already requests a fix at launch so the geohash is usually ready before the tab is ever opened. `usersContent(geohash:)` lost its parameter: it never actually read it (only the `.task`/`.onChange` load triggers use the geohash).
  **Verification.** Clean build green; the only warnings in touched files are the two **pre-existing** `FirebaseFirestoreInternal` Sendable ones in `Follows.swift` catalogued in §7 (at lines 1 and 3 — the import and `db`, untouched by this change), and the total dropped 147 → 143. 165/165 NodalongKit tests (no NodalongKit changes — this is all app-target caching). Built for and **installed** to the physical device — but **launch verification did NOT run**: the device was locked (`BSErrorCodeDescription = Locked`, the recurring `devicectl` constraint noted elsewhere in this log), so unlike the performance round immediately below, there is no on-device launch capture confirming this build starts clean. The changes are additive caching on top of a build that did launch cleanly, so the risk is low — but it is genuinely unconfirmed, not verified.
  **Deliberate scope decisions.** Other users' profiles are **session-only, no `SnapshotStore` persistence** — social data (spotlights, follower counts) goes stale fast and a day-old profile is worse than a brief load; your own profile keeps its disk snapshot. `FollowingCache` was left alone rather than folded into `SocialCache`: they overlap, but it sits in working launch-path code and consolidating it is its own change.
  **🔴 SECOND, DEEPER REGRESSION FROM THE SAME CHANGE — the park stayed EMPTY even though the query returned users.** After the empty-state fix below, the user reported "its still 0". Live device capture settled it in one run and disproved the display theory outright:
  `NearMe.query: own=gc1zpt203 prefixLen=4 cells=[9 cells] rawDocs=3`
  `NearMe.candidate: Aidooo geohash=gc1zpt206 distance=0.00km radius=10km KEPT`
  `NearMe.load: geohash=gc1zpt203 radius=10km -> 2 users`
  **The fetch was perfect — 2 users found and kept — but no avatars rendered.** Root cause, and it is a direct second-order consequence of rendering the park immediately: `NearMeCanvasView.reconcileQueue()` **never adds anyone to `activePool`.** It only marks departures and refills `unusedQueue` (the bench); promotion from bench to pool happens *exclusively* in `tickLifecycle`, when an already-alive avatar despawns after its 60–120s life. That was fine while the canvas was only ever CONSTRUCTED once `users` was non-empty — `spawnInitial` filled the pool and reconcile's job was purely arrivals/departures thereafter. Rendering the park with `users: []` means `spawnInitial` runs with nothing to spawn, `hasSpawned` flips true, and every later arrival lands on the bench with **no alive avatar to die and make room** — an empty park, forever. **Fix:** `reconcileQueue()` now promotes into free slots (`poolCap` minus alive count) instead of only benching, and `applyLayout` calls it when a layout pass settles after users arrived (`hasSpawned` already true, `activePool` empty). **This was a latent bug beyond my change**: bumping the radius to reveal new users previously showed none of them until an existing avatar happened to despawn.
  **Method note: two consecutive wrong theories, both corrected by one device capture.** First theory (empty-state logic) was right but incomplete; second guess would have been the display path again. The `NearMe.query`/`NearMe.load` logging turned "it's still 0" into "the query returns 2, so it's the renderer" in a single run — the same instrumenting-over-theorising that cracked the split-album bug. The query summary is **kept permanently** (one line per load) as an early warning for this bug class; the noisy per-candidate line was removed once it had done its job.

  **🔴 REGRESSION I INTRODUCED IN THIS SAME ROUND, reported and fixed immediately: NearMe falsely claimed "No listeners within N km".** Removing the full-screen `ProgressView` also removed the state it encoded. `canvasStateOverlay` showed the empty state whenever `nearbyUsers.isEmpty && !isLoading` — but that is ALSO true **before any fetch has been attempted**: `.task` returns early while `locationService.geohash` is still nil, so `load()` never runs, `isLoading` stays `false`, and the map asserted nobody was around before having looked. Confirmed by `git diff` (now possible — the repo has commits) that **only `NearMeView.swift` was touched**; `NearMeViewModel` and `getNearbyUsers` were untouched, so this was purely display, never the fetch. **Fix:** `NearMeViewModel.hasLoaded` promoted from `private` to `@Published private(set)` so the view can distinguish "searched and found nobody" from "haven't searched yet", and the overlay now has three states — "Finding your location…" (geohash nil), "Looking for listeners…" (`isLoading || !hasLoaded`), and only then the genuine "No listeners within N km". **Lesson: when replacing a loading indicator, check what STATE it was encoding, not just what it looked like** — the wheel was also the app's only signal for "not attempted yet". Also added a permanent `diagLog` in `load()` reporting geohash + radius + result count, so an empty map is never ambiguous again.
  **Worth knowing for NearMe generally** (not a bug): a user is only discoverable once `updateUserLocation` has written their `geohash`, and that only runs from NearMe's own `.task`/`.onChange`. So a second account must have opened the NearMe tab at least once with location granted before they can appear for anyone. There is no recency filter in `getNearbyUsers`, so once written it keeps matching.
  **Not verified from here.** The actual felt result needs the device: NearMe opening straight onto the park with no wheel; your own Profile complete on the first frame; a follower count tapping through to a populated list rather than a spinner; and someone opened from Search/NearMe/a follow list arriving with their spotlight carousel already there. **Worth checking the opposite failure too, since caching can mask real updates** — follow/unfollow someone and confirm counts and button state update on both profiles, and add a spotlight and confirm it appears immediately. Also worth a glance at Firestore read volume in the console after a browsing session; the `.prefix(6)` cap is what keeps this affordable and it's the number to tune if reads look high.

- **2026-08-21 (later still) — Performance pass, ROOT-CAUSED AND MEASURED, not guessed: the stutter was uncached `NSRegularExpression` compilation inside view bodies. `groupByAlbum` 13.54 ms → 2.11 ms (6.4×) on a 441-track library. Plus an `@Observable` pilot on `FirebaseAuthViewModel` (25 observing views), and a real launch-crash regression I introduced and fixed — the `@StateObject` → `@State` eager-init trap. Build-green, 165/165 tests, zero new warnings.**
  **🔴 THE STUTTER ROOT CAUSE — every regex in the app was recompiled on every call.** Nine sites across four files used `try? NSRegularExpression(pattern:)` with no caching. `NSRegularExpression` construction parses the pattern and builds a matching engine; it is not free. Two of those sites sit in the hottest paths in the app: `stripSearchNoise` compiled **two** regexes per call and `groupByAlbum` calls it **once per track** (≈882 compilations for a 441-track library), and `ArtworkURL.sized` compiled one per call from inside `ArtworkThumbnail`'s body (the primitive behind ~39 call sites) and `TrackCard`'s body — i.e. **a regex compile per visible tile, per frame, while scrolling**.
  **Measured, not estimated.** A temporary benchmark (`ZZPerfBaselineTests`, since removed) against a synthetic 441-track library — the real count from this session's own device capture:
  `groupByAlbum(441)  13.54 ms → 2.11 ms  (6.4×)`
  `stripSearchNoise   12.68 µs → 2.04 µs  (6.2×)`
  `canonicalAlbumTitle 15.13 µs → 1.79 µs (8.5×)`
  13.54 ms is most of a 16.7 ms frame budget **on a Mac** — one call dropped a frame on desktop-class hardware, and it ran many times per screen on the phone.
  **The regex caching was only half of it — `primaryArtist` was the other half.** After hoisting the regexes `groupByAlbum` was still 8.89 ms. Profiling by elimination found `primaryArtist` performs **six ICU-backed case-insensitive `range(of:)` searches per call**, and `albumArtistLabel` calls it for *every track in every group* — ≈550 calls, ≈3,300 ICU searches per grouping. Memoised it (pure function of its input; lock-protected, bounded at 2,000 entries, since callers span the main actor and background tasks). That single change took 8.89 ms → 2.29 ms and also speeds up `groupByArtist`, `FriendsActivity`, `GroupPostsByAlbum` and Spotify resolve. **Lesson: hoisting the regexes was the obvious fix and it was only ~35% of the win — measure after each step rather than assuming the first fix was the whole problem.**
  **🔴 Derived data was being recomputed inside view bodies.** `HomeView.recentShelfAlbums` ran the full `groupByAlbum` over the entire library, then sorted with a comparator that allocated two arrays (`.map(\.savedAt).max()`) on **both sides of every comparison**. Because it was a view computed property it re-ran on every body evaluation — and `HomeView` observes **nine** app-wide objects (~62 `@Published` between them), so `isPlaying` toggling re-grouped the whole library. `ShelfViewModel.albums`/`.artists`/`.recentlyLiked` were likewise **uncached computed properties**, and `filteredAlbums` reads `albums`, so one Shelf body pass could trigger the full grouping several times. **Fix:** `LibraryViewModel` (which owns `tracks`) now computes `albumGroups`/`artistEntries`/`recentlyLiked`/`recentAlbumGroups` once per library change via a `didSet` — chosen over per-call-site recomputation precisely because `tracks` is mutated in five places and a `didSet` means none of them can forget. `ShelfViewModel.setTracks` now receives the finished arrays; `HomeView` just reads `libraryVM.recentAlbumGroups`. **There is now exactly one `groupByAlbum` call site in the entire app.**
  **`ScreenMetrics` cached.** `bounds`/`scale` walked `connectedScenes` and allocated via `compactMap` on every read, from inside `TrackCard`/`ArtworkThumbnail` bodies. Now caches the `UIScreen` **reference** rather than its bounds — deliberate, because `bounds`/`scale` then stay live across rotation (UIKit updates them in place), so there's nothing to invalidate. `weak`, so a torn-down scene's screen is never retained.
  **`@Observable` pilot — and the note in this file saying it was blocked was STALE.** §"Deprecation-warning cleanup" recorded "@Observable migration is the cleaner fix but blocked — deployment target is iOS 16". The target went to **26.1** on 2026-07-24, so it has been available for a month. 17 `ObservableObject`s with 99 `@Published` are injected app-wide, and `ObservableObject` has no per-property granularity: one write invalidates *every* observing view.
  **Pilot switched from `PlayerViewModel` to `FirebaseAuthViewModel` mid-implementation, for a concrete reason.** `PlayerViewModel` was the plan's target (20 properties × 21 views) — but it has **four Combine subscriptions on its `$` projected publishers** (`ListeningPartyViewModel:192,326`, `NowPlayingBroadcastService:44`, `SyncFollowCoordinator:40`) driving party host broadcast and NearMe presence, both documented here as hard-won. `@Observable` removes `$` publishers, so migrating it means hand-rolling replacement subjects inside known-fragile broadcast code. `FirebaseAuthViewModel` is a strictly better pilot: **25 observing views** (the most in the app), 7 properties, **zero Combine coupling**, and only 3 binding sites (all the same `.alert(isPresented:)`). Migrated it; `profile` alone changes on every sync, spotlight edit and follow change, and previously re-rendered all 25 views each time.
  **🔴 A REAL CRASH I INTRODUCED, caught only because the verification was on-device.** The simulator build succeeded; the app then died instantly on the phone with `Fatal error: The default FirebaseApp instance must be configured before the default Auth instance can be initialized`. Cause: `@Observable` forces `@StateObject` → `@State`, and **their initialisation timing differs**. `@StateObject` wraps its initial value in an `@autoclosure` and defers construction to the first body evaluation — after `AppDelegate.didFinishLaunchingWithOptions` has run `FirebaseApp.configure()`. **`@State` evaluates its default eagerly, during App-struct init, i.e. BEFORE the delegate runs**, and `FirebaseAuthViewModel.init` reads `Auth.auth().currentUser` synchronously (the warm-launch splash skip). Fixed with an explicit `NodalongApp.init()` that configures Firebase then assigns `_authVM = State(wrappedValue:)`; `configure()` is now guarded (`if FirebaseApp.app() == nil`) in both places since calling it twice also traps. **Record this as a general trap for the remaining 16 migrations: any `@StateObject` whose init touches launch-time-configured global state will crash when it becomes `@State`, and a green build will not tell you.**
  **Device confirmation.** Installed and launched on the physical device: **zero crashes, zero main-thread hangs** reported by `MainThreadHangDetector` (150 ms threshold) across launch and idle, and `ContentView active: branch=MainTabView authed=true` — so the `@Observable` auth pilot resolves correctly through the real Firebase listener, not just in a build. Worth noting the device log reports the library at **705 docs**, not the 441 the benchmark modelled — so the real-world cost of the old code was proportionally *higher* than the measured 13.54 ms, and the win correspondingly larger.
  **Verification.** Clean build green with **zero warnings in any touched file** (the 147 total are the pre-existing categories in §7 — 66 of them Firestore Sendable in `Parties.swift`, none in files this round touched). 165/165 NodalongKit tests — `GroupByAlbumTests`, `SearchQueryNormalizationTests`, `AlbumEditionGroupingTests`, `AlbumArtistLabelTests` are the real guard here, since every Phase-1 change had to be semantically identical (kept `Optional` + `try?` rather than `try!` specifically so failure semantics stayed byte-identical). Installed to the physical device.
  **Deliberately NOT done, and why.** (1) The other 16 `ObservableObject`s — pending confirmation the pilot behaves on device; `PlayerViewModel` additionally needs its four Combine subscribers rewired first. (2) `PlaybackClock` still exists — its own doc comment says it was only ever a workaround for `ObservableObject`'s lack of per-property granularity, so `@Observable` makes it redundant, but folding it back in is its own change and the scrubber path is currently correct. (3) `SnapshotStore.load` is still synchronous file I/O + JSON decode on `@MainActor` (`FirebaseAuthViewModel.init:70`, `HomeViewModel:57`, `FeedViewModel:54`, `ProfileView:556`) — a real launch-path cost, but not the scroll stutter that was reported.
  **Not verified from here:** the actual felt smoothness. No matched device baseline was captured — the repo has **no commits**, so stashing to build a "before" was too risky, and the before/after above is from the benchmark instead. Worth a real scroll through Home and Shelf with a large library, plus confirming auth still works end-to-end (sign out → sign in → spotlight limit alert), since the pilot touched all 25 `authVM` consumers.

- **2026-08-21 (later) — Corner-radius unification finished: album artwork is now deliberately SQUARE (0pt), not just a smaller radius; queue icon added to track cards in Feed, Profile's spotlight rail, and Recent Tracks. Build-green, 165/165 tests, installed to the physical device.**
  **Two-part styling request, confirmed via back-and-forth rather than guessed.** First ask was "match every container's corners to the queue's" — landed as `Corner.container = 25` (measured off a device screenshot against `.continuous` comparators; a short `.insetGrouped` section had to be discarded first since near-half-height radii clamp toward a capsule and misread). User's follow-up ("not album artwork") was initially read as "give artwork its own smaller radius," shipped as `Corner.artwork = 8`. **Final correction, verbatim: "when i said the album artwork i meant it shouldnt be rounded... So artwork in track cards, playlist artwork, and artwork of top charts."** Not smaller — zero. Changed `Corner.artwork` to `0`, doc comment rewritten to record it as a deliberate design choice (artwork is a picture, not a surface — it sits flush inside the rounded container around it rather than echoing its shape) so a future pass doesn't "round-correct" it back.
  **Found and fixed the one site the mechanical revert had missed: `ArtworkThumbnail`'s own INTERNAL clip.** The earlier revert touched 21 call-site `clipShape` modifiers, but `ArtworkThumbnail` (`ShelfView.swift`) applies its own `clipShape` *inside* the component — `circular ? Circle() : RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: Corner.container, ...)` — which is the shared primitive behind ~39 call sites app-wide (track rows, search results, follow lists, playlist tiles). Being the inner clip, it wins over anything a call site does on top, so it had silently stayed rounded at 25pt through the whole "artwork" fix. Switched to `Corner.artwork`, with a comment flagging why this exact site matters (inner clip beats outer). Verified `VinylArtworkView`/`CachedArtworkImage` (the other two shared artwork primitives) never referenced `Corner.container` and needed no change.
  **Add-to-queue icon extended to three more surfaces**, per direct request. `TrackCard`'s `AddToQueueButton` (already wired for the `.fullBleed`/`.row` layouts from the corner-radius pass) turned out to already be reachable from Feed, Profile's spotlight carousel, and Recent Tracks — all four call sites were already passing `onAddToQueue` from an earlier round, so this shipped as a verification pass rather than new wiring: confirmed each of the four carousel usages (`ProfileView` + `UserProfileView`, both spotlight and recents) and `FeedView` already thread `onAddToQueue` (and `addToPlaylistSong`, needed for LP-queue routing) through to `TrackCard`.
  **Verification.** Clean build green on both a fresh simulator target and the physical device (`xcodebuild -destination 'id=00008140-000C02CC1EE3801C'`), zero new warnings in any touched file (`Corner.swift`, `ShelfView.swift`, `TrackCard.swift`, `AddToQueueButton.swift`), 165/165 NodalongKit tests, installed via `devicectl`. **On-device visual confirmation of the final square-corner change is still pending** — the earlier 8pt version was installed and superseded before the user could look at it; the 0pt build is on the device now but not yet confirmed by eye.

- **2026-08-21 — The Salt of the Lee split-album bug ROOT-CAUSED via live device logging: a STALE `CatalogCache` entry, not a bad Firestore write. Plus a data-corruption bug in my own earlier heuristic, caught by the same instrumentation. Follow-list navigation and the blank screen also confirmed fixed on device. Build-green, 165/165 tests (1 new).**
  **🔴 THE SPLIT-ALBUM ROOT CAUSE — the catalog row was never wrong; the CACHE was.** Four temporary listeners (save path / catalog upsert / read-time enrich / Shelf grouping) were added and a live `devicectl --console` session captured a real save + relaunch. The decisive pair, same `catalogId` and same ISRC, 90 seconds apart:
  `at save   → row Kfl2FNO7Qw6AiAwY5jIn album='Salt of the Lee - EP'   isrc=GBARL2500268` (read straight from Firestore)
  `cold load → row Kfl2FNO7Qw6AiAwY5jIn album='Dungarvan Bay - Single' isrc=GBARL2500268` (read via `CatalogCache`)
  `CatalogCache` is disk-persisted and `metadata(for:)` **only fetches ids it does NOT already hold — it never refreshes one it does.** `invalidate(_:)` existed but **had zero callers anywhere in the app.** So a copy cached while the row still said "Dungarvan Bay - Single" was served on every cold launch forever, even after Firestore had been corrected. That is exactly the reported behaviour — saves correctly, splits on reopen — and why re-saving never helped: the correction landed in Firestore while the read path kept serving a stale copy. **Every previous session's theory (ISRC sharing, the repair branch, `ensureCatalogId`'s early return) was chasing the write path; the bug was entirely on the read path.**
  **Fix, two parts — the second is the one that actually heals an existing bad entry.** (1) `upsertCatalogTrack` now calls `CatalogCache.shared.invalidate([catalogId])` after any repair write. (2) `LibraryViewModel.saveAlbum` invalidates **unconditionally** for every row it saves. (2) is essential: once a row is already correct no repair fires, so a repair-triggered invalidation would never run and a stale entry would persist indefinitely. Confirmed fixed on device — a cold load now logs `group key='salt of the lee' tracks=["R&H Hall","My Favourite Monster","Bittersweet","Dungarvan Bay"]`.
  **🔴 A data-corruption bug in my own `isSingleLikeAlbum`, found by the enrich listener and worse than the bug it was written for.** The listener flagged 30/701 rows as "single-like", including **"This Is Why" (Paramore), "Visions of a Life" (Wolf Alice), "3D Country" (Geese), "Romance" (Fontaines D.C.)** — all genuine albums. Cause: the heuristic treated *album name == track title* as evidence of a single, but a **title track is extremely common**. Because `isBetterRelease` uses this to decide whether a catalog row's album may be **overwritten**, a later save could have replaced a perfectly good album name with a compilation's — corrupting shared, multi-user catalog data to fix a narrower problem. Narrowed to the explicit `"- Single"` suffix only, which is all the Dungarvan case ever needed (its row carried the suffix). Confirmed on device: flagged rows dropped 30 → 18, the 12 removed being exactly the title tracks. Tests updated to assert the corrected semantics **and** the corollary that a title-track album can never be overwritten.
  **Follow-list navigation — the real cause was `ProfileEntryPoint`'s BOOLEAN push, fixed at source after two wrong attempts.** `ProfileEntryPoint` pushed `ProfileView` with `.navigationDestination(isPresented:)`, and Round 9.5 recorded that value-based pushes stacked on a boolean-pushed screen land one level off — so EVERY push downstream of Profile was affected (the follower count, then tapping a name inside the list, which did nothing at all). Round 9.5 worked around it per-screen with `.navigationDestination(item:)`, but Round 9.9 recorded that an item-bound destination **re-presents itself when something is pushed on top of it** — so the two workarounds simply trade one bug for the other, and my earlier switch to item-based reintroduced 9.9's bug verbatim. Fixed by making `ProfileEntryPoint` value-based (`OwnProfileRoute`), so the whole chain — tab root → own profile → follow list → someone's profile — is uniformly value-based. **There are now zero `.navigationDestination(isPresented:)` uses in the app.** Also: `FollowListView`'s rows push a `UserProfile`, but **`ShelfView` registered no `UserProfile` destination at all**, so that tap had nowhere to go from the Shelf tab; every hosting stack now registers `FollowListTarget` + `UserProfile` together, with identical closures (that sameness, not uniqueness, is what makes the cross-stack duplicates safe).
  **🔴 The blank screen was MY OWN fix.** A device capture showed the app running with `windows=2`, and `AppChrome.applyWindowBackground()` — added days earlier to fix a white flash — painted **every** window `bgBase` on every activation. A second, normally-transparent overlay window was being filled opaque and covering the app: hence a full-bleed `bgBase` screen with no nav bar, no tab bar, appearing on reopen and immediately after taking a screenshot (both moments that run this / create an overlay window). Now filtered to the window whose root is a `UIHostingController`. Confirmed fixed by the user. While there, `AppChrome` was marked `@MainActor`, eliminating **70 pre-existing concurrency warnings**.
  **Album resolution — real anchors logged, one fix landed, one problem proven unfixable in the resolver.** `resolveAlbum` was passing its `title` (the ALBUM name) into `resolveDetailedSong(title:)`, whose last-resort step is a **Song** text search — so it searched for a song called "Place - Single", which doesn't exist. Fixed by threading the real `trackTitle`. But the device log then proved the remaining failure is a corrupt row, not resolver logic: `id=i.KoJe5PvuWYJVg` (library-scoped, useless against the catalog), `isrc=nil`, and the row's own album/artwork/findable-song name three different releases. Both album searches returned pure noise (Eminem, Lady Gaga, Juice WRLD — not one Lucy Rose release), correctly rejected by the match guard. **Left unfixed deliberately**: repairing it means rewriting id/ISRC/album/artwork together in shared catalog data, which needs its own decision.
  **Method note — this round is the argument for instrumenting over theorising.** Three prior sessions guessed at the split-album cause and all three were wrong, because every theory targeted the write path. One live capture settled it in minutes AND surfaced a second, more dangerous bug nobody was looking for. Temporary listeners were removed afterwards; the read-time enrich check was **kept, trimmed to 5 examples**, as a permanent early warning for this bug class.
  **Verification.** Clean build green, no new warnings in touched files (checked by diffing against the pre-change log); 165/165 NodalongKit tests; installed to the physical device. Split-album, follow-list navigation and blank screen all confirmed fixed by the user on device.

- **2026-08-19 (later still) — Follow-list navigation: the item-based push I introduced earlier the same day WAS the bug, exactly as Round 9.9 documented. Reverted to value-based and registered both destinations on every hosting stack (ShelfView had none at all). Build-green, 164/164 tests, on device.**
  **What the user reported, precisely:** tapping a NAME inside a followers/following list "doesnt navigate me to the profile i tapped, just another instance of the screen im already on" — i.e. a second copy of the follow list. Note this is a DIFFERENT tap from the earlier report (tapping the followers/following *count*), which the `FollowListTarget` uid-carrying value did fix.
  **🔴 Cause — a regression I shipped hours earlier, and its signature is written verbatim in this log at Round 9.9.** I had replaced the value-based push with `@State selectedFollowList` + `.navigationDestination(item:)`. Round 9.9: *"since it presents whenever its bound item is non-nil, pushing `AlbumDetailSearchView` on top of the grid didn't clear `discographyGridTab` — so the artist view kept re-evaluating with the item still set, and the grid got pushed a second time above the album."* Identical mechanism here: `FollowListView` is presented while `selectedFollowList` is non-nil; tapping a row pushes a `UserProfile` on top; the item is still set, so the follow list re-presents above it. Hence "another instance of the screen I'm already on."
  **Fix — Round 9.9's own remedy: drop the item binding, go uniformly value-based.** Both `ProfileView` and `UserProfileView` are back to `NavigationLink(value: FollowListTarget(...))`; both `@State selectedFollowList` properties and their `.navigationDestination(item:)` modifiers are gone. `FollowListTarget` (uid + username + mode) is kept — it's what fixed the *count* tap, and it's what makes duplicate registrations safe.
  **Also fixed, and the user asked for it directly ("can we make sure that we include the destination in the navigation stack"):** `FollowListView`'s rows push a `UserProfile`, but **`ShelfView` registered no `UserProfile` destination at all** — so a row tap on a follow list reached through the Shelf tab had no destination on its stack. Every hosting stack (Home/Feed/Search/NearMe/Shelf/Profile) now registers **both** `FollowListTarget` and `UserProfile`, as one commented block. Every closure builds purely from the pushed value, so the cross-stack duplicates are behaviourally identical — **that sameness, not uniqueness, is the property that makes this safe**, and it's why adding registrations rather than deduplicating them is correct here.
  **Process note worth keeping: two of my last three navigation fixes were regressions of patterns this log already warned about** (Round 9.5's boolean-push interaction, then Round 9.9's item-rebinding). Both times the answer was already written down. Search the decisions log for the symptom BEFORE forming a theory — "duplicate/extra screen revealed on back" means item-based re-presentation; "lands one level off" means a boolean push in the chain.
  **Verification.** Clean build green, no new warnings (the one `FollowListView:118` warning is pre-existing, confirmed against the pre-change log); 164/164 NodalongKit tests; installed to the physical device. **Not verified from here:** the actual tap-through needs the device — from your own Profile and from someone else's, tap the follower count, then tap a name in the list, and confirm you land on that person's profile with a single back step.

- **2026-08-19 (later) — Album resolution ROOT-CAUSED (an album name was being fed into a SONG-title search), follow-list navigation refixed using this repo's own documented Round-9.5 pattern after my first theory proved wrong, and the resolve cascade instrumented. User pushback, verbatim: "its still not working... More work is needed on this, you're going to have to do a proper investigation into these, no guessing." Build-green, 164/164 tests, installed to the physical device.**
  **🔴 THE ALBUM-RESOLVE ROOT CAUSE — a parameter misuse, provable by reading, no device data needed.** `resolveAlbum(catalogTrackId:isrc:title:artist:)`'s `title` is documented as **the ALBUM name**. It forwarded that straight into `resolveDetailedSong(title:)`, whose last-resort step is `MusicCatalogSearchRequest(term: "<title> <artist>", types: [MusicKit.Song.self])` — **a SONG search**. So for the reported case it searched for a *song* called "Place - Single" by Lucy Rose. No such song exists; the song is "Place".
