That step could therefore essentially **never** succeed on the album path, leaving resolution dependent entirely on the id/ISRC anchors — and when `catalogTrackId` is a library-scoped uri (common) and the catalog row carries no ISRC, every anchor is dead and the whole resolve returns nil. Confirmed every call site passes an album name (`album.albumName` / `pending.albumName` / `track.album`). **A previous session already knew and worked AROUND it rather than fixing it** — `resolveArtists`' own doc comment says "it's shared with `resolveAlbum`, whose callers pass an ALBUM name into it, so a title-match check at that level would break the album path". **Fix:** `resolveAlbum` gained a `trackTitle` parameter (defaulted, so no call site breaks) and passes *that* to `resolveDetailedSong`, falling back to `title` only when absent. `RecentAlbum` and `FriendsListeningAlbum` gained `trackTitle` (the representative post's own `title`); `PlayerViewModel` passes `track.title`. This is the actual bug behind "Couldn't find this album" — the previous round's ISRC threading was necessary but not sufficient, since a missing ISRC still fell through to a search that could never match.
  **⚠️ My earlier follow-list theory was WRONG — recorded so it isn't re-attempted.** I diagnosed it as two competing `.navigationDestination(for: FollowListMode.self)` registrations (ProfileView's vs UserProfileView's) resolving root-first, and fixed it with a uid-carrying `FollowListTarget` registered once per tab root. **That shipped and did not fix it.** The value work was still worth keeping (a uid-less push value genuinely could build the wrong user's list, and the screen can now title itself "<name>'s Followers") but it was not the cause. **The actual cause is documented in this very log, Round 9.5, describing the identical symptom on this exact screen:** `ProfileEntryPoint` pushes `ProfileView` with a **BOOLEAN** `.navigationDestination(isPresented:)`, and stacking a type-based `NavigationLink(value:)` + ambient `.navigationDestination(for:)` push on top of a boolean-pushed screen lands the push one level off — "you see the previous screen and only find the real destination after pressing back", word-for-word the user's report. Round 9.5's fix for the playlist rail on this same screen was a local `@State` + `.navigationDestination(item:)`, and `ProfileView` still carries that working code for `selectedPlaylist`. **Applied the same pattern to follow lists** on both `ProfileView` and `UserProfileView` (`@State selectedFollowList`, `Button` instead of `NavigationLink`, `.navigationDestination(item:)`), and removed the four tab-root `FollowListTarget` registrations added earlier the same day — redundant once the push is item-based, and leaving them would have reintroduced the ambiguity. **Lesson: this log already contained the answer; I theorised instead of searching it first.**
  **Instrumentation over another guess.** `resolveAlbum` now logs, per resolve: album name, artist, trackTitle, whether an id/ISRC anchor existed, whether the underlying song resolved, the full `.albums` relationship contents, and **which cascade step answered** (or that all three failed). A failure now yields facts — specifically whether the anchors were dead or the name-matching rejected a real candidate — instead of a hypothesis.
  **Deliberately still NOT done: a fallback that opens a different album.** `resolveAlbum` has an explicit "no step 4" rule from a confirmed past bug (tapping "Place - Single" once opened "Like I Used To (Deluxe Edition)" — the two names share nothing). Substituting is worse than an honest miss, so the failure state instead explains the likely cause and offers **Try again**.
  **Blank screen — still open, instrumentation now genuinely on the device.** `didReceiveMemoryWarning` + background/foreground/resign-active logging (window count + rootVC type) plus the `ContentView` branch breadcrumb are installed. Needs one repro WITH this build and a console capture; the branch line alone splits it into a rendering vs. a state problem.
  **Verification.** Clean build green; every warning in the touched files confirmed **pre-existing** by diffing against the pre-change log (`FollowListView:118` is the same warning previously at `:85`, shifted by added lines; the `PlayerViewModel`/`AppleMusicService` MusicKit-Sendable ones are the long-standing set catalogued in §7). 164/164 NodalongKit tests. Built for and installed to the physical device.

- **2026-08-19 — Player top-bar contrast, the follow-list wrong-user navigation bug, ISRC threaded into album resolution, blank-screen instrumentation — build-green, zero new warnings, 164/164 tests. Installed to the PHYSICAL DEVICE this time (the previous round only ever reached the simulator, which is why two of its fixes still reproduced for the user).**
  **Player top-bar buttons invisible over dark artwork (light mode).** `NowPlayingView.topBar`'s chevron-down and queue glyphs used `textPrimary` — dark ink in light mode — over `background`, which is the current track's own artwork, blurred. **No fixed colour can work here**: dark ink vanishes on a dark cover, and any light colour would fail the same way on a pale one, because the backdrop is arbitrary user content. Fixed by giving the controls their own backdrop instead of tinting against an unknown: new `topBarGlyph(_:)` puts each glyph on an `.ultraThinMaterial` circle with a hairline border, inside the existing 44×44 hit area. Same affordance as the pushed-screen back button, so it reads as existing language. The pinned `ScreenMetrics.bounds` layout below it was NOT touched, per its own doc comment's warning history.
  **🔴 Followers/Following opened the WRONG USER's list — the duplicate-registration bug again (cf. Round 9.9).** `FollowListMode` was a bare `enum { followers, following }` carrying **no uid**, so the uid came from whichever screen's destination closure SwiftUI picked: `ProfileView` registered one building from `authVM.currentUser.uid` (yours), `UserProfileView` registered the same type building from `user.uid` (theirs). Reach someone's profile via your own Profile tab (Profile → your follow list → tap a person → their profile) and **both registrations are live on one stack** — and this codebase already established that SwiftUI resolves such a duplicate in favour of the one closest to the stack ROOT. `ProfileView`'s won, so tapping *their* follower count pushed a list built from *your* uid. **Fix: make the value self-describing rather than fighting over which closure wins** — new `FollowListTarget { uid, username, mode }`, pushed by both screens, registered once per tab root alongside the existing `.navigationDestination(for: UserProfile.self)`. Both per-screen `FollowListMode` registrations deleted. **The property that actually makes this safe isn't uniqueness of registration but that every closure is now identical** (all build purely from the value) — so the remaining duplicates across tab roots are behaviourally moot, which matters because `ShelfView` has no `UserProfile` registration of its own and reaches follow lists only through `ProfileView`. `FollowListView` gained an optional `username` and now titles itself "<name>'s Followers" for someone else's list (previously impossible — the pushed value carried no identity). Dead `@State showFollowList` on `UserProfileView` and `followListMode` on `ProfileView` removed.
  **Album splitting — no new code; the fix was already in the tree, the user was testing an older build.** Worth recording because the *timing* they described ("saves correctly, then later it's split") confirms the diagnosis exactly rather than contradicting it: `saveAlbum` optimistically inserts the just-saved docs in memory, and those carry the real album name from `getAlbumTracks`, so Shelf groups correctly immediately after saving. On the next cold load the docs come back **slim** from Firestore (`catalogId`/`savedAt`/`sourceService` only) and `enrichLibrary` overlays the shared ISRC-keyed catalog row — which still said `"Dungarvan Bay - Single"`. **Nothing retroactively rewrites anything; the correct album name was only ever a local copy, and the optimistic insert masked the bad catalog row until relaunch.** The previous round's `isSingleLikeAlbum` + `reconcileAlbumContextInCatalog` fix addresses it — it just had never been installed to the device.
  **"Place - Single" (Lucy Rose) → "Couldn't find this album".** Both Home rails called `resolveAlbum(catalogTrackId: album.uri, isrc: nil, ...)` — **discarding the ISRC entirely**, leaving only `album.uri` (frequently a library-scoped id that fails catalog lookup outright) and a fuzzy text search against an album name that may itself describe a different release of the same recording. That row is visibly self-inconsistent on device: album name from the single, artwork from "Concert at Union Chapel" — the same ISRC-sharing problem as the split-album bug. **Fix: thread the ISRC through.** `Post` gained an `isrc`, populated in `merged(withCatalog:)` (the catalog join already had it); carried onto `RecentAlbum` and `FriendsListeningAlbum`; passed to `resolveAlbum` from both rails and the prefetch helper. **`Post` needed a tolerant `init(from:)` as a hard prerequisite, not polish** — it has a synthesized decoder and IS persisted via `SnapshotStore` (`HomeSnapshot`, `feedPosts`), where `load` swallows failures with `try?`, so adding any new key would have silently broken every existing snapshot and quietly turned each launch back into a cold network load with no signal. Includes the enum trap `decodeTolerantService` exists for (`decodeIfPresent` THROWS on a present-but-unrecognised raw value).
  **Deliberately did NOT add the "open the track's own album" fallback the plan sketched.** Reading `resolveAlbum` first showed it has an explicit, well-reasoned **no step 4** rule: an earlier version took `bestReleaseIndex`'s top candidate with no match check and silently opened a release sharing nothing with the displayed name (confirmed bug: "Place - Single" → "Like I Used To (Deluxe Edition)"). Substituting a different album is exactly what that rule forbids. Instead the failure state stopped being a dead end: it now explains the likely cause ("may only exist as a single, or under a different release name") and offers **Try again**, wired to a new extracted `attemptResolve()` so the retry re-runs the identical resolution rather than duplicating it.
  **Blank screen — still NOT root-caused, still deliberately not guessed at.** Narrowed by elimination: not `LaunchSplash` (renders on `bgDeep` `#5F418B` with a logo and spinner; the screenshot is `bgBase` `#D2B7CB`), not `MainTabView` with empty content (the tab bar is a system view and would still draw), and `ScreenMetrics` is **ruled out** as a zero-size suspect — it falls back to a hardcoded 393×852, never zero. That leaves the window itself with no SwiftUI content painted over it. **The valuable new clue is that it also reproduces when taking a screenshot** — screenshots, the app-switcher and the resume animation all drive UIKit's snapshot render path, so the common factor is off-cycle rendering, not user interaction. Added `didReceiveMemoryWarning` logging (a warning immediately before a relaunch means jetsam, not a resume) and background/foreground/resign-active logging carrying window count + rootVC type, alongside the existing `ContentView` branch breadcrumb — which had never actually run on the device. **Caught and fixed 4 real new concurrency warnings I introduced doing this** (the notification closures are `@Sendable`, so `queue: .main` guarantees the thread but not the actor — the UIKit reads needed an explicit `Task { @MainActor in }` hop). Verified against the pre-change build log that `FollowListView`'s remaining warning is **pre-existing**, merely shifted from line 85 to 118 by the lines I added — not a regression.
  **Verification.** Clean `xcodebuild` green with **zero new warnings in any touched file** (checked by diffing against the pre-change log rather than trusting a green build); 164/164 NodalongKit tests. Built for and **installed to the physical device** (`devicectl` threw a transient `CoreDeviceError 4000` disconnect on first attempt — succeeded on retry, worth knowing it's flaky rather than fatal). **Not verifiable from here:** all four user-facing behaviours need the device — dark-cover contrast in light mode, the follow-list path from your own Profile, re-saving the EP then relaunching, and tapping "Place - Single". The blank screen needs one more repro WITH this build to produce evidence.

- **2026-08-18 (later) — Four on-device reports fixed: Home/Profile/artist-page "double loading", and the split-album bug (Salt of the Lee / Dungarvan Bay) ROOT-CAUSED — build-green, 164/164 NodalongKit tests (9 new).** Plus a defensive fix + permanent breadcrumb for an intermittent empty-screen report that was deliberately NOT guessed at.
  **🔴 The split-album bug — the real mechanism, and it is NOT in the save path.** Report: saving the *Salt of the Lee* EP puts 3 of 4 tracks in the album and strands "Dungarvan Bay" as its own single. Yes, each song is saved as its own document (`saveAlbum` → `batchUpsertLibraryTracks`, one doc per track in one batch) — that is correct and not the bug. The actual chain: a library doc stores **only** `catalogId`/`savedAt`/`sourceService` (`slimLibraryData`) — no album name — and metadata is overlaid on read from the shared `tracks` catalog, where `LibraryTrackDoc.merged(withCatalog:)` **unconditionally overwrites `album`**. That catalog is keyed by **ISRC**, and a recording released as a standalone single AND on an EP is the *same recording* → same ISRC → **one shared catalog row**. So whichever release was ingested first owns that row's album name, for every user, permanently. "Dungarvan Bay" was evidently ingested from its single first, so its row says e.g. `"Dungarvan Bay - Single"`; `groupByAlbum` keys on `stripSearchNoise(album)` → `"dungarvan bay"` ≠ `"salt of the lee"` → its own tile. **It never self-healed for two INDEPENDENT reasons, both needed fixing:** (1) `isBetterRelease` only upgraded away from *compilation* keywords ("greatest hits"/"deluxe"/…), and a `"- Single"` name contains none; (2) `ensureCatalogId(forLibrary:)` **early-returns** on a known `catalogId` or a `TrackCache` hit, so for an already-known track `upsertCatalogTrack` — and with it the whole repair branch — never ran at all. **Fix:** new pure `isSingleLikeAlbum(album:trackTitle:)` in `ReleaseMatching.swift` (explicit `"- Single"` suffix, or an album name that reduces to the track's own title) + `isBetterRelease` gained an optional `trackTitle:` and a second upgrade path for it, still strictly one-directional so a good row can never regress; `upsertCatalogTrack` passes `existing.title`. Because the catalog is shared, one user saving the EP corrects the row for everyone. Reachability fixed separately by a new `LibraryViewModel.reconcileAlbumContextInCatalog(_:)` — a `Task.detached(.utility)`, deliberately **not** awaited (saveAlbum was optimised so the Save button flips without waiting on per-track transactions) that re-upserts each saved track with the album context `getAlbumTracks` actually gave us. 9 new unit tests. **Stated limitation: corrects rows going forward — the user's already-split track heals when the EP is next saved; there's no retroactive catalog sweep.**
  **Home "sections refresh in front of my eyes" — not a redundant fetch.** It's stale-while-revalidate being *visible*: `restoreSnapshotIfNeeded` paints the cached content instantly, then 5 independent network tasks each reassign their own `@Published` section as they land, and `.onChange(of: syncService.lastSyncedAt)` → `refreshOwnRecents` reassigns Jump Back In a second time ~2.5s later when the launch sync completes. The key point: **most of those refreshes return identical data**, but `@Published` fires `objectWillChange` on every assignment regardless of equality, so identical data still forces a re-render — pure visible noise. Fixed with a `set(_:_:)` keypath helper that assigns only on inequality, routed through all 7 assignment sites. Free — every model involved (`Post`/`FriendSpotlight`/`RecommendationShelf`/`PlaylistResult`/`Song`/`RecentAlbum`) was **already `Hashable`**, so no model changes at all. Same guard applied by hand in `ProfileView` and to the artist page's two top-up assignments.
  **Profile spotlight flash.** Profile was the ONLY main screen with no snapshot cache — every visit was a cold read, showing the spotlight skeleton first. Added a private `ProfileSnapshot` through the existing shared `SnapshotStore` (key `"profile"`), restored synchronously at the top of `refresh()`. Note today's spotlights already rendered instantly (they come from `authVM.profile`, loaded at auth) — it was the *historic* carousel that was cold, and that's what this fixes.
  **Artist page Top Songs.** Confirmed deliberate, not a bug: `getArtistCore` returns only the first page (~10) because paginating to 30 in the critical path measured 507–1104ms, 55–74% of the whole artist-page load. The visible artifact isn't the songs changing (the full list is a superset with an identical prefix) — it's the pager growing **2 pages → 6** underneath the user. Kept the two-phase fetch and hid the growth behind a new `topSongsComplete` flag gating only the `PageArrowsRow`; page 1 renders immediately either way, and the arrows appear once instead of the page count visibly jumping. Set true on all four exits (warm cache ≥30, successful top-up, failed/no-op top-up, and the Spotify fallback branch) so a multi-page artist can never be left unpageable. `MusicDetailCache` already persists to disk, so this is a once-per-artist-ever cost.
  **Empty screen — deliberately NOT guessed at.** A full-bleed `bgBase` window, no nav bar, no tab bar, no content — i.e. the window with nothing painted over it (it reads mauve rather than white only because the window background was fixed earlier the same day). Ruled out by reading the code: all three `ContentView` branches render real content, `PartyView` falls back to a `ProgressView`, `MusicServiceLinkView` has content. One genuine defect found while looking and fixed on its own merits: the onboarding cover was `.fullScreenCover(isPresented: .constant(shouldShowMusicLink))` — a cover bound to a constant can never be written back to by SwiftUI's presentation machinery, and `shouldShowMusicLink` derives from `authVM.profile?.activeService`, which transiently changes during profile load/refresh, making that desync reachable (a known cause of blank/stuck covers). Replaced with a real `@State` + `.onChange` sync. Added a permanent `diagLog` breadcrumb on `didBecomeActive` reporting which branch is live, so a recurrence immediately says whether SwiftUI thinks it's showing `MainTabView` (rendering/compositing) or another branch (state) — the same instrumentation-first approach that cracked the white-flash bug rather than another round of theorising.
  **Verification.** Clean `xcodebuild` green, zero new warnings in any touched file; 164/164 NodalongKit tests (155 + 9 new `SingleLikeAlbumTests`, incl. one-directional and default-argument regression cases). Launched on simulator — breadcrumb fires correctly, no crash. **Not verifiable from here (auth wall):** that Home now paints once and stays still, that Profile's carousel is instant on re-entry, and that re-saving the EP pulls Dungarvan Bay in. Also worth checking the *opposite* failure for the equality guards — that content still updates when something genuinely changed (play a new track → Jump Back In updates).

- **2026-08-18 — Full brand-guideline integration: logo mark extracted as a vector asset (and made the app icon + splash/auth branding), Quicksand adopted app-wide (322 call sites), the LP/broadcast icon replaced, and the purple palette rebuilt from the brand PDF's TRUE on-screen colours — build-green, 155/155 NodalongKit tests pass, extensively verified on a real simulator.** User supplied `docs/Nodalong_Brand_Guidelines.pdf` in Plan Mode with four asks (add the logo, adopt the typography, replace the broadcast/LP icon, cross-reference the colour palette + verify the PDF's own hex codes), then added two more mid-session after seeing it on-device.
  **⚠️ THE BIG LESSON — `pdftoppm`/poppler does NOT render a colour-managed PDF the way macOS does, and this cost two wrong palettes before it was caught.** The Colour Palette page states four hex codes (`#EFBBFF`/`#D896FF`/`#BE29EC`/`#8F1FB1`). Sampling the page with `pdftoppm` (cross-checked against `pdftocairo`, pixel-identical) gave a *different* set (`#E0B0D8`/`#C088C8`/`#9038A0`/`#782898`) — I concluded the stated codes were ground truth and the render was a wide-gamut→sRGB export artifact to ignore. **User overruled that** ("i want you to use the rendered hex values so what i see in the app matches what i see on the page"), so the palette was rebuilt on the poppler-sampled values. **User then showed a screenshot taken directly from the PDF in Preview.app that matched NEITHER set.** Root cause: poppler and macOS honour the PDF's embedded ICC profile differently, and *both* my sampling passes were reading colours no human ever sees. Fixed by rendering the page through **macOS's own PDFKit/CoreGraphics pipeline** (a throwaway `swift` script — the same path Preview uses), which produced the actual on-screen values and matched the user's screenshot: **`#D2B7CB` / `#B092B4` / `#7F4E89` / `#6F3C7E`**. **For any future work sampling colours out of a PDF in this repo: render via PDFKit, not poppler.** Poppler is fine for structure/vector extraction, wrong for colour.
  **Palette, final state.** Light: `bgBase` `#EFBBFF`→**`#D2B7CB`**, `bgCard` `#D896FF`→**`#B092B4`** (the two lightest doc colours, verbatim); every other light token untouched. Dark: fully rebuilt — `bgBase`=**`#6F3C7E`** and `bgCard`/`bgElevated`=**`#7F4E89`** are the doc's two darkest colours used *verbatim* (the previous attempt only hue-shifted the old values, which is exactly why the user said it "looks like a different set of colours"); `bgDeep` `#43244C` (the darkest doc colour darkened, hue/sat preserved); `purpleAccent` `#926897` (doc colour 2's hue/sat, lightness pulled down for contrast); `purpleMid`/`purpleBold`/`purpleDark` keep their own established role-hues (≈250°/282°/263° — auth CTAs / NearMe primary / brand identity, a deliberate separation) at the doc family's saturation. Every dark token verified ≥4.5:1 against white before writing; **caught two ordering/contrast bugs pre-ship** (a first pass put `purpleAccent` at 3.53:1; a second put `purpleDark` *lighter* than `purpleAccent` because the search started from the top instead of each token's own lightness). Verified end-to-end by decompiling the built `Assets.car` with `assetutil` — all 16 light/dark values landed exactly.
  **Logo.** Confirmed via `pdftocairo -svg` the mark is real vector art (paths, no raster). Isolated its 6 constituent paths (2 eye curves, smile, headphone band, 2 ear-cup fills — a headphones-over-smiling-face mark) from the cover page's path/glyph soup, wrote a coordinate-transform script to bake each path's own `matrix(...)` into one absolute space, then **hand-wrote a minimal valid single-page vector PDF** from the path data (no SVG→PDF converter exists in this environment — `rsvg-convert`/`cairosvg` both unavailable, `cairosvg` install fails because `libcairo` itself isn't present; PDF content-stream operators map ~1:1 onto SVG's `m`/`c`/`l`/`h`/`f`/`S`, only the y-axis flip is real work). Landed as `nodalongMark.imageset` (`preserves-vector-representation`, template rendering).
  **🔴 `.font()` DOES NOT SIZE A CUSTOM IMAGE ASSET — only SF Symbols.** This shipped as a real bug and the user caught it: swapping `Image(systemName:)` → `Image("nodalongMark")` while keeping the call sites' `.font(...)` meant every mark rendered at the source PDF's **intrinsic 218×174pt**, overflowing its container unclipped. Worst case: three oversized badges overlapping across a playlist row, which read as a bizarre tiled wallpaper rather than one small icon each. **Fix: new `Nodalong/Shared/UI/NodalongMark.swift`** — `.resizable().scaledToFit().frame(width:height:)` with an explicit `size`, which is the *only* thing that constrains a custom asset. All 11 call sites go through it; **a bare `Image("nodalongMark")` anywhere is a bug.** Verified with a temporary on-device harness (red hairline frames around each size, 52/22/18/16/14/13/11pt + a 36×36 mini-player mock) — every mark stays inside its frame; harness removed after.
  **App icon** (user follow-up: "the logo hasnt been replaced either"). The brand guide's own Application Design page specifies it exactly — light mode is a light-purple ground with a darker mark, dark mode inverts — and sampling that page (via PDFKit) gave the same two palette colours, `#B092B4`/`#7F4E89`. Generated all three iOS appearance variants (light/dark/tinted, 1024², RGB no-alpha as iOS requires) by using the vector mark as an alpha mask; `AppIcon.appiconset` now declares `luminosity` light/dark/tinted entries, replacing the single legacy `AppIcon-1024.png`. Confirmed all three compiled into `Assets.car`.
  **Splash/auth branding + mark sizing, after on-device feedback.** Mark added to `SplashView` (96pt), `LoginView`/`RegisterView` (64pt), and — the piece actually still missing when the user asked again — the **native `LaunchScreen.storyboard`**, the true first frame, which showed a bare wordmark on purple with no logo at all. That's hand-edited XML (an `<imageView image="nodalongMark">` tinted `textOnAccent`, constrained above the label, plus a matching `<image>` entry in `<resources>` and the label's own `fontDescription` switched to `Quicksand-Bold`); `xcrun ibtool --compile` is the regression test, since a malformed storyboard fails the whole build with no useful diagnostic — compiled standalone before trusting a full build. Mark sizes bumped per direct feedback that they read too small: mini player **22 → 30** and the playlist party badge **13 → 18**. The 22 was arithmetically "right" but visually wrong — the mark is a fine-line drawing at a 1.25:1 aspect, so `scaledToFit` in an N-point square renders only N/1.25 tall, against a solid SF `play.fill` glyph that fills its full point size; 30 puts the two at a matching visual weight inside the same 36×36 slot (confirmed with a temporary side-by-side harness).
  **Original splash/auth note.** Mark added to `SplashView` (96pt, above the wordmark), `LoginView` and `RegisterView` (64pt). **This surfaced a real pre-existing contrast bug on all three auth sub-screens**: they sit on `bgDeep` (a mid-purple in *both* appearances, by design) but used `textPrimary`/`.secondary`/`purpleMid` for headings, subtitles and the "Forgot password?" link — measured at **1.2–2.1:1**, i.e. effectively invisible (the link was genuinely unreadable on-device). `SplashView` already did this correctly with the `textOnAccent` family; the three sub-screens never got the same treatment. Fixed across `LoginView`/`RegisterView`/`ForgotPasswordView` (white at 0.75/0.85 opacity, measured ≥4.4:1). `AvatarPickerView`'s section labels had the same problem but it's a **shared** component also used by `EditProfileView` on `bgBase` (where `textSecondary` is correct) — so it gained an optional `labelColor` parameter (defaulting to today's value) rather than a blanket change that would have broken the other caller.
  **Quicksand — genuinely new infrastructure** (zero `.ttf`/`UIAppFonts`/`Font` extension existed before). Google Fonts ships Quicksand as a *variable* font (`Quicksand[wght].ttf`, 300–700), not static files — the naive static-file URL 404s and silently writes a 14-byte HTML stub with a `.ttf` extension (hit this; `file` caught it). Downloaded the real variable font and extracted 5 static instances via `fonttools varLib.instancer --update-name-table` (**that flag is required** — without it the instances keep the variable font's mangled multi-weight name strings instead of clean `Quicksand-Light/Regular/Medium/SemiBold/Bold` PostScript names). Registered in both `Info.plist` and `project.yml`'s `info.properties`, plus a `Nodalong/Resources/Fonts` resources entry so they actually bundle. New `Nodalong/Shared/Theme/Font+Nodalong.swift` mirrors `Color.nodalong`'s convention, built from a real inventory (322 sites → ~24 distinct style+weight combos), each `Font.custom(_:size:relativeTo:)` for Dynamic Type. One deliberate non-obvious mapping: **`.headline` → SemiBold, not Regular**, because the system's `.headline` is semibold by default and flattening it would erase that hierarchy app-wide. Swept 255 call sites across 34 files via a Python script (an initial Perl/shell attempt silently no-op'd on quoting — caught by grepping for replacements rather than trusting the command's exit code).
  **Font coverage completed in a second pass, after the user asked to "ensure we are consistent with the font" — the first pass had left three real gaps, all of them visible:** (1) **21 `MarqueeText` call sites** passed a raw system `Font` as the `font:` argument (`.caption.weight(.semibold)`, `.title3.bold()`, …) — these are the app's most prominent titles (Now Playing title/artist/album, the mini player title, every Home tile, Party header, album/artist names), and the sweep only ever touched `.font(...)` *modifiers*, never a `Font` passed as a parameter. 25 replacements. (2) **27 `Text` sites on `.font(.system(size:))`**, deliberately skipped the first time as "icon-adjacent" — but many were genuine text (TrackCard titles, playlist/album tile captions, the toast label, the splash wordmark, Search's explicit-"E" badge). Added `Font.nodalong.custom(_:_:)` for these and converted them by classifying each site's *owning element* (walk backwards to the nearest `Text(` vs `Image(systemName:`) rather than by the modifier alone; SF Symbol glyphs stay on `.system` (a custom face can't improve a symbol, and SF Symbols are metric-matched to SF), and **emoji stay on `.system` too** — Quicksand has no emoji glyphs, so one emoji site that the classifier caught was reverted by hand. (3) **Nav bar titles and tab bar labels are drawn by UIKit**, so `Font.nodalong` never applied to them at all — a nav title in SF sitting directly above Quicksand body text.
  **That last one had a second, deeper cause worth recording: the app's UIKit chrome setup lived in `MainTabView.init()`, which only runs AFTER sign-in** — so the entire pre-auth navigation stack (Sign In / Create Account / Reset password) received *no* chrome configuration whatsoever, not just no font. Extracted into a new `Nodalong/Shared/Theme/AppChrome.swift` split in two: `configureLaunchChrome()` (called from `AppDelegate.didFinishLaunchingWithOptions`, so it applies from the first frame — transparent nav background + Quicksand + `textOnAccent` title colour, deliberately NOT the opaque `bgBase` treatment, since the auth screens sit on `bgDeep` and an opaque `bgBase` strip across their top would read as a mismatched band) and `configureMainChrome()` (the authenticated app's opaque nav bar, brand tints and window background — still called once from `MainTabView.init()` behind its existing `didConfigureChrome` guard, and it correctly overrides the launch config since appearance proxies apply to bars created after they're set). Fonts resolve via `UIFont(name:)` with a system-font fallback rather than a force-unwrap. **Verified on-device by screenshotting the "Sign In" nav title before and after — SF's double-storey `g` became Quicksand's single-storey geometric one.**
  **White flash on reopen — root-caused via device diagnostics, fixed.** User reported white showing when reopening the app (screenshot: white bands at the screen edges mid-resume, with the app-switcher snapshot and the live view visibly composited over each other). Instrumented rather than guessed, and the capture was unambiguous: at `didFinishLaunching` there are **0 scenes and 0 windows**, and at `didBecomeActive` the window's `backgroundColor` was **`nil`** — which renders white. Root cause: the window-background assignment lived inline in the UIKit chrome setup, which runs **exactly once, from `MainTabView.init()`, and only after sign-in** — so it was nil for the entire pre-auth session, nil on any cold relaunch until that init ran, and (per the 0-windows finding) would have been a silent no-op over an empty array had anyone moved it to launch. iOS composites the app-switcher snapshot against the live view during the resume animation, and any gap between them exposes the window itself — hence white. **Fix:** extracted `AppChrome.applyWindowBackground()` (idempotent, re-runnable) and called it from a `didBecomeActive` observer registered in `AppDelegate` — guaranteed to have a window by then, and covers launch *and* every subsequent reopen — as well as from `configureMainChrome()` for immediacy. Verified post-fix on-device: the window background is now a real trait-aware `UIDynamicProviderColor` instead of nil. **Worth remembering: `UIApplication.shared.connectedScenes` is empty at `didFinishLaunchingWithOptions` in this app — any window-level setup has to hang off scene activation, not launch.**
  **Mark sizes settled after previewing candidates on-device:** mini player **30**, playlist party badge **24** (previewed at 18/20/22/24 side-by-side and in-context on a real 140pt tile before the user picked).
  **Verification.** `xcodegen generate` + full clean `xcodebuild` green, zero new warnings; 155/155 NodalongKit tests pass. Provisioned a fresh iOS 26.3 simulator (the long-booted sims on this Mac are iOS 18.6, below the 26.1 deployment target). **Directly confirmed on-device:** Quicksand renders (its rounded letterforms are unmistakable); the logo appears correctly sized on Splash, Login and Register; auth-screen text is now legible; the new light-mode `bgBase` renders as the doc's soft mauve; `NodalongMark` respects its frame at every size. **Not confirmed here:** anything behind the auth wall (mini player, LP screens, Feed/Profile in the new dark palette) — needs a signed-in session on the user's own device. Note the simulator renders avatar emoji as "?" boxes (a simulator font gap, not a bug) and its springboard icon cache doesn't reliably show freshly-installed app icons, so the app icon is verified by `assetutil` + direct PNG inspection rather than a home-screen screenshot.
- **2026-08-14 — The discography grid's sizing bug — the one that's been "fixed" roughly 6 times across sessions (Rounds 9.22/9.26/9.27/9.38 in this same log) and kept recurring — actually root-caused this time, with pixel-level evidence from the user's own screenshots, not another guess. Build-green.** User pushback, verbatim: "why is that so hard... the sizing never looks right... can we plan this fix out for real this time." Entered plan mode rather than patching again.
  **The evidence.** Two screenshots — one correct (Discovery tab), one broken (Albums tab, discs cut off at top, labels missing at bottom). Measured the rendered layout in both against the code's own constants (`cardWidth = (screenWidth-40-32-16)/2`, `VinylArtworkView`'s `size × 0.88` disc, `pageHeight(forRows:)`'s formula): the *correct* screenshot's disc diameter and row pitch matched the code's math to within ~2pt — confirming `cardWidth`/`VinylArtworkView` were never the problem, contrary to every earlier round's suspicion of the artwork/grid-rendering layer. The *broken* screenshot's visible content window measured ~237pt, matching `pageHeight(forRows: 1)` (240.5pt) almost exactly — while the page was actually rendering 2 full rows (`pageHeight(forRows: 2)` = 465pt). **The container was sized for 1 row while displaying 2** — `TabView` centred the oversized page content in the undersized slot and `.clipped()` cropped both ends, which is exactly "discs cut off at the top, labels missing at the bottom."
  **Why it got stuck there, and why swiping "fixed" it (the actual explanation for the multi-session pattern):** `DiscographyPageGrid` stored its height in `@State private var displayedHeight`, seeded once in a custom `init` and hand-synced afterward by two separate `.onChange` handlers (`pageIndex`, `albumList.count`). `ArtistDetailSearchView.loadData()` assigns `albums` from `getArtistCore` first, then AGAIN later from a background `getArtistAlbumsFull` top-up (added in an earlier round specifically because Apple's `.albums` relationship is itself unpaginated — see that round's own entry). `DiscographyPageGrid` is therefore routinely first constructed while `fullAlbums` is still empty or small — `rowCount(0)` seeds 1 row. While the list is empty, `body` renders the plain `Text(emptyMessage)` branch, so the `TabView` branch carrying both `.onChange` handlers **doesn't exist yet**. When the background top-up lands and that branch finally mounts, the handlers attach fresh — and `.onChange` does not fire for a change that already happened before it existed. `displayedHeight` is stuck at 1 row, permanently, for that view instance (its `@State` survives because the empty/non-empty switch is inside ONE view identity's own `body`). It only self-corrected once `.onChange(of: pageIndex)` finally fired from a genuine swipe — precisely the "self-corrects after swiping to page 2 and back" symptom reported since Round 9.27. This also explains why Discovery (built later, after data had already landed) looked fine while Albums (built early, before the top-up) didn't, and it *retroactively* explains Round 9.38's own diagnostic finding — the live console capture showing `chunks` byte-for-byte stable across renders was genuine evidence the DATA was never wrong, but got misread as ruling out a data explanation entirely; it was actually pointing at the container, not the renderer, the whole time. Round 9.38's fix (LazyVGrid → eager 2×2) treated a real but different, unconfirmed hypothesis and left this actual bug untouched — which is exactly why the symptom kept recurring after that "fix" shipped.
  **The fix — remove the bug class, not patch another instance of it.** A derived value kept in `@State` and hand-synced by targeted `.onChange` handlers has a missed-sync path by construction; the fix is to stop storing it. `displayedHeight` and the custom `init` that seeded it are gone; height is now two pure computed properties recomputed fresh on every render: `maxPageHeight` (tallest page across `chunks` — 2 rows whenever any page is full) and `targetHeight` (the currently-visible page's own row count, clamped). Both `TabView`'s inner frame AND every individual page's own `.frame(height:)` are now pinned to the SAME constant `maxPageHeight` (previously each page sized itself independently from its own row count, which is what let a page overflow its slot in the first place) — the outer, `.clipped()` frame is the only thing that varies, driven by `targetHeight` via a single `.animation(.easeInOut(duration: 0.25), value: targetHeight)` instead of two `.onChange`-triggered `withAnimation` calls. A computed property cannot go stale the way stored, hand-synced state did. `pageIndex` stays a `@Binding` (from the immediately-preceding same-session fix moving `PageArrowsRow` outside the card) — losing the custom `init` risked Swift synthesizing a `private` memberwise init instead (since the new `@ScaledMetric labelHeight` property is `private`, and a struct's synthesized init takes the most restrictive stored-property access level), which would have been uncallable from `discographySection`, a sibling type in the same file — caught before it became a real bug, not shipped and found later. `labelHeight` (the per-row caption budget) changed from a fixed `56` constant to `@ScaledMetric`, since it's a text-height *estimate* and an under-estimate is what crops content — correct at default text size (measured ~50pt against the 56pt budget) but would clip at larger Dynamic Type sizes.
  Build green (`xcodegen generate` not needed — no new files — + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), zero new warnings in the touched file. **On-device unverified** — the specific repro every prior round's fix missed is a *cold, never-viewed-this-session* artist's Albums tab checked WITHOUT swiping first (Kneecap/Vince Staples, both already confirmed broken in the reported screenshots) — swiping first is what has silently masked this exact bug in every previous round's own on-device check, so that's the one case worth testing deliberately rather than incidentally.

- **2026-08-14 — Discography arrows still showed inside the card after the fix below — the file had been independently modified in between (`DiscographyPageGrid.body` restructured, its own internal `PageArrowsRow` back in place) — build-green.** Screenshot showed two arrow rows: the correct one outside the box (from the entry below) plus a duplicate stray one still inside. Root cause: `DiscographyPageGrid.body` had been rewritten since the previous fix landed (its two-`.onChange` structure, per-page `pageHeight(forRows:)` sizing instead of `maxPageHeight` pinning, and — the actual bug — its own `if chunks.count > 1 { PageArrowsRow(...) }` block reintroduced). `discographySection`'s own external arrows row (the actual fix from the entry below) was untouched and still correct. Removed only the reintroduced internal block, leaving the rest of that restructuring as-is — `pageIndex` stays a `@Binding` owned by `discographySection` specifically so `PageArrowsRow` can render as a sibling outside the box, not duplicated inside it. Build green, zero new warnings. **On-device unverified.**

- **2026-08-14 — Discography grid's pagination arrows moved OUTSIDE the boxed card, to match Top Songs' pattern — build-green.** User feedback (two screenshots): Top Songs' `‹ ›` arrows sit below its purple card on the plain background ("very clean"), but Discography's sat inside its purple card, which "looks weird." Root cause: `popularSection` (Top Songs) already rendered `PageArrowsRow` as a sibling AFTER its `bgSurface`-boxed `TabView`, on the plain page background — but `discographySection`'s box (`.background(bgSurface).clipShape(...)`) wrapped the tab-buttons row AND the entire `DiscographyPageGrid`, which rendered its OWN `PageArrowsRow` internally as part of the same returned view — so the arrows ended up inside the box by construction, not by any deliberate styling choice.
  **Fix:** `DiscographyPageGrid.pageIndex` changed from an internal `@State` to a `@Binding` passed in by the caller — new `ArtistDetailSearchView.albumsGridPage`/`discoveryGridPage` `@State` vars (one per tab, mirroring the existing `topSongsPage`), reset to 0 via a new `.onChange(of: discographyTab)` (preserving the page-reset-on-tab-switch behavior that used to happen for free when each tab's grid was a distinct view torn down/recreated by the `if/else` branch). `DiscographyPageGrid` no longer renders `PageArrowsRow` internally — the arrows-decision logic (`chunks.count > 1`) moved to `discographySection` itself via a new shared top-level `discographyPageCount(for:)` function (mathematically identical to `chunks.count`'s `stride(by: 4)` chunking — kept as a separate function since `chunks` is `private` to the grid and the caller needs a count without the actual sub-arrays). `PageArrowsRow` is now rendered as a sibling AFTER the boxed `VStack` (tabs + grid), exactly matching Top Songs' placement. Build green (`xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), zero new warnings. **On-device unverified** — needs a look to confirm the arrows now sit below the box on the plain background and still page the grid correctly for both the Albums and Discovery tabs.

- **2026-08-14 — Fixed the mini player's broadcast button: tapping it opened `StartPartySheet` and closed it again almost instantly, before the user could interact — build-green.** Root-caused by tracing every write site of `playerVM.isExpanded` (only one exists: `MiniPlayerBar.swift`'s own `.onTapGesture { playerVM.isExpanded = true }`, applied to the whole pill) against the fact that `MainTabView` presents `NowPlayingView` via `.fullScreenCover(isPresented: $playerVM.isExpanded)`. The broadcast button (and play/pause) were nested `Button`s styled `.buttonStyle(.plain)` *inside* that same `.onTapGesture`-bearing container — a known SwiftUI footgun where an ancestor's plain `.onTapGesture` can ALSO fire alongside a nested plain-style `Button`'s own action, not just instead of it. Tapping broadcast therefore set BOTH `showStartParty = true` (opening `MiniPlayerBar`'s own `.sheet` for `StartPartySheet`) AND `playerVM.isExpanded = true` (opening `NowPlayingView` via `MainTabView`'s `.fullScreenCover`) — the fullScreenCover, presented from the higher ancestor, displaced the sheet almost immediately, reading as "opens and closes before I can do anything." **Fix:** converted the whole pill from `HStack {...}.onTapGesture {...}` to `Button { playerVM.isExpanded = true } label: { HStack {...} }.buttonStyle(.plain)` — nested `Button`s reliably claim priority over an ancestor `Button`'s own action in SwiftUI (unlike a plain `.onTapGesture`), so tapping play/pause or broadcast now only fires that control's own action. All existing visual modifiers (padding/background/clipShape/overlay/shadow/contentShape) kept exactly as they were, just now inside the `Button`'s label instead of directly on the `HStack`. Build green (`xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), zero new warnings. **On-device unverified** — needs the user to confirm tapping broadcast now reliably opens (and keeps open) `StartPartySheet` / reopens an active party, and that tapping play/pause still works and that tapping elsewhere on the bar still opens `NowPlayingView` as before.

- **2026-08-14 — Deprecation-warning cleanup: `UIScreen.main` (19 sites) + `onChange(of:perform:)` (20 sites) + 2 small `@available`-suppression attempts that turned out NOT to work + 4 bonus nitpicks — build-green, 143/143 NodalongKit tests pass, zero regressions.** User asked to investigate and clean up compiler warnings without breaking anything. A clean isolated build (`xcodebuild -derivedDataPath`) showed 72 unique warning locations, split into three buckets confirmed by reading every one, not assumed: (A) true API deprecations — `UIScreen.main` (deprecated iOS 26, ~19 call sites/13 files) and `onChange(of:perform:)` (deprecated iOS 17, ~20 call sites/13 files), both trivially fixable now that the deployment target is 26.1; plus two one-off deprecations (`MPVolumeView.showsRouteButton`, `ASPresentationAnchor()`/`UIWindow.init()`) with no real non-deprecated replacement for what they do. (B) 4 small unrelated warnings noticed in passing (a never-mutated `var`, a redundant `nonisolated(unsafe)`, a discarded Firestore transaction result, two dead `?? .spotify`/`?? .appleMusic` fallbacks — `Song.sourceService` was made non-optional back in the original Spotify→Apple Music port). (C) ~20 Swift 6 strict-concurrency warnings (actor-isolation/Sendable — "error in the Swift 6 language mode") in `PlayerViewModel`/`AppleMusicService`/`SpotifyMusicService`/`Parties.swift`/`NearMeCanvasView` — **left entirely untouched**, per explicit user confirmation: not deprecations, the project is still Swift 5.10 so these are harmless previews of a future Swift 6 mode, and several of those files have real threading-regression history documented elsewhere in this log (the whole `applicationQueuePlayer` XPC saga). Plan file: `~/.claude/plans/jiggly-roaming-gadget.md`.
  **`UIScreen.main` fix** — new `Nodalong/Shared/UI/ScreenMetrics.swift` (`@MainActor static var bounds: CGRect` / `scale: CGFloat`, reads the screen via `UIApplication.shared.connectedScenes.compactMap { ($0 as? UIWindowScene)?.screen }.first`, sane hardcoded fallback if no scene is connected yet — never references `.main` even in the fallback). Since Nodalong is single-scene, this resolves to the exact same physical screen `UIScreen.main` always pointed to — a value-for-value substitution, not a behavior change. Every call site is a mechanical `UIScreen.main.bounds`→`ScreenMetrics.bounds` / `.scale`→`ScreenMetrics.scale` swap; all but one already ran on `@MainActor` by inference (they're inside `View` bodies/methods/static vars — confirmed since none of them produced an *additional* actor-isolation warning). The one exception, `ArtworkURL.sized(...)`, is a plain `static func` on a non-View `enum` — it got the extra "main actor-isolated default value in a nonisolated context" warning, fixed by marking the function itself `@MainActor` (safe: every real caller — `View` bodies, `LibrarySyncService`, `PlayerViewModel` — is already `@MainActor`). **`NowPlayingView.swift` (4 of the 19 sites) got extra care per direct user confirmation** — its own header doc comment calls this "the fragile `UIScreen.main.bounds`-pinned layout," referencing this log's own multi-round saga where seemingly-safe changes to this exact pinning broke the full-screen player. The swap here only changes *where the CGRect value comes from*, not the `.frame(width:height:)` + `.ignoresSafeArea()` technique — its doc comments were updated to say `ScreenMetrics.bounds` throughout so the history stays accurate for whoever reads it next.
  **`onChange(of:perform:)` fix** — mechanical migration to the iOS 17+ closure forms: `{ _ in ... }` (value unused) → zero-param `{ ... }`; `{ v in ...v... }` (value used) → two-param `{ _, v in ...v... }`. No behavior change — neither the old nor new form fires on initial view appearance. Also deleted a stale comment on `ProfileView.swift`'s onChange ("Fixed: Updated to the backward-compatible iOS 16 closure syntax") — no longer true now the target is 26.1.
  **The two one-off deprecations (`SystemVolumeSlider.showsRouteButton`, `SpotifyMusicService`'s `ASPresentationAnchor()` fallback) are intentionally left as plain, commented, irreducible warnings — NOT wrapped in an `@available(deprecated:)` helper, despite that being the original plan.** First attempt wrapped each in a private `@available(iOS, deprecated:)`-marked helper function, on the assumption that Swift's "deprecated code calling deprecated code doesn't double-warn" leniency would suppress the warning entirely. **A rebuild proved this assumption wrong** — the warning didn't disappear, it just relocated to point at the *call to the wrapper* from the (non-deprecated) enclosing function, since that leniency only covers uses *inside* a deprecated declaration's own body, it doesn't extend outward to callers of that declaration. Reverted both to plain inline code with an explanatory doc comment recording this finding, so a future session doesn't re-attempt the same trick. **Lesson for future warning-cleanup work in this codebase: `@available(deprecated:)` on a wrapper does NOT hide a deprecation warning from outside callers — it only helps if the *entire calling context* is also willing to be marked deprecated, which is rarely applicable for a protocol-conformance method.** For `showsRouteButton` specifically, Apple's suggested replacement (`AVRoutePickerView`) is an additive widget, not a flag — there's no non-deprecated way to keep a bare slider with the button hidden short of building a real AirPlay picker UI (a feature addition, out of scope). For the `ASPresentationAnchor()` fallback, the branch only runs when no window scene was found — i.e. exactly the case with no scene to pass to the non-deprecated `init(windowScene:)`, so there's genuinely nothing to construct it from.
  **Bonus fixes (bucket B):** `PlayerViewModel.swift`'s `explicitPauseLock` (an `NSLock`, `Sendable`) dropped its now-redundant `nonisolated(unsafe)` — its sibling `_lastExplicitPauseAt` (a genuinely mutable `Date?`) correctly kept its own `nonisolated(unsafe)`, since that one IS a real unsafe escape hatch the lock makes safe in practice but the compiler can't verify. `updateNowPlayingInfo`'s `var info` → `let` (never mutated). `Posts.swift`'s `tryIncrementNod` — `try await db.runTransaction { ... }` → `_ = try await db.runTransaction { ... }`, making the intentional discard explicit (the function communicates its result via a captured `wasNew` var, not the transaction's own return value — confirmed by reading the full function, not just the warning). Two dead `?? .spotify` / `?? .appleMusic` fallbacks removed in `ArtistDetailSearchView.swift`/`AlbumDetailSearchView.swift` (`Song.sourceService` is non-optional, confirmed via `NodalongKit/.../Models/Song.swift`).
  **A real bug was introduced and caught by the verification build, not shipped:** the first draft of `ScreenMetrics.currentScreen` (the private helper backing `bounds`/`scale`) was missing its own `@MainActor` annotation — `UIApplication.shared.connectedScenes` and `UIWindowScene.screen` are themselves main-actor-isolated in the iOS 26 SDK, so this produced two brand-new concurrency warnings inside the new file. Caught by diffing the before/after warning sets (not assumed fixed from a green build alone — a build can succeed with new warnings) and fixed by adding `@MainActor` to `currentScreen` too.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + 3 rounds of isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` (each round's warning diff is what caught the two real bugs above, rather than trusting "BUILD SUCCEEDED" as sufficient). Final state: 44 raw deprecation-warning locations → 2 (both explained, unavoidable, unrelated to any behavior); the 4 bonus warnings → 0; zero new warnings anywhere in any touched file; bucket C's ~20 concurrency warnings byte-for-byte unchanged (confirmed via diff, not just "left alone in theory"). `swift test` in `NodalongKit/` — 143/143 pass. **On-device verification NOT done as part of this session** (this environment can't drive the actual app) — every change is either a pure value-substitution (`UIScreen.main`→`ScreenMetrics`, same screen/bounds/scale in this single-scene app) or a closure-signature migration with identical firing semantics, so risk is low, but the plan's own verification section calls out `NowPlayingView` (drag-to-dismiss, artwork sizing, scrubber, volume slider, light+dark mode), `PartyView`, Search's debounce/scope-switch behavior, NearMe's radius slider + scene-phase tick gating, and the Spotify connect flow as the surfaces worth a real device pass before considering this fully closed.

- **2026-08-14 — Round 9.38: mini player un-collapsible + broadcast button, artist page defaults to Discovery when Albums is empty, and the discography grid's real root cause finally found — a `LazyVGrid` under-rendering on first layout inside a `TabView(.page)` page, not a data-instability bug at all — build-green, installed on-device.**
  Three independent items in one pass. **(1) Mini player, per direct product decision:** `MainTabView`'s `.tabBarMinimizeBehavior` (previously `.onScrollDown` when something's playing, merging the bar into the tab bar row while scrolling) is now unconditionally `.never` — this was the confirmed source of real on-device visual glitches during that system-driven `.expanded`/`.inline` placement transition (the spinning disc briefly escaping its circular mask, documented at length in `MiniPlayerBar`'s own prior doc comments). Removed the now-dead `isKeyboardVisible` state + its two `.onReceive` handlers, which existed solely to force `.never` during that transition — moot once it's unconditionally `.never`. `MiniPlayerBar` simplified to one chrome (dropped `@Environment(\.tabViewBottomAccessoryPlacement)`/`isInline` and every `isInline ? … : …` ternary — always the floating pill now, never the stripped-down inline variant). **Dismiss (✕) removed entirely** — the bar is no longer dismissable from itself (stopping playback is via the transport controls, or `NowPlayingView`'s own "Leave" for a sync-follow session). Replaced with a **broadcast button** (`dot.radiowaves.left.and.right`, matching the icon already used for party/radio concepts elsewhere) that starts or reopens a listening party — the exact `partyVM.isActive ? reopen() : showStartParty=true` action that used to live as the `party.popper` toolbar button in `FeedView`, moved here per direct request ("can remove it from top of feed") since it's now reachable from every tab and anchored to "what's currently playing" rather than a nav bar disconnected from playback state. Hidden while `playerVM.followContext != nil` (tuned into someone else's broadcast — starting your own doesn't make sense there, and that state already shows its own live-indicator icon in the transport slot). `FeedView`'s leading toolbar button + its `showStartParty` state + `.sheet` are gone; `partyBanner` (the "return to your party" / "X is hosting" discovery banner) is untouched, still reads `partyVM` directly.
  **(2) Artist page discography — new bug report, fixed:** an artist with zero "album"-type releases (only singles/EPs/features) landed on a dead "No albums" `Albums` tab by default, with their real content one unlabeled tap away on `Discovery`. New `ArtistDetailSearchView.preferDiscoveryTabIfAlbumsEmpty()` — guards on `discographyTab == .albums` (never steers away from a tab the user already manually picked) `&& fullAlbums.isEmpty && !discoveryAlbums.isEmpty`, called after both `core` (Apple path) and `extras` resolve (`featuresOn`, part of `discoveryAlbums`, only lands with `extras` — each call is idempotent/harmless if the other hasn't landed yet) and once for the Spotify-suspended fallback branch. An artist with genuinely nothing in either tab still shows "No albums" as before.
  **(3) The REAL root cause of the long-running "discography page 1 shows fewer albums than it should, self-corrects after swiping to page 2 and back" bug — finally found, after Round 9.27 flagged it, left a diagnostic print in place, and it went unconfirmed for multiple sessions.** Investigated fresh this round with a live device console capture (`xcrun devicectl device process launch --console`, working this time since the device was actually unlocked) while the user reproduced the exact repro. The still-live `[Nodalong.Diag] DiscographyPageGrid.body` print (never removed since 9.27) showed `chunks` **byte-for-byte identical across many consecutive re-renders** — proving the underlying `fullAlbums` data was never unstable at all, directly contradicting the working theory every prior round had been operating under. That ruled out a data/fetch problem entirely and pointed at the RENDERING layer instead. Re-examined `DiscographyPageGrid`'s `LazyVGrid` (each page's 2-column album grid) in that light: it's nested inside a `TabView(.page)` page that itself has an externally-pinned frame (`maxPageHeight`, deliberately fixed regardless of content so the underlying UIKit paging mechanism never has to relayout mid-swipe — Round 9.22's own established design). `LazyVGrid` decides what to instantiate based on the space PROPOSED to it at first layout — a known class of SwiftUI bug where, nested inside a page whose true final size isn't yet settled at that first pass, a lazy grid's initial measurement can land on an ambiguous/stale proposal and simply not render everything it should, only catching up once forced to redo its layout by becoming visible again (a swipe away and back). This exactly matches the reported symptom in every dimension: dead space exactly where the missing cards belonged (the OUTER frame was already sized correctly for 4 items — only the grid's own rendering under-delivered), and "fixed" by any interaction that forces a fresh layout pass. **Fix:** replaced `LazyVGrid` with a fully eager, hand-rolled 2×2 `VStack`/`HStack` grid, indexing directly into the (always ≤4-item, by construction of `stride(by: 4)`) chunk array — a lazy container was never buying anything here given the hard-capped, small, known-shape content, and removing the laziness removes the entire timing window this bug lived in. Empty slots (an odd-count final page) render as a plain `Color.clear` filler, no tap target — matches the original grid's visual gap exactly. `cardContent`'s own already-fixed `cardWidth` (computed to exactly fit two columns + `rowGap`, matching what the old flexible-column `LazyVGrid` proposed to each column) needed no extra width modifier to line up identically. The `DiscographyGridView` ("See all" full page) was deliberately left as `LazyVGrid`, unchanged — it's a plain top-level vertically-scrolling grid with potentially dozens of albums, not nested inside a `TabView(.page)` page with an externally-pinned frame, so it doesn't share this specific risk profile and genuinely benefits from laziness there. The `[Nodalong.Diag] DiscographyPageGrid.body` print (finally) removed.
  Build green (`xcodegen generate` + full clean isolated-`derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`, both simulator and the real device target), zero new warnings in any touched file — same pre-existing categories already catalogued in this log (`UIScreen.main` deprecation, the pre-existing harmless nil-coalescing warning in `ArtistDetailSearchView`). Installed and launched on the user's physical device. **On-device confirmation for (1) and (2) still pending** as of this entry — the user's own screenshots directly informed (2) and (3) (confirmed the bug, confirmed my classification/tab-default fixes from Round 9.38's own earlier sibling work, then confirmed the LazyVGrid-under-render repro precisely via a fresh page-1→page-2→page-1 sequence) but hadn't yet re-checked the new build with the eager-grid fix at the time of this entry.

- **2026-08-14 — Round 9.37: `MarqueeText` — single-loop-per-focus made the default (continuous kept only for mini/full player), plus a self-healing watchdog for the "carousel interaction permanently kills the outer gate" bug — build-green, single-loop confirmed on-device via isolated repro; the underlying stuck-gate mechanism could not be reproduced via synthetic simulator touch, so it's fixed defensively rather than root-caused.**
  Direct follow-up to Round 9.36. User reported that touching Home's horizontal carousel stopped every marquee under the page's outer gate from ever looping again, and separately asked (a) how long the current gap between loops is and (b) whether marquee text could do just ONE loop per focus everywhere except the mini/full player, rather than looping forever.
  **Investigated the carousel bug empirically again** — rebuilt the temporary debug harness (`Features/Debug/MarqueeDebugView.swift` + a `-marqueeDebug` launch-argument bypass in `NodalongApp.swift`, both removed again once done) this time mirroring Home's REAL nested-scroll structure: an outer vertical `ScrollView` gated with `.gatesMarqueeToScrollSettle()`, containing a nested horizontal carousel `ScrollView` ALSO gated, plus sibling `MarqueeText`s outside the carousel to watch whether the OUTER gate breaks. Drove real touch input via `mcp__Claude_Code_iOS_Simulator__control` (`swipe`, then a more realistic multi-point `touch_path`) rather than reasoning from code alone. **Could not reproduce the stuck state** — in every attempt, `.onScrollPhaseChange` never fired a SINGLE non-idle phase transition at all (confirmed via a temporary print inside the gate's own callback), even though the carousel visibly scrolled — meaning synthetic simulator touch input doesn't drive SwiftUI's scroll-gesture recognizer through a realistic tracking→decelerating→idle sequence in this environment, so this path can't be used to pin down the exact internal mechanism. Given the user's own real-device report is specific and repeatable (drag the inner horizontal carousel → nothing on the page ever loops again), the most plausible explanation — consistent with a known class of SwiftUI issue — is that the touch gets "claimed" by the inner horizontal recognizer mid-tracking, so the OUTER vertical `ScrollView`'s own gesture cycle never receives the matching completion callback that would report `.idle` again, leaving `MarqueeScrollGate.isSettled` stuck `false` for the rest of the session.
  **Fix, since the exact mechanism couldn't be confirmed: made the gate structurally unable to stay stuck, rather than chasing the precise SwiftUI internals further.** `MarqueeScrollGate` (`MarqueeText.swift`) now arms a bounded watchdog (`Task`, 3s) the instant it observes ANY non-idle phase; if no further phase update arrives before the watchdog fires, it force-sets `isSettled = true` itself. A genuine still-in-progress scroll keeps resetting the watchdog via its own normal phase callbacks (each one cancels and re-arms it), so this only ever engages for the "abandoned mid-gesture, no more callbacks ever arrive" case — a real drag/fling/decelerate sequence never comes close to 3s of silence. Matches this whole component's established "fail safe" philosophy (worst case: a marquee starts ~3s later than ideal after a rare stuck gesture, never a permanent freeze).
  **Second, larger change — `repeats: Bool = false` added to `MarqueeText`, flipping the default from "loop forever" to "scroll through once per focus, then rest."** Implemented by extending `restart()`'s existing async pause-then-scroll `Task`: after `isScrolling = true` starts the pass, if `!repeats` a second `Task.sleep` for exactly `cycleSeconds` (the time one full traversal takes) is awaited, then `isScrolling = false` freezes the `TimelineView`. No separate "snap back to start" logic was needed — `xOffset(at:)` is periodic with period `travelDistance` (already established when this component was built: two side-by-side copies mean offset 0 and offset `travelDistance` render identically, copy 2 sitting exactly where copy 1 started), so freezing right at the seam is visually indistinguishable from resting at the untouched start position. Confirmed on-device via the same debug harness: outer sibling titles played their full pass once, then settled back to their original truncated text and stayed there — re-verified they don't restart on their own; a fresh "focus" event (the existing `restart()` triggers — coming back on screen, an ancestor scroll settling, `canAnimate` flipping true again) is what re-arms the next single pass, matching "only loop once when in focus" literally.
  **`repeats: true` set at the two exempted call sites** — `MiniPlayerBar.swift`'s title, and all three `NowPlayingView.swift` usages (title/artist/album) — preserving today's continuous-ticker behavior there exactly. Every other call site (Home's tiles/`AlbumCard`, Search's Albums carousel, `ArtistDetailSearchView`, Shelf, `PartyView`, `NearMeUserSheet`) picks up the new single-loop default with zero per-call-site changes, since none of them passed `repeats` explicitly before.
  **Answered the user's timing question directly rather than just fixing code:** the "gap" is the same `pause = 1.6s` value used both for the initial wait before the first scroll and (in `repeats: true` mode only, now that single-loop is the default everywhere else) the rest held between the end of one lap and the start of the next — unchanged by this round, still a per-instance-overridable parameter.
  Build green (`xcodegen generate` + full clean isolated-`derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), zero new warnings — same pre-existing categories as Round 9.36 (`PreferenceKey` Sendable, `UIScreen.main` deprecation, `onChange(of:perform:)` deprecation). Debug harness and all diagnostic prints fully removed post-verification. Installed and launched on the user's physical device (`xcrun devicectl`) for their own check. **Honest limitation, stated plainly:** the single-loop behavior and the timing answer are both directly confirmed (isolated repro + screenshots). The watchdog fix for the stuck-carousel-gate bug is NOT independently confirmed to resolve the user's exact real-device repro — it's a structurally-sound defensive fix for a bug whose precise trigger couldn't be reproduced in this environment; if the user still sees a stuck page after this, the watchdog's 3s bound means it should now self-recover rather than staying broken for the rest of the session, which is itself a useful diagnostic (does it recover after ~3s, or does it still never come back).

- **2026-08-14 — Round 9.36: `MarqueeText` ROOT-CAUSED FOR REAL — Round 9.35's on-screen-visibility gate silently pinned every marquee to `isVisible=false` forever, which is why NOTHING scrolled anywhere in the app despite five prior "fixed" rounds — found via an isolated on-device repro (temporary debug harness + `simctl` screenshots + targeted print diagnostics), fixed by swapping the hand-rolled `PreferenceKey` for `.onGeometryChange`, build-green.**
  User report: "none of the marquee text scrolls" — contradicting 9.35's own admission that the gating it added was "still needs the user's own eyes on their device," i.e. never actually confirmed working. Rather than read-and-guess a sixth time (this component's own history: 9.29 rewrite broke it, 9.31/9.32 found and fixed two DIFFERENT regressions from that rewrite, 9.33 rebuilt the loop timing, 9.35 added visibility+scroll-settle gating on top of a mechanism that had itself never been visually confirmed) — built an actual empirical repro. Added a temporary `#if`-free debug harness (`Features/Debug/MarqueeDebugView.swift`, wired behind a `-marqueeDebug` launch argument in `NodalongApp.swift`, both fully deleted once done) rendering 3 `MarqueeText`s with guaranteed overflow, with zero auth/app dependency, built for and installed on a real booted iOS 26.3 simulator, and took screenshots 5 seconds apart — confirmed the bug reproduces in total isolation, ruling out anything screen-specific.
  Added temporary `print()` diagnostics inside `restart()`/the visibility handler/the raw `GeometryReader` body and captured console output via `simctl launch --console-pty`. This is what actually found it: `restart()`'s own log showed `isVisible=false` FOREVER, for every instance, never once flipping true — pinning `canAnimate` false, so `TimelineView`'s `paused:` never released regardless of `overflow`/`isScrolling`. Drilling into why: the `MarqueeVisibilityKey` `PreferenceKey` (fed by a `.background(GeometryReader{...})` on the outer `ZStack`, read by a THIRD `.onPreferenceChange` chained after two OTHER preference-key readers on the same node) had its `onPreferenceChange` closure fire **exactly once per instance**, always with the key's `.zero` default — never the real value. Proven definitively by adding a print INSIDE the `GeometryReader`'s own closure (not just the `onPreferenceChange` handler): it correctly computed and set the true global rect (`(16, 274, 355, 24)` etc.) on **every** one of 5 layout passes, including the very FIRST one — yet `onPreferenceChange(MarqueeVisibilityKey.self)`'s handler only ever received the stale zero default, ONE time, and was never invoked again despite the underlying preference value genuinely being set to a different, correct value on that very same first pass. This is a real propagation failure specific to chaining a `.background(GeometryReader)`-fed preference key's `.onPreferenceChange` behind two OTHER `.onPreferenceChange` handlers (for keys fed from nested descendants, not a sibling `.background()`) on the same node — not a timing/re-fire/coalescing issue that a guard tweak would fix.
  **Fix:** replaced the `MarqueeVisibilityKey` `PreferenceKey` + its `.background(GeometryReader)` + its `.onPreferenceChange` entirely with `.onGeometryChange(for: CGRect.self, of: { $0.frame(in: .global) }) { rect in ... }` applied directly to the ZStack (iOS 18+ API, well within this app's 26.1 target) — a single, modern, purpose-built modifier that doesn't route through the multi-key preference-chain machinery at all. `MarqueeContainerWidthKey`/`MarqueeTextWidthKey` (the other two, PROVEN working via the same diagnostic pass — their `restart()` logs showed correct, repeatedly-updating `containerWidth`/`textWidth` values) were deliberately left untouched, since they aren't broken and touching working code isn't worth the risk. Re-verified on the same isolated harness: `restart()` now logs `isVisible=true`/`canAnimate=true` and `isScrolling=true` for all three instances, and `simctl` screenshots 2s apart show the text genuinely shifted left ("This Is A Very Long S" → "s A Very Long Song T") — real, confirmed motion, not just state flags.
  All diagnostic `print()`s and the debug harness (`MarqueeDebugView.swift`, the `NodalongApp.swift` launch-argument branch) were removed once confirmed; `MarqueeText.swift`'s own doc comment now records the root cause and why `onGeometryChange` was chosen, so a future session doesn't reintroduce the `PreferenceKey`-chain pattern for this. Build green (`xcodegen generate` + a full clean isolated-`derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), zero new warnings — the only warnings left in the file are the same pre-existing categories already catalogued elsewhere in this log (`PreferenceKey` Sendable, `UIScreen.main` deprecation, `onChange(of:perform:)` deprecation). **This was independently confirmed working on-device (via the isolated harness), unlike every prior round's "install succeeded, launch verification blocked" pattern** — but the harness only exercised the base scrolling mechanism itself (visibility + timing), not every real call site's own layout (e.g. whether a given screen's `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity)` wrapping is correct) — a real in-app pass across Home/Search/NowPlayingView/MiniPlayerBar/PartyView is still worth the user's own look to confirm the FULL effect, though the root mechanism that was silently blocking ALL of them is now fixed.

- **2026-08-14 — Round 9.35: `MarqueeText` extended to Home's tile titles (+ the shared `AlbumCard`/`AlbumsCarousel`, so Search's Albums carousel gets it too), and given real on-screen + scroll-settle gating — build-green, confirmed on-device via a clean launch.**
  Direct follow-up screenshot from Home: "can we get it to scroll on home page too? Id also like text to only scroll when it is in focus/ on screen. i scroll down the home and see a list of albums, then i stop and begin to read. THEN any text in the list of albums im viewing scrolls (if needed)." Two asks bundled together — extend marquee coverage to Home, and change WHEN marquee is allowed to animate, not just where it appears.
  **Coverage.** Converted the title `Text` in all six of Home's tile builders (`recentAlbumTile`, `friendsListeningTile`, `mostNoddedRow`, `topChartTile`, `madeForYouTile`, `recentlyAddedShelfTile`) to `MarqueeText`, plus the shared `AlbumCard` (`SearchView.swift`, used by both Home's "Recommended for You" rail via `AlbumsCarousel` and Search's own Albums carousel — one change covers both screens). `mostNoddedRow`'s title needed the same fix already established this session for `NearMe`'s join banner: it sat ahead of a trailing `Spacer()`, so it was claiming only its own natural (never-overflowing) width — replaced the `Spacer()` with `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)` directly on the title/artist `VStack`, giving `MarqueeText` real room to detect overflow against. Every other Home tile already sat inside a fixed `.frame(width: 140, alignment: .leading)`, so those were a straight drop-in — matches the pattern already proven in `ArtistDetailSearchView.albumCard`, confirmed by reading it first: `MarqueeText(text:font:color:)` with no explicit width needed, since the ambient `.frame(width:)` on the enclosing `VStack` already constrains it correctly.
  **Gating — the bigger, genuinely new piece.** `MarqueeText` previously animated any time it was mounted and overflowing, regardless of whether the user was actively scrolling past it or whether it was even on screen (Home's carousels are plain `ScrollView(.horizontal) { HStack {...} }`, not `LazyHStack` — every tile is a live, continuously-updating view the whole time the screen is mounted, clipped but never torn down). Added two independent, composable gates, both fail-safe (worst case: doesn't animate, never a crash or stuck state):
  1. **On-screen visibility** — new `MarqueeVisibilityKey` `PreferenceKey` publishes each instance's own `proxy.frame(in: .global)` via a `.background(GeometryReader{...})`; `onPreferenceChange` compares it against `UIScreen.main.bounds.intersects(rect)`. This falls out for free with no `LazyHStack` migration needed: a plain `ScrollView`'s content is genuinely translated as the user scrolls, so a descendant's `.global` frame shifts with it — the same mechanism classic scroll-offset/parallax tricks rely on — meaning an item scrolled off either edge (horizontally past a carousel's bounds, or vertically off Home's own scroll) correctly and automatically reports itself as not visible, with zero coordination needed between Home and the component.
  2. **Ancestor-scroll-idle** — new opt-in `\.isScrollSettled` environment key (default `true`, so every pre-existing call site — mini player, `NowPlayingView`, `PartyView`, `NearMeUserSheet`, `ArtistDetailSearchView`, `ShelfView` — is completely unaffected unless a screen explicitly opts in) + `MarqueeScrollGate` `ViewModifier` (`.gatesMarqueeToScrollSettle()`) tracking a `ScrollView`'s `ScrollPhase` via `.onScrollPhaseChange` (iOS 18+ API, well within this app's 26.1 deployment target) and republishing `newPhase == .idle` down the tree. Applied to Home's own outer vertical `ScrollView` AND to each of the 5 horizontal carousels that host a converted tile (`jumpBackInRail`, `friendsListeningRail`, `topChartsRail`, `madeForYouRail`, `recentlyAddedFromShelfRail`) plus the shared `AlbumsCarousel` — a nested environment write only overrides its own descendants, so applying it at multiple levels composes correctly with no coordination: dragging a horizontal carousel gates just that carousel's own tiles without needing to know about the outer vertical scroll's state, and vice versa. `mostNoddedList` (a plain `VStack`, not its own `ScrollView`) needed no separate gate — it inherits the outer environment value naturally.
  Both signals combine into `canAnimate = isVisible && isScrollSettled`, consulted in three places: `restart()` (won't even begin counting down the pre-scroll pause unless both are true — deliberately restarts the WHOLE "settle, then move" cycle from scratch once they become true again, rather than resuming mid-cycle, matching the user's own framing of "I stop, THEN it scrolls" as a fresh event, not a resume); the `TimelineView`'s `paused:` expression (defensive, redundant with `restart()`'s own gating via `isScrolling`, but consistent with the existing multi-condition style already used there for `scenePhase`); and implicitly via the `.onPreferenceChange`/`.onChange` handlers that re-trigger `restart()` whenever either signal flips.
  Build green (`xcodebuild`, no new files so no `xcodegen generate` needed), zero errors, no new warnings in either touched file. Installed and launched clean on-device — `[Nodalong.Diag]` console capture confirmed a normal cold-launch/snapshot-restore sequence with no crash or exception, though the actual on-screen animation behavior (does scrolling genuinely pause every visible marquee, does it resume correctly once settled, do off-screen carousel tiles stay still) still needs the user's own eyes on their device — a console log can confirm the app didn't crash, not that the motion looks and feels right.
