  **Scope note:** deliberately left ungated/unconverted: Search's own outer vertical `ScrollView` (only the shared `AlbumsCarousel` picked up gating, as a side effect of it being reused by Home — Search's Songs/Artists lists weren't touched), and every other screen with existing `MarqueeText` usage from earlier this session (mini player, full-screen player, Party, NearMe, artist/Shelf pages) — all keep their prior "always eligible once overflowing and mounted" behavior via the environment key's default, since none of those were part of this specific request and none of them sit in a fast-scrolling context the way Home's carousels do.

  User: "ive noticed that when tapping a person in the near me, their minified profile tab takes over the whole screen? And if i view full profile it too takes over the whole screen? and the subsequent taps into playlists from their profile takes over the full screen? Tab bar and mini player is never visible in these scenarios when it should be, seems like an edge case from earlier." An Explore agent traced the exact chain rather than guessing: `NearMeView` presents `NearMeUserSheet` via `.sheet(item: $selectedUser)` — a genuine modal, not part of `router.nearMePath` — and the sheet closure wrapped `NearMeUserSheet` in its OWN brand-new, disconnected `NavigationStack` with its own separate `.navigationDestination(for: UserProfile.self)`. "View Profile" was a plain `NavigationLink(value: user)` pushing onto THAT isolated stack, so `UserProfileView` (and anything reachable from it, e.g. its playlists — which already had a perfectly correct `.navigationDestination(for: Playlist.self)` registered on itself) stayed trapped under the sheet regardless of how deep the user navigated, since `MainTabView`'s tab bar/mini player live below/outside the sheet's presentation layer entirely — this is exactly the same architectural bug class already found and fixed for `NowPlayingView`/`PartyView` earlier this session (see the "tab bar accessible from every screen" plan), just not caught in NearMe's flow at the time.
  **Fix, same proven pattern, no new mechanism invented:** `NearMeUserSheet` gained `@EnvironmentObject private var router: AppRouter`; `viewProfileButton` changed from `NavigationLink(value: user) { ... }` to `Button { router.dismissThenPush(user) { dismiss() } } label: { ...identical label... }` — dismisses the sheet, then (after the established 0.35s beat) pushes `user` onto `router.nearMePath`, which already had `.navigationDestination(for: UserProfile.self)` registered from the router migration earlier this session — confirmed via direct read before writing any code, so no new destination registration was needed there. `UserProfileView` itself needed zero changes — confirmed (via the same Explore pass) it already has a working `.navigationDestination(for: Playlist.self)` and plain `NavigationLink(value: playlist)` rows; the only thing ever wrong was WHERE it was being pushed, not how it navigates once there. `NearMeView`'s `.sheet(item: $selectedUser)` closure was simplified from `NavigationStack { NearMeUserSheet(user:).navigationDestination(for: UserProfile.self) {...} }` down to a bare `NearMeUserSheet(user: user)` — confirmed via grep that the view has zero `.navigationTitle`/`.toolbar` dependency, so the wrapping stack was serving no purpose once its one destination registration became dead code.
  Build green, zero errors, no new warnings in either touched file. **On-device launch verification blocked again this round** by the same intermittent device-lock issue documented repeatedly earlier this session (`CoreDeviceError 10002`, "device was not, or could not be, unlocked") — install succeeded, launch did not run. Needs the user to confirm on their own unlocked device: tapping a NearMe avatar → "View Profile" now dismisses the sheet and pushes the full profile onto the NearMe tab's own stack (tab bar + mini player visible throughout), and tapping into a playlist from there behaves the same way, not trapped under any modal.

- **2026-08-14 — Round 9.33: continuous scroll rebuilt to pause between loops (not just before the first one); Similar Artists reverted from marquee to 2-line wrap.**
  User, after confirming Round 9.32's clipping fix: "when it finishes the loop of scroll it should pause for a few seconds before it starts again. otherwise it just endlessly loops." A genuinely endless scroll with no rest point reads as relentless — reasonable, matches how real tickers behave (settle, move, settle, move), not what was actually built (settle once, then scroll forever with no further rest).
  **Fix, in `xOffset(at:)`:** each period is now `cycleSeconds + pause` — `cycleSeconds` of continuous scroll (0 → `travelDistance`, wrapping seamlessly as before) followed by `pause` seconds held at the start position, then repeating. Computed via `elapsed.truncatingRemainder(dividingBy: periodSeconds)` and a guard on whether the resulting phase falls inside the scroll window or the rest window — still a pure function of wall-clock time relative to `scrollStartDate`, so the `TimelineView`/`\.scenePhase` safety properties from Round 9.29 are untouched.
  Separately, same session: "maybe for similar artists we dont scroll text? we just let the text overflow to the next line?" — the artist page's Similar Artists row (Round 9.30) reverted from `MarqueeText` back to a plain `Text` with `.lineLimit(2)`/`.multilineTextAlignment(.center)`. Good call, and consistent with this whole feature's own stated scope from Round 9.29: every OTHER marquee spot in the app is one prominent title shown alone; Similar Artists was the one place several sit side by side at once (4-6 avatars in a row), and several independently-scrolling names next to each other reads as busy rather than helpful — exactly the risk flagged (and accepted as a calculated risk) when this spot was first proposed.

Installed AND launched successfully this time (first clean launch confirmation since Round 9.29's rewrite began) — no crash, normal startup activity in the captured console. - **2026-08-14 — Round 9.32: `MarqueeText` regression #2 — the hidden text-width-measuring clone was leaking its full, unclamped width up into the outer container, letting titles bleed past their intended bounds all the way to the physical screen edges.**
  Direct follow-up, screenshots showing `NowPlayingView`'s title running flush against BOTH edges of the screen with no margin at all — ignoring the 24pt horizontal padding every other element on that screen respects. User: "why doesnt the text scale to fit in its container, and then if its too long you make it scroll in a loop" — precisely the intended behavior, so this was a real bug, not a misunderstanding of the design.
  **Root cause, confirmed by tracing the exact modifier chain against how SwiftUI actually composes `ZStack` sizing, not guessed:** Round 9.31's fix correctly stopped the OUTER `ZStack` from being frame-constrained, but left the HIDDEN clone used to measure the text's TRUE full width (`textWidth`, via `.fixedSize(horizontal: true).hidden().background(GeometryReader{...})`) with no frame/clip of its own. A `ZStack` sizes itself to the widest of its children — and an unconstrained hidden element still counts as a child for that purpose, `.hidden()` reserves layout space, it doesn't exempt a view from contributing to its parent's size. For a long title, that hidden clone's TRUE (very wide) natural size became the `ZStack`'s own reported size to its ancestor, which propagated outward past the 24pt padding the call site applied, all the way to the outermost hard `.frame(width: screen.width)` in `NowPlayingView`'s root — which is what actually did the clipping, instead of `MarqueeText`'s own intended bounds. The ORIGINAL (pre-Round-9.29) implementation never had this exposure: its one `fixedSize` element was always the SAME element that also carried the visible content's own frame/clip, never a separate unconstrained sibling.
  **Fix:** added the identical `.frame(width: containerWidth, alignment: alignment).clipped()` pair to the hidden `textWidth`-measuring clone that the visible content already had. Confirmed this doesn't break the measurement itself: `.background(GeometryReader{...})` is attached to the `Text` BEFORE the trailing frame/clip in the modifier chain, so it captures the text's TRUE `fixedSize` width at that point, unaffected by the LATER constraint — the frame/clip only stops that already-captured value from further leaking into the `ZStack`'s own reported size. Both hidden clones and the visible content are now three siblings the `ZStack` sizes against; only the very first one (the container-width probe, which must stay genuinely unconstrained to discover the real available space at all) is exempt.
  Build green, zero new errors, same pre-existing warning categories only. Installed successfully; **launch verification blocked by the locked device for a fifth consecutive attempt** — this fix, like the last one, is grounded in a fully-traced mechanism rather than a guess, but genuinely unconfirmed on a running app as of this entry. Two `MarqueeText` regressions found and fixed in immediate succession (Round 9.31: nothing rendered at all; Round 9.32: rendered but unbounded) is worth flagging honestly as a real cost of Round 9.29's from-scratch rewrite rather than a smaller, additive change — the original bounce implementation, whatever its animation felt like, never had either of these two failure modes.

- **2026-08-14 — Round 9.31: `MarqueeText` was fully broken — a real regression from Round 9.29's continuous-scroll rewrite, found and fixed by tracing the actual layout mechanics, not guessed.**
  User, once finally able to unlock and look: "the marquee text is broken everywhere, it doesnt even appear nevermind scroll" — with screenshots showing genuinely blank space where titles should be (`NowPlayingView`'s artist/album, the discography grid's album names) across multiple screens.
  **Root cause, confirmed by reading the exact modifier chain, not assumed:** Round 9.29's rewrite moved `.frame(width: containerWidth, alignment: alignment).clipped()` from wrapping only the VISIBLE content onto the OUTER `ZStack` — which also wraps the always-present, `.hidden()` measuring `Text` used to discover `containerWidth` in the first place. On the very first render `containerWidth` starts at `0` (`@State private var containerWidth: CGFloat = 0`), so that outer frame squeezed the measuring `Text` itself down to zero width — meaning it could never report its true available width via its `GeometryReader`-backed preference key, meaning `containerWidth` could never advance past `0`, meaning the `if containerWidth > 0 { ... }` branch holding ALL real content (both the static-text case and the scrolling case) never rendered at all. A hard, permanent chicken-and-egg deadlock, not a timing race — explains "doesn't even appear," not just "doesn't scroll." The original (pre-Round-9.29) bounce implementation never had this bug because it applied that same frame/clip pair only to the inner visible `Text`, never to the outer measuring `ZStack` — lost in the rewrite.
  **Fix:** moved `.frame(width: containerWidth, alignment: alignment).clipped()` back to wrapping only the visible content (now a `Group` containing either the scrolling `TimelineView` or the static fallback `Text`), leaving the outer `ZStack` — and critically, the hidden measuring `Text` inside it — completely unconstrained, exactly matching the original's structure. Build green, zero errors. Installed successfully; **launch verification blocked by the locked device yet again** (fourth consecutive attempt) — genuinely unconfirmed on a running app as of this entry, though this fix is grounded in a fully-traced, understood mechanism (not a guess) and the original structure it restores is known-good from before Round 9.29.
  Separately, the user's first screenshot also showed the full-player vinyl disc rendering upside-down — traced and confirmed this is NOT a bug: `SpinWhilePlaying`'s rotation is a pure function of absolute wall-clock time with no "start upright" anchoring, so a continuously-spinning disc will legitimately pass through every angle including ~180° at any given instant a screenshot happens to catch it — the play button in that screenshot showed "pause" (i.e. actively playing), consistent with genuine mid-spin motion, not a frozen/broken state. Flagged to the user as an observation, not fixed, pending confirmation of whether "always start upright on open" is actually wanted (a real design question, not implied by the report).

- **2026-08-13/14 — Round 9.29: discography grid arrow spacing fixed for real this time (I'd described the fix in a comment without actually writing the code), 6 prominent single-title spots converted to `MarqueeText`, then `MarqueeText` itself rebuilt for continuous seamless scrolling instead of bounce.**
  Follow-up feedback after Round 9.28's repositioning, in three parts. **(1) "midway between under the year and track numbers and bottom of container"** — a real gap: the previous "fix" changed `VStack(spacing: 8)` to `spacing: 12` but never actually added the bottom padding the accompanying comment claimed existed — caught by re-reading the code after the user's clarification, not assumed correct from the comment. Added the missing `.padding(.bottom, Self.pagePadding)` to `PageArrowsRow` in `DiscographyPageGrid`, giving genuine, roughly symmetric clearance above and below. Left Top Songs/Recent Tracks alone for this specific fix — their arrows sit outside any rounded-card boundary (unlike Discography's, which shares a card with the grid), so they were never at the same edge-crowding risk.
  **(2) "id also like every text that doesnt fit in its place to scroll"** — a broad ask (72 truncated `.lineLimit(1)` texts across 18 files, confirmed via `grep` before proposing anything). Asked via `AskUserQuestion` rather than guessing scope on something that size; user picked "prominent single titles only," explicitly excluding dense repeating lists (Search results, Queue/playlist track rows, Feed cards, Home's carousels) — matching real-world convention (Apple Music/Spotify only marquee the now-playing title, never list rows). Read surrounding context for every ambiguous call site before classifying header-vs-row. Converted 6: `ArtistDetailSearchView`'s `albumCard` and `DiscographyGridView` card titles (the direct visual trigger for the request — 4 albums on screen at once, not a long scroll, closer in spirit to "a few" than "a list"), `PartyView`'s header title and now-playing-hero artist name, `NowPlayingView`'s artist/album labels beneath the already-marqueed title, and Shelf's own `AlbumHero` title/artist. Deliberately left the artist-page hero name (`ArtistDetailSearchView.swift`) untouched despite being a single prominent title — its own doc comment records a five-round bug history for exactly that element, not worth the risk for a cosmetic addition. Two conversions needed an explicit width added (`PartyView`'s artist button, `ShelfView`'s `AlbumHero`) — `MarqueeText` measures overflow against whatever width its container negotiates, and a `Text`/`Button` with no `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity)` ancestor just hugs its own natural (never-overflowing) width, silently never triggering a scroll; the other 4 sites already sat inside a hard `.frame(width:)`/`.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)` and needed no change.
  **(3) "make all marquee text scroll continue... not bounce"** — `MarqueeText` fully rebuit. The original (built earlier this session for the mini player) paused, scrolled right, paused, scrolled back left, repeated — real bounce. New design: two copies of the text side by side with a fixed gap, animating continuously leftward; by the time the first copy scrolls fully out of view the second sits exactly where the first started, so the wrap reads as one continuous loop with no visible jump or reversal. **Deliberately did NOT implement this with `withAnimation(Animation.linear(...).repeatForever(autoreverses: false))`**, despite that being the obvious/simplest SwiftUI idiom for a continuous loop — a genuine standing `repeatForever` CAAnimation is the exact same failure category as the "purple screen on reopen" bug this session already root-caused and fixed once for `MiniPlayerBar`'s disc spin (the render server can't service a continuous animation commit while the screen is locked, and this app's `UIBackgroundModes: audio` keeps the process alive through that state). Used the same proven-safe `TimelineView(.animation(minimumInterval:paused:))` pattern instead — offset computed as a pure function of wall-clock time (elapsed since a captured `scrollStartDate`, modulo one loop's travel distance), gated on `\.scenePhase` internally so it freezes while backgrounded and resumes smoothly rather than jumping. Caught one subtle bug before shipping: an initial draft computed offset from absolute `date.timeIntervalSinceReferenceDate` directly, which would make scrolling *begin* at a random phase of its cycle (whatever the absolute wall-clock time happened to correspond to) instead of smoothly from position 0 — fixed by anchoring elapsed time to a `scrollStartDate` captured at the exact moment scrolling starts. Since `MarqueeText` is one shared component, this single-file change applies everywhere it's already used — the mini player's title, `NowPlayingView`'s title, and all 6 new conversions from part 2 — with no other files touched.
  Build green across all three parts, zero new errors, only pre-existing warning categories (`UIScreen.main` deprecation, `onChange(of:perform:)` deprecation, and two `PreferenceKey` Swift-6-concurrency warnings confirmed pre-existing since that struct code is byte-identical to the original file). Installed successfully both times; **launch verification blocked by the device being locked both attempts** (`CoreDeviceError 10002` — a device-state issue, not a code problem) — genuinely unconfirmed on-device as of this entry, unlike most of today's other rounds.

  Same evening, two more rounds of direct feedback. First: "the bottom left arrow overlaps with text, we need to make the arrow less prominant (ditch the border circle) and give a bit of top padding — arrows should also be present in all carousels." Investigated rather than guessing a new style from scratch: `SpotlightCarousel` already has exactly this — bare chevron glyphs, `textTertiary`-dimmed + `.disabled(...)` at either end, no background/border, positioned in its own header row above the paged content — and it had never been reported as a problem. Extracted that exact pattern into a new shared `Nodalong/Shared/UI/PageArrowsRow.swift` (`pageIndex: Binding<Int>`, `pageCount: Int`, optional `label:`) rather than restyling the floating-corner-overlay arrows from Round 9.25, which is what had caused the text-collision in the first place — a dedicated row has nothing to collide with, structurally, unlike an overlay sitting on top of content. `SpotlightCarousel` itself was left untouched (already correct, no reason to touch working, previously-fragile carousel code for a pure DRY win). Wired the new shared row into: `DiscographyPageGrid` (replacing the deleted overlay/`pageArrowButton`), `RecentTracksCarousel` (added above its existing dots, which stay), and the artist page's Top Songs section (`popularSection`) — which had NO `selection:` binding on its `TabView` at all before this, so a genuinely new `@State private var topSongsPage` had to be added first before arrows were even possible there. Free-scrolling (non-paged) rails elsewhere in the app — Home's Jump Back In/Friends Listening/Top Charts/etc., Search's Artist/Album carousels — were confirmed via `grep` to have no `TabView(.page)`/page-index concept at all, structurally different from what an arrow can drive; left out of this pass and flagged rather than silently skipped.
  **Second, more serious report, mid-investigation of the above:** a real repro sequence — "on first entry its 2 on page 1, then i turn to page 2 and its four and then back to 1 and its four? whats going on" — with three screenshots showing page 1 legitimately displaying 2 albums (GNX, Mr. Morale) on first view, then 4 different albums (including two NOT seen originally) after paging away and back. Traced `DiscographyPageGrid`'s data path in full: `albumList` is a `let` (immutable for the view's lifetime), `albums` (`ArtistDetailSearchView`'s `@State` source) is assigned exactly once per `loadData()` call (confirmed via `grep` — one assignment site per platform branch, guarded by `hasLoaded`), and `chunks`/`fullAlbums` are pure computed properties with no other state dependency — nothing in the code as read should be able to produce different chunking for the same page index within one view's lifetime. Rather than invent a fifth hypothesis blind (this file already had two real, confirmed-by-reading bugs fixed earlier this same evening — a padding/spacing drift and a row-count/album-count conversion bug — so "assume a third subtle math bug" felt like exactly the kind of guessing this session's own established discipline warns against), added a **temporary** `[Nodalong.Diag]` print inside `DiscographyPageGrid.body` logging `albumList.count` and every chunk's actual album names on each body evaluation — turns the next repro into real evidence (which specific hypothesis is true: `albumList` genuinely changing shape mid-session vs. a `chunks`/`pageIndex` desync vs. something else) instead of more static-code speculation.
  Build green, zero new errors/warnings (checked `PageArrowsRow.swift` specifically — clean; every other warning in touched files is the same pre-existing `UIScreen.main`/`onChange(of:perform:)`/nil-coalescing category catalogued repeatedly today). Installed and launched clean, no crash — the diagnostic print already fired once during normal background prefetch (Mac Miller's discography, `albumList.count=12`, 3 stable 4-album chunks), confirming the logging itself works correctly. **Two things genuinely unresolved and pending the user:** (1) visual confirmation the new arrow style reads as intended and no longer overlaps any text, across all three carousels it now appears in; (2) the discography data-instability bug needs the EXACT repro reproduced again (visit an artist's discography, note page 1's albums, page to page 2, page back to page 1, compare) with this build's console captured live, so the next diagnosis is evidence-based rather than a guess.

- **2026-08-13 — Round 9.26: discography grid's real self-sizing bug found — a page's raw ALBUM count was being passed straight into a function expecting ROW count.**
  Immediate follow-up to Round 9.25's fix, same evening. User screenshot: a 2-album page showed a huge dead gap below the single row, with the pagination arrow floating far down near "Similar artists" — and asked directly: "its a max of 4 per page? why is there lots of blank space? if 1/2 on the page we should [show] one row, if its 3/4 on the page we show 2 rows?" — correctly diagnosing the exact rule that was supposed to already be in place.
  **Root cause, confirmed by reading the code, not guessed:** `DiscographyPageGrid`'s `.onChange(of: pageIndex)` handler computed `displayedHeight = pageHeight(forRows: chunks[newIndex].count)` — but `chunks[newIndex].count` is the page's ALBUM count (e.g. 2), passed directly as if it were already a ROW count, into a function (`pageHeight(forRows:)`) that expects rows. For a 2-column grid, 2 albums is 1 row, not 2 — so a 2-album page was allocated height for a full 2-row (4-album) page. `init`'s own seeding logic already did this conversion correctly (`max(1, Int((Double(count)/2.0).rounded(.up)))`) for the FIRST page only; the `.onChange` handler reached on every subsequent page swap never got the same treatment — the same class of "two sites that need to agree, only one was updated" drift as Round 9.25's padding/spacing bug, just a different pair of sites.
  **Fix:** factored the album-count → row-count conversion into a single `private static func rowCount(forAlbumCount:)`, called from both `init` (replacing its inline duplicate of the same formula) and the `.onChange` handler (replacing the raw, unconverted `chunks[newIndex].count` pass-through) — the two sites can no longer independently drift, since there's only one implementation now.
  Build green, zero new warnings (same pre-existing `UIScreen.main`/nil-coalescing categories as every prior round today). Installed and launched clean, no crash. **On-device visual confirmation still pending** — needs the user to swipe to a partial (1–3 album) page and confirm the height now genuinely shrinks to one row instead of leaving the dead gap shown in the screenshot.

- **2026-08-13 — Round 9.25: discography grid top-row clipping actually root-caused (a real padding/spacing drift, not the density issue the earlier "cramped" fix addressed), plus pagination arrows added since the dots are hidden.**
  Direct on-device follow-up, three screenshots. User: "the discography carousel still doesnt scale properly. We need to have arrows in the bottom left/right to indicate its swipeable (pagination)." Screenshots showed the top row of the Albums-tab carousel with its discs visibly sliced by a hard flat line (only the bottom ~70% of each circle visible), while the SAME data rendered perfectly in the "See all" `DiscographyGridView` (a separate, unrelated grid implementation) a screenshot later — confirming this was specific to the swipeable carousel (`DiscographyPageGrid`), not a data or artwork-rendering problem.
  **Root cause, found by re-reading the file rather than guessing again:** the earlier same-day "kinda cramped" fix updated `discographyCardWidth`'s formula and introduced named `Self.rowGap`/`Self.pagePadding` constants (both `16`) for the HEIGHT formula (`pageHeight(forRows:)`) — but the `LazyVGrid`'s own actual body still used hardcoded `spacing: 12`/`.padding(12)`, never updated to match. The height formula and the real rendered geometry had silently drifted apart. Fixed by replacing both hardcoded `12`s with `Self.rowGap`/`Self.pagePadding` directly, so they can't diverge again.
  **Also hardened against the underlying ambiguity, not just the specific number mismatch:** added `.frame(height: maxPageHeight, alignment: .top)` to each page's own content (previously no explicit alignment — left it up to `TabView(.page)`'s own default placement of a page whose content may be shorter than the pinned frame, which the visual symptom is consistent with reading as center-anchored rather than top-anchored) and added `alignment: .top` to the outer `.frame(height: displayedHeight).clipped()` (was defaulting to `.center`, which would clip symmetrically from both edges during the height-change animation between pages rather than only trimming the bottom, which is the intended "page collapses toward the bottom" visual). Both are defensive, not just number-matching — they remove ambiguity about vertical placement that a future formula tweak could reintroduce even with the constants correctly in sync.
  **Pagination arrows** — small circular chevron buttons (`bgElevated` fill, hairline border, 28pt) added via `.overlay(alignment: .bottomLeading)`/`.bottomTrailing` on the grid, since `.tabViewStyle(.page(indexDisplayMode: .never))` (added in an earlier same-day round specifically to hide the default dots) left zero visual cue the grid is swipeable at all. Left arrow hidden (not just disabled) below page 0, right arrow hidden past the last page — nothing dead-looking to tap at either end. Tapping animates `pageIndex` directly, which the existing `.onChange(of: pageIndex)` height-transition handler already reacts to.
  Build green, zero new warnings (checked the exact touched file — every warning present is the same pre-existing `UIScreen.main` deprecation/nil-coalescing category already catalogued in §7). Installed and launched clean, no crash. **On-device visual confirmation that the clipping is actually gone (not just theoretically fixed) is still pending** — the fix is grounded in a confirmed, concrete code mismatch this time (not a guess), but this file has a documented history this session of fixes that looked right on paper and weren't; worth an explicit re-check rather than assuming it's resolved.

- **2026-08-13 — Round 9.24: vinyl artwork extended a second time — small groove-less vinyl for every track row app-wide, plus both players' main artwork now becomes a spinning circular vinyl disc in Vinyl mode — planned via `EnterPlanMode` again given the scope jump, build-green, confirmed on-device via a real capture.**
  Same-day follow-up to Round 9.23. User: "i think i want to move ahead with the small versions too... mini player is small vinyl, still spinning, same as normal. full screen player is big vinyl spinning... can we preview?" Built a live HTML/CSS preview first (small 44pt track rows with no visible grooves, a small spinning mini-player mock, a big spinning full-player mock, all with photo-style SVG-data-URI artwork, matching Round 9.23's approved preview style) — approved with no changes requested. Two `AskUserQuestion` rounds then confirmed the real scope: **every track row app-wide** (not just the three named examples — Feed, Search, Queue, Party, NearMe, playlists, Top Songs, Liked all included), and the full-screen player's artwork **genuinely changes shape** (rounded-square → circular), not just gaining a spin while staying square. Given this touches two of the session's most historically fragile files (`MiniPlayerBar`'s multi-round-fixed spin logic, `NowPlayingView`'s `UIScreen.main.bounds`-pinned layout with its own documented "purple screen" history) and roughly doubles the call-site count, re-entered `EnterPlanMode` rather than extending Round 9.23's implementation inline — the first Explore agent sent to catalog the remaining call sites failed on a session-limit error mid-run, so the investigation (fresh `grep` of every `ArtworkThumbnail(` site, direct reads of `MiniPlayerBar.swift`/`NowPlayingView.swift`'s `artworkSection`/`PartyView.swift`'s `nowPlayingHero`) was done directly rather than re-delegated.
  **The key design finding, not obvious from the first round:** the same preview surfaced that a 44pt "small vinyl" track row needs to look genuinely different from the already-shipped 44pt "Alternative versions" ALBUM row (Round 9.23) — same pixel size, but the album row shows grooves (approved as-is) while the new track row deliberately omits them (approved as "beautiful" without grooves) — so `VinylArtworkView` needed an explicit `showsGrooves: Bool` flag, not a size threshold, since two 44pt call sites now render differently on purpose.
  **Shipped:** `VinylArtworkView` gained `var showsGrooves: Bool = true` (default preserves every Round-9.23 `isAlbum` call site byte-for-byte). `ArtworkThumbnail` gained a second, independent `var isTrack: Bool = false` (deliberately not merged with `isAlbum` into one enum — would have meant re-touching the 12 already-wired sites for no benefit) — three-way branch: `isAlbum` → grooved vinyl, `isTrack` → groove-less vinyl, neither → unchanged `plainBody`. Wired `isTrack: true` at 13 track-level call sites: Home's "Most nodded this week" row, both playlist-detail screens' track rows (kept their existing `circular: true` alongside, for correct square-mode fallback), Party's queue row and add-sheet row (NOT its now-playing hero — see below), Search's song rows, the artist page's Top Songs row, Shelf's Liked list, `QueueView`'s Now Playing/Up Next rows, and NearMe's three track/post rows. **`MiniPlayerBar.swift` — exactly one line changed**: `ArtworkThumbnail(url: track.artwork, size: 44)` → `...isTrack: true)`. Deliberately did NOT touch anything else in that file — its `TimelineView`-based spin, outer `.clipShape(Circle())`, and the specific `.compositingGroup()` placement all have a documented multi-round regression history in this file's own doc comments; the one-line change means Square mode still falls through to the exact same `plainBody` render it always has (the existing outer clip already forces that into a circle today, independent of any vinyl concept), so "same as normal" for non-vinyl users was structurally guaranteed rather than manually verified.
  **New `Nodalong/Shared/UI/SpinWhilePlaying.swift`** — a `ViewModifier` extracting ONLY the rotation mechanism from `MiniPlayerBar`'s proven pattern (byte-identical `TimelineView(.animation(minimumInterval: 1/30, paused:))` + rotation formula + internal `\.scenePhase` gating, the same fix that closed the "purple screen on reopen" bug — this app's `UIBackgroundModes: audio` means a naive `.repeatForever` while locked wedges the render-server fence). Used ONLY by the two genuinely NEW spinning contexts (`NowPlayingView`, `PartyView`) — `MiniPlayerBar` was deliberately NOT refactored onto it, to keep zero risk to its already-correct, hard-won implementation.
  **`NowPlayingView.artworkSection`** rewritten as an explicit branch (unlike every `isTrack`/`isAlbum` site elsewhere, this one couldn't be a parameter flip — the SHAPE changes, and Square mode should show zero spin, unlike the mini player where spin predates and is independent of the vinyl concept): Vinyl mode renders `VinylArtworkView(...).spinWhilePlaying(playerVM.isPlaying).clipShape(Circle()).compositingGroup()`; Square mode keeps the byte-identical original `CachedArtworkImage`/`RoundedRectangle(24)` rendering. Critically, the trailing `.shadow`/`.frame(maxWidth:...)`/`.offset(y: dragOffset)`/`.gesture(dragDownGesture)` chain stays applied at the exact same outer position regardless of branch — nothing about the drag-to-dismiss gesture or this screen's `UIScreen.main.bounds`-pinned layout was touched, by construction, not just by care. **`PartyView.nowPlayingHero`** got the identical branch/spin treatment (same "big, what's playing" role) — new `heroIsPlaying` computed property reads `playerVM.isPlaying` for the host (real local playback) and `partyVM.remoteNowPlaying?.isPlaying ?? false` for a remote (who has no local playback state at all in this DJ model, per `currentTrack`'s existing doc comment — reused the already-published `remoteNowPlaying` RTDB-broadcast field rather than adding new plumbing).
  **`TrackCard.swift` (Feed cards, Profile's Recent Tracks/Spotlight carousels) confirmed and flagged as explicitly out of scope, not silently dropped** — it renders full-bleed (the entire 160pt card's background IS the artwork photo, text/gradient overlaid on top, `CachedArtworkImage` directly), with no discrete thumbnail region a disc could occupy without restructuring the card into a fundamentally different layout — a separate visual-redesign decision, not a parameter toggle like everywhere else in this round.
  **Settings label renamed** "Album Artwork" → "Artwork Style" in `ProfileView.swift` (now covers tracks too) — the underlying `AlbumArtworkStyle` enum, `albumArtworkStyleKey`, and `albumArtworkStyleRaw` property names were deliberately left as-is (purely internal, 25+ call sites now reference them, not worth a rename for a user-facing label change alone).
  Build green (`xcodegen generate`, 1 new file), zero new warnings — every warning line in a full clean-log grep of every touched file was a pre-existing category (`UIScreen.main` deprecation, `onChange(of:perform:)` deprecation) already catalogued in this file's own §7, none introduced by this round. Installed via `xcrun devicectl device install app`, launched via `device process launch --console --terminate-existing` with a captured console log — confirmed clean startup, no crash, normal album/artist prefetch and resolve activity. **On-device visual confirmation is still pending from the user** — specifically flagged in the plan as needing real device eyes, not just a clean launch, on: every track row listed above actually showing small groove-less vinyl; the mini player's `.inline` (scroll-collapsed) state specifically (given its own multi-round bug history this session); `NowPlayingView`'s drag-to-dismiss gesture in BOTH Square and Vinyl mode; and the Square-mode fallback genuinely reverting everything (including the mini player, which keeps spinning a plain circle — its own pre-existing, toggle-independent behavior — rather than stopping).

- **2026-08-13 — Round 9.23: vinyl-disc album artwork extended app-wide + made user-configurable (Square/Vinyl toggle in Profile) — planned via `EnterPlanMode`, scope narrowed via two `AskUserQuestion` rounds plus a live preview, build-green, confirmed on-device via a real capture.**

User asked to extend it to every album artwork context app-wide, with a way to turn it off. Entered plan mode given the scope (dozens of call sites, a new persisted setting, real design judgment about which sizes/contexts qualify).

Built a live HTML/CSS preview (the visualize tool, phone-mockup style already established this session) at both large-carousel and 44pt list-row sizes — first pass used flat mockup colors and the user pushed back ("i just want to see a preview, more concerned about the artwork on disc rather than the grooves"), so the preview was rebuilt with a photo-like SVG data-URI cover cropped into the disc's label instead of a flat swatch (external image CDNs are blocked by the widget sandbox's CSP, so a same-origin `data:` URI was the only way to show something photo-realistic) — approved ("beautiful", "as long as the artwork is in there great"), which settled the open 44pt question: **include it**.

Confirmed `TrackCard` and `NowPlayingView` bypass `ArtworkThumbnail` entirely (`CachedArtworkImage` directly, full-bleed/no-shape) — architecturally separate, correctly out of scope rather than an oversight. Found and catalogued 12 genuinely album-shaped call sites (vs. one — `HomeView.swift`'s `topChartTile` — that looked similar but is `Song`-typed, correctly excluded) across Home (Jump Back In, Friends listening, Recently Added, a hero header), Search (Albums carousel, `AlbumDetailSearchView`'s hero + its "Alternative versions" row), the artist page's own `DiscographyGridView` ("See all" grid), Shelf (album hero, `AlbumHero`, `AlbumTileView` grid cell), and `ResolvingAlbumView`'s placeholder hero. **Flagged a real pre-existing inconsistency found during this pass**: the artist page's discography carousel already rendered vinyl (hardcoded, no toggle) while its own "See all" grid — the same underlying data — rendered plain squares; fixed as part of this work rather than left as a second, separate bug.
  **Shipped, matching the plan exactly:** new `AlbumArtworkStyle` enum (`Nodalong/Shared/Theme/AlbumArtworkStyle.swift`, mirrors the existing `AppearanceMode` pattern precisely — raw-`String` enum + module-level `let albumArtworkStyleKey`) — `.square`/`.vinyl`, **default `.vinyl`** (a deliberate choice, not the conservative "default to old behavior" — vinyl is already shipped/loved on one screen, and "make every album artwork resemble the vinyl design" reads as wanting it as the norm with Square as the explicit opt-out). New `Nodalong/Shared/UI/VinylArtworkView.swift` — the existing `vinylArtwork` ZStack composition (radial-gradient disc, 2 groove rings, inner `ArtworkThumbnail(circular: true)` label, spindle dot) extracted verbatim into a standalone `View`, math unchanged (disc = 88% of cell, label = 44% of disc). `ArtworkThumbnail` gained `var isAlbum: Bool = false` + `@AppStorage(albumArtworkStyleKey)`, branching to `VinylArtworkView` only when both `isAlbum` is true and the setting is `.vinyl` — default `false` means all 38 pre-existing call sites are untouched unless explicitly opted in, and `VinylArtworkView`'s own inner artwork call deliberately leaves `isAlbum` at its default to avoid infinite recursion. `isAlbum: true` wired onto exactly the 12 confirmed sites (a one-line addition each, no other changes). `ArtistDetailSearchView.albumCard` now calls `ArtworkThumbnail(..., isAlbum: true)` instead of its own local `vinylArtwork` — the private function was deleted entirely, its logic now living solely in the shared `VinylArtworkView` — which is what makes the artist page's discography properly toggle-respecting for the first time, and simultaneously fixes the "See all" grid inconsistency found above (also wired to `isAlbum: true`). New `ProfileView.albumArtworkSection` — identical segmented-`Picker` pattern to `appearanceSection`, placed directly beneath it. **No root-level wiring needed** (unlike `AppearanceMode`, which needs `.preferredColorScheme` applied once at `NodalongApp`'s root) — `@AppStorage` inside `ArtworkThumbnail` reacts per-instance, reactively, wherever it's mounted.

Installed via `xcrun devicectl device install app`, launched via `device process launch --console --terminate-existing` with a captured console log — confirmed clean startup, no crash, normal artist-detail/cache activity in the log. **On-device visual confirmation of the actual vinyl-vs-square rendering across all 12 sites, and of the toggle itself, is still pending from the user** — the build/install/launch/no-crash cycle is done and gives real confidence nothing is structurally broken, but hasn't yet been visually walked through screen-by-screen on the device.

- **2026-08-13 — Round 9.22: tab bar made reachable from every screen (`AppRouter`, planned + approved before implementing), and the artist-page discography redesigned into a self-sizing 2×2 vinyl-record grid — build-green throughout, both confirmed on-device via real captures.**
  **Tab-bar-accessible navigation.** User surfaced the bug directly (screenshots) and asked "can we explain why this happens" before any fix: tapping an artist/album name from the full-screen player, an artist name from the party screen, or "View Playlist" from the party-ended summary, opened the destination as a `.sheet` stacked on top of a `.fullScreenCover` — two presentation layers below `MainTabView`, which owns the tab bar and mini player, so all three left the user with no way back to a tab or to see what's playing without fully dismissing first. User's rule, stated directly: **the tab bar should be visible from every screen** — and explicitly required planning + approval before any code changed ("lets plan it out... dont make any code changes before i approve"), so this went through `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode` properly rather than being fixed inline. **Architecture:** new `AppRouter` (`Nodalong/Features/Main/AppRouter.swift`, `@StateObject` on `MainTabView` — resets on sign-in like `partyVM`/`homeVM` already do) owns `selectedTab` (was private `@State` on `MainTabView`) plus one `NavigationPath` per tab (Home/Feed/NearMe/Shelf/Search — NearMe previously had no programmatic path at all). Its `dismissThenPush(_:dismiss:)` captures the currently-selected tab up front (so a fast tab switch mid-animation can't steal the push), calls the passed-in `dismiss` closure, waits the same 0.35s the existing Queue→Party cover-swap already established as the safe minimum for a dismiss animation to finish, then appends the destination onto that captured tab's path. New `PendingArtistResolve`/`PendingAlbumResolve` marker structs (`Nodalong/Shared/PendingTrackResolve.swift`, mirroring the app's own established `PendingArtistLookup`/`PendingCatalogArtistLookup` pattern) plus shared `resolvePendingArtists`/`resolvePendingAlbum` functions — extracted from `NowPlayingView`'s old private resolve methods so `PartyView`'s artist tap gets the SAME precise MusicKit-relationship + prefetch-cache resolve path `NowPlayingView` already had, instead of its previous blind `searchAll` fuzzy fallback. Migrated: `MainTabView` (`TabView(selection: $router.selectedTab)`, `.environmentObject(router)`), `HomeView`/`SearchView`/`ShelfView`/`FeedView` (private `path` → `router.xPath`, each also gained whichever `.navigationDestination(for:)` registrations it was missing — Feed had NONE of `ArtistResult`/`AlbumResult`/`Playlist`/the two Pending types before this), `NearMeView` (built out from scratch — previously a bare pathless `NavigationStack { }` with no `playerVM`/`musicService`/`spotifyService` env objects at all), `NowPlayingView` and `PartyView` (both lost their own `NavTarget` enum/`.sheet(item:)` machinery entirely, replaced by `router.dismissThenPush(...)` calls). **Accepted, stated trade-off:** the old instant-sheet-on-top-of-the-still-visible-player UX for `NowPlayingView`'s artist/album tap is gone — the cover now has to finish dismissing (~0.35s) before anything, even the placeholder, can appear. Framed to the user as unavoidable, not a bug: there's no way to have both "zero-latency sheet" and "tab bar still reachable," since reachability requires actually leaving the modal stack first. For `PartyView`'s artist tap this is a strict improvement, not a trade-off — it previously did a fully blocking network resolve before showing anything.
  Build green (`xcodegen generate`, 2 new files), installed via `xcrun devicectl device install app`, launched via `xcrun devicectl device process launch --console --terminate-existing` with a captured console log — confirmed clean startup, no crash, no error-level log lines. **On-device interactive confirmation (tapping through from each of the 5 tabs specifically — the part a Home-only test pass would miss) is still pending from the user** — the build/install/launch/no-crash cycle is done and gives real confidence nothing is structurally broken, but "does the tab bar actually stay visible and reachable after the artist page pushes, from Feed/NearMe/Shelf/Search as well as Home" hasn't been explicitly confirmed yet.
  **Discography redesign, three linked rounds of direct feedback, all shipped and confirmed on real artwork.** Follow-up to Round 9.21's track-row work — user asked to change how albums/singles/EPs are shown on the artist page. (1) **2×2 grid, self-sizing.** The outer Albums/Discovery switch became a plain button toggle (deliberately NOT a second swipeable `TabView` — nesting two swipeable `.page`-style TabViews makes horizontal-swipe resolution ambiguous between "next grid page" and "switch tabs," so only the inner grid pages swipe now); the inner content is a new `DiscographyPageGrid` (private struct) rendering albums 4-per-page in a `LazyVGrid`. First cut used a flat always-2-rows-tall container — user caught it immediately via screenshot (a 1-album page left a large dead gap below it) and pointed straight at the existing fix for exactly this shape of problem: "think about how the spotlight adapts to its size." Rebuilt `DiscographyPageGrid` around `SpotlightCarousel`'s already-proven two-layer technique — the inner `TabView`'s own frame is PINNED to a fixed max height (2 full rows) so the underlying UIKit paging mechanism never has to relayout mid-swipe (animating that frame directly was tried once before this session, on the mini player's own spin fix, and caused real stutter — deliberately not repeated here), while a separate OUTER frame is what actually animates, `.clipped()` down to whatever height the CURRENT page needs via `.onChange(of: pageIndex)`. (2) **Page dots hidden** — `.tabViewStyle(.page(indexDisplayMode: .never))` on both the Top Songs pagination and the new discography grid, per direct request ("make the page number dots... further away and less visible... can we hide"). (3) **"Singles & EPs" renamed "Discovery," merged with the standalone "Features on" section** (Apple Music's "Appears On" data) into one combined tab, deduped by album id (`discoveryAlbums` computed property) — `featuresOnSection` removed entirely as its own section. (4) **Vinyl-disc artwork**, the biggest visual swing — user's own idea: "instead of a square rectangle shape do a black vinyl disc (render a circle with an inner circle of the album cover) so it looks like a vinyl," then explicitly asked for the real album art in place of solid mockup colors once the shape was approved. Confirmed via an HTML/CSS mockup preview (a phone-mockup device frame using the app's real hex values, the established pattern for previewing native UI this session) before writing any Swift. Shipped as `vinylArtwork(url:size:)` — a `RadialGradient`-shaded black disc, 2 faint concentric groove rings, the real album cover as a smaller inner circle via `ArtworkThumbnail(..., circular: true)` (reuses the existing decoded-image cache/blur-up/failure-state chain — nothing new to load or cache), and a tiny spindle-hole dot at center. Confirmed on-device with a real screenshot showing genuine album covers (not placeholder colors) rendering correctly inside the discs.
  **Immediate follow-up, same round: "great idea but kinda cramped."** The discs were rendered at the FULL width of their grid cell with only a 12pt gap between the two per row — read as visually tight, and a pink sliver visible at the top of one card in the screenshot raised (unconfirmed) suspicion of a marginal height-clipping issue on top of the density complaint. Fixed three things together: widened the grid's own spacing and the page's outer padding (12pt → 16pt each, `discographyCardWidth`'s formula updated to match); the disc itself now renders at 88% of its allotted cell width, centered within the still-full-size `.frame` (so `DiscographyPageGrid`'s height math, keyed off the outer `size`, doesn't need to change) — this is what actually creates visible breathing room between two adjacent discs; groove rings reduced from 3 to 2 (spread further apart, opacity lowered further) for a calmer, less busy look at this size. Also bumped the per-row title/caption height buffer 48→56pt and pulled the row-gap/page-padding values out into named constants shared between the height formula and the grid's own actual spacing — specifically so the two can't silently drift apart again the way the first pass's inline `12`s did, and to rule out the apparent clipping being a real (if marginal) height-estimate shortfall rather than pure density. Build green, installed, launched clean (no crash, no error lines in the captured console). **On-device visual confirmation of this specific spacing pass is still pending** — awaiting the user's next look at the grid to confirm it reads as intended now.

- **2026-08-12 — Round 9.21: track-row redesign (album drops artist line, playlist drops number for circular artwork) shipped clean; mini-player `.inline` collapse investigation — real progress made, one regression caught and reverted, one residual issue accepted rather than chased further blind.**
  **Track-row redesign, planned via `EnterPlanMode` (user explicitly asked for "planning mode"), confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` to apply to both screens per context, build-green, launched clean.** Two screenshots (Hunky Dory album, Heavy Rotation playlist) showed the same issue from two angles: album rows repeated the artist name on every track even though the whole screen already establishes it (11 "David Bowie"s on one page); playlist rows showed a bare number with no artwork at all. **Album rows** — `AlbumDetailSearchView.albumTrackRow` and Shelf's own `AlbumDetailView.albumTrackRow` (the two screens kept deliberately visually unified since an earlier round) both dropped the `Text(track.artist)` line, keeping the track-number column (still meaningful — real album position). **Playlist rows** — `PlaylistDetailSearchView.playlistTrackRow` (catalog "Made For You" mixes) and `PlaylistDetailView.trackRow` (user-created Nodalong playlists) both replaced/upgraded to a circular `ArtworkThumbnail(..., circular: true)` + a matching stroke-border overlay, mirroring `MiniPlayerBar`'s exact circular-artwork treatment minus the spin (static rows, not the actively-playing bar) — `PlaylistDetailSearchView`'s row dropped its `number` parameter entirely (unused once the number column is gone); `PlaylistDetailView`'s row already had square artwork and no number, so only needed the circular clip added. Title/artist both kept on playlist rows (still meaningful — a playlist spans multiple artists, unlike an album). Confirmed on-device via a real launch, no crash.
  **Mini-player `.inline` (tab-bar-merged, scroll-collapsed) state — a real, multi-round investigation, not a one-shot fix.** User reported (with screenshots) the collapsed pill was genuinely broken: colorless, artwork centered oddly, play button fully off-screen, visible stutter. **Root cause of the two most severe symptoms, confirmed by re-reading the code against the screenshots:** the `.inline` placement's content (title/artist `VStack` with `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity)`, a 36pt play button) was built assuming roughly the same width as `.expanded`'s floating pill, but the system's actual `.inline` accessory slot (next to the Home/Search tab icons) is narrower — the play button was being pushed off the visible area, and with no explicit alignment, the artwork rendered centered in whatever space was actually granted rather than hugging the leading edge. **First attempt — stripped `.inline` to artwork-only** (matching Apple Music's own convention for this slot): built, verified no crash, but the user explicitly rejected it on testing — they want title/artist/play visible in the collapsed state, not simplified away, and even artwork-alone still drifted off-center and outside its container, disproving the "too much content" hypothesis entirely. **Second attempt, informed by that correction — restored title/artist/play/dismiss-gating exactly as before, added explicit `alignment: .leading` on the outer `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, ...)` (SwiftUI's default is center, which is what was producing the "dead center" symptom), and added `.compositingGroup()` after the artwork's rotation+clips+border** (a standard SwiftUI technique: flattens content-with-effects into one rasterized layer so an external animated transform — here, the system's placement-change transition — can't desync the clip from the rotated content mid-flight). **Confirmed genuinely better on-device** ("overall much better") — color, layout, and button visibility all fixed; the one remaining issue was the artwork still visibly drifting outside its circular boundary while actively spinning (not just during the placement transition). **Third attempt — moved `.compositingGroup()` to sit between the rotation and the outer clip instead of after everything** (the textbook-"more correct" ordering per how compositing groups are documented to interact with masks) **and added a second one on the outer chain too — this made things WORSE on-device**, regressing back toward the original failure (missing play button, colorless/garbled pill), confirmed via a fresh screenshot. **Reverted immediately to the confirmed-better second-attempt state** rather than layer a fourth guess on top of a regression — this environment has no interactive/visual debugging access for this system-level tab-bar-accessory API, and two consecutive blind code-reasoning attempts (one neutral-to-positive, one a clear regression) is the signal to stop guessing, not keep pushing. **Accepted, stated plainly: the artwork's residual drift while spinning is NOT fully resolved** — left as a known, lower-priority cosmetic issue. If picked up again, this needs either a way to visually inspect the live render (screen recording, not just static screenshots — the user's own report distinguished "during the scroll transition" from "while just spinning," which static screenshots can't fully disambiguate) or accepting that `TimelineView`-driven rotation composited under a system-controlled external animation (the `.tabViewBottomAccessoryPlacement` transition) may have a real, non-trivial SwiftUI/Core Animation interaction that a config-level fix (frame/alignment/compositingGroup placement) can't fully close — don't re-attempt more compositingGroup reordering guesses without new evidence.
  **False-alarm follow-up, worth recording as a process note:** immediately after the revert, the user reported "still broken" with a screenshot showing the exact regressed symptoms (missing play button, colorless artwork) — reading the file back confirmed the revert HAD landed correctly and matched the confirmed-good state byte-for-byte, so no further code change was made. Asked the user to force-quit (not just background) and cleanly reopen the app rather than guess again; this was a stale-build/timing artifact — the screenshot was taken before the fresh install/relaunch had actually taken over — and a clean reopen confirmed the reverted build is genuinely correct ("Looks good"). **Lesson**: when a bug report arrives seconds after a fresh install+relaunch and shows symptoms matching an OLDER build rather than the just-verified one, verify the code first before assuming a new regression — re-reading the file cost nothing and avoided a wasted fourth guess.

- **2026-08-12 — Round 9.20: load-time overhaul Batch B (album/artist tap-to-open specifically) — artist top-songs pagination deferred to background, browse-time prefetch extended across 6 surfaces, disk-persisted `MusicDetailCache`, currently-playing-track prefetch, blur-up placeholders, zoom transitions, album skeleton row-count fix — build-green throughout, every piece confirmed on-device via real captures, one real bug found and fixed mid-session.**
  Direct follow-up to Round 9.19: user's specific complaint was that album/artist tap-to-open still felt slow "from both search and tapping artist name when in album view mode," despite Batch A's Home-load wins. Same discipline as 9.19 — every claim below is grounded in a real device capture, not assumed.
  **Artist pagination deferred (the real fix for the artist-page half of the complaint).** Fresh device capture (3 separate artist opens) showed `paginatedTopSongs`'s "2 extra batches" eating **55–74% of `getArtistCore`'s total time** — 507–1104ms of 925–1488ms — even though a screen only needs ~10 songs to render anything real. `AppleMusicService.getArtistCore` now returns the first relationship page only (one round trip, no pagination); new `getArtistTopSongsFull(artistId:limit:)` does its own lighter `.topSongs`-only fetch and pages to 30 in the **background**, called from `ArtistDetailSearchView.loadData()` right after core renders — `topTracks` (and `MusicDetailCache`, via new `updateTopTracks`) top up once it lands, `trackPages` re-paginates reactively with no other UI change. **Confirmed via a second real capture**: `getArtistCore` dropped to 430–675ms across three fresh artists (from 925–1488ms), with the deferred pagination cost (510–611ms) now off the critical path entirely.
  **Blur-up placeholders for hero artwork.** Root cause, found by tracing `ArtworkURL`'s own bucket table (160/320/600px): a 110pt artist-carousel tile buckets to 320px, but the artist hero (screen-width) buckets to 600px — different bucket, different `ArtworkMemoryCache` key, so the big photo could never reuse what the just-tapped tile had already decoded. (Album tiles at 140pt already bucket to 600px too, same as the album hero, so this specific gap was artist-only — verified by checking every Home rail's tile size before claiming the album path needed the same fix.) `CachedArtworkImage` gained an optional `lowResURLString` param — checked synchronously in `init` alongside the primary URL; a hit renders scaled-up-and-blurred immediately instead of a blank shimmer, cross-fading to the real image once it loads. `ArtworkThumbnail` gained a `showsLowResPlaceholder` flag (forces the 160px bucket as the placeholder candidate — the size most likely already resident from ANYWHERE in the app) wired into the album hero; the artist hero (already calling `CachedArtworkImage` directly) passes it inline.
  **Zoom transitions** (`.navigationTransition(.zoom(sourceID:in:))`, iOS 26+) for the tapped tile's artwork to visually expand into the destination screen — wired for `AlbumsCarousel`/`ArtistsCarousel`'s `NavigationLink`+`.matchedTransitionSource` (both gained an optional `namespace: Namespace.ID?` param, `nil`-safe for callers that haven't opted in) and the corresponding `.navigationDestination` registrations on `SearchView`/`HomeView`. Confirmed on-device, user-approved ("looks good") before moving to Artist.
  **Browse-time prefetch — the core "preload trick" of this round, extended across six surfaces per direct request ("prefetching should be extended in most places where it makes sense").** All route through the SAME `MusicDetailCache.tracks(albumId:)`/`.core(artistId:)` closures the real detail screens already call, so a genuine tap either lands on a completed hit or joins an in-flight fetch — never a duplicate request. Bounded to the first 6 of whatever's on screen (not the full result set) at every site: (1) `SearchView` — artist/album results, keyed `.task(id:)` on the result ids so it re-fires only when results genuinely change; (2) `HomeView` — first 2 Recommended-for-You shelves × 6 albums; (3) `ArtistDetailSearchView` — its OWN Discography, Features On, and Similar Artists carousels, fired the instant `loadData()`'s core/extras land, so browsing INTO an artist page warms what you'd tap NEXT (chained one level deep). (4) Album-header artist-name tap — new `MusicDetailCache.resolveArtistByName(_:fetch:)` (name-keyed, a deliberately separate identity space from the id-keyed `artistCore`, since this flow only ever has a display string, not a resolved id); `AlbumDetailSearchView`'s own `.task` speculatively warms it the instant the album's tracks start loading (the artist name is already known then), and the `PendingArtistLookup` destination's real resolve closure reads the identical cache key.
  **`ResolvingArtistView` rebuilt to match the real artist hero's shape** (340pt full-bleed rectangle, top-of-screen bleed, bottom-left gradient+name) instead of a small centered circle+spinner — this was the actual "jarring" complaint behind "tapping an artist name in an album selected from Home": the old placeholder and the real `ArtistDetailSearchView.heroSection` were two completely different-shaped layouts, so even a fast resolve still read as an abrupt jump-cut on the swap. Deliberately duplicated rather than extracting `heroSection` into a shared component — that view has a five-round bug history documented in its own doc comment, and this only needs to approximate its look. Added `.transition(.opacity)` + `withAnimation` on the resolve→loaded swap for a cross-fade instead of the old un-animated snap.
  **Disk-persisted `MusicDetailCache`** — was session-only (a plain in-memory actor), meaning every cold launch re-fetched every artist/album from scratch even for content already viewed. Added `Codable` to `ArtistCoreDetails`/`ArtistExtraDetails` (safe now that `AlbumResult`/`ArtistResult`/`Song` all have tolerant decoders from Round 9.19's Phase 0), a private `Snapshot` struct bundling all four dictionaries (`albumTracks`/`artistCore`/`artistExtras`/`artistByName`), persisted via the existing `SnapshotStore` under a **fixed pseudo-uid** (`"_catalog"`) rather than the real uid — deliberate, since catalog data is user-independent and this must survive a sign-out/sign-in of a different account; safe from the store's own clear-race concern because nothing anywhere calls `SnapshotStore.clear(uid: "_catalog")`. Persisted on every successful cache-populating write (fire-and-forget, already off-main); capped at 300 entries per dictionary via a crude "drop the overflow" trim on write — no real LRU, flagged honestly as a known limitation rather than over-built for this app's realistic volume. **Confirmed with real evidence, not assumed**: a genuine app relaunch (`--terminate-existing`) logged `MusicDetailCache: restored 15 albums, 4 artists from disk`, immediately followed by a burst of `cache hit` lines for album tracks — the disk restore and the browse-time prefetch compounding correctly on a cold process.
  **Real bug found and fixed mid-session — a genuine on-device regression, not a hypothetical.** First attempt at "prefetch the currently-playing track's artist/album" hooked a `.task(id: track.uri)` onto `MiniPlayerBar`. Device capture showed 344 calls to `resolveDetailedSong` in one short session — including many actual fresh `total XXXms` resolves (not just cheap memo hits) alternating between two different queued tracks, thrashing the single-entry memo. Root cause: `MiniPlayerBar`'s own doc comment already documents that the system tears down and recreates it during its `.inline`/`.expanded` scroll-driven placement transitions — a `.task(id:)` restarts on every such recreation regardless of whether the `id` value actually changed, so ordinary scrolling was refiring the prefetch dozens of times. **Fixed by moving the trigger out of any View entirely** — `PlayerViewModel.nowPlaying` is now `{ didSet { guard nowPlaying?.uri != oldValue?.uri else { return }; prefetchDetailsForCurrentTrack() } }`, hooked at the one stable point every `nowPlaying` assignment already flows through, immune to any view's teardown/recreation. New `configure(musicService:)` (mirrors the existing `configure(spotifyService:)` pattern) wires a weak `AppleMusicService` reference from `ContentView`. **Re-verified after the fix**: a fresh capture showed zero spam. New `PlayerViewModel.prefetchedAlbumForUri`/`prefetchedArtistsForUri` (tuple-typed, keyed by uri to guard against a stale previous-track's resolve being mistaken for the current one) are read first by `NowPlayingView.resolveAlbumResult`/`resolveArtistResults` before falling through to a cold resolve. **Lesson for future prefetch work in this codebase**: never hang a `.task` off a view already documented as being torn down/recreated by the system (search for existing "the system moves this between states" style comments first) — hook the underlying state mutation instead.
  **Album skeleton row-count fix** — user's follow-up report ("still a load/pop in when viewing album and its tracks") after the artist fix landed well ("loading artists is much improved"). Root c30use: `trackListCard`'s loading skeleton always rendered exactly 5 shimmer rows regardless of the real track count, while `album.totalTracks` (part of the already-known `AlbumResult` passed in from the tap, no network needed) was sitting right there unused — so even with the earlier staggered-row cross-fade animation, the card visibly grew/shrank the instant real tracks (commonly 8–14) replaced the fixed 5-row skeleton, which is what actually read as a "pop." Changed `ForEach(0..<5, ...)` to `ForEach(0..<max(album.totalTracks, 1), ...)` so the skeleton is already the correct height before the fetch ever lands. **Build-green, installed, launched clean — visual confirmation from the user still pending as of this entry.**
  **On-device verification status, stated precisely per this session's own standard:** artist-page timing improvement — confirmed via two separate real captures with numbers. Prefetch-storm bug and its fix — confirmed via before/after captures. Disk persistence — confirmed via restore-count + cache-hit log evidence. Zoom transitions and blur-up placeholders — confirmed working by the user directly ("looks good"). Album skeleton row-count fix — **confirmed by the user directly ("pop in is gone")**. **Honest remaining gap, stated by the user in their own words**: "the tracks loading is still not where I want it at speed wise" — the genuinely-cold-fetch case (nothing prefetched, no disk cache hit) is still a real, unmasked MusicKit round trip; the user explicitly chose to defer further work on this rather than keep chasing it this round.

- **2026-08-11 — Round 9.19: load-time overhaul (Batch A of a planned 8-phase effort) — decoded-image cache, profile-fetch consolidation, two-phase library load — build-green, 131/131 NodalongKit tests pass (8 new). Real before/after device captures, not estimates.**
  User pushed back hard on the prior round's framing ("you say home is fast, i wouldnt consider 2000ms fast") — a fair, correct correction, kept verbatim in the plan. Full plan mode: 3 parallel Explore agents inventoried every `AsyncImage` call site, every caching layer, and every Firestore fetch pattern before any code was written. Plan file: `~/.claude/plans/temporal-baking-hollerith.md`.
  **Phase 0 — verified (not assumed) whether the stale-while-revalidate snapshot restore was actually working.** Hypothesis going in: `AlbumResult` gained two non-optional fields (`isExplicit`, `alternates`) with no custom decoder, and synthesized `Codable` doesn't apply `init` defaults on decode — so a snapshot written before those fields existed would fail to decode, `SnapshotStore.load`'s `try?` would swallow it silently, and every launch would quietly become a cold network load. Added one-line HIT/MISS `diagLog` to `HomeViewModel.restoreSnapshotIfNeeded`/`FeedViewModel.load` and captured a real launch: **both logged HIT** — the fast path was never broken. Hypothesis disproven, which is itself the useful result of instrumenting instead of guessing. Kept the hardening anyway since it prevents a REAL latent landmine from ever firing: added tolerant `init(from:)` decoders to `AlbumResult`/`ArtistResult`/`PlaylistResult` (`NodalongKit/.../CatalogSearchResults.swift`), mirroring `Song`'s existing pattern. **Caught two real bugs while writing the guarding tests, not before:** (1) my first test fixture used `"appleMusic"` as the service raw value — the actual `MusicServiceType` raw value is `"apple_music"`; wrong fixtures would have made the tests lie. (2) `decodeIfPresent` on an enum **throws** (not returns nil) when the key is present but holds an unrecognized raw value — meaning even the "tolerant" decoder would have hard-failed on a future service rename. Added a shared `decodeTolerantService` helper that folds "missing" and "unrecognized" into the same fallback. 8 new NodalongKit tests (`DecodeToleranceTests.swift`) decode payloads shaped like an old on-disk snapshot (missing newer keys) plus the unknown-enum-value case explicitly.
  **Phase 1 — decoded-image memory cache.** Confirmed via the Explore agents: zero `NSCache` anywhere in the app. `URLCache.shared` (the only configured cache) stores compressed bytes, not decoded bitmaps — so `AsyncImage` re-decoded every JPEG on every reappearance (scrolling a carousel off/back, popping a nav stack), with the shimmer placeholder flashing again each time. A genuine regression from removing Kingfisher — its in-memory image cache went with it, and `URLCache` was mistakenly treated as an equivalent replacement in that removal's own commit message. New `Nodalong/Shared/UI/ArtworkMemoryCache.swift` (`NSCache<NSString, UIImage>`, 64MB cost-tracked-in-bytes limit, `nonisolated(unsafe)` since `NSCache` is documented thread-safe but not `Sendable` — the annotation is load-bearing since it's read synchronously on the main actor and written from background decode tasks) + `Nodalong/Shared/UI/CachedArtworkImage.swift` (`AsyncImage` drop-in; reads the cache **synchronously in `init`** so a hit renders on the first frame with zero placeholder frames — doing the lookup in `.task` instead would still cost one frame, which is exactly what reads as pop-in; decodes off-main via `byPreparingForDisplay()`). Swapped into every real call site: `ArtworkThumbnail` (39 call sites via one shared component), `TrackCard`, both `NowPlayingView` images, both artist heroes (`ArtistDetailSearchView`, Shelf's `ArtistDetailView`), and the easily-missed `ResolvingArtistsView` picker row. Preserved the existing `sizedURL.isEmpty` guard at every site (an empty/absent URL must render the failure state immediately, not enter `CachedArtworkImage` at all — same reasoning as the already-documented `AsyncImage`-parks-in-`.empty`-forever bug this guard exists for). Zero `AsyncImage` remain in the app (confirmed via grep). One real warning caught and fixed during this phase: `NSCache` as a plain `static let` triggered a genuine Swift 6 Sendable warning, not a false positive — fixed with `nonisolated(unsafe)` plus a comment explaining why that's correct here rather than a silencer.
  **Phase 2 — `UserProfileCache` + fixed two N+1s inside `getUserProfiles` itself.** Exploration found the same followed-user profile set was fetched **three times concurrently** at cold launch: `getFriendsSpotlightsToday` (batched), `HomeViewModel.loadLiveNow` (batched again, same task, moments later), and `getFollowingFeed` (N *individual* `getDocument()` calls, one per followed user). `FollowingCache` already deduped the follow *list*; nothing deduped the profile *documents*. New `Nodalong/Services/UserProfileCache.swift` (mirrors `FollowingCache`'s actor/TTL/in-flight-sharing pattern exactly). Routed all three launch paths through it, and rewrote `getFollowingFeed` to fetch profiles once via the cache **before** the per-user task group, dropping the individual `getDocument()` entirely (`spotlitIds` now reads `catalogId` off the typed `UserProfile.spotlightTracks` instead of a raw dictionary `trackId` — confirmed equivalent per the 2026-07-05 entry establishing `SpotlightTrack.trackId` is always set to the catalogId). Also found and fixed a SECOND, nested N+1 while in this code: `getUserProfiles`'s own chunk loop was sequential (`for chunk in uids.chunked(into: 30) { try await ... }`), unlike `CatalogCache.metadata`'s own chunking which already parallelizes — and worse, `decodeUserProfile` called `enrichSpotlights` (a `CatalogCache` round trip) **per profile document inside the loop**, so 100 follows meant 100 sequential catalog fetches. Split into a sync `decodeUserProfileRaw` (back-fill + decode only) + parallelized `withTaskGroup` chunk fetch, then ONE combined `CatalogCache.metadata` call across every profile's spotlight ids at the end. **Verified on device, not assumed:** `spotlights+liveNow` (the historically dominant single cost in Home's load, 739–1897ms across earlier captures) dropped to **180ms** on a warm-session reload — roughly a 10x reduction, the single largest win in this round.
  **Phase 3 — library two-phase load.** `getLibraryTracks` had no `.limit`, read the whole collection, then unconditionally ran `enrichLibrary` (13–15 batched `CatalogCache` queries) over every track — 1608–2450ms observed on device, blocking Home, Feed, *and* Search, even though 11 of 13 real consumers of `LibraryViewModel` only ever call `isInLibrary(...)` and never read `.tracks` at all. New `getLibraryTracksRaw(uid:)` (`Library.swift`, one round trip, no enrichment); `LibraryViewModel.load` now populates `tracks`/`catalogIds` from the raw fetch immediately (unblocking `isLoading`), then enriches in a background `Task` guarded by `loadedForUid == uid` (so a stale enrichment can't clobber a later account switch), setting `tracks`/`uris` again once it lands. **Accepted, documented trade-off:** `uris` (the fallback matcher for search results, which carry no catalogId) stays unfilled for the enrichment's duration and fills in reactively — everything matching by catalogId is correct immediately. **Verified on device:** blocking cost dropped from 1608–2450ms to **900ms** (a single round trip for 441 raw docs), with the 1275ms enrichment now happening invisibly in the background instead of gating anything visible.
  **Phase 4 — adaptive feed fan-out**, folded into the Phase 2 diff: `getFollowingFeed` fetches `N × limit` docs then keeps only the top 50 combined — 30/user was a ~12x over-fetch at 20 follows, but a flat cut would have *shrunk* the feed for a small follow graph (3 follows × 10 = 30 candidates for 50 slots). `following.count > 10 ? 10 : 30` trims only where there's genuine waste.
  **Honest, stated-plainly result:** the FIRST cold launch in the after-capture still totaled 2896ms — not dramatically better than before, because a growing library (369→441 tracks) plus a fresh `runSync` ingest plus a cold `CatalogCache` were all genuinely competing for the same network at once, and Phases 1–4 don't eliminate that contention, they eliminate *redundant* work layered on top of it. What DID change: the snapshot still renders real content in ~40–90ms regardless of any of that (confirmed unrelated to this round — Phase 0 found it was already working), and the SECOND load in the same session — the more representative steady-state case — dropped from an 822–2983ms range to **1323ms total**, with the specific bottleneck this round targeted (`spotlights+liveNow`) down roughly 10x. Phase 1's actual effect (no more shimmer flash on reappearance) cannot be measured from timing logs at all — needs the user to visually confirm on device.
  **Batch B (phases 5–8: prefetch the playing track's artist/album resolve, render an artist's first page of top songs immediately instead of paginating in the critical path, disk-persist `MusicDetailCache`, deprioritize below-the-fold Home fetches) deliberately NOT started this round** — sequenced to follow once Batch A is confirmed on-device, per the plan's own stated ordering.
  **On-device confirmation still needed from the user:** Phase 1's shimmer-flash elimination (scroll a carousel off/back — visual only, unverifiable from logs); that Feed spotlight badges still render correctly given Phase 2's `spotlitIds` source change; that search-result hearts fill in correctly after Phase 3's background enrichment lands.

- **2026-08-10 — Round 9.18: RTDB security rules deployed for real (fixes silently-broken Live Now/party presence), Home load timing confirmed fast (~0.8–2s), and artist-page load genuinely sped up by splitting one heavy MusicKit request into two — build-green.**
  **RTDB rules deploy.** User shared real console output: `[FirebaseDatabase] setValue:... at /users_live/{uid}/nowPlaying failed: permission_denied` and the identical error for `/parties_live/{partyId}/nowPlaying` — both self-write/host-write paths that `database.rules.json` already correctly defines (confirmed by reading the file: `users_live/{uid}/nowPlaying` requires `auth.uid == $uid`; `parties_live/{partyId}/nowPlaying` requires the caller to be the party's own `hostUid`). The rules were correct in the repo but had evidently never been pushed to the live project — a "must deploy" item flagged in earlier decisions-log entries (the Phase-2 party work) that never actually got run. **Deployed on explicit, direct user instruction** ("firebase deploy --only database run it for me") after confirming CLI auth (`firebase login:list` → already authenticated) and the project id (`nodalong-d87ad`, read from `GoogleService-Info.plist`, no `.firebaserc` present). `firebase deploy --only database --project nodalong-d87ad` succeeded. Scoped narrowly (RTDB rules only, no data mutated) and the rules content was read and understood before running — judged this met the bar for proceeding on direct instruction rather than asking a second confirming question.
  **Home load timing — instrumented, then confirmed genuinely fast.** Added `diagLog` timing (previously only covered Search's path from Round 9.13) to `HomeViewModel.load`'s 5 task-group children, `HomeView.load`'s combined `homeVM`+`libraryVM`+`feedVM` wrapper, and `FeedViewModel.load`. Real on-device capture: Home's full load (all three view models, concurrent) finished in **822ms–2022ms** across two separate runs, with `spotlights+liveNow` (the followingUids-gated pair) consistently the slowest single piece (739ms–1897ms). No main-thread hangs during Home's load in either capture. This directly contradicted the user's "still not satisfied" complaint for Home specifically — asked for a more precise repro rather than guessing further, which the user gave (Search + artist/album pages specifically, not Home).
