  **Artist-page load — the real, confirmed slow path, root-caused and fixed.** User's precise repro: tapping an artist name from an album, or from the full-screen player, has "a consistent and noticeable delay... slower than how Spotify/Apple Music load." Instrumented both tap paths (`resolveDetailedSong` in `AppleMusicService`, the `searchAll` fallback in `AlbumDetailSearchView`'s `PendingArtistLookup`, and `MusicDetailCache`'s artist-details fetch) and captured a real trace: **player artist tap totaled ~2.9s** — `resolveDetailedSong` (identify which artist) 796ms + `getArtistDetails` (fetch their page) 2121ms. A second, already-identified-artist tap still cost 1584ms just for `getArtistDetails` alone.
  **Root cause:** `getArtistDetails` was ONE `MusicCatalogResourceRequest<Artist>` fetching FOUR relationships at once via `.properties` — `.albums`, `.topSongs`, `.similarArtists`, `.appearsOnAlbums` — so the whole screen (including Discography/Top Songs, the primary content) sat behind a skeleton until all four resolved, even though only two are needed for what's visible first. `ResolvingArtistsView`'s "instant sheet" work (Phase 5) already fixed the *navigation* half of this complaint — the sheet appears immediately — but the *content* behind it was still gated on the full combined fetch.
  **Fix:** split `getArtistDetails` into `getArtistCore(artistId:)` (`.albums`/`.topSongs` only) and `getArtistExtras(artistId:)` (`.similarArtists`/`.appearsOnAlbums` only) — two independent MusicKit requests instead of one combined one. `MusicDetailCache` restructured to match: `ArtistDetails` (one bundled struct) split into `ArtistCoreDetails`/`ArtistExtraDetails`, each with its own cache dict + in-flight-sharing (mirrors the existing `tracks(albumId:fetch:)` pattern exactly). `ArtistDetailSearchView.loadData()`'s Apple-Music branch now fires both via `async let` concurrently, assigning `albums`/`topTracks` the instant core resolves and `similarArtists`/`featuresOn` independently when extras resolves — no visual change needed, since the view's four `@State` vars and their `if !X.isEmpty` section guards were ALREADY structured for independent population; only the underlying fetch needed splitting.
  **Verified with a real before/after capture, not assumed:** first artist tap dropped to core (942ms, running concurrently with extras' 225ms) + resolve (309ms) ≈ **~1.25s total, down from ~2.9s**. A second artist's core-alone cost (1565ms) was comparable to the OLD combined fetch's cost for a similar case — an honest, non-oversold finding: the split's win is real (extras no longer blocks core) but bounded — for an artist whose `.albums`/`.topSongs` are themselves the slow part, this doesn't shrink that cost. **New lead, surfaced but NOT pursued this round:** `getArtistCore` internally pages through `paginatedTopSongs` (up to 5 *extra* sequential MusicKit round trips to build a 30-song list, when an artist has more than the API's default ~10-song page) — a plausible next place to look, not yet confirmed as the dominant cost.
  **Diagnostic capture was cut short twice by a real device/network connection drop** (`CoreDeviceError 3` / `RemoteXPCPeerConnection` invalidated — NOT a lock-screen issue this time, a genuine tunnel instability), so a fully clean, uninterrupted second capture was never obtained — the numbers above are real and confirmed, just from a partial run. The same partial capture also showed `runSync` (background library ingest) taking **9.4 seconds** in one run vs. under 3 seconds in earlier captures — a reminder that a meaningful share of perceived app-wide slowness is genuinely network-condition-variable, not a fixed, fully-controllable cost.
  **On-device confirmation still pending from the user** for whether the artist-page speedup is actually felt as "less noticeable" now, and whether the `runSync` variability recurs. The `[Nodalong.Diag]`/`MainThreadHangDetector` instrumentation from Round 9.13 remains live in the tree — still useful, still not removed.

- **2026-08-09 — Round 9.17: two new friend-driven Home sections shipped ("Friends are listening to", "Most nodded this week") + Apple's own dynamically-titled "Recently Played" shelf filtered out of Recommended for You — build-green, 123/123 NodalongKit tests pass (11 new).**
  Follow-up to a real user-supplied screenshot that resolved the earlier "why is Jump Back In out of sync with Recently Played" ambiguity: the mismatched "Recently Played" wasn't Profile's Recent Tracks at all — it's a shelf Apple Music's OWN `MusicPersonalRecommendationsRequest` dynamically titles itself, nested inside Home's "Recommended for You" (same mechanism produces the "More from The Strokes" shelf visible right below it in the screenshot). Two systems both claiming the same "recently played" label, one fresh (Nodalong's own, just fixed in Round 9.16) and one on Apple's own refresh cadence — not a bug, but confusing and worth removing since it duplicates Jump Back In's job with staler content. **Fix:** `AppleMusicService.getPersonalRecommendations` now drops any shelf whose title case-insensitively equals "recently played" before returning — filtered at the source, so both Home's inline section and the "See All" detail screen (`RecommendedForYouView`, same underlying call) get the fix with one change.
  **Two new sections, both approved via the visualize tool across 2 rounds before implementing** (mockup showed avatar-stack album tiles + a numbered flame/nod-count list; user picked both, declined to remove Jump Back In after seeing them side by side — kept as-is per direct instruction). Both are PURE CLIENT-SIDE aggregations over `FeedViewModel.posts` (`getFollowingFeed`, already fetched for the Feed tab — capped at 100 combined posts across followed users, sorted by recency) — **zero new Firestore queries**, just two new grouping functions in `NodalongKit/Utils/FriendsActivity.swift` (11 new unit tests, mirroring `GroupPostsByAlbumTests`'s existing style):
  - `friendsListeningAlbums(from:)` — mirrors `recentAlbums(from:)`'s exact album-grouping key convention (`primaryArtist(_:)`-normalized, so a featured-artist credit doesn't split an album into two groups) but tracks WHO listened (deduped `FriendListener` list per uid) instead of a replay count, ranked by distinct-listener-count then recency. Deliberately no minimum-listener threshold — a quieter follow graph would otherwise leave this permanently empty, so a single-listener album still shows (with that one listener's avatar) at the bottom of the ranking rather than being dropped entirely.
  - `mostNoddedPosts(from:since:limit:)` — aggregates `Post.nods` per unique `catalogId` within a time window (Home passes a 7-day cutoff), summing nods across every followed user's own play of that track, returning each group's most-recent `Post` with `nods` overridden to the summed total (reuses `Post`'s existing fields/`Song.from(post:)` conversion directly rather than inventing a parallel model). Tracks with zero total nods are dropped — this rail is meant to be genuine social proof, not just more recent-activity noise Feed/Jump Back In already cover.
  **`HomeView` wiring:** new `feedVM: FeedViewModel` `@EnvironmentObject`, loaded concurrently in `load()` alongside `homeVM`/`libraryVM` (same "already-guarded, safe/cheap to call from multiple screens" reasoning as the Round 9.15 `libraryVM` fix). Both sections placed right after Friends' Spotlights, before Top Charts — keeps the social/friend-driven cluster together, transitioning into the impersonal chart/algorithm content after. **Friends are listening to** reuses the exact `path.append(album)` → `ResolvingAlbumView` → `resolveAlbum(catalogTrackId:isrc:title:artist:)` pattern Jump Back In already established, with a new `FriendsListeningAlbum` `.navigationDestination`; tiles show up to 3 overlapping listener avatars bottom-left over the artwork. **Most nodded this week** is DELIBERATELY a vertical numbered list (bgSurface rows, rank + flame icon + count), not another horizontal tile carousel — Home was leaning on one layout shape (horizontal carousel of square tiles) for nearly every section before this, so this doubles as the visual-variety idea from the same brainstorm; tapping any row plays the whole list starting there, mirroring Top Charts' existing tap-to-play-in-context convention exactly.
  **On-device unverified** — installed but not yet interacted with; needs confirmation that both sections render with real data (needs an account with actual overlapping follow-graph listening activity + nodded tracks to look meaningfully populated — may read sparse/empty on a small follow graph, which is expected, not a bug), that tapping a Friends-Listening album resolves and opens correctly, that tapping a Most-Nodded row plays the list starting at the right track, and that the stale Apple "Recently Played" shelf is actually gone from Recommended for You now.

- **2026-08-09 — Round 9.16: found and fixed why Home's "Jump Back In" could visibly lag behind Profile's "Recent Tracks" — build-green.**
  User noticed the two disagreeing and asked why, plus what the actual difference between them is. Both draw from the exact same underlying data (`getYourMusicFeed(uid:)`) — Profile's "Recent Tracks" shows it ungrouped, track-level; Home's "Jump Back In" groups the same posts by album (`recentAlbums(from:)`), so there was never a data-source mismatch to explain the "out of sync" symptom.
  **Real cause, confirmed by comparing the two screens' refresh triggers directly:** `ProfileView` has `.onChange(of: syncService.lastSyncedAt) { Task { await refresh() } }` — it re-fetches automatically every time a background library sync completes (which fires silently on cold launch and on every foreground-return, per `ContentView`'s `willEnterForegroundNotification` handler). `HomeView` had no equivalent — its `recentPosts`/`recentAlbums` only refreshed on the first time Home was shown each session (gated by a local `hasLoaded` `@State`) or an explicit pull-to-refresh. So after a new play, Profile picked it up on its own while Home kept showing whatever it last loaded until manually refreshed.
  **Fix:** added `syncService: LibrarySyncService` as a new `@EnvironmentObject` on `HomeView` (already injected app-wide, per `ProfileView`'s existing usage — just wasn't declared here) and a matching `.onChange(of: syncService.lastSyncedAt) { _, _ in Task { await load() } }`. Used the two-parameter `onChange` closure form (iOS 17+) rather than mirroring `ProfileView`'s older single-parameter style — `HomeView` already has one other `onChange` site (`canPlayFullTracks`) using the newer form, and the app's real deployment target is iOS 26.1, so matching the file's own newer convention is more correct than copying `ProfileView`'s older, pre-deployment-bump style verbatim.

- **2026-08-09 — Round 9.15: Home's "Recently Added" re-sourced from Nodalong's own Shelf instead of the user's Apple Music library — a deliberate, direct user request, not a bug fix — build-green, 112/112 NodalongKit tests pass.**
  User noticed a real discrepancy (screenshot): Home's "Recently Added" showed albums they hadn't put in Shelf. Root cause explained first, then fixed on request: `getRecentlyAddedAlbums` read `MusicLibraryRequest<Album>` — the user's literal Apple Music library (anything added via the Music app, Siri, another device) — which has always been, by design, a completely separate collection from Shelf (per this file's own §1: Shelf is "a personal, curated library separate from their Apple Music likes"). The two collections were never meant to be the same thing, but the shared "Recently Added" label made that easy to miss.
  **Fix — swapped the data source, not just relabeled it.** `HomeView.recentShelfAlbums` (new computed property) derives directly from `libraryVM.tracks` (already loaded app-wide, zero new fetch) via the EXISTING `groupByAlbum` (`NodalongKit`, the same grouping Shelf's own Albums grid uses) — re-sorted by each group's most-recently-saved track (`max` of `LibraryTrackDoc.savedAt` per group) instead of Shelf's own artist-then-release-date order, then capped to 10. Because this is a plain computed property over an already-`@Published`, already-shared `LibraryViewModel.tracks`, the rail updates live the instant the user hearts/unhearts anything anywhere in the app — no extra plumbing, and strictly fresher than the old network-fetch version ever was.
  **`AlbumGroup` (`NodalongKit/Utils/GroupByAlbum.swift`) gained `Hashable`/`Identifiable`** (`id` = its existing compound `key`) — needed for `.navigationDestination(for: AlbumGroup.self)`; synthesis was free since its one non-trivial field, `[LibraryTrackDoc]`, was already `Hashable`.
  **Did NOT reuse Shelf's own `AlbumDetailView`** for the tap-through, despite it doing almost exactly what was needed — traced its actual signature and found it's tightly coupled to Shelf's own screen: it takes an `albumKey: String` and looks itself up via `shelfVM.albums.first(where: { $0.key == albumKey })` (requires `ShelfViewModel` already loaded — not guaranteed if the user opens Home first, before ever visiting Shelf) and pushes its own artist/album-name-tap catalog lookups (`PendingCatalogAlbumLookup`/`PendingCatalogArtistLookup`) onto a `@Binding var path: NavigationPath` that only Shelf's own stack has destinations registered for — pushing it from Home's stack would silently fail to navigate on those specific taps. Rather than take on the larger refactor of extracting that catalog-lookup machinery into something both screens could share, built a new, smaller, Home-owned `ShelfAlbumQuickView` (private struct in `HomeView.swift`) taking `AlbumGroup` directly by value — same header/track-list/play-in-context/remove-from-shelf structure, minus the artist/album catalog tap-through (a Shelf-specific nicety, not essential for a quick glance from Home).
  **`HomeView.load()`** now runs `homeVM.load(...)` and `libraryVM.load(uid:)` concurrently (`async let`) instead of just the former — needed because `libraryVM.load` was previously only ever triggered from `SearchView`'s `.task`, meaning a user who opens Home first (now the default landing tab) and never visits Search would see an empty "Recently Added" section even with a populated Shelf. `LibraryViewModel.load`'s own `loadedForUid` guard makes the redundant call from both screens safe and cheap. `showFirstLaunchSkeletons` extended to also gate on `libraryVM.isLoading`/`libraryVM.tracks.isEmpty` so the skeleton row doesn't disappear prematurely while only the library fetch is still in flight.
  **Cleanup — deleted `AppleMusicService.getRecentlyAddedAlbums` entirely** (its only caller, confirmed via grep before removing), along with its `HomeViewModel` plumbing (`@Published recentlyAddedAlbums`, its `HomeSnapshot` field, its `restoreSnapshotIfNeeded`/`persistSnapshot` lines, its task-group child). Its private helper `searchAlbumCatalog` (used internally for the old function's artwork-repair step) was confirmed to have a second, still-live caller (`resolveDetailedSong`'s fallback cascade) before being left alone. Updated one stale doc comment in `CatalogSearchResults.swift` that named the now-deleted function by name, explaining why `AlbumResult.artwork` is `var`.
  **On-device unverified** — installed but not yet interacted with; needs confirmation that "Recently Added" now shows real Shelf saves (and is empty for an account with nothing saved to Shelf, even if their real Apple Music library has plenty), that tapping a tile opens the new lightweight detail view with the right tracks, and that "Remove from Shelf" correctly pops back and updates the rail.

- **2026-08-09 — Round 9.14: Search redesign shipped — mockups approved via the visualize tool across 3 rounds (Option B "discovery-first" + Top Charts dropped as a Home duplicate), then implemented — build-green, 112/112 NodalongKit tests pass.**
  **Filter row** rebuilt from plain underline tabs to icon+label pill chips (`filterPill`, capsule background highlight for the selected tab) — same self-collapsing People↔Music↔4-scope behavior as before, purely a visual swap.
  **Browse state** (`SearchView.browseState`, replaces the old bare icon+text `promptState` whenever `query` is empty — `promptState` itself is kept only as a final graceful fallback when there's genuinely nothing else to show): **Music mode** — recent search chips → a static "browse by mood and genre" grid (`genreTiles`, 6 hardcoded tiles; tapping one sets `query`/`mode`/`scope` and re-runs the EXISTING search pipeline — deliberately not a new MusicKit request type, just a convenience shortcut into search) → a "Popular albums" rail. **People mode** — recent chips → a "Live now" row (green-ring avatars, reused `LiveNowEntry`/`UserPresenceService`/`FollowingCache` exactly as `HomeViewModel.loadLiveNow` already does, duplicated rather than shared since it's ~15 lines and the two view models have no other coupling) → "People you may know".
  **Explicitly dropped from the original mockup, per direct user pushback:** a "Top charts" rail in the Music browse state — the user correctly flagged this as pure duplication of Home's own Top Charts section; removed before implementation, keeping the two tabs' browse content non-overlapping (Home = personalized "for you", Search browse = pure discovery: genres, popular albums, who's around, who you might follow).
  **New data, all verified before writing code (per this project's own established discipline):** `AppleMusicService.getPopularAlbums(limit:)` — mirrors `getTopChartSongs` but for `MusicCatalogChartsRequest(types: [Album.self])` / `resp.albumCharts`, confirmed real via an `xcrun swiftc -typecheck` probe before use (clean compile, first attempt) — **deliberately labeled "Popular albums" everywhere, not "New releases"**, since this is a popularity chart, not a recency feed, and MusicKit's public API has no genuine new-releases endpoint to back that label honestly. `getSuggestedUsers(excluding:limit:)` (`Users.swift`) — a cheap, HONEST simplification of "people you may know": one query ordered by `createdAt` descending, NOT true mutual-follow-count suggestions (would need either server-side aggregation or an N+1 client fan-out across every followed user's own follow list — exactly the kind of expensive query this same session's Round 9.11–9.13 spent hours root-causing as a perf problem elsewhere; not worth reintroducing here for a "may know" rail). The mockup's "3 mutual follows" subtitle text was dropped for this reason — shipped rows show no reason subtitle, just the username + Follow button.
  **Recent searches** — a genuinely new concept, didn't exist before this round. `SearchViewModel.RecentSearchEntry` (local struct, not NodalongKit — no cross-cutting reuse, matches how `SearchMode`/`MusicSearchScope` are already file-local nested types) persisted via plain UserDefaults JSON, keyed per-uid (`"nodalong.recentSearches.\(uid)"`), capped at 10, upsert-to-front dedup by (kind, displayName) — deliberately local/on-device only, not synced to Firestore, since it's browsing history not app data. Recorded ONLY on a genuine search-result tap (person row, artist/album card, song play) — NOT on browsing the static genre grid or the Popular Albums/Live Now/Suggested rails, since those are ambient browse content, not something the user "searched for." Tapping a chip re-populates `query`/`mode`/`scope` and re-runs the existing search pipeline (`selectRecentSearch`) — no new resolve/navigation logic, same mechanism as typing.
  **`isExplicit` added to `Song` and `SearchItem`** (both `NodalongKit`) so song rows can show the same "E" tag albums already show — `AppleMusicService.toSong` now sets it from `s.contentRating == .explicit`; `SearchViewModel.songToSearchItem` threads it through. **`Song`'s change needed care**: it's the one of the two directly persisted via `SnapshotStore` (Home's cached `topChartSongs: [Song]` snapshot) — a synthesized `Decodable` would require the new key present, breaking decode of an already-persisted pre-this-change snapshot file. Added a custom `init(from decoder:)` with `decodeIfPresent(...) ?? default` for every field (not just the new one), matching this session's own earlier "decode-tolerant" convention from the track-metadata-normalization work. `SearchItem` isn't persisted anywhere found, so it got a plain default-valued property with no custom decoder — lower risk, kept the change smaller where the risk didn't justify the same treatment.
  **Song rows** (`TrackSearchRow`) now show duration (`formatTrackDuration`, already existed, just not wired into this row before) and the explicit tag inline in the subtitle.
  **Live Now tap navigation** required promoting `SearchView`'s bare `NavigationStack { }` to `NavigationStack(path: $path)` (mirrors `HomeView`'s exact pattern) — a `.simultaneousGesture` button can't do the "await an async profile fetch, then push" flow a `NavigationLink(value:)` alone can't express; existing `.navigationDestination(for:)` registrations are unaffected, since a path-bound stack still supports both `NavigationLink(value:)` and direct `path.append(...)` interchangeably. "People you may know" rows use the same `path.append(user)` pattern directly (no async fetch needed, already have the full `UserProfile`).
  **Incidental fix, unrelated to Search:** rebuilding for this pass on a clean derived-data path surfaced ONE new warning at `AppleMusicService.swift:161` — "sending value of non-Sendable type '@MainActor (MusicSubscription) -> Void'" — introduced by this same session's earlier Round 9.13 subscription-observer fix but only visible on a full clean rebuild (the incremental check right after that fix landed showed zero warnings for the file, which is why it wasn't caught then). Fixed by marking the `onUpdate` closure parameter `@Sendable @MainActor` instead of just `@MainActor` — the standard, correct combination for handing a MainActor-bound callback into a `nonisolated` function. Confirmed the warning is gone and nothing else changed via a follow-up clean rebuild.
  **On-device unverified** — installed to the user's device but not yet interacted with; needs a real pass confirming: pill tabs render/animate correctly; Music browse shows genre tiles + popular albums (gated correctly behind Apple Music auth); People browse shows Live Now (only with a real followed-and-listening account) + suggested users; tapping a genre tile actually searches; recent searches populate after a real result tap and persist across a relaunch; the explicit "E" tag and duration show correctly on song rows.

- **2026-08-09 — Round 9.13: real on-device main-thread-hang instrumentation set up (per direct user request to stop guessing), found and partially fixed a genuine main-thread-blocking bug in `AppleMusicService`'s subscription observer — build-green, confirmed via two real device captures that the fix reduces but does NOT eliminate the fresh-install Search lag. Investigation deliberately paused here at the user's request to pivot to a Search visual/UX redesign conversation — pick this back up before declaring it resolved.**
  **Instrumentation added** (all explicitly tagged TEMPORARY, not yet removed — still in the tree): new `Nodalong/Services/DiagLog.swift` — `diagLog(_:)` (timestamped, thread-tagged `print`) and `MainThreadHangDetector` (posts a ping from a background queue to `DispatchQueue.main.async` every 150ms and logs when the actual delay exceeds the threshold — direct, non-inferential evidence of real main-thread blocking, not client-side guessing). Wired into `AppDelegate.didFinishLaunchingWithOptions` (`MainThreadHangDetector.start()`) plus `diagLog(...)` call sites added to `ContentView`'s launch-sync `.task`, `LibrarySyncService.runSync`/`ingestToCatalogOnly`, `CatalogCache.metadata(for:)`, `SearchView`'s `.task`, and `LibraryViewModel.load`. Captured via `xcrun devicectl device process launch --console` piped to a scratchpad log file on the user's real physical iPhone (not simulator) — this required the device to be unlocked first (`CoreDeviceError 10002`/"Locked" otherwise, exactly as documented in this project's own earlier entries) and used two DIFFERENT device-identifier schemes that are NOT interchangeable: `devicectl`'s own UUID vs. the ID `xcodebuild -destination` expects (found via a failed build's own "Available destinations" list).
  **First real capture, before any further fix, showed THREE genuine main-thread hangs** — `3559ms` right at launch (before `ContentView`'s own `.task` even fires), `1077ms` right as `LibraryViewModel.load` starts, `1526ms` while `resolveCatalogArtwork` calls were pending — and `SearchView`'s own concurrent `.task` (`loadFollowing` + `libraryVM.load`, already parallelized in Round 9.11) took **9291ms** total end to end. This is real, direct, non-inferential confirmation that the "severe lag" complaint is genuine main-thread blocking, not perception.
  **Root cause of at least part of it, found in the SAME capture's console noise, not guessed:** a MusicKit console warning reading verbatim *"it has recently become deprecated to request the music subscription status in process; the new supported code path fetches it in itunescloudd, but you need to add com.apple.security.exception.mach-lookup.global-name com.apple.itunescloud.music-subscription-status-service to your sandbox in order to opt in to this new code path"* — preceded by ~20 repeated `ICError -7013 "Client is not entitled to access account store"` failures from `ICUserIdentityStore`/`ACAccountStore`/`MPCloudController`. MusicKit is falling back to a deprecated, SYNCHRONOUS, in-process subscription-status resolution path (the required entitlement is a private Apple mach-lookup sandbox exception, not grantable to third-party apps via normal provisioning — same dead-end category as the earlier `com.apple.developer.musickit` entitlement attempt documented elsewhere in this file; **do not attempt to add it to the entitlements file, it will fail codesign/provisioning the same way**). `AppleMusicService.startSubscriptionObserver()` created `Task { [weak self] in for await sub in MusicSubscription.subscriptionUpdates { ... } }` written DIRECTLY inside a `@MainActor`-isolated method — per Swift's closure-isolation inference (SE-0338), that closure inherits MainActor isolation, so the ENTIRE `for await` loop — including whatever synchronous blocking daemon IPC MusicKit's deprecated fallback does internally on each iteration — ran on the actual main thread. Being a long-lived loop (lives for the app's session, not a one-shot call), it plausibly re-blocks on each re-resolution/retry, which is consistent with hangs recurring several seconds apart rather than just once at launch.
  **Fix, deliberately narrow and NOT touching playback/queue code** (per this project's own documented caution about MusicKit-threading changes going wrong, from the earlier multi-round freeze saga): the actual `for await` consumption moved into a new `nonisolated private static func observeSubscriptionUpdates(onUpdate: @escaping @MainActor (MusicSubscription) -> Void) async` — a `nonisolated` function, so calling it from the MainActor-isolated `startSubscriptionObserver()` genuinely leaves the MainActor executor for the loop's duration; only the per-update `await onUpdate(sub)` hops back to MainActor to publish. This avoided needing `Task.detached` (which would've required making `AppleMusicService` itself `Sendable` to legally capture `weak self` in a `@Sendable` closure — a materially bigger, riskier change) by keeping `self` capture entirely on the MainActor-isolated outer `Task`, with only the leaf resolution work marked `nonisolated`. Verified this compiles clean under this project's `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY: complete` with zero new warnings anywhere, including in the edited file itself.
  **Second capture, after the fix, confirmed real but partial improvement — HONEST result, not oversold:** hangs dropped from 3 (3559/1077/1526ms) to 2 (1180/1838ms); total `runSync` time dropped from ~11s to ~5.3s; `SearchView`'s own `.task` completion time similarly dropped. But the user directly confirmed on-device that the lag is **still present** — the subscription-observer fix was real and measurably helped, but is NOT the full explanation. The `1180ms`/`1838ms` residual hangs in the second capture still correlate with the same `ICUserIdentityStore`/-7013 storm (still present, just a shorter burst) and with the `resolveCatalogArtwork`/`upsertCatalogTrack` burst-completion pattern (many concurrent tasks completing at the exact same millisecond, the signature of "all blocked behind something, then released together") — meaning either the deprecated subscription-status path is still partially blocking somewhere not yet isolated, or a second, distinct blocking mechanism exists that hasn't been identified yet.
  **Deliberately NOT pursued further this round, per direct user request to pivot to Search's visual/UX redesign instead** — investigation is genuinely unfinished, not abandoned. **Next steps for whoever picks this back up:** (1) confirm via a fresh capture whether the residual hangs still correlate with `ICUserIdentityStore`/-7013 activity specifically (the current diagLog timestamps make this checkable by eye against the captured log); (2) if so, the next suspect is `MusicAuthorization.currentStatus`/`refreshAuthStatus()` (also `@MainActor`, also touches account/auth state, never audited for the same closure-isolation bug this round fixed) or MusicKit's `MusicCatalogSearchRequest`/`MusicDataRequest` machinery itself synchronously touching account state on first use per process; (3) if NOT correlated with the -7013 storm, the residual hangs are a genuinely separate mechanism and need their own root-cause pass — don't assume it's more of the same bug. **The diagnostic instrumentation (`DiagLog.swift`, `MainThreadHangDetector.start()`, and every `diagLog(...)` call site) is still live in the tree, explicitly tagged temporary — leave it in place until this is actually resolved**, since it's exactly the tool that made this round's real progress possible (evidence over inference, per the user's own explicit instruction this round) — do not remove it prematurely just because a session is wrapping up.

- **2026-08-09 — Round 9.12: found the actual dominant contributor to fresh-install Search lag — `LibrarySyncService`'s recent-tracks ingest loop was sequential — build-green, 112/112 NodalongKit tests unaffected. User's own on-device screen recording couldn't be used (macOS blocked shell access to `~/Downloads` for this session — confirmed via both `ffmpeg` and plain `cp` failing identically; asked the user to move the file into the project folder instead, which they didn't pursue — this round proceeded on further static tracing instead).**
  Round 9.11's fixes (concurrent `CatalogCache` batches, concurrent Search-tab `.task`) were real but almost certainly minor next to this: `ContentView.swift`'s own `.task` (line ~101) fires `LibrarySyncService.sync(uid:service:)` automatically at COLD LAUNCH, unconditionally, whenever Apple Music is already authorized — running well before, and concurrently with, whatever the user does next, including navigating straight to Search. Its `runSync` fetches up to 25 recently-played tracks and processed them in a plain sequential `for song in validRecent { await ingestToCatalogOnly(...) }` loop. On a fresh install, `TrackCache` (the in-memory dedup cache `ingestToCatalogOnly` checks first) is empty — EVERY one of those ~25 tracks is a cache miss, meaning each one pays the FULL cost: a MusicKit catalog search (`resolveCatalogArtwork`, only reached when artwork isn't already a fetchable URL — common for library-matched recent plays) plus a Firestore TRANSACTION (`upsertCatalogTrack`), one completely after another. 25 sequential round trips of real network + Firestore transaction latency is a genuinely plausible multi-second stall, and it starts automatically at launch — squarely explaining "fresh install, opens straight into several seconds of lag" better than anything found in the previous round.
  **Fix:** both `runSync` and its Spotify sibling `runSpotifySync` now fire all tracks' ingestion concurrently via `withTaskGroup` instead of one at a time. Checked `upsertCatalogTrack` (`Catalog.swift`) before assuming this was safe to parallelize — it transacts against `isrc_index/{sanitizedIsrc}`, a document keyed per-ISRC; since 25 distinct recently-played tracks almost always have 25 distinct ISRCs, these transactions target different documents and don't contend with each other, so firing them concurrently doesn't introduce new retry overhead.
  **Honest caveat, same as last round:** both `AppleMusicService` and `LibrarySyncService` are `@MainActor` classes, so each concurrent task still briefly touches the MainActor to kick off its work before suspending on the actual network/Firestore await — this fix overlaps the DOMINANT cost (network + transaction latency, which is genuinely concurrent regardless of actor isolation) but does not eliminate MainActor involvement entirely. Given this project's own documented history of MusicKit-threading changes going wrong (the multi-round playback-freeze saga earlier in this file), a deeper fix — removing `@MainActor` from `AppleMusicService` or extracting a nonisolated catalog-search-only path — was deliberately NOT attempted this round; it's a materially bigger, riskier change than converting a sequential loop to a concurrent one.
  **On the video**: the user offered a screen recording to pin this down more precisely; this environment's shell couldn't read it (macOS blocks Downloads-folder access here, confirmed by both `ffmpeg -i` and `cp` failing with the identical "Operation not permitted" for the same file) and the user chose not to move it into a reachable folder. Worth remembering for a future session: request video/screenshot files be placed inside the project directory (or the scratchpad path) from the start, since Downloads is not reliably reachable from this shell.
  **On-device unverified** — needs the user to confirm whether fresh-install Search-tab responsiveness is now meaningfully better; if a lag still remains, the next lead (not yet pursued) would be whether `AppleMusicService`'s `@MainActor` binding is itself the remaining bottleneck, which would need real device profiling to confirm before attempting the riskier isolation change.

- **2026-08-09 — Round 9.11: fresh-install Search-tab keyboard lag investigated — a real sequential-batch inefficiency found and fixed, honestly flagged as a probable-not-certain root cause given no live device profiling available.**
  User report: on a fresh install, navigating to Search causes several seconds where keyboard taps don't register. Traced Search's `.task` (`SearchView.swift`): on the FIRST visit to Search in a session (`LibraryViewModel.load`'s `loadedForUid` guard means this only ever runs once), it calls `searchVM.loadFollowing` then `libraryVM.load` — SEQUENTIALLY, though they're fully independent (follow graph vs. personal library). `libraryVM.load` → `getLibraryTracks` → `enrichLibrary` → `CatalogCache.metadata(for:)`, which fetches the user's library's catalog metadata in Firestore's `in`-query batches of 30 ids — but AWAITED ONE BATCH AT A TIME in a plain `for` loop. On a fresh app install, this is the first time an account's (potentially already-large, pre-existing-in-the-cloud) library gets synced to this device — a library of a few hundred tracks means 10+ sequential network round trips stacking up before the tab has anything to show, all before the user can meaningfully interact with it.
  **Fixed both real inefficiencies found:** `CatalogCache.metadata(for:)` now fetches all missing batches CONCURRENTLY via `withTaskGroup` instead of one at a time — turns N sequential round trips into roughly one round trip's worth of wall-clock time; batch-level Firestore/decode work happens in child tasks, `cache`/`result` mutations happen only in the consuming loop (the actor's own isolated context), so no data-race risk. `SearchView`'s `.task` now runs `loadFollowing`/`libraryVM.load` concurrently via `async let` instead of sequential `await`s.
  **Honest caveat, stated directly to the user:** these are real, concrete, and clearly-relevant inefficiencies for the exact scenario reported (large-library-on-fresh-install), and both compound in the same direction the user described — but confirming they're the FULL explanation for keyboard-input-specifically lagging (as opposed to, say, general jank/backlog) isn't possible without live device profiling, which this environment doesn't have. Checked `TrackCache` (the sibling actor cache) for the same pattern — it's pure in-memory, no batched-fetch loop, not affected.
  **On-device unverified** — needs the user to confirm whether this measurably improves (or resolves) the fresh-install Search-tab lag; if it persists, the next step would need a real device profile (Instruments/Time Profiler) rather than further static-code guessing, per this project's own established lesson about not chaining blind fixes.

- **2026-08-09 — Round 9.10: Search's real responsiveness bug found — every refinement wiped the results list to a blocking full-screen spinner and repopulated it — build-green, 112/112 NodalongKit tests unaffected.**
  Direct follow-up to the earlier stale-result-race fix (which addressed a *correctness* bug, not the "feels slow" complaint itself). `SearchView`'s top-level branch was `if searchVM.isSearching { ProgressView()... } else { ...resultsScroll }` — unconditional: ANY time `isSearching` went true (every keystroke pause, every scope/mode tap, even when the *previous* search's results were still perfectly valid to keep looking at), the entire results list was torn down and replaced by a blank full-screen spinner, then rebuilt from scratch a few hundred ms later. That teardown/rebuild cycle, repeated on every pause in typing, is what "doesn't feel responsive" was almost certainly describing — not raw network latency.
  **Fix, two parts.** (1) The big blocking spinner is now gated on `isSearching && !hasAnyResults` — reserved for a genuinely empty state (first search, or one that's cleared everything). Once there ARE results on screen, `resultsScroll` stays mounted through a refinement instead of being torn down. (2) `resultsScroll` itself now dims to 40% opacity and overlays a small top-aligned spinner while `isSearching` is true, so a refinement still gives feedback that something's happening — without hiding what's already there. **Known, accepted trade-off:** during a scope switch specifically, the still-visible results are the PREVIOUS scope's (e.g. old Song rows for a moment after tapping "Albums") until the new fetch lands — `results` is one struct that flips entirely on completion, not fetched-and-swapped per-section. Judged better than the blank-flash alternative it replaces, since it's transient (typically sub-second) and self-corrects; not fixed further this round.
  **Separately:** scope/mode taps (`.onChange(of: searchVM.scope/mode)`) were going through the SAME 300ms debounce built for "user might still be typing" — but a scope tap is one discrete, deliberate action with nothing more coming, so the debounce was pure added lag with no purpose there. `search(via:debounce:)` gained a `debounce` parameter (default `true`, preserving the query-typing path exactly); the scope/mode `.onChange` call sites now pass `debounce: false`, firing the fetch immediately on tap.
  **On-device unverified** — needs the user to confirm search now feels smoother while typing (no more visible flash-to-spinner-and-back on every pause) and that switching scope/mode feels snappier.

- **2026-08-07 — Round 9.9: discography grid navigation genuinely fixed — the item-based `discographyGridTab` binding itself was the bug, not which destination type it competed with — build-green, 112/112 NodalongKit tests unaffected.**
  User's own repro nailed it: Discography → See all → Donda → Donda (again) → back → back landed on the artist page, with the intermediate screens showing TWO stacked "Donda" screens. That's the signature of `.navigationDestination(item: $discographyGridTab)` re-presenting the grid on top of itself: since it presents whenever its bound item is non-nil, pushing `AlbumDetailSearchView` on top of the grid didn't clear `discographyGridTab` — so the artist view kept re-evaluating with the item still set, and the grid got pushed a second time above the album, invisibly, explaining both the "back to see-all" symptom and the duplicate screens revealed by pressing back twice.
  **Round 9.7's fix was also built on a wrong premise** — its comment claimed "closest ancestor wins" for duplicate `.navigationDestination(for:)` registrations of the same type; SwiftUI actually resolves in favor of whichever registration is closest to the stack's ROOT. The local `AlbumResult` destination added to `DiscographyGridView` could therefore never have taken effect — it was inert, not the fix it was believed to be. The real problem was never *which* destination handled the album push; it was that the grid's own presentation mechanism re-fired underneath it.
  **Actual fix — removed the item binding entirely, made discography navigation uniformly value-based like the rest of the screen.** `discographyGridTab: DiscographyTab?` and its `.navigationDestination(item:)` are gone. `DiscographyTab` is now itself registered via `.navigationDestination(for: DiscographyTab.self)` (a type unique to this screen, no duplicate-registration risk), reached via a plain `NavigationLink(value: discographyTab)` on the "See all" button. **The retap-to-grid gesture (tap an already-selected tab to open the grid) is removed** — it depended on the same programmatic-push mechanism that caused the bug, was never discoverable on its own (which is why the explicit "See all" link was added in an earlier round), and a tab that behaves differently on a second tap is an unusual pattern regardless. Tabs now only switch the carousel. `DiscographyGridView`'s inert local `AlbumResult` destination (from 9.7) was removed along with its incorrect justifying comment; album taps resolve exactly the way every other album push in the app does — via the ambient registration on whichever stack this screen sits in (Home/Search/Shelf/the player's sheet).
  **On-device unverified** — needs the user to confirm the exact repro (Discography → See all → tap an album → back) now returns to the grid, not the artist page, and that "See all" still opens the grid correctly now that it's a plain `NavigationLink`.

- **2026-08-07 — Round 9.8: full-bleed artist hero restored, using a structurally different approach (extend the SCROLL VIEW, don't offset the hero) — build-green.**
  Requested back after Round 9.7 root-caused the title clipping, since that had been the main thing making the bleed look broken. **What's different from the four attempts that failed:** all of those tried to make the HERO reach upward past its own slot — a taller frame plus `ignoresSafeArea`, a bottom-aligned oversized `.background`, a `GeometryReader`-measured `.offset`. Every one of them needed a NUMBER (how far up, how much taller), and every number was correct in some presentation contexts and wrong in others — most visibly the measured-offset version, which was right when pushed from Search/Home/Shelf and wrong inside `NowPlayingView`'s sheet. **This version computes nothing:** `.ignoresSafeArea(edges: .top)` is applied to the SCROLL VIEW, so its first child — the hero — simply begins at the top of the available space, with the system supplying the geometry in every context (pushed, sheet, any device). There is no value here that can be right in one place and wrong in another, which was the actual failure mode all four previous times, rather than any specific arithmetic being off. Paired with `.toolbarBackground(.hidden, for: .navigationBar)` on both screens (otherwise the nav bar paints its own opaque background over the photo — the purple band the user kept reporting). Hero heights raised to compensate for the ~100pt now sitting behind the status bar + nav bar: `ArtistDetailSearchView` 260 → 340, Shelf's `ArtistDetailView` 220 → 300.
  Shelf's hero needed no structural change — unlike `ArtistDetailSearchView`'s, it already had the photo inside an `.overlay` and its button in an after-the-frame `.overlay`, which is precisely why its "Full discography" button never suffered the Round 9.7 clipping bug.
  **On-device unverified** — needs confirmation that the photo reaches the very top with no purple band, the artist name is visible, and that BOTH entry paths behave (a normal push from Search/Home/Shelf, and the sheet from Now Playing's artist tap — the context that specifically broke last time).

- **2026-08-07 — Round 9.7: ROOT CAUSE FOUND for the long-running "artist name missing" bug (a ZStack sizing problem, not a rendering/legibility one), plus the discography album loop fixed by resolving grid taps locally — build-green.**
  **(1) The missing artist name — actually root-caused, after several rounds of wrongly treating it as a contrast/z-order/stale-build problem.** The hero was one `ZStack` containing the photo AND the title as siblings, with `.frame(height: 260).clipped()` applied to the whole stack. But `AsyncImage` + `.scaledToFill()` has a natural size FAR larger than 260pt, and **a `ZStack` sizes itself to its largest child** — so the stack was hundreds of points tall, the `.bottomLeading`-aligned title anchored to the bottom of THAT (well below the 260pt frame), and `.clipped()` then removed it entirely. This also retroactively explains the earlier status-bar-bleed screenshots, where the title appeared exactly half-on/half-off the hero's bottom boundary: same bug, slightly different effective geometry. Every previous theory (contrast, z-order, empty data, stale build) was wrong because the title was genuinely being laid out correctly — just far outside the visible box. **Fix:** the photo now goes in an `.overlay` on a base already constrained to 260pt (so it can never inflate the layout), `.clipped()` applies there, and the gradient + title go in a SECOND `.overlay` applied AFTER the clip — sized to the real 260pt box, impossible to clip away. This is exactly the structure Shelf's own `ArtistDetailView` hero uses for its "Full discography" button, which has rendered correctly this entire time — a working in-repo reference that was sitting right there and should have been compared against much earlier. Title restored to plain white-on-gradient (no pill) per the user's preference for the earlier look, with a drop shadow for contrast.
  **Lesson worth keeping:** when a view is confirmed present in source, compiling, and correctly z-ordered but still invisible, suspect LAYOUT (something sizing/positioning it outside the visible bounds) before suspecting rendering, data, or the build. A `.scaledToFill()` image as a `ZStack` sibling is a specific, repeatable instance of this — it silently inflates the container's size for every other child.
  **(2) Discography album loop ("tap album → See all list again, endlessly").** Round 9.6 replaced the two-item-based-bindings arrangement (which caused a back button that skipped a level) with item-based-for-grid + type-based-for-album, both declared on `ArtistDetailSearchView` — which produced this new infinite loop instead. Rather than keep permuting which binding style lives on that one view, grid taps are now resolved LOCALLY: `DiscographyGridView` declares its own `.navigationDestination(for: AlbumResult.self)`, and since closest-ancestor-wins, an album tapped in the grid pushes as a child of the grid — correct back behavior by construction, and it never interacts with the artist view's item/type binding pair at all. The artist view keeps its own type-based album destination for the carousel and features-on taps (which only fire when the grid isn't pushed, so the problematic combination can't arise there).
  **On-device unverified** — needs confirmation that the artist name now renders on both entry paths (search push and Now Playing sheet), and that all three album entry points (carousel, features-on, grid) open the album and return to the right place on back.

- **2026-08-07 — Round 9.6: discography-grid back button regression fixed (a NEW bug from Round 9.1's own fix), and the missing-artist-name mystery given a robust, higher-contrast treatment after ruling out every code-level explanation I could find — build-green, 112/112 NodalongKit tests unaffected.**
  **Back button, discography → See all → tap album → back landed on the ARTIST PAGE, skipping the grid entirely.** This is a genuine regression from Round 9.1's own fix for the original "tap loops back to the same page" bug. Root cause, now better understood: that fix gave `AlbumResult` navigation its OWN separate `@State selectedAlbum: AlbumResult?` + `.navigationDestination(item:)`, declared on `ArtistDetailSearchView` ALONGSIDE the pre-existing, already-working `discographyGridTab: DiscographyTab?` + its own `.navigationDestination(item:)` — i.e. TWO independent item-based destination bindings on the SAME screen. That combination is what actually broke: popping the innermost (Album) push correctly cleared `selectedAlbum`, but appears to have also disturbed `discographyGridTab`'s own bookkeeping, popping TWO stack levels instead of one. Rebuilt around the pattern SwiftUI is actually designed for "reachable from multiple points, correct depth at each": a single, LOCAL, TYPE-based `.navigationDestination(for: AlbumResult.self)` on `ArtistDetailSearchView` (closest-ancestor-wins, so it takes precedence over the ambient registrations on Home/Search/Shelf/the player's sheet without needing to touch any of them), with `NavigationLink(value: album)` restored at all three tap sites (discography carousel, features-on carousel, and inside `DiscographyGridView` — its `onSelectAlbum` closure parameter removed again). `discographyGridTab`'s own item-based binding is untouched — it was never reported broken on its own, only in combination with the second one.
  **Missing artist name — exhaustively checked, no code-level explanation found, so the rendering approach was changed rather than continuing to assert the old one was fine.** User confirmed a genuinely clean build (Clean Build Folder → build → run) across multiple rounds, still no title visible on either the search-accessed or Now-Playing-sheet-accessed artist page. Checked and ruled out: a duplicate/stale `ArtistDetailSearchView.swift` (confirmed exactly one file in the project via `find`), the file's actual compiled content (re-read directly, `Text(artist.name)` present and unchanged), a fresh isolated `xcodebuild` (compiles and links cleanly), Z-ordering within the `ZStack` (`Text` is the LAST child, so topmost by construction), an empty-string `artist.name` (the same `ArtistResult.name` field renders correctly elsewhere on the identical screen, in the "Similar artists" row labels, ruling out a data-level cause). With no further code-level lead, the white-text-on-gradient rendering was replaced with a solid `Color.black.opacity(0.55)` pill behind the name (`RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: 12)`) — both a strictly more robust, guaranteed-contrast rendering regardless of what's behind it, and a sharper diagnostic: if a solid pill still doesn't render, that's much stronger evidence of something deeper than a legibility issue.
  **On-device unverified for both** — needs the user to confirm the full discography flow (carousel tap, features-on tap, AND grid tap, each followed by back) now returns to the correct place at each step, and to report plainly whether the artist name now shows inside a solid pill or is still entirely absent — the latter would mean this needs a different kind of investigation than more source-reading can provide.

- **2026-08-07 — Round 9.5: artist hero simplified per direct product decision (bleed effect dropped after four failed attempts), and the SAME navigation-loop bug found and fixed a second time on Profile's playlist tiles — build-green, 112/112 NodalongKit tests unaffected.**
  **Hero bleed — abandoned, by explicit choice, not by further guessing.** The Round 9.4 measured-geometry version broke a FOURTH way: correct when `ArtistDetailSearchView` is reached via a normal push (Search/Home/Shelf), but visibly wrong (small, oddly cropped, rounded-corner artifacts) specifically when reached inside `NowPlayingView`'s `.sheet` (the artist-tap flow) — sheets have their own presentation-layer coordinate-space behavior that `GeometryReader`'s `.global` frame doesn't uniformly reflect across contexts. Rather than attempt a fifth theory, this was surfaced directly via `AskUserQuestion` — four attempts each wrong a different way is exactly the "stop guessing, get better information or a decision" situation this project's own decisions log already names from an earlier, unrelated saga (the playback-freeze bug). **User chose to drop the bleed effect entirely.** Both `ArtistDetailSearchView.heroSection` and Shelf's `ArtistDetailView.heroSection` are now back to a plain, ordinary fixed-height view (260pt / 220pt) with no `ignoresSafeArea`, no `GeometryReader`, no `.offset` — the photo+gradient+title-overlay visual design is unchanged, it simply sits normally below the system status bar / nav bar in every context, like every other screen in the app. `.toolbarBackground(.hidden, for: .navigationBar)` (added in Round 9.2 specifically to support the bleed) was reverted on both screens too, since a plain opaque nav bar is the correct, unremarkable choice once nothing needs to show through it.
  **Profile → tap a playlist → lands back on Profile (revealed correctly only after popping back) — the exact same failure mode as the discography-grid loop bug, confirmed by the user's own comparison.** Root cause, by direct analogy: `ProfileView` is itself reached via a BOOLEAN `.navigationDestination(isPresented:)` push (`ProfileEntryPoint`, the avatar button used app-wide) — stacking a TYPE-based `NavigationLink(value: playlist)` + ambient `.navigationDestination(for: Playlist.self)` push on top of that boolean-pushed screen is the same combination that broke album navigation on the artist page. Fixed with the identical, now-proven pattern: `PlaylistsProfileSection` gained an `onSelectPlaylist: (Playlist) -> Void` closure, replacing its internal `NavigationLink(value: playlist)` with a plain `Button`; `ProfileView` gained a local `@State private var selectedPlaylist: NodalongKit.Playlist?` + `.navigationDestination(item: $selectedPlaylist)`, replacing its old `.navigationDestination(for: Playlist.self)`. (Needed an explicit `NodalongKit.Playlist` qualification on the new `@State` type annotation — `ProfileView` also imports `MusicKit`, which declares its own unrelated `Playlist` type; the old `NavigationLink(value:)`/`.navigationDestination(for:)` call sites had always been disambiguated implicitly by the surrounding array-element type, but a bare explicit type annotation has nothing to infer from.) Deliberately scoped to `ProfileView`'s OWN playlist rail only — `UserProfileView`'s separate inline `NavigationLink(value: playlist)` (for viewing someone ELSE's playlists) uses a different presentation pathway (not `ProfileEntryPoint`'s boolean push) and wasn't reported broken, so left untouched rather than preemptively "fixed" without evidence.
  **Worth flagging for a future session, not fixed now:** `PlaylistTile`'s own artwork sizing ALSO uses a `GeometryReader` nested inside what's now a `Button` label (`PlaylistsProfileSection.swift`) — the SAME shape of thing that caused the discography grid's separate, ALREADY-FIXED tap-target-collapse bug (a different symptom from this round's loop bug, but the same risky pattern). Not touched this round since it wasn't implicated in what was actually reported, and `PlaylistTile` is deliberately reused across both a fixed-width rail and a flexible grid context (per its own doc comment), so it can't be replaced with a plain computed width as easily as the grid's cells were.
  **On-device unverified for both** — needs the user to confirm the artist hero now looks like a normal (non-bleeding) screen in every context including from Now Playing, and that tapping a playlist from Profile now opens the playlist directly instead of looping back.

- **2026-08-07 — Round 9.4: artist hero rebuilt a THIRD time, this time using measured geometry instead of an assumed formula — build-green.**
  Two prior attempts (Round 9.1's `frame(height:) + ignoresSafeArea`; Round 9.2/9.3's `.background(alignment: .bottom)` decouple) were each built on a specific mental model of how `ignoresSafeArea` repositions a fixed-height view relative to its `VStack` siblings — and both were contradicted by on-device screenshots (a purple gap still at the top; the title still cut off at the bottom, in slightly different ways each round). Rather than derive a THIRD formula from more reasoning about `ignoresSafeArea`'s semantics — the actual point of failure, given two independently-reasoned formulas both turned out wrong — this version **measures instead of assumes**: `heroSection` wraps the hero in a `GeometryReader`, reads `geo.frame(in: .global).minY` (where this slot ACTUALLY sits on the real screen, whatever the true interaction between the ScrollView/NavigationStack/safe-area stack happens to be — no formula to get wrong), and uses that measured value directly as `bleed` — the visual layer is sized `260 + bleed` tall (or `220 + bleed` for Shelf's `ArtistDetailView`) and shifted up by `.offset(y: -bleed)`, while the OUTER `GeometryReader` itself stays pinned to a plain, untouched `.frame(height: 260)` — so the rest of the screen's layout is built around an unambiguous, unchanging 260pt slot regardless of what the visual layer inside it does. `ignoresSafeArea(edges: .top)` is kept, now only responsible for not letting the offset content get clipped at the safe-area boundary — not for any height/position math, which is the part that kept going wrong. Applied to both `ArtistDetailSearchView` and Shelf's `ArtistDetailView`; the now-unused `keyWindowSafeAreaInsets()` helper (`Nodalong/Shared/UI/DeviceSafeArea.swift`, added in Round 9.1) was deleted since nothing calls it anymore.
  **Known, accepted trade-off, not a bug**: `bleed` is a LIVE `GeometryReader` read, not frozen at first appearance — as the user scrolls down, the hero's measured `minY` decreases toward (and past) 0, so `bleed` shrinks toward 0 over the first ~60-100pt of scrolling. This produces a smooth shrink/parallax-style transition as scrolling begins, similar to how Apple Music's own artist hero behaves, rather than an instant snap — flagged here in case it reads as unexpected rather than intentional.
  **On-device unverified, and flagged plainly**: this is a genuinely different technique from the two previous attempts (measured rather than formula-derived), but given both prior rounds looked correct on paper and were wrong on-device, this needs an actual device check before being trusted — specifically confirm no purple gap at the very top AND the artist name fully visible (not clipped) at the bottom, on the same screen in the same session.

- **2026-08-07 — Round 9.3: marquee ellipsis actually reveals text now, hero title cut-off fixed via decoupled layout, and a real wrong-album-resolved bug fixed (single/feat. edition winning over the true album) — build-green, 112/112 NodalongKit tests pass (3 new).**
  **(1) Marquee — the ellipsis wasn't being replaced, just slid sideways.** Real bug in the previous version's design: the VISIBLE text was still width-constrained (`.frame(maxWidth: .infinity)`, no `fixedSize`), so it was ALREADY truncated with "…" baked into its rendered glyphs at layout time — applying `.offset()` to an already-truncated Text just translates that fixed rendering sideways, it can't reveal characters that were never laid out in the first place. Fixed by using TWO copies of the text, each doing one job: a hidden, plain (non-`fixedSize`) copy negotiates layout exactly like the old `Text` did (unchanged surrounding behavior); the visible copy IS `fixedSize` (renders its true, full, un-truncated width so there's real content to reveal) but is then hard-clamped with `.frame(width:)` — a concrete value read from the hidden copy's measurement, not `maxWidth` — so its true width can't leak into the surrounding layout. `.offset` + `.clipped` on that clamped frame is what actually exposes hidden characters as it scrolls.
  **(2) Hero title cut off at the bottom of the photo.** Root cause: the previous version mixed two concerns into ONE view — "how much space does this reserve in the surrounding `VStack`" and "how far does this visually bleed under the status bar" — both driven by the same `.frame(height: 260 + topInset).ignoresSafeArea(edges: .top)` combo. The exact interaction between `ignoresSafeArea` and a VStack's layout-reservation math for a taller-than-260 view didn't hold the way the math assumed, so the visual layer's actual bottom edge didn't reliably land where the bottom-anchored title's padding assumed it would, cutting the title off right where the next section began. Fixed by fully decoupling the two: a plain `Color.clear.frame(height: 260)` (untouched by `ignoresSafeArea`) is now the ONLY thing that reserves space in the `VStack` — exact, unambiguous, always 260pt. The taller visual layer (photo + scrim + title, `260 + topInset`) is attached via `.background(alignment: .bottom)`, which extends it upward past the spacer's top edge purely through oversized-background-plus-bottom-alignment — a mechanism with no layout-reservation ambiguity at all, since `.background()` never affects its host's reported size. Applied to both `ArtistDetailSearchView` and Shelf's `ArtistDetailView` (the latter's "Full discography" pill button had the identical structural risk, even though it wasn't specifically reported as broken).
  **(3) Wrong album loaded from Jump Back In — a real, separate resolution bug, not a repeat of the earlier "wrong release artwork" fix.** Confirmed via the user's screenshot: tapping "We Will Always Love You" by The Avalanches resolved to "We Will Always Love You (feat. Blood Orange) - Single" (1 track) instead of the actual album. Root cause: `resolveAlbum`'s step 1 (matching the tapped track's own `.albums` relationship) used `albumNamesMatch`, which deliberately tolerates "- Single"/"- EP" store-suffix variance via substring containment (built for a DIFFERENT, opposite bug — a pre-release single whose relationship only listed its upcoming parent album). That same leniency meant: when a recording's own relationship contains ONLY a promotional single/feat. edit sharing the true album's base name (a real, distinct catalog release — not a data error), it wins step 1 by containment and the cascade returns immediately, NEVER trying step 2 (a full catalog search across the WHOLE library by name, which very likely would have found the actual full album as a separate, exact-titled catalog entry). Fixed by adding `albumNamesExactMatch` (`ReleaseMatching.swift`, normalized equality only, no containment leniency — 3 new tests) and reordering the cascade: an EXACT relationship match still wins immediately (unchanged, still the highest-confidence case); otherwise the catalog SEARCH is now tried BEFORE falling back to the relationship's looser containment-level match — giving the search a real chance to find the true album before settling for whatever the one specific recording's own relationship happens to contain. The relationship's containment-match and last-resort steps are both kept, just demoted to steps 3 and 4, preserving the original pre-release-single fix's behavior for that case.
  **On-device unverified for all three** — needs the user to confirm a long title genuinely reveals hidden characters while scrolling (not just sliding an unchanged "…"), the artist name is fully visible (not clipped) at the bottom of both hero photos, and re-tapping "We Will Always Love You" (or any other track whose Jump Back In tile previously resolved to a single/feat. edition) now opens the real, full album.

- **2026-08-07 — Round 9.2: marquee-scrolling song titles, discography album navigation rebuilt around a LOCAL destination (fixing the loop bug for real this time), and the artist-hero "hairline" root-caused to the system nav bar's own opaque layer — build-green, 109/109 NodalongKit tests unaffected.**
  **(1) Marquee title, mini player + full-screen player.** New `Nodalong/Shared/UI/MarqueeText.swift` — a drop-in replacement for `Text(...).lineLimit(1)` that, once its content overflows the space it's given, pauses (1.6s), scrolls left over `overflow/32pt-per-second` to reveal the rest, pauses again, scrolls back, and repeats — matching Apple Music's own now-playing marquee instead of a permanent "…". Measures its true (possibly very wide) intrinsic width via a HIDDEN `fixedSize` clone placed in `.background()` — background never influences its host's own reported size upward, so the clone's width can't leak into the surrounding HStack/VStack negotiation the way a directly-`fixedSize` visible `Text` would (a real risk investigated and avoided, not assumed away). **`MiniPlayerBar.swift`**: swapped the title `Text` for `MarqueeText`, and — since its `HStack` had no explicit width reservation for the title column, just a bare `Text` + a trailing `Spacer()` soaking up leftover space — replaced the `Spacer()` with `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)` on the title/artist `VStack` itself. These are equivalent for a short title (both end with the title column left-aligned near the artwork and buttons pinned to the trailing edge) but the frame version gives `MarqueeText` a concrete, bounded width to actually scroll within, which a Spacer-derived "whatever's left after everyone else" allocation can't reliably expose to a child. **`NowPlayingView.swift`**: swapped the title `Text` for `MarqueeText` directly — its surrounding layout already used `Spacer(minLength: 12)` (not a bare `Spacer()`) specifically to bound the text column's width, per an existing in-code comment from an earlier session ("without it the column can claim unbounded width... clipping the start of each line") — confirming this exact class of "two greedy siblings" layout risk was already known and handled here, so no structural change was needed beyond the direct swap.
  **(2) Discography album taps — the ACTUAL root cause, found after a fix that didn't hold.** The GeometryReader/tap-target fix from the previous round did NOT resolve the reported loop ("tapping an album brings us to the see all albums list again") — same underlying bug, still present. Rather than chase a third theory about `NavigationLink(value:)` + ambient `.navigationDestination(for: AlbumResult.self)` resolution (registered on whichever of four different ancestor stacks — Home/Search/Shelf/the player's sheet — this screen happened to be pushed from), **all album navigation on this screen was rebuilt to be local and explicit**, mirroring the exact `discographyGridTab` pattern that was ALREADY working correctly on this same screen: new `@State private var selectedAlbum: AlbumResult?` + `.navigationDestination(item: $selectedAlbum) { AlbumDetailSearchView(album: $0) }` declared directly on `ArtistDetailSearchView`. The discography carousel, the features-on carousel, and `DiscographyGridView` (which gained a new `onSelectAlbum: (AlbumResult) -> Void` closure parameter) all switched from `NavigationLink(value: album)` to a plain `Button { selectedAlbum = album }`. This sidesteps the whole question of "which ancestor stack resolves `AlbumResult`" instead of trying to answer it — the same local, explicit, item-based approach is what `discographyGridTab` already proved reliable for the retap-to-grid flow. Similar-artists navigation (a different type, `ArtistResult`, no bug reported there) was left on `NavigationLink(value:)` — scoped to the type that was actually broken.
  **(3) Artist hero "hairline"/"doesn't fill the top" — root cause was never the hero at all.** User's own diagnostic prompt ("is the top part seen as its own section? notice the small line separating content from the header — visible on every screenshot, including Home/Feed/Near Me") was the actual lead: that hairline is the SYSTEM NAVIGATION BAR's own background/separator — a completely separate opaque UIKit layer that paints ON TOP of scrolled content, independent of the SwiftUI view hierarchy underneath it. `.ignoresSafeArea(edges: .top)` (shipped in the two previous rounds) only affects the STATUS BAR safe-area inset — it has no effect on the nav bar's own chrome, which is why the hero kept stopping short of the very top no matter how the `ZStack`/frame/clip modifiers were reordered. Fixed by adding `.toolbarBackground(.hidden, for: .navigationBar)` to both `ArtistDetailSearchView` and Shelf's own `ArtistDetailView` — makes the bar's fill and hairline fully transparent for these two screens specifically, letting the hero photo show through uninterrupted all the way to the status bar, matching Apple Music's own artist page. Not touched elsewhere — every other screen's nav bar (Home, Feed, Search, etc.) keeps its normal opaque bar, which is correct for them.
  **On-device unverified for all three** — needs the user to confirm: a long song title now scrolls in both players instead of truncating (and that a SHORT title's layout — position of play/pause/dismiss buttons in the mini player — looks unchanged); tapping through multiple albums from the discography carousel, features-on, AND the "see all" grid all now correctly open that album's track list, repeatedly, without looping back; and both artist hero photos now reach the literal top edge of the screen with no separator line at all.

- **2026-08-07 — Round 9.1: discography grid tap-dead-zone bug fixed, explicit badge redesigned to inline "🅴" per direct request, both artist-hero screens fixed to genuinely bleed under the status bar with a legible title, and a stale-search-result race in `SearchViewModel` fixed — build-green, 109/109 NodalongKit tests unaffected.**
  **(1) Discography grid — "any subsequent tap of an album just brings me to the page im already on."** Root cause: `DiscographyGridView`'s album cells sized their artwork via a `GeometryReader` nested inside a `NavigationLink`'s label, inside a `LazyVGrid` cell — a `GeometryReader` in that exact position is a known source of the link's tap target collapsing to zero on cell reuse (e.g. the grid reappearing after popping back from a viewed album), which matches "works once, then nothing" exactly. `albumCard(_:)` (the carousel version, never reported broken) already avoided this by using a plain computed width instead. Fixed by replacing the `GeometryReader` with a computed `cellWidth` (matching the `LazyVGrid`'s own 2-column/14pt-gap/20pt-padding math) and adding `.contentShape(.rect)` on the cell so the whole row (not just the visibly-rendered content) is a guaranteed tap target.
  **(2) Explicit badge redesigned.** User: the corner-stamped black "E" square "looks weird" — wanted the standard boxed-E convention other apps use, inline with the title rather than on the artwork. Removed the `.overlay(alignment: .bottomTrailing)` corner badge from both `albumCard(_:)` and `DiscographyGridView`'s cells; both now show `"🅴"` (the standard Unicode boxed-E glyph) inline in an `HStack` right after the album name, sized/colored to sit quietly next to the text rather than stamped on the art.
  **(3) Artist hero images — genuinely fixed this time, not just re-flagged.** Root cause, found by reading the actual composition: `.ignoresSafeArea(edges: .top)` was applied to the BASE `Color.bgSurface` layer only, before `.overlay { AsyncImage }` was attached — since a fixed-height view's `ignoresSafeArea` repositions it upward WITHOUT growing it, and the overlay's photo content didn't inherit that repositioning the same way in practice, the placeholder color bled under the status bar while the actual photo stayed confined below it — a purple gap where photo should be. Fixed in both `ArtistDetailSearchView` (catalog artist page) and Shelf's own `ArtistDetailView` (personal-library artist page, which had NO `ignoresSafeArea` at all before this — its "cut off" report was literally that): composed color + photo + gradient scrim + title as ONE `ZStack`, THEN applied `.frame(height: 260/220 + topInset)` → `.clipped()` → `.ignoresSafeArea(edges: .top)` as the LAST modifiers on the whole unit, so everything bleeds together; height is now `base + topInset` (read via a new shared `keyWindowSafeAreaInsets()` in `Nodalong/Shared/UI/DeviceSafeArea.swift`, mirroring `NowPlayingView`'s own established `deviceSafeAreaInsets` pattern — deliberately NOT reused by `NowPlayingView` itself, whose pinned layout stays untouched per this session's own standing rule) instead of a bare constant, since ignoring the safe area repositions rather than grows a fixed-height view — without the compensation, the extra bleed room comes at the cost of showing less photo below the fold, not in addition to it. **`ArtistDetailSearchView` also gained the gradient-scrim-plus-overlaid-title pattern Shelf's `ArtistDetailView` already used successfully** — the artist name moved from a separate plain sibling `Text` below the hero into the hero itself, protected by a `LinearGradient` scrim (clear → black 75%) so it's legible regardless of what's directly behind it, rather than depending on the plain page background always being there. Shelf's `ArtistDetailView` hero was otherwise left alone (its gradient/button overlay was already correct) — just added the missing top bleed and height compensation.
  **(4) `SearchViewModel` stale-result race fixed, found while investigating the reported search slowdown.** `search(via:)` already debounces correctly (300ms sleep + `Task.isCancelled` guard before starting real work) — but nothing re-checked cancellation AFTER the network call returned. A task cancelled mid-flight (superseded by a later keystroke) isn't guaranteed to have its underlying MusicKit/Firestore call actually interrupted — if it completes normally after being superseded, `results`/`userResults` was assigned unconditionally, meaning a stale, older query's answer could silently overwrite a newer, correct one moments after it was shown — visible as results flickering backward while typing, which reads as the whole feature being slow or broken even though each individual request was fine. Fixed by adding `guard !Task.isCancelled else { return }` immediately before both `results = ...` and `userResults = ...` assignments in `perform(...)`, for both the People and Music branches. **Flagged, not changed:** `searchAll`/`searchTracks`/`searchArtists`/`searchAlbums` were all confirmed already efficient — one combined `MusicCatalogSearchRequest` for `.all` scope (not three separate calls), `LibraryViewModel`'s `isInLibrary` checks are `Set`-backed O(1) (not a linear scan), and `LibraryViewModel.load`/`loadFollowing` are already guarded against redundant re-fetching on tab revisit. The debounce and network-call shape look sound from code reading alone — genuine network latency (the reported screenshot showed a weak 2-bar cellular signal) can't be ruled out as a contributing factor and isn't something a code change can fix.
  **On-device unverified for all four** — needs the user to confirm: tapping a second/third album in the discography grid now navigates correctly; the 🅴 badge reads better than the corner stamp; both artist hero photos now visibly reach the very top of the screen with a legible title, on artists with busy/dark photos specifically; and whether the stale-result fix meaningfully reduces the perceived search slowdown, or whether it's still network-latency-bound.

Mockup shown and confirmed via visualize before implementing, per the standing instruction.**
  **Discography retap indicator.** The existing retap-to-grid gesture (round 6) had no visual cue it existed. `ArtistDetailSearchView.discographySection` header row gained an explicit "See all ›" button next to the "Discography" title, wired to the same `discographyGridTab` mechanism — the retap gesture still works too, this just makes the destination discoverable without relying on a hidden second tap.
