New `NodalongKit/Sources/NodalongKit/Utils/AlbumEditionGrouping.swift`: `canonicalAlbumTitle(_:)` strips edition-describing noise ("(Deluxe Edition)", "(Explicit)", "- Remastered 2011", etc. — narrower than the existing `stripSearchNoise`, which also strips featured-artist credits that shouldn't affect edition grouping) so different pressings of the same release collapse to one grouping key; `albumEditionLabel(_:)` extracts a human label from that same noise ("Deluxe Edition"); `dedupeAlbumEditions(_:)` groups an artist's raw album list by (canonical title, artist), picks the primary per the user's explicit product call — **explicit always wins**, then more tracks (a deluxe edition is typically a superset), then original order — and files the rest under a new `AlbumResult.alternates: [AlbumResult]` field, each labeled via content-rating diff ("Clean"/"Explicit") when that's what differs, or `albumEditionLabel` otherwise. 13 new unit tests (`AlbumEditionGroupingTests.swift`), all passing alongside the existing 96. `AlbumResult` gained `isExplicit: Bool`, `editionLabel: String?`, `alternates: [AlbumResult]` — all with default values so every existing call site (Spotify's parallel `toAlbumResult`, `PreviewFixtures`, snapshot tests) compiles unchanged; alternates never nest more than one level deep (each alternate's own `.alternates` is set to `[]`). Wired into `AppleMusicService.getArtistDetails` — the raw album list is run through `dedupeAlbumEditions` before returning, so Discography/the "See all" grid/`fullAlbums`/`singleAlbums` all get deduped albums with zero further plumbing. `toAlbumResult` now sets `isExplicit: a.contentRating == .explicit`. Discography album cards (both the carousel and the grid) gained a small black "E" badge on the artwork's bottom-right corner when `isExplicit == true`, matching Apple's own convention. **Deliberately scoped to Apple's own discography (`getArtistDetails`'s `albums`), not `featuresOn`/Spotify's `getArtistAlbums`** — no reported duplicate-albums complaint there, and Spotify's catalog doesn't split explicit/clean the same way.
  **"Alternative versions" list, at the user's explicit request (not just a single "clean version" link — "list the other versions, Non explicit or Deluxe versions for example").** New section on `AlbumDetailSearchView`, shown only `if !album.alternates.isEmpty`, right below the track list — same `bgSurface`/`clipShape(12)` card style, one row per alternate (artwork thumbnail, name, `editionLabel` caption, chevron), each a `NavigationLink(value: alt)` pushing that alternate's own `AlbumDetailSearchView` — resolves independently via the existing ambient `.navigationDestination(for: AlbumResult.self)` (already registered on every stack this screen can be reached from; no new registration needed, same as how Discography's own album taps already worked before this change).
  **Features on was sorted after Similar artists — the user asked for it above.** One-line reorder in `ArtistDetailSearchView.body`. Its emptiness for some artists (Apple genuinely has no "appears on" data for them) is a real, separate possibility, flagged in the mockup discussion — the reorder doesn't force it to appear, only fixes its position when it does have data.
  **Top Songs extended from 10 to 30**, per direct follow-up ("lets set the top songs be the top 30 if possible"). New `AppleMusicService.paginatedTopSongs(_:limit:)` pages `artist.topSongs` forward via `MusicItemCollection.nextBatch()` until either 30 items are reached or the catalog runs out, bounded to 5 extra round trips regardless of `limit` so a misbehaving `hasNextBatch` can't spin forever. `trackPages`'s existing `stride(by: 5)` needed no change — more `topTracks` just means more pages, rendered by the same `TabView(.page)`.
  **Artist hero image was cut off at the top.** `heroSection`'s fixed-height `Color.bgSurface` container gained `.ignoresSafeArea(edges: .top)` so it bleeds under the status bar and floating back button, matching Apple Music's own artist page — previously it sat flush below the top safe area, leaving a flat purple gap above it.
  **On-device unverified for all six** — needs the user to confirm the "See all" link opens the grid, Similar artists now shows its card, an artist with a known explicit/clean or deluxe duplicate now shows one card with the right badge and an "Alternative versions" list at the bottom of that album's screen, Features on renders above Similar artists, Top Songs now offers up to 6 pages, and the hero image genuinely extends behind the status bar/back button.

Fixed by making `GroupByArtist.swift`'s copy delegate to the same shared `primaryArtist(_:)`, identical to `GroupByAlbum.swift`'s existing fix. Worth a grep for a THIRD undiscovered copy of this same feat.-only splitting logic if this recurs.
  **(2) Home: Jump Back In / Top Charts / Made For You missing the rounded `bgSurface` container** that Recently Added has. Root cause: Recently Added uses the shared `AlbumsCarousel` component (which has its own `.background(bgSurface).clipShape(...)`), but the other three rails (`jumpBackInRail`/`topChartsRail`/`madeForYouRail`) are hand-built directly in `HomeView.swift` and were bare `ScrollView(.horizontal) { HStack {...} }` with no background at all — the shared `section(title:)` wrapper they're passed through has no styling of its own (title + content only), so nothing was ever adding the card. Fixed by giving each of the three rails the identical `.padding(12)` inner / `.background(bgSurface).clipShape(RoundedRectangle(12))` / `.padding(.horizontal, 16)` outer treatment `AlbumsCarousel` already uses. Scoped to exactly the three sections named — "Live Now" and "Friends' Spotlights" (avatar-circle rails, not rectangular album/song tiles) were left untouched since they weren't reported and a card behind circular avatars is a different visual call.
  **(3) Recently Added's real root cause was NOT just the artwork bug from round 3 — it was a second, more serious id-space bug hiding behind it.** On-device: an album resolved with correct header/artwork/title now (round 3's fix working), but "Tracks" rendered completely empty with no shimmer, no error, nothing. Traced: `getRecentlyAddedAlbums`'s `toAlbumResult` uses `MusicKit.Album.id` straight from the `MusicLibraryRequest` response — but a LIBRARY request's `id` is a library-scoped identifier, not a catalog id (the identical id-space distinction already documented elsewhere in this file for library-matched TRACKS). `AlbumDetailSearchView` → `getAlbumTracks` does a CATALOG lookup with that id (`MusicCatalogResourceRequest<Album>(matching: \.id,...)`), which silently returns zero items for a library-scoped id — no error, no crash, just an empty array, which is why the Tracks section showed nothing rather than any kind of failure state. Fixed by extending round 3's existing empty-artwork-triggered catalog search: instead of only backfilling `.artwork` from the found catalog match, the WHOLE library `AlbumResult` entry is now replaced with the catalog match's own complete `AlbumResult` — its `id` is genuinely catalog-compatible (fixes the empty Tracks section), and as a bonus its other fields (track count, release date) reflect the real release rather than just whatever subset happens to be in the user's personal library (e.g. a single showing "1 tracks" when that's just what's saved locally, not the true single's track count).
  **On-device unverified for all three** — needs the user to confirm Shelf's Artists tab now collapses correctly, all three Home rails show the rounded container, and a Recently Added album now shows its actual track list.

Wired via `@State discographyGridTab: DiscographyTab?` + `.navigationDestination(item:)` (iOS 17+, fine at this project's 26.1 minimum) rather than a `NavigationLink`, since the trigger is a `Button` action, not a direct link tap.

- **2026-08-07 — Round 4: cut album/artist MusicKit round trips in half app-wide via `properties`, not just the already-optimized `ResolvingAlbumView` paths — build-green.** User asked generally "how can we speed up album track loads." Investigation found a real gap in the loading-elimination work earlier this session: it only sped up the THREE `ResolvingAlbumView` call sites (Jump Back In, player tap, Shelf) — but Search results, Home's Recommended/Made For You/Recently Added rails, and artist-page discography all push an already-known `AlbumResult` DIRECTLY to `AlbumDetailSearchView` (`.navigationDestination(for: AlbumResult.self)`, no resolve step at all) — these are very likely the MOST COMMON way an album actually gets opened, and none of them benefited from anything shipped earlier today.
  **Root fix, orthogonal to caching: `getAlbumTracks`/`getArtistDetails`/`resolveDetailedSong`'s id+isrc branches each did TWO sequential MusicKit round trips** (`MusicCatalogResourceRequest(...).response()` to fetch the resource, THEN a separate `.with([...])` call to hydrate its relationships) where ONE suffices. Verified via the project's established `xcrun swiftc -typecheck` probe convention (not assumed) that `MusicCatalogResourceRequest.properties` can request relationships as part of the SAME request — confirmed working for `Album.tracks`, `Artist.albums`/`.topSongs`, and `Song.albums`/`.artists` (both id- and isrc-matched lookups); confirmed NOT available on `MusicCatalogSearchRequest` (no `properties` member — that text-search fallback branch stays two round trips, the least-hit tier of the cascade anyway). Applied `properties` to all four fetches, replacing the request→`.with([...])` pattern with request→read-directly. Cuts round trips roughly in half for: every cold `AlbumDetailSearchView` open (the Search/Home-rails/Shelf/artist-discography paths above), every artist-detail open, and the id/isrc branches of `resolveAlbum`/`resolveArtists` (which — since `resolveAlbum` ALSO calls the now-faster `getAlbumTracks` as its own prewarm step — makes the `ResolvingAlbumView` paths faster on top of round 2's caching, not instead of it).
  **On-device unverified** — needs the user to confirm album/artist screens (especially ones reached via Search/Home rails, which see this fix for the first time today) feel meaningfully faster on first (uncached) open.

- **2026-08-07 — Round 3 follow-up: "Recently Added" artwork was genuinely EMPTY, not just mis-rendered — backfilled from the catalog, build-green, 96/96 NodalongKit tests pass.** The `ArtworkThumbnail` fix above was confirmed correct (no more infinite shimmer) but on-device it surfaced the real problem underneath: every "Recently Added" tile showed a flat "no artwork" placeholder, including inside the album detail screen — the underlying `AlbumResult.artwork` was genuinely `""`, not just mis-displayed. Root cause: `getRecentlyAddedAlbums` uses `MusicLibraryRequest<Album>` (the user's personal library), and `MusicKit.Album.artwork` from a library request very often resolves to a device-local `musicKit://` reference rather than a fetchable https CDN URL — `toAlbumResult`/`artworkURL` already correctly filters that to `""` (the fix that's been correct all along), but nothing ever backfilled a REAL catalog artwork URL in its place, unlike the equivalent track-level fix (`resolveCatalogArtwork`) shipped in an earlier session.
  **Fix:** `getRecentlyAddedAlbums` now backfills, concurrently, any returned album whose artwork came back empty by looking up its real catalog counterpart via `searchAlbumCatalog(named:artist:)` — reusing the exact release-matching helper `resolveAlbum` already uses (no new resolution logic). A catalog hit's artwork is always a real https URL; the library item's own identity/metadata (id, track count, etc.) is kept, only the artwork field is swapped in from the catalog match. Required making `NodalongKit.AlbumResult.artwork` a `var` (was `let`) so it can be updated in place post-fetch — confirmed via `swift test` this doesn't break `Codable`/`Hashable` synthesis (96/96 still pass, no test changes needed).
  **On-device unverified** — needs the user to confirm Recently Added now shows real album covers instead of flat purple tiles, and that the fix doesn't meaningfully slow down Home's load (worst case is `limit` concurrent catalog searches, same concurrency pattern as the rest of this session's prefetch work).

- **2026-08-07 — Round 3: found a real `ArtworkThumbnail` bug affecting "Recently Added" (and any other caller with genuinely empty artwork), fixed a mini-player spin edge case, removed the replay-count caption — build-green.**
  **"Recently Added" perpetual shimmer — real bug, not a network/loading issue.** Traced `getRecentlyAddedAlbums` → `MusicLibraryRequest<Album>` (the user's personal library, not the catalog) → `toAlbumResult` → `artworkURL(_:)`, which already correctly filters `musicKit://` on-device-only artwork references to `""` (an existing, working fix). The bug was one level up: `ArtworkThumbnail` (`ShelfView.swift`) passed `sizedURL.isEmpty ? nil : URL(...)` into `AsyncImage` — but `AsyncImage` with a `nil` URL is Apple-documented to stay in `.empty` PERMANENTLY (nothing to load, so it never transitions to `.success` or `.failure`), and this component's switch mapped `.empty` → `ShimmerView()` — i.e. any track/album with genuinely no fetchable artwork showed a shimmer that would spin forever, never resolving to the "no artwork" placeholder. Library-sourced albums (Recently Added's whole data source) are exactly the class most likely to hit the `musicKit://` filter, hence the section being the most visible victim.
  **Notably: this exact bug class was already found and fixed once before, in a DIFFERENT component** — `TrackCard.swift`'s 2026-07-05 decisions-log entry ("empty artwork now skips AsyncImage entirely... instead of sitting in .empty forever") shipped the identical fix for `TrackCard`'s own artwork, but `ArtworkThumbnail` — a second, separate shared artwork component used by Home/Shelf/Search — never got the same treatment. Applied the same fix here: check `sizedURL.isEmpty` before ever calling `AsyncImage`, rendering the `bgElevated` placeholder directly for a genuinely empty URL instead of routing a `nil` through `AsyncImage` at all. Worth a grep for any THIRD undiscovered artwork-rendering call site with the same pattern if this recurs elsewhere.
  **Mini-player spin escaping its circular mask during the `.inline`/`.expanded` tab-bar-placement transition.** User-reported: scrolling causes the persistent player bar to animate between merged-into-tab-bar and floating-pill placements (`@Environment(\.tabViewBottomAccessoryPlacement)`), and during that system-driven transition the always-rotating disc could visibly render outside its circle. Root cause not fully certain (Apple's placement-transition internals are private), but the mechanism is plausible: the disc's rotation is driven by an independent 30fps `TimelineView` on its own wall-clock schedule, decoupled from the system's own transition timing — a rotated circle is rotationally invariant and stays bounded under normal uniform layout, but a system-driven asymmetric resize/reposition happening mid-flight, at the same instant as an unrelated rotation update, could momentarily desync the inner clip's geometry from what the rotation assumes. Fixed defensively: added a second `.clipShape(Circle())` on the OUTER (correctly system-tracked) 44×44 frame, on top of the existing inner one — purely additive, cannot change steady-state behavior, guarantees the final render can never show content past that boundary regardless of what the inner content does mid-transition.
  **Removed the "🔁 Played N×" caption** under Jump Back In tiles per direct request — `RecentAlbum.totalReplayCount` and the underlying replay-tracking logic in `NodalongKit` are untouched (still computed, just no longer displayed); only the `HomeView.recentAlbumTile` caption was deleted.
  **On-device unverified for all three** — needs the user to confirm Recently Added now shows real artwork (or the "no artwork" placeholder) instead of eternal shimmer, the mini player's disc stays within its circle through a scroll-triggered placement transition, and the Played caption is gone.

- **2026-08-07 — Follow-up round 2: unified the resolving/loaded album screens into one continuous visual (per explicit user feedback on round 1), and fixed a real "Save to Shelf" delay — build-green.**
  **Album screen unification.** User feedback on round 1's fix: keep the resolving screen's 160pt centered artwork size exactly as-is ("looks like the right size"), but make the REAL album screen (`AlbumDetailSearchView`, previously an 80pt inline thumbnail in a small HStack) match it, and stop showing a separate spinner screen at all. `AlbumDetailSearchView`'s header is now a centered `VStack` at 160pt (`static let heroArtworkSize`, deliberately non-`private` so `ResolvingAlbumView` references the exact same constant and the two can never drift apart). `ResolvingAlbumView`'s unresolved-state placeholder was rewritten from a standalone `VStack` + spinner to the SAME `List`/`Section`/shimmer-row structure as the loaded screen (same header size/position, "Tracks" section with `ShimmerView` rows instead of a spinner) — so resolving → loaded is now a header that doesn't move plus shimmer rows becoming real rows, not a jump between two different loading styles.
  **"Save to Shelf" delay — root cause and fix.** `LibraryViewModel.saveAlbum` runs `ensureCatalogId(forLibrary:)` for every unsaved track BEFORE its "optimistic" UI update — and `ensureCatalogId` had zero caching, meaning every tap did a real Firestore transaction (`upsertCatalogTrack`) per track, concurrently but still all-must-complete, before the button could flip. The codebase already has `TrackCache` (isrc/title+artist → catalogId) built for exactly this, but it was wired ONLY into `LibrarySyncService`'s recents-sync path — `ensureCatalogId(for song:)` (Catalog.swift) and `ensureCatalogId(forLibrary:)` (Library.swift) never checked or populated it. Fixed by adding the same lookup-then-upsert-then-store pattern `LibrarySyncService.ingestToCatalogOnly` already uses to both functions. Then extended `resolveAlbum`'s existing track-list prefetch (from round 1) to ALSO prewarm `TrackCache` for the whole album via a background (NOT awaited) `Task` calling `ensureCatalogId(for:)` per track — deliberately not blocking the resolver's return, since awaiting ~10 more Firestore transactions there would have undone round 1's "one continuous load" fix. By the time a user has looked at the track list and decided to tap Save, the transactions have normally already completed quietly in the background, so `ensureCatalogId(forLibrary:)` finds a cache hit instead of a fresh round trip. Best-effort throughout — any failure just falls back to the original (slower but correct) per-tap resolution.
  **On-device unverified — needs the user to confirm both**: album screens (all three entry points — Jump Back In, player album tap, Shelf) now show one continuous load with a stable-sized header, and Save Album flips noticeably faster after having viewed the track list for a few seconds.

- **2026-08-07 — Two follow-up UX fixes after the freeze fix landed, both build-green.**
  **(1) `ResolvingAlbumView`/`ResolvingArtistView`/`ResolvingArtistsView` black bands.** Their unresolved-state placeholder sized its purple background to its own content only (`.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .top)` — width fills, height doesn't; `.background()` applied after). Everything above/below the content-sized box fell through to the plain system background — black in dark mode — producing black bands sandwiching a purple island, confirmed via a user screenshot whose proportions matched the code exactly. Every other full-screen view in the app fills available space some other way (`ScrollView`, `List`); these three were the only ones that didn't. Fix: added `maxHeight: .infinity` to all three. On-device confirmed fixed by the user.
  **(2) "Double load" feel when opening an album.** Real, not a bug: `ResolvingAlbumView` resolves WHICH album (via `AppleMusicService.resolveAlbum`), then swaps to `AlbumDetailSearchView`, which runs its OWN separate `getAlbumTracks` fetch with its own skeleton rows — two genuinely sequential network round trips (can't fetch an album's tracks before knowing which album), shown as two different loading UIs back to back. Fixed by prewarming `MusicDetailCache` with the track list INSIDE `resolveAlbum` itself, awaited before it returns — so the combined wait is covered by `ResolvingAlbumView`'s one spinner, and `AlbumDetailSearchView` mounts already populated (or finds the fetch already in-flight, since `MusicDetailCache.tracks` shares in-flight work — no duplicate fetch). Single change point: all three `ResolvingAlbumView` call sites (Now Playing's album tap, Home's Jump Back In, Shelf) already funnel through `resolveAlbum`, so this fixes all of them at once rather than needing three separate call-site edits. Scoped to the Apple path only (Spotify's fallback `searchAll` in each caller is untouched — suspended feature, doesn't share `MusicDetailCache`'s cache-hit path anyway). **On-device unverified — needs the user to confirm the two-spinner sequence is now one.**

- **2026-08-07 — ✅ FREEZE ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED (this entry supersedes every freeze analysis below it, including the "correction" entry that follows).**
  **Cause: `syncNativeQueueNeighbors()`'s PREVIOUS-neighbour branch mutated `player.queue.entries` directly.** MusicKit's `MusicPlayer.Queue.EntryInsertionPosition` only offers `.afterCurrentEntry` and `.tail` — there is no "before current" — so an earlier session worked around it by reaching into the live queue's backing array: `player.queue.entries.insert(Queue.Entry(song), at: currentIdx)`. That corrupts the queue's internal state, after which **any** subsequent player access blocks forever in `-[MPMusicPlayerController playbackState]` → `_establishConnectionIfNeeded` → a synchronous XPC wait on `mediaplayerd` that never returns. Because that call is synchronous and on the main thread, the entire app locks with no recovery. The NEXT-neighbour branch in the same function was always fine — it uses the sanctioned `try await player.queue.insert(_:position:)`.
  **Fix: delete the previous-neighbour branch, keep the next-neighbour branch.** Control Center's *next* still advances properly; Control Center's *previous* restarts the current track (which is also Apple Music's own behaviour unless you're within the first few seconds). No other code change — this is a deletion, not a mitigation.
  **How it was actually found, after FOUR failed code-reading-driven fixes (see the superseded entry below):** two pieces of evidence, both from the user, neither obtainable from the console logs. (1) **A main-thread backtrace captured during a live freeze** (Xcode → Debug → Pause → `bt`), which named the blocking frame outright — the console had only ever shown a generic `_establishConnectionIfNeeded timeout` that was identical across every hypothesis and therefore discriminated nothing. (2) **Behavioural asymmetries**: Feed taps (single-item queue ⇒ nothing mirrored) never froze, album/Top-Charts taps (multi-item queue ⇒ neighbours mirrored) froze from the second tap on, in-app *next* and Control Center next/prev kept working. Those pointed at mirroring; a clean A/B build with mirroring disabled confirmed it; inspecting the two branches then isolated the raw-`entries` mutation as the only unsanctioned player access.
  **Also worth recording: `MPMusicPlayerController.playbackState` is a BLOCKING synchronous XPC call**, not a cached local value. An earlier entry below asserts the opposite ("locally-cached published values that never blocked") and used that to justify leaving the progress timer's per-tick reads alone — that assertion is **wrong**. The timer polling it at 2–10 Hz on the main thread is safe only while the queue's internal state is intact; it is the mechanism by which a corrupted queue turns into a frozen UI. If a freeze ever recurs without raw `entries` mutation being reintroduced, the timer's polling is the next thing to move off the main thread / replace with state observation.
  **Process lesson:** when a fix doesn't land, get a backtrace or a differential repro before theorising again. Four consecutive fixes were built on inference from a log that could not distinguish between causes, each was shipped with confident "root-caused" language, and each was wrong. The cost was a full session and a hand-revert of `PlayerViewModel.swift` (this repo has NO commits — a baseline commit would have made that a `git checkout`).

- **2026-08-07 — ⚠️ SUPERSEDED by the entry above; retained only as a record of what was ruled out.** The sub-entries labelled (2), (2c) and (2d) below each claim to have "root-caused" the playback freeze. **All three were wrong**, and are kept only as a record of what was ruled out. They were written from console logs + code reading, with confident language that the evidence did not support — do not trust their conclusions. What they got wrong, in order: (2) blamed an unprotected synchronous `player.queue = [...]` write blocking the main thread; (2c) blamed `syncNativeQueueNeighbors`' synchronous reads as an additional unprotected site; (2d) blamed a cross-thread deadlock caused by (2)/(2c)'s own background-threading "fix" (that last one was at least directionally right that the background-threading was harmful — it has been reverted).
  **The actual mechanism was identified by the USER, from four behavioural observations no log revealed:** Feed taps never freeze; album/Top-Charts taps freeze on the *second* tap onwards; in-app *next* works repeatedly; Control Center next/prev work. The common factor: **freezes happen only when MusicKit's NATIVE queue holds more than one entry.** Feed builds a single-item queue ⇒ `syncNativeQueueNeighbors` mirrors nothing ⇒ native queue stays single-entry ⇒ `player.queue = [song]` replacement is safe. Album/Top Charts build a multi-item queue ⇒ neighbours get mirrored into the native queue ⇒ the next tap-to-play replaces a **live multi-entry native queue**, and *that* is what wedges MusicKit's XPC connection. `next` was unaffected because it moves `currentEntry` within the existing queue rather than replacing it.
  **Two further fixes were attempted from this mechanism and BOTH FAILED on-device — the mechanism above is therefore also unproven, and possibly wrong.** (i) Disabling neighbour-mirroring entirely (restoring the strict single-entry invariant): fixed nothing, and was in any case correctly rejected by the user as an unacceptable trade since it degrades Control Center next/prev to restart-current. (ii) `player.stop()` before the `player.queue = [...]` assignment in `playCurrent()`, so any replacement lands on an idle player, keeping mirroring enabled: also fixed nothing. The album/Top-Charts repro (play a track, then tap a different track in the same list) still fails identically after both.
  **ALL of this session's `PlayerViewModel.swift` changes have been REVERTED** — `playerConnectionSuspect`, `nativeQueueMirroringEnabled`, `advanceViaMirroredEntry`, the stop-before-replace, and every associated guard. The file is back to its pre-session state and the freeze is back to its original, pre-session behaviour. Nothing here was an improvement; the revert is the honest end state.
  **What is actually established (and should be the starting point for whoever picks this up):** the freeze is real and predates this session. It correlates with the app queue holding MORE THAN ONE item — Feed taps (single-item queue) never fail; album / Top Charts taps (multi-item queue) fail on the second tap onwards. That correlation is solid, reported consistently by the user across many runs. What is NOT established is the causal mechanism: four separate theories (unprotected synchronous main-thread write; additional unprotected sync sites; cross-thread deadlock; live-multi-entry-queue replacement) were each implemented and each failed. **Do not attempt a fifth code-reading-driven theory.** The console logs are non-discriminating — MusicKit reports the same generic `_establishConnectionIfNeeded timeout` / `xpc service connection interrupted` regardless of cause — so more log-reading will not resolve this.
  **Suggested next step instead:** get a real thread backtrace at the moment of the freeze (Xcode → Debug → Pause, or a spindump), which shows precisely which call is blocked and on what. That is the one piece of evidence that would discriminate between the remaining possibilities, and it costs one repro. Also worth ruling out environment: EVERY log from this session contains `ICError -7013 "Client is not entitled to access account store"`, `ACAccountStore: Failed to fetch the iTunes accounts`, and `AMSAcknowledgePrivacyTask: Privacy acknowledgement is needed` — Apple's account daemon refusing to resolve any Apple ID for this app, which would leave MusicKit's subscription/playback state permanently unresolved. The user reports testing on a real device, but that signature is not normal for a healthy signed-in device with an active subscription and should be checked directly (Settings → Media & Purchases; MusicKit enabled for the App ID under App Services).
  **Process lesson for future sessions:** four rounds of code-reading-driven fixes made this worse, because MusicKit's failure surfaced as a generic `_establishConnectionIfNeeded timeout` regardless of cause, so the logs could not discriminate between hypotheses. The behavioural asymmetries (which surfaces break, which don't) were the only discriminating evidence, and they came from the user. When a fix doesn't land, prefer asking for a differential repro over inferring a new mechanism from the same undiscriminating log.

- **2026-08-07** — **Same-day on-device bug round after the loading pass: player freeze analysis (SUPERSEDED — see correction above), album tap resolving to the wrong release (compilation vs. real album) fixed, Made For You duplicate-ID ForEach warning fixed — build-green.** User rebuilt on their real device and reported: (1) second Top Charts play froze the entire app; (2) pressing next froze the app (next track's metadata appeared first); (3) tapping the album name on the big player opened a WRONG album (a "NOW That's What I Call Music!" compilation instead of the track's own album); plus pasted console logs showing the known `_establishConnectionIfNeeded timeout [ping did not pong]` / `xpc service connection interrupted` signature and a `ForEach<Array<PlaylistResult>>` duplicate-ID warning.
  **(1)+(2) The freeze — a genuinely NEW understanding of the long-running XPC flakiness, not another mitigation of the same theory.** Every prior mitigation (the `withTimeout` on `play()`, the foreground staleness reset — see the 2026-07-28 entries) assumed the hang was an *async suspension* a `Task` race could bound. Tracing the exact code paths against the new logs showed the real mechanism is **synchronous main-thread blocking**: `player.queue = [musicKitSong]` (`playCurrent()`) is a plain synchronous property write that blocks the calling thread inside MusicKit's `_establishConnectionIfNeeded` when the XPC connection to the media daemon is dead — it sat OUTSIDE the `withTimeout` wrapper (which only covered the async `play()` on the next line), and no `Task` timeout can interrupt a synchronous call anyway. Compounding it, the progress timer's ApplicationMusicPlayer branch reads `state.playbackStatus` + `playbackTime` + `queue.currentEntry` — three more synchronous XPC-backed accesses — every 0.5s on the main thread, re-wedging the UI tick after tick once the connection dies (`Gesture: System gesture gate timed out` in the user's log is the classic blocked-main-thread symptom), and `syncNativeQueueNeighbors()` does sync `queue.entries` reads/inserts. Why "second song"/"next" specifically: the first play establishes the connection and works; the log shows `xpc service connection interrupted` shortly after, so the next play action is the first synchronous touch of a dead connection. **Fix (user-approved A+B, `PlayerViewModel.swift`):** (A) the queue assignment moved INSIDE the existing `withTimeout(seconds: 8)` closure — the `@Sendable` task-group child runs off the main actor, so a dead-connection block now stalls a background thread (the stuck sync XPC call itself can't be cancelled — accepted) for a bounded wait and falls into the existing preview-fallback path instead of freezing the UI; (B) new `playerConnectionSuspect` circuit-breaker flag — set when the play timeout trips (`error is PlaybackTimeoutError`), gates the progress timer's entire ApplicationMusicPlayer branch AND `syncNativeQueueNeighbors()` so nothing touches the dead connection's synchronous surface, cleared only by the next `play()` that actually succeeds (demonstrated recovery). **Known residual, flagged not fixed:** a connection dying mid-playback could still block the timer ONCE before any timeout has armed the breaker — fully closing that means moving every tick's reads into a background snapshot returning only Sendable data, a real refactor of the queue-reconciliation logic; deferred unless a freeze recurs from pure idle ticking (both observed freezes started from a play/next action, which A now bounds).
  **(2d) FOURTH freeze report — "no sound at all, total lockup, no recovery" — invalidated the background-threading approach itself, not just its coverage. A real device (confirmed by the user), so the earlier no-account theory doesn't explain this one.** The user reported the freeze as unrecoverable even after waiting, which contradicts a bounded (4–8s) timeout ever firing — meaning the background-thread `withTimeout` wrapping from (2)/(2c) wasn't just incomplete, it was plausibly the WRONG mechanism. Reasoning: `ApplicationMusicPlayer` compiles clean as a bare synchronous property access from a nonisolated `@Sendable` closure (confirmed: no `await`/`MainActor.run` required anywhere), which is strong evidence it's not `@MainActor`-isolated at the Swift type-system level — but Swift's actor system isn't the only thing that can enforce thread affinity. Apple's older media-player frameworks routinely have undocumented main-thread requirements enforced below the Swift layer (XPC proxy / ObjC runtime), independent of what the type checker permits. If that's true here, touching the player from a background thread (my fix) WHILE the main-thread progress timer also touches it (unavoidable — the timer can't be paused mid-tick) creates a genuine cross-thread deadlock: one thread stuck inside the dead connection, another also stuck contending for the same internal resource, neither ever returning — which matches "no sound, total lockup, no recovery" exactly, and is WORSE than the original single-threaded block (which, being on one thread, would eventually either succeed or throw once the OS's own internal retry/timeout resolved).
  **The decisive, independent clue: the user reported Control Center's own next/previous buttons work fine on the same device, in the same broken-connection state, right when the in-app button doesn't.** This isn't circumstantial — it's a real difference in *what MusicKit operation runs*, not a threading question. `syncNativeQueueNeighbors`'s own doc comment (written earlier this session) already states Control Center's next/previous bypass the app's registered `MPRemoteCommandCenter` targets entirely and are handled internally by MusicKit, moving `player.queue.currentEntry` within its EXISTING native queue — a lightweight in-place operation. Our own `skipToNext()`/`skipToPrevious()`/any tap-to-play instead always funnel through `playCurrent()`, which does `player.queue = [singleSong]` — a full queue TEARDOWN AND REBUILD, every single advance, even when the target track was already sitting pre-mirrored in the native queue for exactly this purpose. Verified `ApplicationMusicPlayer.Queue.currentEntry` is genuinely settable via a throwaway `xcrun swiftc -typecheck` probe (clean compile) before writing any code, per this project's established verification discipline.
  **Fix, two parts:** (a) REVERTED all background-thread `withTimeout` wrapping around synchronous player calls introduced in (2)/(2c) — `playCurrent()`'s `player.queue = [...]`, `syncNativeQueueNeighbors`'s two branches, and `seek(to:)`'s `playbackTime =` are all back to plain synchronous main-actor calls (only the genuinely-async `player.play()` stays raced against `withTimeout`, which was always safe — a real suspension point, not a synchronous call forced cross-thread). `playerConnectionSuspect` is kept as a pure AVOIDANCE gate (skip these calls once known-bad, set only from the safe `play()` timeout) rather than something derived from timing out the synchronous calls themselves. (b) New `PlayerViewModel.advanceViaMirroredEntry(mirroredId:target:)` — when `skipToNext()`/`skipToPrevious()`'s target is already mirrored into the native queue (`mirroredNextItemId`/`mirroredPreviousItemId` match), moves `player.queue.currentEntry` directly (the exact lightweight operation confirmed reliable via Control Center) instead of calling the heavy `playCurrent()` at all. Deliberately does NOT duplicate the `currentIndex`/`nowPlaying`/`currentDuration` bookkeeping — the progress timer's EXISTING reactive drift-detection block (written earlier this session specifically to reconcile a Control-Center-driven `currentEntry` change) notices this identically on its next tick and performs that bookkeeping already, since from the timer's perspective a `currentEntry` change is a `currentEntry` change regardless of who caused it. Falls back to the heavy `playCurrent()` path (now deadlock-free per the revert, though still potentially blocking for up to 8s on a genuinely dead connection) when the target isn't mirrored yet.
  **Known gap, explicitly NOT fixed this round:** tapping an arbitrary NEW track from a list (`play(_:startingAt:)` — e.g. a different Top Charts entry) still goes through the full queue-replace path unconditionally, even when the tapped track happens to already be the mirrored next-in-queue neighbor (as it was in the reported "Rein Me In" repro, since Top Charts order matched the existing queue order). Extending the fast path there is harder — `play(_:startingAt:)` replaces the ENTIRE `queue` array, not just one step, so the reactive drift-detection's neighbor bookkeeping would need to run against a queue that's already been swapped out from under it. Deferred pending confirmation of whether the deadlock revert alone (no more permanent freeze, worst case a bounded ~8s block) is sufficient for this specific action, rather than guessing at a second structural change blind.
  **On-device unverified, this round specifically:** needs the user to re-run the exact two repros — pressing next after playing a track, and tapping a different Top Charts song — and report whether either the app now never locks up (ideal), locks up briefly then recovers (the accepted residual for the untouched `play(:startingAt:)` path), or is still a hard freeze (would mean the cross-thread-deadlock theory is wrong and needs further investigation, not a threading fix).

  **(2c) THIRD freeze report proved the breaker alone wasn't enough — the freeze can strike IMMEDIATELY AFTER a successful play.** New console log walked the exact sequence: play #1 succeeds → prefetch + queue-mirroring resolve the next track → user taps Top Charts #2 → plays via the prefetch cache (cache hits don't print — worth remembering when reading these logs) → prefetch/mirror resolve the new next track → mirror resolves the PREVIOUS neighbor → `xpc service connection interrupted` → frozen. The connection died right after `play()` succeeded (which had just correctly CLEARED the suspect flag), and the next line of code was `syncNativeQueueNeighbors`' previous-branch — a synchronous `player.queue.entries` read/insert on the main thread. Key inference from three logs' worth of evidence: the blocking calls are all queue-surface WRITES (`player.queue =`, `queue.insert`, `entries.insert`, `playbackTime =`); the timer's per-tick READS (`state.playbackStatus`/`playbackTime`/`queue.currentEntry`) appear to be locally-cached published values that never blocked across any session — so bounding the writes is the right scope, and the tick loop was deliberately NOT refactored onto a background snapshot (protecting the hard-won end-of-track edge detector from re-entrancy). **Fix:** every player touch in `syncNativeQueueNeighbors` now runs inside a bounded `withTimeout(seconds: 4)` off-main closure (the previous-branch returns `Bool` so `mirroredPreviousItemId` bookkeeping stays correct), a timeout trips `playerConnectionSuspect` and aborts the sync; `seek(to:)`'s synchronous `playbackTime =` write got the same treatment (fire-and-forget bounded Task — `progress` is already published before the write, so the scrubber UI is right either way). Mirroring is best-effort by design (Control Center next/previous degrades to restart-current without it) — a frozen UI is never an acceptable price for it. Remaining unbounded sync calls, deliberately left: `player.pause()` (evidence shows it errors fast rather than hanging — `prepareToPlay failed` came back as a thrown error) and the timer's reads per the cached-value inference above; if a freeze EVER recurs with no play/next/scrub action in flight, the tick loop is the next suspect and needs the background-snapshot refactor.
  **(3) Wrong album on tap — `resolveAlbum` took `.first` from the `.albums` RELATIONSHIP.** `NowPlayingTrack.album` comes from `Song.albumTitle` (MusicKit's own canonical album for the track — correct, e.g. "petal"), but `resolveAlbum` re-fetched the song and blindly took `song.albums?.first` — the `.albums` relationship legitimately lists EVERY release the recording appears on (own album/EP, deluxe reissues, various-artists compilations), in no useful order, so a "NOW That's..." compilation could win by sort position. First fix: score all candidates with the already-existing `bestReleaseIndex(albumNames:expectedAlbum:)` (`NodalongKit/ReleaseMatching.swift` — built for exactly this ambiguity class in the 2026-07-13 artwork work, never wired into this path) against the known `albumTitle`. `resolveArtists` left untouched (credited artists don't have this multiplicity problem — returning all of them is the feature).
  **(3b) Scoring alone was NOT enough — second on-device report (Jump Back In → "Every Single Weekend" single opened the "No Bad Memories" parent album) exposed the deeper hole: for a PRE-RELEASE single, the song's `.albums` relationship can omit the single's own release entirely and list only the upcoming parent album — so name-scoring had no matching candidate to prefer and the wrong release still won as "least bad." User also (rightly) flagged that album resolution wasn't consistent across the app (player/Jump Back In used the relationship; Shelf used a raw `searchAll` text search taking `.first`). Unified fix: `resolveAlbum` is now THE app-wide album resolver with a layered, name-gated strategy — (1) relationship candidate whose name MATCHES the expected album name (new public `albumNamesMatch(_:_:)` in `ReleaseMatching.swift`: normalized exact-or-containment, so " - Single"/"(Deluxe)" store suffixes on one side still match; 4 new tests, 96/96 pass); (2) else a direct `MusicCatalogSearchRequest` ALBUM search by the known name + artist (new private `searchAlbumCatalog(named:artist:)`), again returning a hit only on a name match — this is what fixes the pre-release-single case, since the name IS Apple's own `albumTitle` and the single release is findable by name even when absent from the relationship; (3) else the relationship's best-scored candidate as a last resort (related release beats a dead end). `ShelfView`'s `PendingCatalogAlbumLookup` destination now routes its Apple path through this same resolver (the fuzzy `searchAll` kept only for the suspended-Spotify path, which has no resolver equivalent) — so the player's album tap, Jump Back In, and Shelf all resolve identically. Key lesson recorded: the `.albums` relationship answers "which releases contain this recording," NOT "which release does this album name denote" — when the UI already displays a trustworthy name, resolution must be name-gated, with the relationship as corroboration.
  **(4) Made For You duplicate-ID warning:** `getMadeForYouPlaylists` flatMaps playlists across recommendation groups and the same playlist can appear in several — deduped by id (keep first, preserve order) at the service, fixing the undefined-results `ForEach` warning at its source.
  **Environment finding worth remembering:** the Mac's long-booted "iPhone 16" simulator runs iOS 18.6, below the app's 26.1 deployment target — `xcodebuild` refuses it as a destination outright, so anything running there is a stale pre-bump install; agent verification must target an iOS 26.x device (used the iPhone 16e / 26.3 runtime; app builds, installs, launches to the auth screen — full flows still need a signed-in session). **On-device unverified:** the freeze fix needs the user to reproduce the same two actions (second Top Charts play; play → next) on their device — expected behavior on a dead connection is now ~8s of silent wait then preview fallback + a console `play timed out — marking player connection suspect`, never a frozen UI; the album tap should land on the track's real album even when a compilation exists.

- **2026-08-07** — **Loading-elimination pass: launch-cache SWR + progressive Home load + detail-screen caching + instant player navigation + artwork bucketing — build-green, 92 NodalongKit tests pass (2 new).** User's on-device narration of a full session: purple splash on open, Home popping in section-by-section, Feed blank until posts pop in, then in the big player — album tap → wait → sheet → wait again for songs, artist tap → spinner → albums pop in, tap an album on the artist page → wait again. Two Explore agents mapped the real cause first: **nothing was ever cached across launches** (Firestore's local cache exists but nothing opts into a cache-first read), Home had **no loading state at all** (just the greeting, with sections literally `if !array.isEmpty`, popping in as each of 6 fixed-order `async let`s resolved — gated by the slowest predecessor even though most are independent), Feed showed a bare spinner then dumped the whole list at once, `NowPlayingView`'s album/artist taps resolved over the network (2–6 MusicKit round trips via `resolveDetailedSong`'s cascade) **before** presenting the sheet with only a tiny inline spinner as feedback, `AlbumDetailSearchView`/`ArtistDetailSearchView` had zero session memory (every revisit re-fetched, `ArtistDetailSearchView` cost 4 round trips — 2 redundant `Artist` resource fetches — where 2 would do), and one album's artwork could be downloaded at up to 8 distinct pixel sizes across the app (`ArtworkURL.sized` baked the exact point size into every request). User confirmed two scope-defining calls via `AskUserQuestion`: **app-wide stale-while-revalidate** (render last session's content instantly, refresh silently — accepting a briefly-stale feed over a blank one) and **instant sheet presentation** on the player's album/artist taps (reversing the earlier "player is the most fragile surface, defer this" call from the 2026-07-28 entry below — the Jump Back In pattern is proven, apply it here too).
  **New file `Nodalong/Services/SnapshotStore.swift`** — generic `enum` (not an actor; no shared mutable state) doing synchronous JSON `load`/async-detached `save`/`clear`, one directory per uid under Application Support (not `UserDefaults` — Feed's ~50 posts and Home's six arrays are too large to be comfortable there). This is the one new piece of cross-launch persistence infrastructure everything else in this pass builds on; the codebase's only prior precedent was `ShelfViewModel.artistImages` (a single `UserDefaults` dictionary) and `PlayerViewModel`'s queue persistence — neither generalized.
  **Launch (`FirebaseAuthViewModel.swift`, `ContentView.swift`):** `init()` now checks `Auth.auth().currentUser` (restored synchronously from the keychain at launch) against a saved profile snapshot — if both exist, `profile`/`currentUser`/`isInitializing = false` are set immediately, before `startAuthListener()` even runs, so `MainTabView` can render on the very first frame; the listener still fires and `loadProfile` refreshes from the network moments later (last-write-wins). `loadProfile` was hardened so a background-refresh failure/empty-result can never blank or error out an already-rendered cached profile (`hadProfile` guard on both branches) and now persists via `SnapshotStore.save` on every success. **Fixed a real pre-existing bug found while doing this:** the 400ms `showPostAuthBridge` splash-hold was triggered by `.onChange(of: authVM.isAuthenticated)`, which also fires on cold launch (the moment the profile lands) — meaning every launch, cached or not, already ate 400ms of pure waste for a bridge that only makes sense after an interactive sign-in/register. Replaced with an explicit `@Published postAuthBridgeToken` (bumped only from `signIn`'s success path via a `loadProfile`-consumed `pendingInteractiveAuth` flag, and directly from `register`'s success path) — deliberately NOT keyed off `isSubmitting`, since `updateProfile()` also sets that flag and calls `loadProfile` indirectly via `refreshProfile()`, which must NOT flash the splash. `signOut()` now calls `SnapshotStore.clear(uid:)` before `Auth.auth().signOut()` so a later sign-in (same device, any account) can never render a stale snapshot belonging to whoever was signed in before.
  **Home (`HomeViewModel.swift`, `HomeView.swift`):** new private `HomeSnapshot` (Codable struct of everything except `liveNow`, deliberately excluded — a live "who's online" signal would be actively misleading if shown stale) restored synchronously at the top of `load()` before any network call starts, persisted on every `load()` completion. `load()` itself rewritten from 6 `async let`s awaited in fixed source order to **6 independent `Task { @MainActor in ... }` children**, each assigning its own `@Published` property the instant its own fetch lands and only on success — a fast section (Top Charts) now renders before a slow one (Recommendations) instead of waiting behind it, and a failed refresh can never blank content a snapshot already rendered. (First attempt used `withTaskGroup { group.addTask { @MainActor in ... } }`, which compiled and ran correctly but emitted a genuine new "sending closure" data-race warning under strict concurrency on all 6 child closures — switched to the unstructured-`Task`-array pattern instead, which is isolated from creation and has no such boundary to cross; zero warnings, same concurrency.) The genuine `followingUids` barrier (spotlights + live-now both need it) is preserved as the 6th task's own internal sequencing. `HomeView` gained `restoredFromSnapshot` (read from the VM) and a `showFirstLaunchSkeletons` computed property — shown only when there's no snapshot AND nothing has landed from network yet, rendering 3 new `SkeletonRail`s (new `Nodalong/Shared/UI/SkeletonRail.swift`, a `ShimmerView`-tiled horizontal rail matching the carousel geometry) instead of a bare greeting; disappears section-by-section as real data arrives.
  **Feed (`FeedViewModel.swift`, `FeedView.swift`):** `load()` now checks for a `[Post]` snapshot (keyed `"feedPosts"`) before the network call and renders it immediately if present; the network refresh only replaces `posts` when the fresh result is non-empty or `posts` was already empty (so a real "you have zero followed posts" state still renders correctly, but a flaky empty response can't wipe a good cached list). Both `load()` and `refresh()` persist on completion. `FeedView`'s bare `ProgressView` (shown whenever there's nothing to render yet — now only a genuine first-ever load, no snapshot) became `feedSkeleton`, 6 `ShimmerView` cards inside the same `bgSurface` container the real list uses, so the swap doesn't shift layout.
  **Deduped following-list/live-parties reads:** new `Nodalong/Services/FollowingCache.swift` (session actor, `CatalogCache`-pattern in-flight-task-sharing + a 30s TTL) — at cold launch, `HomeViewModel.load`, `FeedView.loadLiveParties`, and `getFollowingFeed` itself all independently read the *same* following list within the same second (3 reads), and `getLivePartiesHostedBy` was read twice (Home + Feed) for the same uid set; both now route through this cache. `followUser`/`unfollowUser` (`Follows.swift`) call `FollowingCache.shared.invalidate()` themselves at the end of their batch commit — deliberately done at the API layer, not chased across the 4 view-level call sites, so a follow-graph change is guaranteed to be picked up by the next load regardless of which screen triggered it.
  **Detail screens (`AlbumDetailSearchView.swift`, `ArtistDetailSearchView.swift`, `AppleMusicService.swift`):** new `Nodalong/Services/MusicDetailCache.swift` (session actor, `CatalogCache` pattern, closure-injected fetch so it never has to import MusicKit — keeps the seam rule) caches `[albumId: [Song]]` and `[artistId: ArtistDetails]` with in-flight sharing; empty results are deliberately NOT cached so a transient failure retries on next visit. New `AppleMusicService.getArtistDetails(artistId:)` — one `Artist` resource fetch + one combined `.with([.albums, .topSongs])`, replacing `getArtistAlbums`+`getArtistTopTracks`'s 4 round trips (each redundantly re-fetching the same `Artist`) with 2; kept off the `MusicService` protocol (Apple-only, same precedent as `getPersonalRecommendations` — the suspended Spotify path keeps compiling against the original pair, now raced via two independent `Task`s instead of sequential `async let` awaits so a fast section still isn't blocked by a slow one). Both views gained a `hasLoaded` guard (revisits skip straight to the cache) and their `isLoading` semantics were fixed so the very first frame — before `.task` has run — doesn't flash an empty "Tracks"/"No albums" section: `AlbumDetailSearchView.isLoading` now starts `true`; `ArtistDetailSearchView`'s skeleton (new `artistLoadingSkeleton`, a titled `SkeletonRail`) shows only while both `albums` and `topTracks` are empty, and each section renders independently the instant it has data. **Also memoized `resolveDetailedSong` in `AppleMusicService`** (single-entry, keyed on the id/isrc/title/artist tuple — the class is `@MainActor` so a plain stored `var` is safe) — tapping artist then album (or vice versa) on the same Now Playing track used to run the whole id→ISRC→text cascade twice since `resolveAlbum`/`resolveArtists` never shared their result; the memoed song already has `.albums`/`.artists` loaded, so the second tap is now free.
  **Player instant navigation (`NowPlayingView.swift`, new `Nodalong/Shared/UI/ResolvingArtistsView.swift`, deleted `ArtistPickerSheet.swift`):** reverses the 2026-07-28 entry's deferral of this exact screen. `NavTarget` gained `.resolvingAlbum(NowPlayingTrack)`/`.resolvingArtist(NowPlayingTrack)` cases; the artist/album buttons now just set `navTarget` synchronously — the sheet appears **instantly** showing `ResolvingAlbumView`/the new `ResolvingArtistsView` (a provisional header built from the track's already-known artwork/name/artist), with the actual `resolveAlbumResult`/`resolveArtistResults` (the old `loadAlbum`/`loadArtist` bodies, including the non-Apple `searchAll` fallback branch) running inside as the injected `resolve` closure. `ResolvingArtistsView` is new — mirrors `ResolvingArtistView` but resolves to `[ArtistResult]`: one result swaps straight to `ArtistDetailSearchView`, several show an inline picker (reusing `ArtistPickerSheet`'s row layout) that swaps on tap — this replaces the old two-`.sheet` `ArtistPickerSheet` + `DispatchQueue.main.asyncAfter(0.35s)` dismiss-then-present hack entirely, since the picker now lives *inside* the one already-presented sheet. `isLoadingArtist`/`isLoadingAlbum`/`artistCandidates`/`showArtistPicker` and the old `loadArtist`/`loadAlbum` functions are all gone; a failed resolve now shows "Couldn't find this album/artist" inside the sheet instead of silently swallowing the tap. `ArtistPickerSheet.swift` had zero remaining call sites after this (confirmed via grep) and was deleted — **note `PartyView.swift` has its own unrelated `isLoadingArtist`/artist-tap flow, untouched, out of scope.** The fragile `UIScreen.main.bounds`-pinned root layout was not touched at all — every change is confined to `NavTarget`, the two button actions, the `.sheet(item:)` builder, and the two renamed resolve functions.
  **Artwork bucketing (`ArtworkURL.swift`):** `sized(_:points:...)` now rounds the computed pixel need UP to the nearest of `[160, 320, 600]` instead of requesting the exact point×scale value — so a 44pt thumbnail (needs 132px at 3x) and a 48pt one (144px) both request the same 160px CDN rendition and share one `URLCache` entry, instead of two separate downloads of the same image; verified against all 15 real call sites across the app (thumbnails at 44–160pt, album/artist heroes at screen-width, the lock-screen art at 300pt) that every one still resolves to a sensible bucket, upscale-only, with the existing 600px cap preserved for anything that would otherwise exceed it.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` (5 new files: `SnapshotStore`, `FollowingCache`, `MusicDetailCache`, `SkeletonRail`, `ResolvingArtistsView`; 1 deleted: `ArtistPickerSheet`) + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green after every phase, zero new warnings anywhere (confirmed via full-log grep against every touched/new file — the only warnings present are the pre-existing ones already cataloged in §7 plus unrelated ones in files this pass never touched, like `PartyView`/`NearMeCanvasView`/`SpotifyMusicService`). `swift test` in `NodalongKit/` — 92/92 pass (90 existing + 2 new `SnapshotCodableTests` covering `FriendSpotlight`/`RecommendationShelf`'s new `Codable` conformance, added specifically because `HomeSnapshot` needed to persist them). **On-device unverified for the actual felt experience** — this environment can't drive the app or measure real launch/navigation latency; needs a real pass confirming: cold launch with a warm snapshot goes straight into a populated app with no splash and a silent refresh; a genuine first-ever launch still shows splash → skeletons → content; sign-out then sign-in as a different account shows no leaked content and the 400ms bridge still appears (only) on interactive sign-in/register, not on relaunch; player album/artist taps show the sheet instantly with a provisional header that fills in, including the multi-artist picker case; revisiting an album/artist is now instant; and artwork doesn't look softer anywhere, particularly the 240pt album-detail header now served at the 320px bucket instead of its own exact size.

- **2026-07-28** — **App-wide seamlessness pass: concurrent independent fetches + navigate-before-resolve — build-green, 90 NodalongKit tests unaffected.** Direct follow-up to "most actions result in a brief pause before I navigate somewhere." Two Explore agents audited the whole app for (1) view-model loads that `await` independent calls sequentially instead of concurrently, and (2) taps that resolve a network call *before* pushing navigation instead of pushing immediately and resolving inside the destination — the pattern the Made For You fix (same day, earlier entry) established.
  **Concurrent fetches:** `HomeViewModel.load(uid:musicService:)` rewritten around `async let` — 6 independent fetches (posts, following list, recommendations, made-for-you, recently-added, top charts) now run in parallel instead of one-after-another, with a genuine barrier preserved only where it's real (`getFriendsSpotlightsToday`/`loadLiveNow` both need `followingUids`, so they still wait on the following-list fetch — but run concurrently with EACH OTHER once unblocked). Total load time drops from roughly the *sum* of every request's latency to roughly the *max*. `FollowListView.load()` similarly hoists `getFollowedEntries(uid: myUid)` (independent of the followers/following list + profile enrichment above it) into its own `async let`. Confirmed via the audit that `ProfileView.refresh`, `UserProfileView.loadData`, `NearMeUserSheet.loadData`, `ArtistDetailSearchView.loadData`, and `Posts.swift`'s `getYourMusicFeed`/`getFollowingFeed` **already** use `async let` correctly — no changes needed there, used as the in-repo style reference for this rewrite.
  **Navigate-before-resolve, generalized:** new `Nodalong/Shared/UI/ResolvingAlbumView.swift` / `ResolvingArtistView.swift` — a small reusable pattern (not tied to one feature): pushed immediately with already-known display data (name/artist/artwork), shows a provisional header + spinner, resolves the real catalog `AlbumResult`/`ArtistResult` via an injected `resolve: () async -> T?` closure, then swaps in the full `AlbumDetailSearchView`/`ArtistDetailSearchView` once ready (or a plain "couldn't find this" state on failure). Wired into two call sites that previously blocked the tap on a network resolve: (1) Home's Jump Back In — `jumpBackInRail` now does `path.append(album)` directly (`RecentAlbum` is already `Hashable`), a new `.navigationDestination(for: RecentAlbum.self)` builds the `ResolvingAlbumView` using the existing `musicService.resolveAlbum(...)` call; `resolvingAlbumId`/`openRecentAlbum`/the per-tile spinner from the immediately-preceding fix are all now dead and removed — instant navigation with an in-place provisional header is a strictly better UX than a spinning tile. (2) Shelf's `AlbumDetailView` album-name/artist-name taps (`ShelfView.swift`) — previously guarded by a shared `isLookingUp` flag rendered as a **full-screen** dim with `.ignoresSafeArea()` (the same nested-view `.ignoresSafeArea()` misuse already identified and removed elsewhere this session as a layout-crash vector) — now push two new lightweight file-scope markers (`PendingCatalogAlbumLookup`/`PendingCatalogArtistLookup`, mirroring `SeeAllRecommendations`'s "marker route" convention) immediately; new destinations on `ShelfView`'s root stack build the `Resolving*View`s using the EXISTING `activeService.searchAll(...)` mechanism (deliberately not swapped for `resolveAlbum`/`resolveArtists`, which are Apple-only — Shelf must keep working for Spotify users). `ShelfView` gained `musicService`/`spotifyService`/`playerVM` env objects + its own `activeService` computed property (previously only `AlbumDetailView`, a separate nested struct, had these) since the new destinations are declared on `ShelfView`'s own stack. `isLookingUp` and both `openAlbumInCatalog`/`openArtistInCatalog` functions are deleted entirely.
  **Found but deliberately deferred, not bundled into this pass** (flagged as bigger/riskier): `HomeView.openLiveNowEntry` and `FeedView.openProfile` both resolve a full `UserProfile` via `getUserProfile(uid:)` before pushing — unlike the album cases, `UserProfileView` takes `user: UserProfile` as an immutable `let` and has no internal loading capability of its own, so applying the same pattern would mean turning `user` into `@State` and adding a re-fetch `.task` — a real refactor of a widely-used view, not a drop-in swap. `NowPlayingView.loadArtist`/`loadAlbum` and `PartyView.loadArtist` have the same resolve-before-present shape but for `.sheet(item:)` presentation rather than stack navigation — same reasoning, plus touching player-adjacent UI (already the most fragile surface this session, per the XPC-connectivity entries above) — left alone.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` (2 new files) + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green, zero errors. `swift test` — 90/90 unaffected (no NodalongKit changes). **On-device unverified** — this environment can't drive the app or measure real latency; needs a real pass confirming Home's load genuinely feels faster, Jump Back In / Shelf album+artist taps navigate instantly with a believable provisional-then-real transition, and Followers/Following loads correctly.
  **Separately, same session: a real console log reproduction settled the still-open Top Charts playback question from the immediately-preceding entry.** Every song resolved successfully this time (`[Nodalong.Resolve] resolved '...' via direct id`, plain numeric catalog ids, zero 404s) — the new success-path logging did its job. The actual, confirmed failure is 100% in playback establishment: `applicationQueuePlayer _establishConnectionIfNeeded timeout`, `applicationController: xpc service connection interrupted`, `applicationMusicPlayer: connection invalidated`, `peer may have been unloaded` — the exact same MusicKit XPC flakiness from this session's own earlier mini-player-resume entries, recurring despite those mitigations (`withTimeout`, foreground-staleness reset) already being in place. Confirmed NOT Top-Charts-specific and NOT caused by this session's Home work. Not re-investigated further in this pass (a different problem category — a hard failure, not a "feels slow" issue — flagged to the user as worth its own dedicated follow-up given it recurred after supposedly-landed fixes).

- **2026-07-28** — **Home redesign, part 4: fixed Jump Back In's whole-carousel loading dim, built a real Made For You playlist screen, investigated (inconclusively) a Top Charts playback failure — build-green, 90 NodalongKit tests unaffected.** Same-day on-device feedback (with a real pasted console log) after part 3 shipped.
  **(1) Jump Back In "the entire carousel loads" — fixed.** Root cause: my own design choice — `isResolvingAlbum: Bool` drove a section-wide dimming `.overlay` over the WHOLE "Jump Back In" title+rail while any one tile resolved. Replaced with `resolvingAlbumId: String?` (keyed by `RecentAlbum.id`) — `recentAlbumTile(_:isResolving:)` now dims only its own artwork when it matches, everywhere else unaffected. `openRecentAlbum` sets/clears the id instead of a blanket bool.
  **(2) Made For You "plays a song instead of showing the playlist" — fixed, reversing part 3's own design choice.** User feedback directly contradicted the earlier scoped-down "tap = resolve + play immediately" decision — a playlist is a browsable collection, like an album, and starting playback with zero visibility into what's actually in it reads as broken, not convenient. New `Nodalong/Features/Search/PlaylistDetailSearchView.swift`, mirrors `AlbumDetailSearchView.swift`'s exact shape (header row with artwork/name/curator/track-count, a "Play" button, `List` of tracks with `TrackActionsMenuButton`/like/duration, tap-a-track plays the whole playlist starting there) — simpler than `AlbumDetailSearchView` since `getPlaylistTracks(playlistId:)` (built in part 3, unused until now) already returns `[NodalongKit.Song]` directly, no `SearchItem` mapping dance needed. `HomeView` gained `.navigationDestination(for: PlaylistResult.self) { PlaylistDetailSearchView(playlist: $0) }`; `madeForYouRail`'s tap action changed from `Task { await openMadeForYouPlaylist(playlist) }` to a plain `path.append(playlist)` — resolving now happens inside the pushed screen's own `.task`, exactly like `AlbumDetailSearchView`. Deleted `isResolvingPlaylist`/`openMadeForYouPlaylist`/the Made For You section's dimming overlay entirely — no longer needed. Deliberately did NOT add a bulk "Save all to Shelf" action (unlike `AlbumDetailSearchView`'s "Save Album") — the user's specific ask was "let me see the playlist," not a bulk-save feature; a scope-narrowing choice, easy to add later if wanted.
  **(3) Top Charts playback failure — investigated, explicitly NOT blindly fixed.** The pasted log showed four consecutive `MusicDataRequest` 404s on direct catalog song-id lookups (`/v1/catalog/ie/songs/i.XXXXX`), then `applicationQueuePlayer _establishConnectionIfNeeded timeout [ping did not pong]` → `prepareToPlay failed [no target descriptor]` → `[Nodalong.Player] togglePlayPause resume failed`. Read `PlayerViewModel.resolve()` (the id→ISRC→text-search cascade) in full before touching anything: it already has failure-path logging for all 3 steps, but **zero success-path logging** — no way to tell after the fact which step actually resolved a track, or whether the direct-id lookup even ran (it's guarded off for `i.`-prefixed ids per an existing, already-documented comment — "library/relationship IDs... are NOT catalog IDs"). Chose NOT to guess-fix the resolve logic itself: the same log also shows an unrelated Firestore `WatchStream` "Network connectivity changed" error immediately before these failures (suggesting a genuinely unstable connection during this specific test), and the XPC `_establishConnectionIfNeeded timeout`/`prepareToPlay failed` signature is the *exact same* MusicKit connectivity flakiness this session already fought and partially mitigated earlier the same day (`withTimeout`, foreground-staleness reset — see the entries above) — not something obviously new or specific to Top Charts. Added success-path `print("[Nodalong.Resolve] resolved '...' via direct id/ISRC/text search")` at all 3 return points in `resolve()` instead, closing the diagnostic gap so the *next* occurrence gives an unambiguous answer (a genuine per-song catalog-availability gap specific to chart items vs. another instance of the known flakiness) rather than more guessing.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` (new file) + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green. `swift test` — 90/90 unaffected (no NodalongKit changes). **On-device unverified for all three** — this environment can't drive the app; (1)/(2) need a real tap-through to confirm the per-tile spinner and the new playlist screen render/behave as intended; (3) needs the failure reproduced again with the new logging active, then the console shared, to actually settle the root cause.

- **2026-07-28** — **Home redesign, part 3: folded On Repeat into Jump Back In, added Top Charts / Made For You / Recently Added — build-green, 90 NodalongKit tests pass (1 new). Stations/Radio deliberately deferred.** User feedback (with a screenshot): Jump Back In and On Repeat read as the same thing — both are "recent activity," just sliced at album vs. track level. Asked to redesign again with genuinely distinct sections and to explore what else MusicKit's API surface offers. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion`: drop On Repeat as its own shelf (fold its replay signal into Jump Back In); add all of Made For You, Recently Added, and Top Charts; defer Stations/Radio.
  **Verified every new MusicKit API against the real SDK before writing any code, not guessed** — wrote a throwaway probe file and typechecked it with `xcrun -sdk iphonesimulator swiftc -typecheck` against this project's actual `MusicKit.swiftmodule` (26.1 target). Confirmed real and exactly as used below on the first attempt: `MusicCatalogChartsRequest(types:)` → `.songCharts`/`.albumCharts` (each with `.title`/`.items`), `MusicLibraryRequest<Album>().sort(by: \.libraryAddedDate, ascending:)`, `Album.libraryAddedDate: Date?`, `MusicPersonalRecommendation.playlists`/`.stations`, `Playlist.name`/`.artwork`/`.id`/`.curator?.name`. Stations was deferred specifically because MusicKit stations play continuously via the native player queue — a genuinely different playback model that doesn't fit this app's fixed `[QueueItem]`-array `PlayerViewModel`; building that felt like its own session, not a slot-in.
  **Fold-in:** `RecentAlbum` (`NodalongKit/.../GroupPostsByAlbum.swift`) gained `totalReplayCount: Int` — the sum of `Post.replayCount` across every track in the album group, computed in `recentAlbums(from:)` alongside the existing "most recently played track is the representative" logic. `HomeViewModel.onRepeatPosts` and `HomeView`'s dead `postsCarouselSection` helper (its only caller) are gone entirely; the underlying `Post.replayCount`/`syncRecentTracks` replay-tracking is untouched, just no longer surfaced as its own shelf. `recentAlbumTile` shows a small "🔁 Played N×" caption under the artist name only when `totalReplayCount > 0`.
  **New model:** `PlaylistResult` (`NodalongKit/.../CatalogSearchResults.swift`, right beside `AlbumResult`/`ArtistResult`, same `key` prefix convention) — id/name/curatorName/artwork/sourceService, for a curated Apple Music playlist mix (genuinely different content type from an album, even sourced from the same request).
  **New `AppleMusicService` methods**, all off the `MusicService` protocol (Apple-only, no Spotify equivalent, same precedent as `getPersonalRecommendations`/`resolveAlbum`): `getMadeForYouPlaylists(maxGroups:)` (flattens the `playlists` relationship on the same `MusicPersonalRecommendationsRequest` payload `getPersonalRecommendations` already reads for albums — previously silently discarded); `getPlaylistTracks(playlistId:)` (mirrors `getAlbumTracks`'s `.with([.tracks])` relationship-loading shape); `getRecentlyAddedAlbums(limit:)` (`MusicLibraryRequest<Album>` sorted by `libraryAddedDate` desc — not subscription-gated, a library read not full playback, same convention as `getSavedTracks`); `getTopChartSongs(limit:)` (`MusicCatalogChartsRequest(types: [Song.self])`, no genre = overall storefront chart — also not subscription-gated, a catalog browse like `searchTracks`). New `toPlaylistResult` conversion alongside `toSong`/`toArtistResult`/`toAlbumResult`, same "returns nil on empty MusicItemID" contract.
  **`HomeViewModel`** gained `madeForYouPlaylists`/`recentlyAddedAlbums`/`topChartSongs`; `load()` fetches Made For You alongside Recommended for You under the same `canPlayFullTracks` gate (they share the identical request), and Recently Added/Top Charts unconditionally (not subscription-gated). The existing subscription-race-fix method (from the same-day six-bugs pass) was renamed `refreshPersonalizedContentIfNeeded` and extended to cover both `recommendationShelves` and `madeForYouPlaylists` together, since they race against the same subscription-observer timing issue.
  **`HomeView`** section order (top to bottom): Live Now → Jump Back In (now with the replay badge) → Friends' Spotlights → **Top Charts** (new, numbered-rank tiles, tap plays the whole chart in context via `playerVM.play(_:startingAt:)`) → Recommended for You → **Made For You** (new, playlist tiles + curator caption, tap resolves tracks via `getPlaylistTracks` then plays in context, guarded by a new `isResolvingPlaylist` state mirroring `isResolvingAlbum`) → **Recently Added** (new, reuses the existing `AlbumsCarousel` verbatim — it already wires `NavigationLink(value: AlbumResult)` internally, so Home's existing `.navigationDestination(for: AlbumResult.self)` picks it up with zero new plumbing).
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green, zero errors. `swift test` — 90/90 NodalongKit tests pass (89 existing + 1 new `testTotalReplayCountSumsAcrossTracksInTheGroup`). **On-device unverified** — this environment can't drive the actual app; needs a real subscriber account to confirm Made For You shows genuine curated mixes and plays correctly, a real library with saved albums to confirm Recently Added, and a live network connection to confirm Top Charts renders a real, current chart. The Jump Back In replay badge only shows for tracks genuinely replayed after the original On Repeat tracking shipped (2026-07-28 — see the entry below), so it may read empty on first check even on an active account.

- **2026-07-28** — **Six small bug/polish reports fixed in one pass — build-green, 89 NodalongKit tests unaffected.** All six surfaced in quick succession right after the Home redesign / Tab Navigation 2.0 work landed.
  **(1) Profile → Followers/Following → white screen → auto pop-back.** Root cause, confirmed by grep: `ProfileView()` has exactly one real (non-preview) call site — `Nodalong/Shared/UI/ProfileEntryPoint.swift`, which pushes it via `.navigationDestination(isPresented:)` onto an existing host stack (Home/Feed/NearMe/Shelf). But `ProfileView` still wrapped its own body in a second, owned `NavigationStack { ... }` — a nested-`NavigationStack` situation, with `.navigationDestination(for: FollowListMode.self)` declared on that now-nested inner stack. Fixed by removing `ProfileView`'s own `NavigationStack` wrapper entirely (unwrapped to a plain `ScrollView` with the same modifiers attached directly — they work identically on an inherited stack); both `#Preview` blocks now provide their own `NavigationStack { ProfileView() }`, mirroring the same pattern `EditProfileView`'s preview already used.
  **(2) Jump Back In album tap "crashes."** Two concrete defects found by reading the new (same-session) code, no console log available to confirm which one was the actual trigger: the loading-state overlay called `.ignoresSafeArea()` on a small section-scoped dimming view nested inside a `ScrollView` — applying `.ignoresSafeArea()` to a non-root view in scrolling/lazy layout is a known SwiftUI layout-assertion crash vector, and it was never needed for a section-scoped overlay like this (removed). Separately, `openRecentAlbum`'s `resolveAlbum` call had zero diagnostic logging — a silent `nil` resolve would make the tap look like it does nothing with no way to tell why; added a `[Nodalong.Home]` print on the nil branch, matching the app's established diagnostic-logging convention. **Honest caveat:** without a crash log this is a best-effort fix based on the one concrete defect found, not a confirmed root cause — if it recurs, the new print will narrow down whether `resolveAlbum` itself is the culprit.
  **(3) Recommended for You sometimes doesn't load on Home.** Root cause: `AppleMusicService.subscription` starts `nil` and only gets its first real value once `MusicSubscription.subscriptionUpdates` delivers, typically the moment after auth — but `HomeViewModel.load()` checked `canPlayFullTracks` exactly once, synchronously, at whatever instant Home's `.task` fires (now the app's first tab, so right at cold launch). A genuine subscriber could lose this race and get `recommendationShelves = []` with nothing ever retrying once the subscription resolved a moment later. Fixed with a new `HomeViewModel.refreshRecommendationsIfNeeded(musicService:)` (a no-op once shelves are populated) wired to a new `.onChange(of: musicService.canPlayFullTracks)` on `HomeView` — deliberately observes the derived `Bool`, not the `MusicSubscription?` itself, to avoid depending on that MusicKit type's `Equatable` conformance. Also converted the existing silent `try?` in `load()` to `do/catch` with `[Nodalong.Home]` logging, so a genuine API failure is now distinguishable from this timing race in the console.
  **(4) NearMe: oversized gap between the park and the mini player.** Root cause: `NearMeView.usersContent` had a manual `Color.clear.frame(height: 70)` clearance spacer *and* the canvas already sizes to `maxHeight: .infinity` inside a non-scrolling `VStack` — but `MainTabView` migrated to the native `.tabViewBottomAccessory` API earlier this session, which auto-reserves the right safe-area space for the mini player (same mechanism as `.safeAreaInset`), making the manual spacer redundant.
