Grepped the app for the same `Color.clear.frame(height: 70)` pattern — found 13 other sites (Feed/Profile/Shelf/Search/Playlist/Party), all inside `ScrollView`s, where the same redundancy is far less visible (just a bit more empty scroll space at the very bottom) and none have been reported as a visible problem — deliberately left untouched, fixed NearMe only since it's the one screen where this spacer sits in a fixed-size, non-scrolling `VStack` and directly, visibly shrinks the canvas by a doubled-up amount. Removed the now-fully-unused `playerVM` `@EnvironmentObject` from `NearMeView` in the same pass.
  **(5) Feed: "Spotlight" badge text wraps to two lines** ("Spotli"/"ght") under real on-device space pressure (badge + like heart + "..." button all competing for room in `TrackCard`'s top-right icon row). Fixed with `.fixedSize(horizontal: true, vertical: false)` on the badge's `HStack` — it now always renders at its natural single-line width; the `userChip`'s username `Text` (which already has `.lineLimit(1)`) truncates further instead under pressure, a strictly better tradeoff than the badge itself wrapping.
  **(6) Moved the "..." button to the bottom-right of every track card**, per direct request. `TrackCard.swift`: removed `TrackActionsMenuButton` from the top-right icon row; restructured the bottom track-info block from a plain `VStack` into an `HStack(alignment: .bottom)` pairing the title/artist `VStack` with the button. Since `TrackCard` is the single shared component (Feed/Profile/Shelf/Search, per its own doc comment), this one change covers "all track cards" with no other call sites touched.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green, zero errors. `swift test` in `NodalongKit/` — 89/89 tests pass (no NodalongKit changes this pass). **On-device unverified for all six** — this environment can't drive the actual app; needs a real device/simulator pass confirming: Followers/Following now navigates correctly from Home/Feed/NearMe/Shelf's Profile entry point; Jump Back In album taps open correctly (or, if not, check console for the new `[Nodalong.Home]` prints); Recommended for You appears reliably on repeated cold launches as a real subscriber; NearMe's bottom gap now looks correct with something playing; the Spotlight badge renders on one line; the "..." button appears bottom-right on cards across Feed/Profile/Shelf/Search.

- **2026-07-28** — **Home redesign, part 2: removed Playlists, Jump Back In now album-level, added On Repeat (new self-replay tracking) and Live Now — build-green, 89 NodalongKit tests pass (4 new).** Follow-up after seeing part 1 (the recommendation-shelf split) on-device. User feedback: remove "Your Playlists" from Home (Profile keeps its own copy, untouched); "Jump Back In" was the "wrong data source" — reworked from a flat rail of individually-played songs into recently-played **albums**, matching Spotify/Apple Music's "resume the context, not just the last song" pattern; add "On Repeat" and "Live Now."
  **Investigated before committing to designs** (per this session's own established discipline) rather than assuming the earlier "On Repeat is nearly free" pitch still held: it didn't — `Post.nods`/`playCount` only counts OTHER users nodding your track (a social signal), never incremented by your own replays, and `listenedAt` only updates when a track moves up in Apple's own ordering. There was no real "how many times have I replayed this" signal to query. Asked the user directly how to handle the gap (build real tracking now / defer / cheap approximation) — chose **build the real tracking now**, then asked (and got confirmed) that self-replay and social-nod counts should stay two genuinely separate fields, not merged into one.
  **On Repeat, the actual tracking fix — `Nodalong/API/Firestore/Posts.swift`'s `syncRecentTracks`:** it already computed exactly the right signal, just for the wrong purpose — `movedUp` (used only to decide whether to refresh `listenedAt`) is `true` for both "genuinely replayed" AND "brand new, never seen before." A genuine replay needs `existingRank[trackId] != nil && movedUp` (had a prior rank AND moved up) — brand-new tracks must NOT count. New `isGenuineReplay` computed this way, feeding a new `replayCount` field (read from + written to the `recent_tracks` doc, preserved across syncs like `playCount` already was) — kept entirely separate from `playCount`/`nods` per the user's confirmed choice. `Post` gained `replayCount: Int = 0` (additive, no existing call site touched — confirmed via grep that `Post` is never `Decoder`-decoded anywhere in the app, only ever built via its manual init, so adding a non-optional field with a default is safe). `HomeViewModel` derives `onRepeatPosts` from the already-fetched `recentPosts` (`.filter { $0.replayCount > 0 }.sorted { $0.replayCount > $1.replayCount }.prefix(10)`) — no new Firestore call. **Honest limitation, stated up front:** the counter only increments going forward from when this ships — On Repeat will read empty for every user until they've genuinely replayed something post-update; there's no way to backfill real history.
  **Jump Back In → albums:** new pure `recentAlbums(from:)` (`NodalongKit/Sources/NodalongKit/Utils/GroupPostsByAlbum.swift`) + `RecentAlbum` model — deliberately NOT a reuse of the existing `groupByAlbum`/`AlbumGroup` (which targets `LibraryTrackDoc` and needs `trackNumber`/`albumReleaseDate`, fields `Post` doesn't carry) — a lighter dedupe-by-album-keeping-the-most-recent-track function instead, reusing `primaryArtist(_:)` for the same feat.-credit-shouldn't-split-the-album reason `groupByAlbum` does. 4 new tests (`GroupPostsByAlbumTests.swift`, same shape as the existing `GroupByAlbumTests`). `HomeView`'s Jump Back In rail now shows album tiles; tapping one resolves the REAL catalog album via the already-built `AppleMusicService.resolveAlbum(catalogTrackId:isrc:title:artist:)` (built earlier this session for the Now Playing album-tap fix — a MusicKit relationship lookup, not a fuzzy text search, so it can't land on the wrong release) before pushing, with a spinner overlay (`isResolvingAlbum`) matching `NowPlayingView`'s established loading-guard convention.
  **Live Now:** new `LiveNowEntry` model (`.hostingParty(ListeningParty)` / `.listening(catalogId:)`) combining two structurally different, previously-unconnected data sources — `getLivePartiesHostedBy(uids:)` (Firestore, already used by Feed) for hosted parties, and a NEW one-time RTDB read `UserPresenceService.getNowPlayingOnce(uid:)` (added alongside the existing continuous `observeNowPlaying`, which is built for NearMe's live-while-viewing use case and doesn't fit Home's load-once/pull-to-refresh convention) fanned out per followed uid via `withTaskGroup`, for solo listening presence. A followed user hosting a party is only ever shown as hosting, never double-counted as also "listening solo." Hidden entirely when nobody's live (confirmed: follows-only, no NearMe-style "anyone nearby"). `UserPresenceService` gained `@unchecked Sendable` conformance to support the concurrent per-uid fan-out — documented as safe since `getNowPlayingOnce` never touches the class's one piece of mutable state (`observations`), which is only ever mutated by `observeNowPlaying`/`detachAll`, both already MainActor-only call sites. Tapping a Live Now avatar resolves the full `UserProfile` via the already-existing `getUserProfile(uid:)` before pushing (kept simple for v1 — navigates to their profile, not a direct join/tune-in action, to avoid duplicating NearMe's/Feed's existing flows).
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green on the first attempt for every piece (confirms `DatabaseReference.getData() async throws`, the `withTaskGroup` fan-out pattern, and `ListeningParty`'s existing `Sendable` conformance all work as assumed — flagged as real uncertainties in the plan, resolved positively by the compiler). One genuinely new concurrency warning surfaced on a full clean rebuild (`HomeViewModel`'s `withTaskGroup` closure capturing non-`Sendable` `UserPresenceService`) and was fixed immediately, not left in. `swift test` — 89/89 NodalongKit tests pass (85 existing + 4 new). **On-device unverified** — needs a real subscriber/follows-graph account to confirm Jump Back In resolves the correct album on tap, On Repeat populates after a genuine replay, and Live Now shows the right badge (dot vs. party icon) and hides correctly when nobody's live.

- **2026-07-28** — **Same-day follow-up: the previous playback-timeout fix missed the actual call site the user hit — mini-player resume, not tap-a-new-song — build-green, 85 tests unaffected.** User reported the previous fix didn't help: a second console log showed the same XPC failure signature, now with `applicationMusicPlayer: connection invalidated` / `Failed to obtain remoteObject: ... peer may have been unloaded` (a notch worse — the system media-player process itself likely died, not just a stale connection) — and this time "crashed, everything frozen," requiring force-quit. Investigated rather than guessing again: asked whether force-quit+reopen recovers it, and got the actually-decisive answer — **"tapping a new song works, pressing play on the mini player crashes."**
  **Root cause, confirmed by reading `togglePlayPause()` directly:** its resume branch (`PlayerViewModel.swift`) had its own separate, unguarded `try await player.play()` — never touched by the previous fix, which only wrapped the call inside `playCurrent()` (the tap-a-new-song path). Same underlying bug, different call site. A second unguarded instance existed in `resumeFollow()` (sync-follow/radio feature). The fact that tap-a-new-song genuinely recovered (not just "didn't crash that time") is itself useful evidence: it confirms the timeout-race approach *does* work against this hang — it's a suspension, not an unfixable thread block — the gap was purely that not every call site got the same guard.
  **Shipped:** both missed call sites now use the same `withTimeout(seconds: 8) { ... }` helper. `togglePlayPause()`'s resume branch goes further than a bare guard — on timeout/failure it now calls `await playCurrent()` instead of just silently setting `isPlaying = false`, giving the user a real second chance (re-resolve + re-queue from scratch) rather than a silent stuck-paused state. Also shipped the proactive mitigation requested for the underlying staleness: new `PlayerViewModel.observeScenePhaseForStaleConnection()` (wired from `init()`, same preview-safety guard as `setupRemoteCommands()`/`restoreQueue()`) tracks `didEnterBackgroundNotification`/`willEnterForegroundNotification`; if backgrounded more than 5 minutes, sets `playerLoaded = false` on return — which reuses `togglePlayPause()`'s **already-existing** `if !isPlaying, !playerLoaded, currentItem != nil { await playCurrent() }` branch, so the very next resume tap after a long background period automatically routes through the fully-guarded path instead of the bare resume call, with zero new branching needed there. Trade-off, accepted: a resume after a long background restarts the current track from position 0 rather than resuming — judged worth it against a frozen app. Notification closures wrap actual state mutation in `Task { @MainActor in ... }`, matching this file's own existing convention for the same API (`playPreview(of:)`'s `AVPlayerItemDidPlayToEndTime` observer).
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green, no new warnings. `swift test` — 85/85 pass (no NodalongKit changes). **On-device unverified** — same limitation as before, needs the actual stale-connection state to reproduce; the one thing confirmable from this environment is that the common case (quick app-switch, resume mid-track under 5 minutes) is untouched by the staleness reset and should behave identically to before.

- **2026-07-28** — **Fixed play silently doing nothing (and staying broken for every subsequent track) after the app sat idle for several days — build-green, 85 tests unaffected.** User pasted a real on-device console log after not using the app for a few days: pressed play in the mini player, nothing happened, then other tracks also did nothing. Most of the log is benign, already-documented MusicKit/system noise (`ICUserIdentityStore`, `MPIdentifierSet EMPTY`, curator-kind warnings — see §7) — but a specific, consistent cluster stood out: `applicationQueuePlayer _establishConnectionIfNeeded timeout [ping did not pong]`, `applicationController: xpc service connection interrupted`, `ASYNC-WATCHDOG-1/2`, `prepareToPlay failed [no target descriptor]`. This is MusicKit's `ApplicationMusicPlayer` failing to (re-)establish its XPC connection to the system media-player process — a genuine OS-level connectivity failure (plausible after days idle, the connection goes stale), not caused by this session's other work.
  **The real app-code gap, found by reading `playCurrent()` (`PlayerViewModel.swift`) directly rather than assuming the OS issue was unfixable:** `try await player.play()` had no timeout. If that XPC ping genuinely hangs rather than promptly throwing, the `await` never returns — `isLoadingTrack` (already `true`) never resets, `isPlaying` was already optimistically set `true` moments earlier, so the UI keeps showing "playing" forever with no sound and no error. Explains both symptoms: the first tap hangs silently, and every subsequent tap starts its own `playCurrent()` against the same broken connection and hangs the same way (nothing serializes concurrent attempts).
  **Fix:** new `withTimeout(seconds:operation:)` helper (`PlayerViewModel.swift`, via `withThrowingTaskGroup` racing the real call against a `Task.sleep` + throw) — doesn't force-cancel a stuck system call (MusicKit's XPC layer doesn't reliably respond to cancellation), it just lets our own code stop waiting and treat it as a failure. Wrapped only the one call demonstrated to hang: `try await withTimeout(seconds: 8) { try await player.play() }`. Deliberately did NOT wrap `resolveUsingCache` (the catalog lookup just before it) — no log evidence pointed at that call hanging. A timeout now flows into the exact same, already-correct `catch` block a real throw already had: falls back to `AVPlayer`-based preview playback (which doesn't go through `ApplicationMusicPlayer`/XPC at all, so it has a real chance of working even when the subscriber pipeline is down), and if that also fails, surfaces an error + skips to the next track with a toast — instead of hanging forever with a silently-lying "playing" UI.
  **Honest limitation, stated in the plan up front:** this makes the failure recoverable, it doesn't fix the underlying OS-level XPC flakiness itself (outside app code) — a genuinely broken connection may still mean preview-only playback until it recovers on its own (typically next relaunch or a media-services reset), but the app stays responsive and honest about it instead of looking frozen. **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green, no new warnings (confirms the `@Sendable` closure capturing `ApplicationMusicPlayer.shared` compiles cleanly under this project's `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY: complete`). `swift test` — 85/85 pass (no NodalongKit changes). **On-device unverified** — reproducing the exact stale-XPC state needs the app genuinely idle for days (or another way to force `applicationQueuePlayer` into a bad state), which can't be forced from this environment; the safest available check is that ordinary playback (a healthy connection) behaves identically post-change, since the 8s timeout should never fire in the normal case.

- **2026-07-28** — **Home redesign, part 1: "Recommended for You" split into its real titled shelves + a "See All" detail page — build-green, 85 tests unaffected.** User asked to redesign Home; discussion converged on a concrete, low-risk first win rather than guessing at a full visual overhaul: `AppleMusicService.getPersonalRecommendations` was flattening every recommendation group Apple Music returns (`response.recommendations.flatMap { $0.albums }`) into one undifferentiated rail, discarding each group's own real title (e.g. "New Music Mix", "Chill Mix") — confirmed via research this was genuinely thrown-away data, not a technical limitation. Mocked up the fix (plus a "Live Now" friends-presence idea, confirmed wanted but not yet built — reuse `UserPresenceService`/live party discovery from the NearMe session, follows-only, hidden entirely when nobody's live) via the visualize tool before writing code.
  **Shipped:** new `NodalongKit` model `RecommendationShelf` (`id`/`title`/`albums`). `AppleMusicService.getPersonalRecommendations` signature changed from `(maxResults:) -> [AlbumResult]` to `(maxGroups:) -> [RecommendationShelf]` — `request.limit` now means "how many recommendation groups" (Apple's own REST API default 5 / max 10), not "how many albums"; each `MusicPersonalRecommendation`'s own `title` is preserved, groups with zero albums (a playlist/station-only group — still v1-out-of-scope) are dropped rather than shown empty. `HomeViewModel.recommendedAlbums` → `recommendationShelves`, fetched with `maxGroups: 5` (Apple's own default — matches what a user would normally see). `HomeView`'s single flattened rail → `recommendedSection`, rendering each shelf under its own subheading, with a tappable "Recommended for You / See All ›" header. New `Nodalong/Features/Home/RecommendedForYouView.swift` — pushed via a new `SeeAllRecommendations` marker route on `HomeView`'s existing `path`, re-fetches independently with `maxGroups: 10` (Apple's documented max) so the detail page has genuinely more content, not just the same 5 shelves re-shown. Doesn't redeclare `.navigationDestination(for: AlbumResult.self)` — it's always pushed onto `HomeView`'s own stack, which already has it.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green (one real compiler catch: `.navigationDestination(for: SeeAllRecommendations.self) { RecommendedForYouView() }` needed an explicit `{ _ in ... }` — Swift won't let a single-argument closure silently ignore its parameter when the body takes no args). `swift test` — 85/85 NodalongKit tests pass (new model has no logic to test). **On-device unverified** — needs a real subscriber account to confirm Home shows genuinely-titled shelves (not a flat rail) and that the See All page's 10-group fetch actually surfaces additional shelves beyond Home's 5, not duplicates. **Not yet built, confirmed wanted**: the "Live Now" friends-presence section — next step whenever picked back up.

- **2026-07-28** — **Fixed a real regression (black area behind the keyboard) and attempted a speculative one (mini player disappearing while Shelf's filter is focused) from the tab-bar rewrite — build-green, 85 tests unaffected.** User screenshots showed both while using Shelf's `.searchable` filter (unrelated to the reverted redirect above — this is Shelf's own pre-existing local search, the keyboard coming up from typing into it directly).
  **Root cause, confirmed by re-reading `QueueToastOverlay.swift`:** when `BottomSection` was stripped down to just the toast pills earlier this stretch, its keyboard-tracking logic (`keyboardVisible` state + `keyboardWillShow`/`keyboardWillHide` observers + `.background(keyboardVisible ? Color.nodalong.bgBase : .clear)`) was removed entirely on the assumption it was specific to positioning the old mini-player pill. It wasn't — that fill's real job was painting over the region behind/around the keyboard, which nothing else covers; removing it re-exposed the raw black UIKit layer. **Fixed, high confidence:** reinstated the exact same tracking + fill in `QueueToastOverlay.swift`, applied regardless of whether a toast is currently showing.
  **Mini player disappearing while the keyboard is up is a different, less certain issue** — the mini player now lives in the system-managed `.tabViewBottomAccessory` slot, and multiple Apple Developer Forums threads (found during the same-day tab-bar research) already document real visibility bugs in that API on this iOS release (accessories disappearing, stale empty slots, no reliable programmatic visibility control). No documented fix exists for "accessory hides when a keyboard/searchable field is focused" specifically. **Attempted, explicitly flagged as speculative:** extended `MainTabView`'s already-conditional `.tabBarMinimizeBehavior` with a new `isKeyboardVisible` tracked state (same notification pattern) — `.never` whenever a keyboard could be up, not just when nothing's playing — on the theory that the system might be treating keyboard-appearance like a scroll-down trigger. Framed explicitly to the user: if the mini player still disappears after this, that confirms it's OS-level `tabViewBottomAccessory` behavior with no public lever to fix, not something worth continuing to chase blind.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green, 85/85 NodalongKit tests pass. **On-device unverified** — the background-fill fix is high-confidence (exact restoration of previously-working code), but whether the mini player actually stays visible needs a real check, and a "still disappears" result is itself useful information (rules out a code fix entirely for this iOS release).

- **2026-07-24** — **Reverted the "Search redirects to Shelf's own filter" half of the entry immediately below, same day it shipped — build-green, 85 tests unaffected.** On-device (screenshots), the redirect worked (tapping Search from Shelf landed back on Shelf with the filter focused/keyboard up), but the mini player didn't appear above Shelf's search bar the way it normally floats above content — rather than chase that interaction further, user asked to revert the redirect outright. Removed cleanly: `MainTabView.swift`'s `lastNonSearchTab` state + its `.onChange(of: selectedTab)` interceptor, `ShelfViewModel.focusSearchTrigger`, and `ShelfView`'s `@FocusState`/`.searchFocused`/`.onChange` wiring — `.searchable(text: $shelfVM.searchQuery, prompt: "Filter…")` is back to its bare pre-redirect form. Search from any tab (including Shelf) once again shows the real global `SearchView()`. **The tab-bar-minimize fix from the same entry (`.tabBarMinimizeBehavior(playerVM.nowPlaying != nil ? .onScrollDown : .never)`) is untouched** — only the search redirect was reverted. If "Shelf search should be screen-specific" comes up again, the mini-player-visibility interaction with a focused `.searchable` field is the thing to solve first, not just redo the same redirect.

- **2026-07-24** — **Same-day follow-up on the new tab bar: Search redirects to Shelf's own filter when reached from Shelf, and the tab bar no longer over-minimizes with nothing playing — build-green, 85 tests unaffected.** *(Reverted the Search-redirect half of this same day, per the entry above — kept here for history.)* Two issues: (1) the original product spec ("if in Shelf, we're searching their library") was still unmet — the new `role: .search` tab always opened the global People/Music `SearchView()` regardless of which tab it was tapped from. (2) A real bug, caught from a screenshot: with the mini player dismissed, scrolling any tab still minimized the tab bar down to just 2 icons (Home + Search) with a big empty gap where Feed/Near Me/Shelf used to be — `.tabBarMinimizeBehavior(.onScrollDown)` was unconditional, but minimizing only makes sense when there's an accessory (the mini player) to expand into the freed space.
  **Fix 1, `MainTabView.swift`:** new `@State lastNonSearchTab` tracks the most recent "real" tab (0–3) via `.onChange(of: selectedTab)`; when the new value is 4 (Search) and `lastNonSearchTab == 3` (Shelf), immediately snap `selectedTab` back to 3 and toggle a new `ShelfViewModel.focusSearchTrigger` instead of ever showing `SearchView()` — reuses Shelf's existing `.searchable` filter entirely, no new search UI. `ShelfView.swift` gained `@FocusState private var isSearchFocused` + `.searchFocused($isSearchFocused)` on its existing `.searchable(...)`, driven by a `.onChange(of: shelfVM.focusSearchTrigger)`. Landing back on 3 re-fires the same `onChange` handler with `newValue == 3`, which just falls into the (harmless, idempotent) `else` branch — no loop. Home/Feed/NearMe are unaffected; Search from those still shows the real global search.
  **Fix 2, `MainTabView.swift`:** `.tabBarMinimizeBehavior(.onScrollDown)` → `.tabBarMinimizeBehavior(playerVM.nowPlaying != nil ? .onScrollDown : .never)` — a computed *value* passed to an always-attached modifier (not a conditionally-attached modifier), so it doesn't risk the conditional-accessory bugs noted in the entry below for `.tabViewBottomAccessory`. Confirmed via research `TabBarMinimizeBehavior` has `.never`/`.automatic`/`.onScrollDown`/`.onScrollUp` cases; `.never` keeps the tab bar always fully expanded.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green, 85/85 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). **On-device unverified**, per this environment's standing limitation: does tapping Search from Shelf land back on Shelf with the filter focused and no visible flash of the global search screen; does the tab bar now stay fully expanded on scroll with nothing playing while the existing minimize/merge behavior is unaffected when something is.

- **2026-07-24** — **Mimicked Apple Music's native tab bar (separated Search tab, minimize-on-scroll, merged mini-player) — deployment target bumped to iOS 26.1, build-green, 85 tests unaffected.** Same-day follow-up: user shared Apple Music screenshots pointing out three things to mimic — Search always a separated circle bottom-right, the tab bar shrinking to just the edge icons while scrolling with the mini player expanding to fill the gap, and the mini player popping back above a fully-expanded tab bar on return to the top. Researched and confirmed this is a real, current SwiftUI feature set, not something to hand-roll: `Tab(role: .search)` (auto-separates a tab to the trailing edge), `.tabViewBottomAccessory(isEnabled:content:)` (persistent accessory slot for the mini player), `.tabBarMinimizeBehavior(.onScrollDown)` (the shrink/expand-on-scroll behavior). Locked two decisions via `AskUserQuestion`: Search becomes a real tab again (reversing the SAME DAY's earlier Tab Navigation 2.0 change, which had made it a toolbar-button + fullScreenCover); deployment target bumps with no iOS-16/17 fallback path (rejected the alternative of `if #available` gating both behaviors side by side).
  **Real minimum version needed turned out to be higher than initially planned — corrected live via the compiler, not guessed:** `project.yml` was bumped 16.0 → "18.0" per the plan (web research had described these as "iOS 18-equivalent" APIs), but the actual SDK on this machine (Xcode's iOS 26.2 SDK) rejected `tabViewBottomAccessoryPlacement` at 18.0/26.0, requiring 26.0, and then rejected `tabViewBottomAccessory(isEnabled:content:)` specifically at 26.0, requiring **26.1** (the `isEnabled:` overload — the one that avoids a documented empty-accessory-slot bug — was added in a later point release than the base modifier). Final: `project.yml`'s `options.deploymentTarget.iOS` AND the separate explicit `IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET` build setting (a duplicate override in `settings.base` that was masking the first change) both set to `"26.1"`.
  **`MainTabView.swift`** rewritten from `.tabItem`-based children to the declarative `Tab(...)` value API: `Tab("Home", systemImage:, value: 0) { HomeView() }` etc. for the 4 grouped tabs, plus a 5th `Tab("Search", systemImage:, value: 4, role: .search) { SearchView() }` — the `role: .search` is what makes iOS render it as the separated trailing circle, no custom layout needed. The old `.safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom) { BottomSection() }` hosting `MiniPlayerBar` is replaced by `.tabViewBottomAccessory(isEnabled: playerVM.nowPlaying != nil) { MiniPlayerBar() }` + `.tabBarMinimizeBehavior(.onScrollDown)`. Deliberately used `isEnabled:` rather than a conditional `if` wrapped around the whole modifier — two separate Apple Developer Forums threads (found during research) report a crash when an accessory's presence is conditioned together with `TabView(selection:)`, and a stale-empty-slot bug when relying on the content view alone returning empty; `isEnabled:` is the documented fix for both, and was designed in before ever hitting either bug. The old `searchVM.isPresented` `fullScreenCover` for Search is removed entirely.
  **`MiniPlayerBar.swift`** now reads `@Environment(\.tabViewBottomAccessoryPlacement)` (`.expanded` vs `.inline`) to adapt: in `.inline` (merged into the tab bar row while scrolling) it drops its own background/border/shadow (the system's glass tab bar already frames it there — doubling up would look wrong) and drops the dismiss (✕) button (no room next to the Home/Search icons in that narrow slot). Everything else (artwork spin, title/artist, play/pause, tap-to-expand) is unchanged in both placements.
  **`BottomSection.swift` renamed `QueueToastOverlay.swift`** — its job shrank to just the queue/party toast pills once the mini player moved to the accessory slot; positioned via `.overlay(alignment: .bottom)` on the TabView with an approximate `.padding(.bottom, 100)` clearance (an estimate, not a measured value — flagged for an on-device look, see below).
  **Reverted the same-session-earlier per-screen Search entry points**: removed the toolbar search buttons + `searchVM` env-object references from `HomeView`/`FeedView`/`NearMeView`, removed `SearchView`'s "Cancel" toolbar button, removed `SearchViewModel.isPresented` entirely. Profile's entry point (`.profileEntryPoint()`, from the earlier Tab Navigation 2.0 work) is untouched — this request was scoped to Search + the mini-player/tab-bar relationship only.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green (three iterations — each iteration's compiler error is what revealed the actual required deployment target, rather than guessing from web research alone). `swift test` — 85/85 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). Zero new warnings. **On-device unverified, explicitly flagged in the plan before implementing:** does Search actually render as the separated trailing circle; does the tab bar genuinely shrink on scroll-down with the mini player merging into that space, then re-expand on scroll-up/tab-switch; does the inline mini player (no dismiss button, no own chrome) look right sitting directly in the glass tab bar; does the toast pill's new fixed bottom padding clear the tab bar correctly in both states; and — since this is genuinely new, recently-shipped Apple API territory — watch for the two point-release bugs named above in case they resurface on whatever exact iOS build the test device runs.

- **2026-07-24** — **Home/Hub + Tab Navigation 2.0 + adaptive "..." action menu + three bugs — all three phases build-green, 85 NodalongKit tests pass (3 new).** User request bundled four independent workstreams: a new "for you" Home/Hub screen (user's own observation — they mostly discover music by directly searching individual songs, not via anything the app surfaces to them), a tab-bar restructure to make room for it, replacing the undiscoverable long-press track menu with a visible "..." button, and three bugs. Explored via 4 parallel Explore agents (tab/nav structure, action-menu wiring, Shelf grouping bug, Now Playing nav bugs) before planning; locked four product decisions via `AskUserQuestion` (Home as a separate tab, not absorbing Feed; Home v1 sections = Jump Back In + Friends' Spotlights + Your Playlists + Apple Music recommendations; adaptive "..." menu; Profile pushes via normal nav, not an overlay). Plan file: `~/.claude/plans/shimmering-munching-sparrow.md`.
  **Bug 1 — Shelf split a featured-artist track off its album into its own "album"** (e.g. Jamie xx "In Waves": the plain tracks group correctly, but "Jamie xx & Honey Dijon"/"Jamie xx & The Avalanches" each became their own single-track group). Root cause: `GroupByAlbum.swift`'s grouping key is `"\(album)||||\(primaryArtistName(artist))"`, and `primaryArtistName` only stripped text after `" feat."` — it never handled `&`/`,`/`with`/`ft.` credits, so a featured track's artist string never matched its album's plain tracks. Fix: `primaryArtistName` now delegates to the already-existing, more complete `primaryArtist(_:)` (`SearchQueryNormalization.swift`, built for Spotify search-query normalization but a strict superset of the old splitting — same module, already public). One function, four lines. New `GroupByAlbumTests.swift` (3 tests) — confirms the Jamie xx shape, confirms different-artist albums still stay separate, confirms the old feat.-only case still works.
  **Bug 2 — Now Playing's album tap sometimes resolved to the wrong album.** Root cause: `loadAlbum` concatenated `"<album> <artist>"` into one fuzzy `searchAll(q, maxPerType: 1)` catalog text search and blindly took `.first` — no ISRC/id anchor, no relationship lookup, so a shared album name (e.g. a deluxe reissue) could resolve to the wrong release. **Bug 3 — artist tap with multiple credited artists silently picked one, no way to choose.** Same root cause class — `loadArtist` sent the raw multi-artist display string straight to a 1-result fuzzy search.
  **Fix for both, `AppleMusicService.swift`:** new `resolveAlbum(catalogTrackId:isrc:title:artist:)` / `resolveArtists(...)` / private `resolveDetailedSong(...)` — uses MusicKit's real `Song.albums`/`Song.artists` relationships (`song.with([.albums, .artists])`, confirmed via Apple's documented relationship API) instead of guessing from text. `resolveDetailedSong` mirrors the existing `resolveCatalogArtwork` id→ISRC→title/artist cascade (a library-matched recently-played track's own id is often a library-scoped id, not a real catalog id). `resolveArtists` returns EVERY credited artist as real `Artist` objects — no string splitting. Kept off the `MusicService` protocol (like `resolveCatalogArtwork`) — Apple-only; Spotify has no reachable entry point today per the 2026-07-17 suspension, so its old `searchAll` fallback is left untouched rather than extended for a dead path. `NowPlayingTrack` (`PlayerViewModel.swift`) gained an `isrc: String?` field (explicit init added — Swift's synthesized memberwise init did NOT expose an inline-defaulted property as an overridable parameter the way expected; worth remembering for next time), populated at all 4 relevant construction sites. `NowPlayingView.swift`: `loadAlbum`/`loadArtist` now take the whole `track` and call the new methods directly when `activeService == .appleMusic`; artist tap shows a new `ArtistPickerSheet` (simple `List` of avatar+name rows, new file `Nodalong/Features/Player/ArtistPickerSheet.swift`) when more than one artist is returned, dismiss-then-present via the same `DispatchQueue.main.asyncAfter` pattern already used for the Queue→Party cover transition (two `.sheet` modifiers changing state in the same tick can conflict).
  **Tab Navigation 2.0** (`MainTabView.swift`): tabs go from Feed/Search/NearMe/Shelf/Profile (5) to **Home/Feed/NearMe/Shelf (4)** — Feed's icon changes `house.fill`→`person.2.fill` since Home now owns the house glyph. Search becomes a `fullScreenCover` (same pattern as NowPlayingView/PartyView) bound to a new `@Published var isPresented` on the already-app-wide `SearchViewModel` — no new coordinator object needed, any screen's toolbar button just flips it; `SearchView` gained a "Cancel" toolbar button since it's no longer a tab. **Profile** becomes a new shared `ProfileEntryPoint` ViewModifier (`Nodalong/Shared/UI/ProfileEntryPoint.swift`) — a self-contained avatar toolbar button + `.navigationDestination(isPresented:)`, applied via one-line `.profileEntryPoint()` to Home/Feed/NearMe/Shelf's own `NavigationStack`s. Confirmed multiple `.navigationDestination` triggers (existing typed ones + this boolean one) coexist safely on one stack — no conflict with Feed's/Shelf's existing typed `NavigationPath` destinations.
  **Adaptive "..." action menu**: deleted the long-press mechanism entirely (`QueueContextMenu.swift`, its `.onLongPressGesture` + `View.queueContextMenu(...)` extension — undiscoverable, no visual cue it existed). New `Nodalong/Shared/UI/TrackActionsMenuButton.swift` — a visible "..." button hosting the same confirmationDialog/party-routing/playlist-sheet logic `QueueContextMenu` had, now tap-triggered. Adaptive by construction: on `TrackCard` (has room) it's added alongside the existing separate visible like/spotlight icons, offering only Queue/Playlist; on cramped list rows (Search's `TrackSearchRow`, `ArtistDetailSearchView`/`AlbumDetailSearchView`/`ShelfView`'s track rows, `PlaylistDetailView`'s `trackRow`) it REPLACES the row's own inline like button, consolidating like+queue+playlist behind one button. `PlaylistDetailView`'s rows gained a like toggle they never had before (a natural inclusion under "all available actions" — needed adding `libraryVM` as a new env object there, already injected app-wide so no extra plumbing). New `Icons.moreActions = "ellipsis"` constant.
  **Home/Hub v1** (`Nodalong/Features/Home/`): `HomeViewModel` (mirrors `ProfileView`'s `hasLoaded` guard convention) + `HomeView`. Four sections: Jump Back In reuses `getYourMusicFeed(uid:)` + the existing `RecentTracksCarousel` verbatim (same data/component `ProfileView` already uses for its own recents — zero new plumbing); Your Playlists reuses `PlaylistsProfileSection()` verbatim; Friends' Spotlights is new — `getFriendsSpotlightsToday(followingUids:)` (`Users.swift`) reuses `getUserProfiles(uids:)` (its decode path already overlays catalog metadata onto `spotlightTracks[]` via `enrichSpotlights` — no extra enrichment needed) filtered to today's `dateKey`, rendered via a horizontal rail of `TrackCard`s reusing its existing `username`/`avatarColor`/`isSpotlit` attribution fields (new `FriendSpotlight` model, `NodalongKit`, composite `id` = `"\(user.uid)_\(track.trackId)"` since two friends can legitimately spotlight the same track same day); Recommended for You calls new `AppleMusicService.getPersonalRecommendations(maxResults:)` (`MusicPersonalRecommendationsRequest`, confirmed real MusicKit API via web search — subscriber + real-device only, gated on `canPlayFullTracks`) — **v1 scope: albums only**, the payload's playlists/stations are deferred (would need new NodalongKit model types + UI). Kept off the `MusicService` protocol — Spotify deprecated its own Recommendations API in Nov 2024 and has no reachable entry point anyway. Reused Search's existing `AlbumsCarousel`/`AlbumCard` for the recommendations rail (both changed from `private` to internal — zero duplicate album-tile component).
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath xcodebuild` green after every phase (hit one real gotcha: new files must go through `xcodegen generate` before a build sees them — "has no member" errors on a freshly-created file's own extension are the tell). `swift test` — 85/85 NodalongKit tests pass (82 existing + 3 new `GroupByAlbumTests`). Grepped for stray `queueContextMenu`/long-press references post-migration — none left except accurate historical doc comments. **On-device unverified for all three phases** — this environment has no way to drive the actual app (no `idb`, no authenticated simulator session per this project's established limitation). Needs a real device/simulator pass: Shelf regrouping with a real featured-artist album; Now Playing album/artist taps against a track with a genuinely ambiguous release or multiple credited artists; the new 4-tab bar + Search/Profile entry points from each tab; the "..." menu's adaptive action set in both contexts; Home's four sections rendering correctly for an account with playlists/spotlit friends/an Apple Music subscription, degrading gracefully without any of those.

- **2026-07-17** — **Fixed Control Center pause racing the auto-advance, leaving the app's published state out of sync with the actual audio — build-green, 82 NodalongKit tests unaffected.** User report: locked-phone playback, queue has a few songs; tapping pause in Control Center sometimes shows the ORIGINAL track paused on reopening the app while the NEXT queued track is audibly playing. Entered Plan Mode; investigated by direct tracing (no subagent needed — already had strong context on `PlayerViewModel.swift` from the same-day sync-playback work) rather than guessing.
  **Root cause, confirmed by reading the exact code, not assumed:** two independent paths mutate `isPlaying`/`ApplicationMusicPlayer.shared` with **no mutual exclusion**. (1) The remote pause command (`setupRemoteCommands()`'s `pauseCommand.addTarget`) stamps `lastExplicitPauseAt` synchronously, then schedules `Task { @MainActor in await togglePlayPause() }` — merely *queued*, not immediate. (2) `playCurrent()` (reached identically from `handleTrackEnd()`, `skipToNext()`, `skipToPrevious()`, `playQueueItem()` — confirmed all four funnel through it) sets `isPlaying = true` + `nowPlaying = <next track>` **synchronously, before** its first real `await` (`resolveUsingCache`, a network/catalog resolve) and long before the actual `player.queue = [...]; try await player.play()`. That gap is a genuine yield point the MainActor can service other queued work in — including an earlier-queued `togglePlayPause()` Task from a DIFFERENT track's pause tap. **Why "often," not rare:** pausing right as a song is winding down is an ordinary listening pattern — track A naturally ends (genuinely, independent of the pause — nothing has called `player.pause()` yet), the progress-timer edge detector's existing 200ms-confirm + `lastExplicitPauseAt` grace-window heuristic (a **time-based** check, not a real synchronization primitive, and known-vulnerable under real locked/backgrounded scheduling delays) lets `handleTrackEnd()` → `playCurrent()` proceed for track B; `playCurrent()` sets `isPlaying = true`/`nowPlaying = B` then awaits resolve; the stale `togglePlayPause()` Task finally runs in that window, reads `isPlaying == true` (for B, before B's audio has started), calls `player.pause()` (no-op — B isn't queued into the player yet) and sets `isPlaying = false`; `playCurrent()` resumes and unconditionally calls `player.play()` for B — real audio starts — but nothing in the success path re-asserts `isPlaying = true`, so the published state is stuck `false` while B genuinely plays. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` that fixing this should let the advance proceed and make the pause land on whichever track ends up loaded (guaranteeing state/reality consistency), rather than the bigger, more invasive alternative of trying to abort the in-flight advance and stay on the ending track.
  **Fix, all in `PlayerViewModel.swift`:** new `pendingPauseIntent` flag alongside the existing `isAdvancing`/`isLoadingTrack`. `togglePlayPause()`'s `ApplicationMusicPlayer` pause branch: if `isLoadingTrack == true` (a transition is already in flight), don't call `player.pause()` against a player that may not have the next track loaded — set `pendingPauseIntent = true` and defer instead of mutating `isPlaying`/`lastPlaybackStatus` immediately. `playCurrent()`'s success path, right after `try await player.play()` succeeds: if `pendingPauseIntent` is set, consume it immediately (`player.pause()`, `isPlaying = false`, `lastPlaybackStatus = .paused`, re-stamp `lastExplicitPauseAt`) — applies the user's pause the instant the new track actually starts, so no observer ever sees the inconsistent intermediate state. `pendingPauseIntent = false` also added to `playCurrent()`'s existing top-of-function reset block (alongside `lastExplicitPauseAt = nil`) so a stale pending-pause from a previous track's transition can't leak onto an unrelated later one (e.g. the user tapping a different song directly). The existing `lastExplicitPauseAt` grace-window check is untouched — confirmed it still correctly protects the **common, non-racing** case (togglePlayPause()'s own `player.pause()`+`isPlaying = false` happen on the same MainActor turn with no intervening `await`, so the timer's next tick sees `isPlaying == false` and exits before reaching the edge-detector at all) — this fix only closes the specific gap where a pause races an **already-in-flight** advance from a different track. Scoped to the Apple Music (`ApplicationMusicPlayer`) branch only — `playCurrentViaSpotify` has an analogous structure but Spotify support is currently suspended (no reachable entry point per the same-day removal work above), so left untouched.
  **Known, accepted residual edge case** (not fixed, benign): if a pause races a track resolve that then FAILS (unplayable track → `skipUnplayableAndAdvance` → recursive `playCurrent()`), the recursive call's own top-of-function reset wipes `pendingPauseIntent` before it's ever consumed — the user's pause tap is silently dropped in that specific narrow case, but this does NOT reproduce the reported bug (no `player.play()` ever ran for the failed attempt, so there's no audio/UI mismatch) — just a rare missed-pause, considered acceptable rather than worth the added complexity to handle.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green, no new warnings. `swift test` — all 82 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). **On-device unverified** — this is fundamentally a real-device timing bug; queue 2–3 songs, lock the phone, tap pause via Control Center at varying points (deliberately near a track's natural end — the reported trigger — and mid-track, which should behave exactly as before) across several repetitions, since the original bug was intermittent by nature and the fix removes the race structurally rather than being provable by a single manual test.
  **Same-day on-device follow-up: the fix's diagnosis was INCOMPLETE.** User confirmed the display-state mismatch is fixed (no longer shows the wrong/previous track as paused), but the skip-to-next-track itself still happens on **every** pause, not just near a track's natural end — this contradicts the narrow race the `pendingPauseIntent` fix targeted (which requires a genuine end-of-track to coincide with the pause) and points to a broader, not-yet-understood cause, likely related to how drastically Task/Timer scheduling is delayed for a genuinely locked (not just backgrounded) app — a hypothesis, not confirmed. Rather than guess further, added extensive `[Nodalong.Player.Diag]` millisecond-timestamped `print()` diagnostics (all tagged for easy removal once root-caused) at every decision point: `pauseCommand`'s fire time + its `togglePlayPause()` Task's completion time, `togglePlayPause()`'s entry state and which branch it takes, every progress-timer tick's `playbackStatus`/`justStopped` read (to reveal the timer's real cadence while locked vs. its configured 0.5s interval), the 200ms-confirm task's grace-window elapsed-time check and outcome, `handleTrackEnd()`'s entry state, and `playCurrent()`'s `pendingPauseIntent` consumption. New `nonisolated private static func diagTimestamp()` (mirrors `markExplicitPause()`'s `nonisolated` pattern — needed since the pauseCommand closure runs off-MainActor). Build green. **Next step, not yet done:** user needs to reproduce once more with this build and share the console output (ideally via Xcode/Console.app while the repro happens) so the actual sequence — real elapsed times between the pause tap, the timer ticks, and `handleTrackEnd()` — can be read directly instead of theorized. Remove the `[Nodalong.Player.Diag]` prints once the real cause is found and fixed.

- **2026-07-17** — **Fixed a stale "X is hosting a party" Feed banner that could survive days after the party actually ended — build-green, verified installed on-device (fix not yet re-confirmed by the reporting user).** User reported (with two screenshots) a test account's Feed showing "bigdeccyh is hosting 'Join this'" while bigdeccyh's own device showed no active party — and, when asked, confirmed the party had been properly ended via the app's own "End" button **days earlier**, not merely abandoned via a crash/force-quit.
  **Investigated as a possible recurrence of the 2026-07-10 "zombie live parties" bug** (`endOrphanedLiveParties`, `endParty`'s always-run `status: .ended` write) — confirmed that mechanism is still correctly wired (`ContentView.swift`'s auth-ready `.task`) and, per its own documented scope, deliberately only runs on a genuine cold app launch (not on foreground-return), since a host is allowed to minimize the party and keep hosting in the background — re-running it on every foreground-return would risk incorrectly ending a legitimately still-active session.
  **Actual root cause, confirmed by reading `FeedView.swift`:** `liveParties` (the list backing the "X is hosting" banner, fetched via `getLivePartiesHostedBy(uids: following)` in `loadLiveParties(uid:)`) was fetched **exactly once per app session**, inside the initial `.task` block alongside `feedVM.load(...)` — unlike the main feed itself, which already refreshes via both pull-to-refresh (`.refreshable`) and `UIApplication.willEnterForegroundNotification`. A party ending correctly in Firestore has no way to reach a device whose Feed was already loaded and has simply been sitting open/backgrounded since — even for days — since nothing ever re-fetches that specific query. The "ended days ago, banner still showing" detail is strong evidence for this (a purely stale local snapshot) rather than a broken Firestore write, though not 100% conclusive without confirming the banner clears after a fresh fetch.
  **Fix:** added `loadLiveParties(uid:)` calls to both of `FeedView`'s existing refresh triggers — the `.refreshable` pull-to-refresh closure and the `willEnterForegroundNotification` handler — mirroring exactly how `feedVM.refresh(...)` is already wired in both spots. **Checked the other two party-discovery surfaces for the same bug class and found neither affected:** `NearMeUserSheet`'s `hostedParty` is fetched fresh on every sheet presentation (a new avatar tap = a new `.task`), not cached per session, so it was never susceptible.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green for both simulator and the real device target; installed to bigdeccyh's physical device. **Not yet re-confirmed by the user** — next step is to pull-to-refresh (or foreground-return) the *test* account's Feed and confirm the stale banner clears; if it doesn't, that would indicate a second, deeper bug in `endParty`'s actual Firestore write (not yet investigated, since this fix wasn't confirmed insufficient).

- **2026-07-17** — **Five independent Profile/UI polish fixes from real-device screenshots — build-green, 82 NodalongKit tests unaffected.** Investigated all five with 3 parallel Explore agents reading the actual code before proposing fixes, per plan-mode workflow (a standing memory now requires showing proposed code in plans, not just prose — added mid-session after a prior plan was rejected for lacking it).
  **(1) Playlists "New" tile misaligned** (`Nodalong/Features/Playlists/PlaylistsProfileSection.swift`) — the rail's `HStack` had no explicit alignment (defaulted to `.center`); `PlaylistTile` is a 3-row `VStack` (artwork+title+subtitle) while `newTile` is only 2 rows (artwork+"New"), so the shorter tile got vertically centered against its taller siblings instead of top-aligned. Fixed: `HStack(alignment: .top, spacing: 12)`.
  **(2) Recent Tracks carousel left dead trailing space on a short page** (`Nodalong/Shared/UI/RecentTracksCarousel.swift`) — `carouselHeight` was one constant (`pageSize * 166`) applied to every page regardless of actual track count, with the absorbing `Spacer` sitting *inside* the `bgSurface`-clipped box, so a short page (fewer than `pageSize` tracks) stretched the rounded box itself. Fixed by porting `SpotlightCarousel`'s already-proven two-layer pattern verbatim: pin the `TabView`'s own frame to `maxPageHeight` (`pageSize * 166`) so its UIKit paging view never relayouts mid-swipe, move the `Spacer` outside the box, and animate a second outer `.frame(height: displayedHeight).clipped()` sized to the *current page's actual* track count (new `pageHeight(for:)`, new custom `init` seeding `displayedHeight` correctly on first render — previews unaffected, same labeled-argument call shape).
  **(3) Spotlight carousel: some days showed more than 3 cards, clipped, with square corners** (`Nodalong/Shared/UI/SpotlightCarousel.swift`) — `maxPageHeight` is deliberately pinned to exactly `maxSpotlights` (3) cards' worth of height (a past-session fix, on the assumption a day can never exceed the cap), but `pageHeight(days:index:)` and the card `ForEach` both used the *actual* `tracks.count` with no clamp — if a day's data ever exceeded 3 (see below), that page's real content became taller than the pinned frame, clipping straight through the extra card's rounded container mid-height. Fixed with a display-layer clamp: `pageHeight` now does `min(maxSpotlights, ...)`, and the `ForEach` iterates `day.tracks.prefix(maxSpotlights)` — guarantees the carousel never renders more than it was sized for, regardless of underlying data. **Known deeper gap, flagged not fixed:** the 3-per-day cap is enforced only client-side against possibly-stale local state (`FirebaseAuthViewModel.addToSpotlight`) — `firestore.rules` has no server-side count check on `spotlight_history`, so a multi-device race could in principle still write a 4th+ entry server-side. This display clamp fully fixes the visual symptom; closing the data-integrity gap would need a Firestore rules change, a separate, security-adjacent piece of work not done here.
  **(4) UserProfileView (and, less visibly, ProfileView) sometimes showed yesterday's spotlights under a "Today" heading** — both `buildSpotlightDays()` implementations (`ProfileView.swift`, `UserProfileView.swift`) blindly assigned the embedded `spotlightTracks[]` field to the "today" bucket (`grouped[todayKey] = active`) without checking those tracks' own `spotlitAt`/dateKey. That field is only pruned to "today only" as a *side effect* of the profile owner's next spotlight write (`addToSpotlight` rebuilds it from date-filtered tracks before persisting) — so after a day rolls over with no new spotlight yet, Firestore still holds yesterday's entries verbatim, and nothing on read filtered them. Far more visible on `UserProfileView` (viewing someone who may not have spotlit anything yet today) than one's own profile (usually viewed right after spotlighting). Fixed identically in both files: `let activeToday = active.filter { dateKey(epochMs: $0.spotlitAt) == todayKey }`, then `grouped[todayKey] = activeToday` instead of the unfiltered `active`.
  **(5) Splash/loading screen background color didn't match either light or dark theme** — root-caused (not the SwiftUI `SplashView`/`LaunchSplash`, both ruled out by an Explore agent since they have extra elements — subtitle+buttons, or icon+spinner — that don't match the screenshot's bare "bold white text on solid purple, nothing else") to the **native `LaunchScreen.storyboard`**: its `<resources>` block caches a design-time-only preview RGB for the `bgDeep` named color (`#5B2D9F`) that doesn't match the *current* live asset-catalog value in either appearance (light `#5F418B` / dark `#3A2E4D`) — a stale snapshot from whenever the storyboard was last saved in Interface Builder, disconnected from later edits to the asset catalog. This cache is required to exist (removing it entirely crashes `ibtool` — confirmed in a past session) but its specific values were stale. Asked the user whether to just resync the cache (a correctness fix) or redesign bgDeep to look closer to the rest of the palette (a bigger decision needing mockups, per this project's own established convention) — **user chose resync only**. Updated the cached `<namedColor name="bgDeep">` RGB to match the current light `bgDeep` exactly.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green for both simulator and the real device target (confirms the storyboard XML edit compiles cleanly too — the build itself is the `ibtool` regression test). `swift test` — 82/82 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). Build installed to the user's physical device for visual confirmation — **on-device visual confirmation of all five still pending** as of this entry.

- **2026-07-17** — **Fixed two real Control Center bugs, both root-caused via on-device diagnostic logging + live console streaming (`xcrun devicectl device process launch --console`) rather than guesswork — build-green, 82 NodalongKit tests unaffected, both fixes confirmed working on the user's real device.** User report: locked-phone playback, pausing via Control Center sometimes caused the current track to "end" and the next queued track to start playing immediately, with the app showing the wrong (previous) track as paused on reopen — a genuine state/reality mismatch, not just a stale UI redraw.
  **First fix attempt (targeted a real but incomplete diagnosis):** traced a genuine race between `togglePlayPause()` (via the remote `pauseCommand`) and the auto-advance chain (`handleTrackEnd()`/`playCurrent()`), both mutating `isPlaying`/`ApplicationMusicPlayer.shared` with no mutual exclusion. Shipped a `pendingPauseIntent` flag: if a pause arrives while `isLoadingTrack` is true, defer it instead of applying immediately; `playCurrent()`'s success path consumes it the instant the new track starts. **On-device test showed the fix was real (no more display/reality mismatch) but the underlying skip still happened on literally every pause, not just near track boundaries** — contradicting a race-condition explanation (races aren't 100% reproducible). This prompted a deeper investigation rather than accepting a partial fix.
  **Root cause, found via live console evidence, not more guessing:** built the app for the user's connected physical iPhone (`xcodebuild -destination 'id=<udid>'`, `xcrun devicectl device install app`, then `xcrun devicectl device process launch --console --terminate-existing` — this pipes the device's real stdout, including `print()`, straight to the agent's terminal; **the device must be unlocked for the initial launch** — CoreDevice error 10002/"Locked" otherwise — but the console stream survives the device being locked again afterward). Added `[Nodalong.Player.Diag]` millisecond-timestamped prints at every decision point (`pauseCommand`/`nextTrackCommand`/`previousTrackCommand` firing, `togglePlayPause()` branch taken, every progress-tick's `playbackStatus`, the 200ms-confirm task's grace-window check). **The captured evidence (2/2 real Control Center pauses) showed `lastExplicitPauseAt is nil` every time, with zero `pauseCommand FIRED` log lines anywhere** — proving `MPRemoteCommandCenter.pauseCommand`'s registered target **never fires** for `ApplicationMusicPlayer` at all; something in MusicKit's own built-in Control Center integration pauses the player directly, invisible to the app's own remote-command registration. Confirmed independently via `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` against Apple's own MusicKit docs: *"ApplicationMusicPlayer automatically reports the now playing information and handles remote commands."* This is why it was 100% reproducible, not timing-dependent — every real Control Center pause looked identical to a genuine end-of-track, since the app's only detection mechanism (a heuristic wall-clock grace window keyed off its own pause-command handler firing) had nothing to key off.
  **Real fix:** replaced the wall-clock heuristic with an evidence-based one. New `lastKnownElapsedWhilePlaying` (updated every tick while `status == .playing`) captures the true elapsed position immediately before any stop. The 200ms-confirm task now computes `remaining = currentDuration - elapsedAtStop` and a new `endOfTrackTolerance` (3.0s): if there's meaningfully more track left, it's *never* a natural completion regardless of what caused the stop — stay on the current track, correctly paused, don't advance. Also fixed the **mirror-image bug** the user's own follow-up testing surfaced: once `isPlaying` is set false, the progress timer used to skip its own body entirely (`guard ... self.isPlaying else { return }`), meaning if MusicKit later resumed playback behind the app's back (confirmed: Control Center's "next" with nothing queued after restarts the current track via an OS-level fallback, also bypassing the app's own `nextTrackCommand` target), the app had zero mechanism to notice and stayed stuck showing "paused" forever while real audio played. Fixed by removing that gate for the `ApplicationMusicPlayer` branch specifically — it now always watches real player state and reconciles `isPlaying` in *either* direction. `dismiss()` was also fixed to actually invalidate `progressTimer` (a separate, pre-existing leak — the timer never stopped on dismiss before), since the always-watching design makes a dead timer non-trivial for the first time.
  **Second, related bug** — Control Center's next/previous buttons were confirmed (via the same diagnostic method, plus `nextTrackCommand.addTarget` never once printing `FIRED`) to bypass the app's registered commands identically to pause, and — unlike pause — this actually matters structurally: only the app's own `skipToNext()`/`skipToPrevious()` know about the real, multi-song Nodalong queue; `ApplicationMusicPlayer.shared.queue` is set to a single entry every track load, so MusicKit's native handling (confirmed to be what's actually active) has nothing beyond that one song to move to and falls back to restarting it in both directions. **User explicitly rejected modeling the fix on the already-shipped Spotify "mirror next-only" pattern** ("we are no longer supporting spotify") and asked for genuine previous support too — researched Apple's actual `ApplicationMusicPlayer.Queue` API fresh via `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` rather than reusing the Spotify-driven design: confirmed `queue.entries` is directly mutable and index-addressable (`entries.insert(_:at:)`), meaning — unlike Spotify's Web API — a real *previous* entry can be inserted before the current one's index, not just appended after. Also confirmed via Apple's own forums that a queue entry's ID never matches the original `Song`'s ID (by design, to allow duplicate tracks in one queue), so identity matching has to go through `isrc`/title+artist, the same cascade `resolve(_:)` already uses.
  **Shipped:** new `mirroredPreviousItemId`/`mirroredNextItemId` + `matches(_:_:)` (isrc/title+artist identity check against a native queue entry) + `syncNativeQueueNeighbors()` — mirrors a real previous/next window into MusicKit's native queue via `queue.insert(_:position:.afterCurrentEntry)` (next) and `queue.entries.insert(_:at:)` positioned at the current entry's index (previous); cheap no-op when neither neighbor changed. Called from every queue-mutation site (`addToQueue`, `moveUpNext`, `removeUpNext`, `reconcileQueue`) and once a track starts playing in `playCurrent()`. New progress-timer detection: compares `player.queue.currentEntry`'s underlying song against what the app expects; a mismatch matching the mirrored next/previous means Control Center's next/previous actually had somewhere real to go — `currentIndex` is adjusted and `nowPlaying` resynced *without* calling `playCurrent()` again (MusicKit already started that audio itself; reloading would double-trigger). A mismatch matching neither (queue boundary, MusicKit fell back to a plain restart) falls through to the existing duration-proximity pause-vs-end logic unchanged, which already classifies a restart-in-place correctly.
  **User feedback, saved as a standing high-priority memory:** always show actual proposed code snippets alongside prose in implementation plans, not descriptions only — the user rejected an `ExitPlanMode` specifically for being prose-only.
  **Verification:** both fixes independently confirmed on the user's own physical device via the live console-streaming method above (2 rounds for the pause fix, 1 round for the next/previous fix — the API assumptions from web research, flagged as uncertain in the plan, compiled and worked correctly on the first attempt). All `[Nodalong.Player.Diag]` diagnostic prints and the `diagTimestamp()` helper removed once both fixes were confirmed. `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green for both simulator and the real device target throughout; `swift test` — 82/82 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes).

- **2026-07-17** — **Spotify support suspended (entry points removed, not the backend) + Apple Music connect button redesigned — build-green, 82 NodalongKit tests unaffected.** User's call: park Spotify as low-priority, but keep it reversible. Confirmed scope via `AskUserQuestion` before touching anything: **hide entry points, don't purge** — `SpotifyMusicService.swift`, `KeychainHelper.swift`, `MusicServiceType.spotify`, the `spotifyService` `@EnvironmentObject` wiring across ~13 feature views (routed through `activeMusicService(playerVM:apple:spotify:)`), and every `.spotify` branch in `PlayerViewModel`/`LibrarySyncService` are all **untouched** — they just become unreachable once no account can ever be linked to Spotify again. **Deleted** (not hidden behind a flag) every literal user-facing Spotify string/button: `MusicServiceLinkView`'s "Connect Spotify" button + `connectSpotify()` + copy mentioning Spotify as an option; `ProfileView`'s `spotifyStatusRow`/`spotifyActionButton`/`connectSpotify()`/`showSwitchToSpotifyConfirm` (+ its alert) — `isSpotifyLinked` itself was kept, since it turned out to have one legitimate remaining use in the pull-to-refresh sync branch; `SearchView`'s "Spotify not connected" empty-state branch; `NowPlayingView`'s "Playing via Spotify" green-dot badge; `PlaybackErrorAlert`'s entire `spotifyPlaybackError`-bound alert (+ `openSpotify()` deep-link opener, + the now-unused `UIKit` import); `MiniPlayerBar`'s green Spotify-active dot overlay. **Migration for already-linked accounts:** new `.task` in `ContentView.swift` (mirrors the existing `endOrphanedLiveParties` launch-cleanup pattern) — if `authVM.profile?.activeService == .spotify`, calls `setActiveMusicService(nil, ...)` so `shouldShowMusicLink` flips true and they're re-prompted to connect Apple Music, rather than being silently stuck on a service with no visible UI to manage it.
  **Apple Music button redesign**, worked out via the visualize tool (mockups shown before implementing, per explicit user request) across two rounds — first a 3-way style pick (icon-only / badge / full-fill red), user picked full-fill; then a follow-up correcting my first icon choice (Tabler/SF Symbol's plain outline Apple logo) — user: "the logo shouldn't be hollowed out... it should be the Apple Music logo." **Shipped:** new `Color.nodalong.appleMusicRed` colorset (`#FA2D48`, universal/non-adaptive, mirrors the already-existing-but-unused `spotifyGreen` pattern). New `Nodalong/Shared/UI/AppleMusicIcon.swift` — a small white rounded-square badge containing a **procedurally-drawn solid** "linked eighth notes" `Shape` (not an SF Symbol outline, not a bundled image asset — matches this project's established preference for procedural rendering over binary assets, e.g. `AvatarView`; also sidesteps embedding Apple's actual trademarked icon artwork, which isn't available as any system asset). One consolidated red button (`ProfileView.appleMusicButton`) replaces what used to be three separate elements — `authStatusRow` + `subscriptionStatusRow` + the old `actionButton`'s three-way branch — cycling **Connect Apple Music → Connected → Preview** (label only; same red fill + badge in all three states, confirmed by the user rather than muting the not-yet-connected state). Tapping while linked now shows a **new confirm alert** ("Disconnect Apple Music?") instead of disconnecting immediately — genuinely new behavior, not just a restyle. "Subscribe to Apple Music" stays a separate small text link under the Preview state (confirmed kept separate rather than folded into one tap); the sync timestamp/spinner moved to a caption under the button rather than being dropped. **Deliberate simplification, flagged in the plan:** dropped the old design's transient "Checking subscription…" fourth state — the button now shows "Connected" optimistically the instant `isAppleLinked` is true (including before `musicService.subscription` has loaded), relabeling to "Preview" only once a snapshot resolves with no active plan. The `isDenied` ("Open Settings") recovery flow stays a separate, unstyled affordance — a different action class (system-permission recovery), not a connection-state toggle. `MusicServiceLinkView`'s onboarding button got the identical red-fill + badge treatment.
  **Verification:** grepped the whole `Nodalong/` tree for `"Spotify"` post-edit — every remaining hit confirmed to be either inside the explicitly-untouched plumbing files, the `spotifyService` env-object wiring in the ~13 unaffected feature views, or a descriptive doc comment (not a user-facing string). `xcodegen generate` (new `AppleMusicIcon.swift`) + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green with **zero new warnings** in any edited file. `swift test` — all 82 NodalongKit tests still pass (no NodalongKit changes). **On-device unverified** — the three-state button cycle (Connect/Connected/Preview), the new disconnect-confirm alert, and the migration path for a pre-existing Spotify-linked test account all need a real device/simulator pass to confirm visually and behaviorally; this session only confirmed the code compiles and the logic is sound by direct trace.

- **2026-07-16** — **Synced ("radio") playback shipped: listening-party sync mode + NearMe "tune in" — build-green, 82 NodalongKit tests pass (4 new). On-device unverified (needs RTDB + two devices).** Plan: `~/.claude/plans/memoized-questing-rossum.md`. Spotify integration explicitly **parked** (user's call) — this is Apple-Music-only. Evolves the party from a pure DJ model (host is the only audio source; remotes watch) into a **listening party** where each participant chooses **In the room** (watch, the existing DJ behavior) or **In sync** (play the same track on their own device, radio-style, within ~10–20s). Same "tune in" extends to **NearMe → Now Playing**.
  **Key insight that kept this small:** every device already plays via its own `ApplicationMusicPlayer`/Apple Music subscription, so syncing does NOT stream audio between devices — it only aligns **playback position** on a shared timeline. Everything reduces to one primitive: **follow a broadcaster's timeline** (broadcaster = party host OR solo NearMe user; follower = party sync-listener OR NearMe tuner). **Locked product decisions (user):** (1) sync is **pure radio / view-only** — a follower has no transport, only Leave; (2) NearMe tune-in is **single-song** (ends when their track changes/stops), party sync is a **continuous station** (follows host track-to-track); (3) **party count only** — party header shows "N in sync • M in the room" via presence; solo NearMe tune-in is anonymous (no new listener index). Sync requires an Apple Music subscription (each device streams the full track) — gated on `AppleMusicService.canPlayFullTracks`; non-subscribers can still join a party in-the-room.
  **Phase 1 — foundation:** new `ServerClock` (`Nodalong/Services/`, RTDB `/.info/serverTimeOffset` singleton) is the clock-skew fix — both broadcaster and follower stamp/read server time so skew cancels. New pure `SyncTimeline` (NodalongKit, `targetPositionMs(serverNowMs:)` extrapolation, 4 unit tests). `UserNowPlaying` gained `positionMs`. `PlayerViewModel` gained a **follow engine** kept free of RTDB/social concerns: `FollowContext`/`FollowMode`, `@Published followContext`, `beginFollowing`/`applyFollowTimeline`/`stopFollowing` (+ `currentPositionMs`, `seekToMs`, `pauseFollow`/`resumeFollow`); `handleTrackEnd` guards so a follower never advances a queue of its own; drift threshold 2.5s. Broadcasters enriched with position + server time + a ~12s keepalive (only while playing): solo via `NowPlayingBroadcastService`/`UserPresenceService` (suppressed while *this* device is itself following, to avoid tune-in chains), host via `ListeningPartyViewModel.hostBroadcast`. **Subtlety handled:** the `nowPlaying`-change sink fires before the new track's position resets, so a track-change broadcast forces position 0; only same-track updates sample live position. Player UI (`MiniPlayerBar`/`NowPlayingView`) hides transport and shows a "Tuned in — Leave" surface when `followContext != nil`. New `Song.from(catalogTrack:catalogId:)` converter.
  **Phase 2 — NearMe tune-in:** new app-wide `SyncFollowCoordinator` bridge (owns a `UserPresenceService` reader + `ServerClock`; observation survives the sheet being dismissed) → `PlayerViewModel.applyFollowTimeline`, single-song. "Tune In" button in `NearMeUserSheet.nowPlayingSection` (subscription-gated). Injected in `NodalongApp`, configured in `ContentView`, added to `PreviewFixtures`.
  **Phase 3 — party modes:** `PartyListenMode` (inRoom/sync) on `PartyParticipant` + RTDB presence (`PartyLiveService.joinPresence`/`observePresence`/new `updateListenMode`). `ListeningPartyViewModel`: `myListenMode`, `setListenMode` (writes presence + begins/ends station follow), `feedSyncUpdate` (feeds the host pointer into the follow engine on each broadcast), `syncListenerCount`/`inRoomCount`; a `$followContext` watcher reconciles `myListenMode` back to in-room if follow ends elsewhere (e.g. mini-player Leave). `PartyView` gained a guest listen-mode segmented control + the counts row. Host never follows (it's the source). **RTDB rules unchanged** — new fields fall under existing node-level write rules (no `.validate` constraints); confirmed, no redeploy needed for this change.
  **On-device unverified** (this env has no RTDB session + needs two devices): NearMe tune-in aligns within ~10–20s and stops on track change; party sync follows the host track-to-track with transport hidden; clock-skew holds across devices; non-subscriber gating; and no regression to the existing DJ/in-room flow, solo playback, or mini-player when not following.
  **Same-day follow-up: gated the BROADCASTER's subscription status too, not just the follower's.** User asked directly whether both sides need Apple Music Premium — surfaced a real gap: the follower-side gate (`canPlayFullTracks`) was already enforced, but nothing checked the broadcaster. A non-subscribed broadcaster's own playback runs on the 30s preview fallback and auto-advances every ~30s (`handleTrackEnd`), so a subscribed follower syncing against them would get yanked into a new track every ~30 seconds — broken, not just degraded. User chose to block sync against a preview-mode broadcaster (not leave it as a known limitation). **Shipped:** new `isPreviewPlayback` field threaded through `SyncTimeline`, `UserNowPlaying`, `PartyNowPlaying` (defaults `false` for source-compat with existing call sites/tests); new `PlayerViewModel.isPreviewPlayback` read-only forward of the existing private `isPreviewMode`; both broadcasters (`NowPlayingBroadcastService`, `ListeningPartyViewModel.hostBroadcast`) now include it. **Primary gate (UI, prevents starting):** `NearMeUserSheet.tuneInControl` and `PartyView.listenModeRow` both check the observed broadcaster's `isPreviewPlayback` before showing the Tune In button / sync picker, showing "Sync unavailable — {name} isn't on Apple Music" instead — mirrors the existing non-subscriber-follower explainer. **Defense in depth (engine, handles broadcaster losing subscription mid-session):** `SyncFollowCoordinator.handle(_:)` and `ListeningPartyViewModel.feedSyncUpdate` both re-check the flag on every update and end the follow / fall back to in-room if it flips true mid-session, rather than following the broadcaster into 30s cycling. Build green, all 82 NodalongKit tests still pass (no test changes needed — default param kept them source-compatible).

- **2026-07-16** — **Spike result: CONFIRMED — the Spotify Web Playback SDK does NOT survive backgrounding on iOS, even in a fully native Swift app. The embedded-SDK direction is dead; spike code removed.** Built and ran a throwaway on-device spike (`SpotifySDKSpikeView`, see plan file `~/.claude/plans/the-next-big-feature-quizzical-treasure.md` for the full build description) specifically to settle conflicting evidence: a 2017 WebKit engine fix suggested background audio should work for any `WKWebView` client, while several Spotify-specific community reports (2021, 2023) said otherwise. Needed a real device + real Spotify Premium session to know for sure, which this environment can't provide.
  **User ran the test and reported all three outcomes:** (1) **Playback does not resume after being backgrounded/locked for 30–60s** — it stops and does not self-recover. This is the decisive result: it's not just "occasionally needs a nudge" like today's `.noActiveDevice` friction, it's a hard, apparently permanent failure on a short fixed timer, worse than the status quo (which is at least recoverable by reopening Spotify). (2) "Nodalong Spike" **did** show up as a real, active device in the Spotify app's Connect device picker — confirms the SDK genuinely creates a live Connect device, so the "register as a device" mechanism itself is real; it's specifically the *background survival* that fails. (3) Control Center **still showed "Spotify Embedded Player"**, exactly matching the sibling RN app's own prior finding (`../nodalong/BUGS.md`) — settles the "does native Swift give more control over this than RN did" question from earlier in this session: no, it doesn't; the attribution is written by the SDK/WebKit's DRM-media pipeline below the level either framework can reach, confirmed now in a real native Swift context, not just inferred from RN's report. (The RN team's "native `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` write can take precedence" finding was never tested here, since finding (1) makes the whole approach nonviable regardless of branding — no point fixing Control Center for a session that dies after a minute.)
  **Conclusion: do not revisit the embedded Web Playback SDK route on iOS without a materially new fact** (an Apple/WebKit or Spotify SDK change specifically addressing DRM/EME background audio — general web-audio background fixes, like the 2017 one, are not sufficient, confirmed by this test). The retry/one-tap-"Open Spotify"/auto-resume-on-foreground-return mitigations shipped 2026-07-15 remain the practical ceiling for the `.noActiveDevice` complaint under Spotify's current iOS constraints — there is no third-party path today to a device that both (a) plays real DRM'd Spotify audio in-process and (b) survives backgrounding.
  **Cleanup:** removed `Nodalong/Features/Debug/SpotifySDKSpikeView.swift` (and its now-empty `Debug/` directory), `SpotifyMusicService.debugCurrentAccessToken()`, the `streaming` OAuth scope (added only for the SDK, no longer needed by anything in the app — reverted to keep the requested scope list minimal), and the `#if DEBUG` entry point button/sheet in `ProfileView.swift`. All disposable per the spike's own design — nothing here was ever meant to survive the result either way. Build green (`xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`) after cleanup.
