- **2026-07-15** — **Investigated and ruled out the Spotify Web Playback SDK (embedded WKWebView) as a fix for "constantly reopen Spotify" / Control Center branding — closing off a direction worth not re-opening without new platform-level evidence.** User pushed hard on eliminating the reopen-Spotify friction entirely and was leaning toward embedding the Web Playback SDK (JS, browser/WKWebView-hosted) so Nodalong itself becomes the Spotify Connect device, then asked whether being native Swift (vs. the sibling RN app) gives more control over Control Center branding.
  **Checked the sibling RN repo's own prior investigation first** (`../nodalong/BUGS.md`, Bug #1): confirmed "Spotify Embedded Player" is written directly to `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` by the SDK's native-level media engine, bypassing `navigator.mediaSession` entirely — unfixable from JS. But the RN team's own Attempt 4 found a real fix: `react-native-track-player`, a NATIVE module writing to `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` from the main app process, **took precedence over** the WebView's own push — blocked only by Expo Go's inability to load native modules, a limitation that doesn't apply to a fully native Swift app. This looked like a genuine, native-Swift-specific advantage worth pursuing.
  **Then searched outside that report (per explicit user request) and found the actual blocker, which is more fundamental than branding:** confirmed via multiple Spotify Community threads that **the Web Playback SDK does not support background playback on iOS at all** — when a WKWebView hosting the SDK is backgrounded, playback stops outright; `AVAudioSession.Category.playback` + the background-audio entitlement don't apply to WKWebView-hosted media the way they do to a native player. Explicitly documented as an iOS-specific gap (works on desktop/Android). Also confirmed Spotify's old native streaming SDK (real in-process audio decode for third parties) was deprecated years ago, and the still-supported **App Remote SDK** is architecturally identical to what Nodalong already does — remote-controls the standalone Spotify app, "can't handle the audio stream itself" per Spotify's own docs — no new capability over the existing Web API integration.
  **Conclusion: there is currently no supported way for a third-party iOS app to become the actual Spotify audio-output device that also survives backgrounding.** The standalone Spotify app is the only thing that can hold that role reliably on iOS today — this is a platform limitation, not an engineering gap. The retry/one-tap-recovery/native-queue-mirroring work already shipped (see entries above) is close to the practical ceiling for this specific complaint under Spotify's current iOS constraints. **Do not re-attempt the embedded Web Playback SDK route without first confirming Spotify or Apple has changed this iOS background-playback restriction** — re-verify via Spotify's Community forum / changelog before resuming this thread, don't just re-derive from first principles, since this was confirmed via live external search, not code reading.

- **2026-07-15** — **Reduced frequency of two recurring Spotify errors — "Nothing to play" (`.noActiveDevice`) and "Couldn't find this on Spotify" (`.notFound`) — via query normalization, a wider retry window, and a one-tap + auto recovery path (build-green, 78 NodalongKit tests pass — 20 new).** User reported seeing both messages "far too often." Investigated via 2 parallel Explore agents reading `SpotifyMusicService.swift`/`PlayerViewModel.swift` in full, cross-referenced against this session's own prior entries on the same topics.
  **`.notFound` root cause, confirmed:** `resolve(_:)`'s final fallback step (`searchByTitleArtist`) built its Spotify query from raw `Song.title`/`Song.artist` with zero normalization — Spotify's `track:`/`artist:` field search is fairly literal, so a title carrying `(feat. X)`, `- Remastered 2011`, `(Deluxe Edition)`, `(Live)`, `[Explicit]`, or a multi-artist string like `"Drake, Future"` routinely returned zero results even when the track genuinely exists under a cleaner title. This step is reached for any cross-service track with no cached catalog Spotify id and no ISRC — a common case for anything nobody's resolved on Spotify before. The 2026-07-13 "wrong release artwork" fix (`ReleaseMatching.swift`'s `bestReleaseIndex`) only scores *among returned candidates*; it does nothing when the query itself returns zero candidates.
  **Fix:** new pure-logic file `NodalongKit/Sources/NodalongKit/Utils/SearchQueryNormalization.swift` — `stripSearchNoise(_:)` (regex-strips the parenthetical/bracketed noise keywords above, plus a trailing `" - <suffix>"` dash pattern) and `primaryArtist(_:)` (splits a multi-artist string on the first of `,`/`&`/`feat.`/`ft.`/`featuring`/`with`, case-insensitive). Both are documented as deliberately heuristic — a legitimate title/artist that happens to contain "(With Love)" or a real dash can get over-stripped — acceptable because they're **only ever tried as fallback tiers**, after a raw, unmodified search has already come back empty; a wrong guess costs nothing further. `resolve(_:)`'s text-search step became a small tiered loop over 3 distinct `(title, artist)` candidates — raw/raw (unchanged first attempt, so already-working tracks hit exactly the same single search call as before), cleaned-title/raw-artist, cleaned-title/primary-artist — stopping at the first non-nil result, deduped so identical pairs aren't retried twice. 20 new unit tests (`SearchQueryNormalizationTests.swift`, mirrors `ReleaseMatchingTests.swift`'s style) cover feat./ft./with/remastered/deluxe/live/explicit/dash-suffix stripping (case-insensitive, multiple-suffixes-in-one-title, clean-title-unchanged) and all 6 artist-separator forms. Deliberately scoped to Spotify only — the complaint was Spotify-specific; the new NodalongKit functions are pure and reusable for Apple Music's resolve cascade later if needed, but that's not part of this pass.
  **`.noActiveDevice` root cause, already well-understood from this session's own prior entries:** Spotify Connect device-presence registration lapses whenever the real Spotify app is backgrounded/idle (confirmed on-device: works right after opening Spotify, fails again minutes later while still running in the background, not force-quit) — inherent to the "pure Web API remote control, no embedded SDK" architecture. Already tried and reverted claiming Nodalong's own `AVAudioSession` to keep Spotify "warm" (see the entry two below this one) — that made things actively worse by interrupting Spotify's real session. `ensureActiveDevice()` already had one 1.5s retry for the transient-gap case, but no proactive check and no automatic recovery existed anywhere — recovery required noticing the modal alert, leaving Nodalong, opening real Spotify, coming back, and manually re-tapping play.
  **Fix, three complementary pieces, deliberately NOT touching `AVAudioSession`/`MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` again given the prior revert:** (a) `ensureActiveDevice()`'s single 1.5s retry became two retries at increasing delays (1.0s, then 2.0s — ~3s added worst-case wait) — the cheapest, most universal mitigation since it helps every failure silently, not just ones the user notices and manually recovers from. (b) `PlaybackErrorAlert.swift`'s existing `"Nothing to play"` alert (bound to `spotifyPlaybackError`) gained an "Open Spotify" button — `UIApplication.shared.open(URL(string: "spotify://")!, ...)` falling back to `https://open.spotify.com` if that fails, no `LSApplicationQueriesSchemes` Info.plist entry needed since `open(_:options:completionHandler:)` (unlike `canOpenURL`) doesn't require one. Collapses the user's own already-established manual workaround into one tap. (c) New `awaitingSpotifyDeviceRetry` flag on `PlayerViewModel`, deliberately **separate** from `spotifyPlaybackError` — the alert's `isPresented` binding clears `spotifyPlaybackError` the instant ANY alert button is tapped (including the new "Open Spotify" one), so it can't double as "there's still a pending retry to attempt" once the alert is dismissed. Set `true` alongside `spotifyPlaybackError` in `playCurrentViaSpotify`'s `.noActiveDevice` catch branch; cleared at the top of every fresh `playCurrentViaSpotify` attempt, or consumed by the new `retryAfterSpotifyDeviceRecovery()` (guards `awaitingSpotifyDeviceRetry && activeService == .spotify && currentItem != nil`, clears the flag immediately to prevent duplicate-fire on rapid foreground/background cycling, then calls `playCurrent()` again — confirmed `stopBackends()` never touches `queue`/`currentIndex`, so this is a genuine retry of the same track). Wired into `ContentView.swift`'s existing `willEnterForegroundNotification` handler alongside the existing Apple Music resync call — matches the confirmed on-device repro exactly (fail → open Spotify → return to Nodalong → automatic retry, no second manual tap). Self-limiting: a retry that fails again re-arms the flag via the same catch branch, and it's gated on a discrete OS notification, not a timer, so there's no infinite-loop risk.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green (Nodalong target). `swift test` in `NodalongKit/` — all 78 tests pass (58 existing + 20 new), zero failures. **On-device unverified for both fixes' real-world effectiveness** — this environment has no live Spotify session, so it can confirm the code compiles and the logic is sound (traced every relevant line, including confirming `stopBackends()`'s queue-preserving behavior and the alert-binding-clears-`spotifyPlaybackError` mechanics before writing the retry-flag design) but not the actual Spotify Connect device-registration timing or real catalog search results. User's to confirm: (1) background/suspend real Spotify for a couple minutes then tap play — does the wider retry window silently resolve more transient gaps; (2) fully quit Spotify, trigger `.noActiveDevice`, tap "Open Spotify," return to Nodalong without manually re-tapping play — does it auto-resume; (3) a track previously known to fail with "Couldn't find this on Spotify" (ideally one with a `(feat. ...)` or `- Remastered` suffix) — does it now resolve.

- **2026-07-14** — **Fixed a "blank purple screen" reported on account creation and login (build-green) — the sign-in/registration transition never had a bridge screen at all, unlike cold launch.** User reported testers seeing a blank purple screen right after creating an account, then confirmed it also happens on login. Traced `ContentView.swift`: `authVM.isInitializing` already gates `MainTabView()` behind `LaunchSplash()` for cold launch, but per its own doc comment that flag is deliberately **not** set during sign-in/registration — so the instant `authVM.isAuthenticated` flips true (from either `register()`'s synchronous `currentUser`/`profile` assignment or the auth listener's `loadProfile` completing after a normal sign-in), `ContentView`'s `Group` does a hard, instant cut straight into constructing a brand-new `MainTabView()`, with nothing shown in between. `MainTabView.init()` does real work on its very first construction in a process (one-time UIKit chrome setup — window background, tab bar/nav bar appearance — plus five `@StateObject`s, then `TabView` layout + `FeedView`'s async Firestore load kicking off); with zero bridge screen, any hitch there shows the window's raw background (`bgBase`, purple) with nothing else painted yet — exactly the reported symptom, and distinct from (though the same underlying class of bug as) the earlier-fixed sign-out→sign-in "purple screen" (that fix, the `didConfigureChrome` static guard, only prevents *repeated* chrome-setup runs within a process — it doesn't help the very first run, which sign-in/registration always is).
  **Fix, `ContentView.swift`:** new `@State private var showPostAuthBridge`, set `true` via `.onChange(of: authVM.isAuthenticated)` the moment it flips true, held for a fixed ~400ms floor (`Task.sleep`) before clearing. `LaunchSplash()` is now shown whenever `isInitializing || showPostAuthBridge` — reusing the exact same already-built, already-branded bridge view cold launch uses, rather than inventing a new one. This gives `MainTabView`'s one-time construction work a guaranteed head start before the user can ever see it, mirroring how cold launch already avoids this same problem via `isInitializing`. Deliberately did **not** touch `isInitializing` itself or its "not set during sign-in" behavior — that's a separate, intentionally-scoped flag with its own doc comment reasoning; this adds a parallel, narrowly-scoped bridge instead of overloading it.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green. **On-device timing verification still needed** — this is fundamentally a race between "how long MainTabView takes to paint its first real frame" and "how long the bridge is held," and this environment can't measure real device timing. 400ms is a reasonable floor chosen to bridge a typical hitch without feeling like an artificial delay on a fast transition, but if reports continue, the next lever is lengthening the floor rather than re-diagnosing the mechanism.

- **2026-07-14** — **Spotify: mirror only the single next-up track into Spotify's own native queue, to fix Control Center Next without repeating the interruption bug from the reverted attempt above (build-green, 58 NodalongKit tests pass).** Direct follow-up: user asked whether Spotify's Web API supports adding to a user's queue, and whether Nodalong could keep its own queue and Spotify's native queue in sync (including during party broadcasts) to fix Control Center Next.
  **Researched Spotify's actual API surface** (official docs + developer community, fetched live): `POST /me/player/queue` (add) and `GET /me/player/queue` (read) are the **only** two queue endpoints that exist publicly — confirmed no Dev Mode restriction on either. **There is no remove, clear, or reorder endpoint at all** — a long-standing, still-unaddressed public limitation (an internal `set_queue` endpoint exists for Spotify's own first-party apps only). Spotify's own docs also warn **ordering isn't guaranteed** across multiple queue-add calls. So "keep both queues fully in sync" isn't achievable as literally asked — only single additions can ever be mirrored, and only reliably one at a time.
  Presented this finding plus other options (a non-exclusive/`.mixWithOthers` audio session — real chance it just doesn't grant Now Playing ownership at all per Apple's own arbitration rules; a Live Activity/Dynamic-Island custom control surface — a real fix, but needs iOS 17+, a bigger deployment-target decision; leaving it as-is). **User picked the scoped-down, lowest-risk option**: mirror only the immediate-next track. This **never touches `AVAudioSession`/`MPNowPlayingInfoCenter`** — a pure REST call against Spotify's own queue, which the real Spotify app already fully owns — so it carries none of the interruption risk that broke the reverted attempt. Fixes Next only (Spotify's queue API has no "previous" concept).
  **Shipped, `Nodalong/Services/SpotifyMusicService.swift`:** `HTTPMethod` gained a `.post` case; `authenticatedCommand` gained a `method: HTTPMethod = .put` parameter (default preserves every existing call site). New `addToNativeQueue(uri:)` (`POST /me/player/queue`, doc comment records the add-only/no-reorder/ordering-not-guaranteed limitation so a future session doesn't try to extend this into a multi-track mirror). New `resolveTrackUri(for:)` (thin wrapper around the existing private `resolve(_:)` cascade, resolve-only, no play command — for pre-queuing a track before it's actually due). New `playReturningResolvedUri(_:)` (same body `play(_:)` already had, now returns the resolved Spotify URI); `play(_:)` — the `MusicService` protocol requirement, fixed signature — becomes a one-line wrapper around it.
  **Shipped, `Nodalong/Services/PlayerViewModel.swift`:** new `spotifyPlayingTrackUri` (the Spotify URI Nodalong itself last told Spotify to play) and `spotifyQueuedNextUri`/`spotifyQueuedNextItemId` (the URI + `QueueItem.id` most recently pre-queued — needed because a queue item's own `Song.uri` can be an Apple Music URI even when played via Spotify through cross-service resolve, so matching a detected track change back to "which `QueueItem` is this" can't be done by comparing raw song URIs). New `syncSpotifyNativeQueue()` — best-effort, no-ops outside Spotify or when `upNext.first` hasn't actually changed since the last call; resolves and pushes that one track via the two new `SpotifyMusicService` methods. Called from `playCurrentViaSpotify()` (after `playReturningResolvedUri` replaces the old bare `play(song)` call) and alongside every existing `persistQueue()` call site that can change `upNext.first` without also calling `playCurrent()` afterward: `addToQueue`, `moveUpNext`, `removeUpNext`, and — this is what makes it work **during party broadcasting**, per the user's original ask — `reconcileQueue` (the host↔player party bridge). Call sites that already lead into `playCurrent()`→`playCurrentViaSpotify()` (`skipToNext`, `skipToPrevious`, `playQueueItem`, `handleTrackEnd`) don't need a second call — the one inside `playCurrentViaSpotify` covers them once the new track actually starts.
  `pollSpotifyPlaybackState()` gained drift detection, checked before the existing duration/progress/near-end logic: if the poll's `trackUri` differs from `spotifyPlayingTrackUri` AND matches `spotifyQueuedNextUri` (confirming it's specifically the track Nodalong itself pre-queued, not some unrelated change), jump `currentIndex` to the matching `QueueItem`, rebuild `nowPlaying`, and immediately call `syncSpotifyNativeQueue()` again so a second consecutive Control Center Next also has something correct queued up. The existing duration/progress block right after handles the new track's progress correctly automatically, since it reads the same poll response either way.
  **Explicitly not handled, matching the user's own accepted scope:** Spotify's native Previous, or any track change that isn't the one specific track Nodalong pre-queued (e.g. browsing to something unrelated inside the real Spotify app) — Nodalong has no way to know about those and keeps showing its last-known state until the user returns to the app or takes another action, same as before this change.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green; all 58 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). **On-device testing still needed** — this environment has no live Spotify session to verify against: play a Spotify track with something queued behind it, lock the screen, tap Control Center Next twice in a row (confirms both the initial pre-queue and the re-queue-after-drift-detection step), and confirm the app shows the right state on reopen. Also worth testing during an active party on the host's device, since that's the specific "broadcasting" scenario that prompted this.

- **2026-07-14** — **Reverted the Control Center Now Playing claim from the entry directly below — confirmed on-device it doesn't just "not help," it actively breaks Spotify playback (build-green, 58 NodalongKit tests pass).** User tested on a real device: Control Center Next still didn't advance the queue, and worse — reopening the app and pressing play produced `SpotifyServiceError.noActiveDevice` ("Nothing to play — open Spotify on a device"), a state that wasn't happening before this change. Root cause, consistent with standard iOS `AVAudioSession` behavior: `activateAudioSessionIfNeeded()`'s `setCategory(.playback) + setActive(true)` call, when Nodalong had no real audio of its own to play, almost certainly sent an interruption to the real Spotify app's own already-active `.playback` session the moment Nodalong's session activated — a normal consequence of two apps both claiming an active (non-mixable) playback session, not a Nodalong-specific bug. That interruption most likely paused Spotify's real playback and dropped its Connect device out of Spotify's own "active device" list, which is exactly the pre-existing, already-understood trigger for `.noActiveDevice` (see the 2026-07-12 entry on `ensureActiveDevice()` retries). So Control Center Next had nothing to actually advance (Spotify wasn't playing), and the failure compounded on reopen.
  **Reverted, in `PlayerViewModel.swift`:** removed `activateAudioSessionIfNeeded()` and the `updateNowPlayingInfo(...)` call from `playCurrentViaSpotify()`, the resync call from `pollSpotifyPlaybackState()`, and the `updateNowPlayingPlaybackState()` call from `togglePlayPause()`'s Spotify branch — restoring the original Stage B architecture exactly (no audio-session activation, no Now Playing writes, for Spotify). Rewrote `playCurrentViaSpotify`'s doc comment to record this attempt-and-revert with the concrete on-device failure mode, and to warn any future session against re-attempting this without first finding a way to claim a Now Playing session *without* taking over the active audio route — nothing found so far suggests iOS offers one for a "pure Web API remote control, no in-process audio" architecture like this one.
  **Explicitly kept, unaffected by the revert:** the scrubber-jitter fix from the same session (`startProgressTimer()`'s Spotify interpolation branch, and calling it from `playCurrentViaSpotify()` alongside `startSpotifyPollTimer()`) — this never touched `AVAudioSession`/`MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` at all, only local `progress` interpolation, so it carries none of the risk that caused the Control Center attempt to fail; also kept the `currentDuration` seed from `song.duration` in `playCurrentViaSpotify` (harmless, still needed by the interpolation fix) and `updateNowPlayingInfo`'s new `elapsed` parameter (inert now that nothing calls it with a non-default value, but a harmless small completeness fix to a function that's still used by the preview path).
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green; 58 NodalongKit tests pass. **User's own device is currently in the broken `.noActiveDevice` state from testing this** — recovery is the same as previously documented (open the real Spotify app so it registers as an active device again, then return to Nodalong), not something this revert fixes retroactively. Next step, per the user's own framing when approving the original attempt: investigate other options for the Control Center issue, now that claiming the surface directly is confirmed unsafe.

- **2026-07-14** — **Spotify: experimentally claimed the OS Now Playing/Control Center surface (reversing a prior deliberate decision, per explicit user request), and fixed the scrubber jitter (build-green, 58 NodalongKit tests pass — no NodalongKit changes).** User reported two Spotify-specific issues: Control Center Next doesn't advance Nodalong's queue during Spotify playback (in-app Next works fine), and the full-screen scrubber jitters instead of sweeping smoothly. Entered Plan Mode; an Explore agent confirmed both root causes by reading `PlayerViewModel.swift`/`SpotifyMusicService.swift` in full and cross-referencing `CLAUDE.md`'s own Stage B entry.
  **Control Center issue — confirmed root cause:** `playCurrentViaSpotify()` never activated Nodalong's own `AVAudioSession` and never wrote `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter.default().nowPlayingInfo` — a **deliberate** Stage B decision (quoted verbatim from the in-code doc comment at the time: "the real Spotify app is the one actually outputting audio, so it — not Nodalong — owns the OS Control Center/lock-screen surface under this architecture"). Since Nodalong never claimed that surface, iOS almost certainly routed Control Center taps to the real Spotify app (which does have an active session) instead of Nodalong's own already-correct, already-service-aware `nextTrackCommand`/`pauseCommand` targets (confirmed these already branch correctly via `activeService` — the routing logic was never the problem, only the missing surface claim). Asked the user directly whether to leave this as the documented trade-off (mitigated today by an in-app "Playing via Spotify" label) or attempt to override it — **user chose to attempt it, explicitly accepting they may need to fall back if it doesn't work.**
  **Shipped, all in `PlayerViewModel.swift`:** `updateNowPlayingInfo(_:duration:)` (previously called only from the preview/`AVPlayer` fallback path, but already fully generic — no track-source-specific logic) gained an `elapsed: TimeInterval = 0` parameter (default preserves the existing preview call site's exact behavior) and now includes `MPNowPlayingInfoPropertyElapsedPlaybackTime` — a small pre-existing gap, since the function never set elapsed time at all before this. `playCurrentViaSpotify()` now: seeds `currentDuration` synchronously from `song.duration` (stored in **milliseconds** per this codebase's established convention) before the first 3s poll ever returns, calls the already-existing `activateAudioSessionIfNeeded()` (previously preview-only), and calls `updateNowPlayingInfo(...)` with the fresh track/duration. `pollSpotifyPlaybackState()` now also resyncs Now Playing info every ~3s to the server's ground-truth elapsed time (idiomatic `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` usage: set elapsed+rate at a known instant, let iOS interpolate locally, correct periodically — no continuous per-tick writes needed). `togglePlayPause()`'s Spotify branch now calls `updateNowPlayingPlaybackState()` after a successful pause/resume, mirroring the preview branch's existing call, so Control Center's play/pause icon updates immediately rather than waiting for the next poll. The stale "No `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` write here" doc comment on `playCurrentViaSpotify` was rewritten to explain this is now a deliberate experiment, reversing the prior decision per explicit user request, and to flag plainly that it may not work.
  **Honest, load-bearing caveat — this is genuinely unverified and might not work:** whether iOS actually grants Now Playing ownership to an app with an active-but-*silent* `AVAudioSession` (Nodalong produces zero real audio samples for Spotify — it's remote-controlling a separate Connect device/app that's the one actually outputting sound) is not something derivable from reading code or from a successful build — it needs a real on-device test (play a Spotify track, lock the screen, tap Control Center Next, confirm it actually advances Nodalong's queue rather than whatever the real Spotify app's own context would do). The change is deliberately small and self-contained (new calls to already-existing generic functions, no new state machines) specifically so it's easy to revert to the prior documented trade-off if the on-device test shows iOS still hands the surface to the real Spotify app. The in-app "Playing via Spotify" label was left untouched regardless of outcome.
  **Scrubber jitter — confirmed root cause and fixed, no architectural uncertainty here:** `pollSpotifyPlaybackState()` polls Spotify's `GET /me/player` every ~3s and set `progress`/`currentDuration` directly from the server response with **zero client-side interpolation between polls** — unlike the Apple Music path (reads live `ApplicationMusicPlayer.playbackTime` at 0.1–0.5s) and unlike the *preview* fallback path (already does local wall-clock interpolation each tick). `progress` sat flat for a full 3 seconds then jumped — exactly the reported "jitter." Already named in `CLAUDE.md`'s Stage B entry as an accepted v1 trade-off ("the scrubber visibly steps rather than sweeps"), but the actual fix was never implemented until now.
  **Fix:** `playCurrentViaSpotify()` now also calls `startProgressTimer()` (previously Spotify only ever started the separate 3s `startSpotifyPollTimer()`) — both timers run concurrently, confirmed via existing joint-invalidation call sites that they already get torn down together wherever playback fully stops, so no new cleanup path was needed. `startProgressTimer()`'s tick closure gained a third branch (alongside the existing `isPreviewMode` and `ApplicationMusicPlayer` branches) for `activeService == .spotify`: interpolates `progress` locally each tick using the exact same formula the preview branch already uses (`progress + progressInterval / currentDuration`, guarded on `currentDuration > 0`, now always true thanks to the synchronous duration seed above). `pollSpotifyPlaybackState()` keeps running independently on its own 3s cadence, unchanged, both to hard-resync `progress`/`currentDuration` from the server (correcting any drift the local interpolation accumulates) and to detect end-of-track / an externally-triggered pause — its existing responsibilities, untouched.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green; all 58 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes — both fixes are entirely within `PlayerViewModel.swift`). **On-device testing still needed for both**: the Control Center claim's real-world outcome (the entire open question) and the scrubber's actual smoothness (build success confirms the code compiles and the interpolation math is wired correctly, but "does it look smooth" needs eyes on a real device).

- **2026-07-14** — **Extended the "muted dusty purple" redesign to `bgDeep` — it was the one token left out of the earlier light-mode redesign, so it still looked jarringly saturated next to everything else (build-green, verified on-device in both appearances).** User flagged (with a real device screenshot) that the splash/loading screen's background felt at odds with the rest of the app's colors. Root cause: the light-mode palette redesign two entries below deliberately left `bgDeep` untouched (reasoned at the time as "brand always-dark, not one of the everyday light-mode surfaces being complained about") — but that meant `bgDeep` kept its old, more saturated purple while `bgBase`/`bgSurface`/`purpleAccent`/etc. all got desaturated, so it now stood out as the one token that didn't match.
  **Fix — same derivation method as the rest of the redesign, applied for the first time to `bgDeep` in BOTH modes** (not light-only this time, since the mismatch is visible regardless of which appearance the user is in): read `bgDeep`'s actual current HSL (light `#5B2D9F` → H264°/S56%/L40%; dark `#382556` → H263°/S40%/L24%), applied the same 0.6494 saturation-scale ratio used throughout the earlier redesign, keeping hue AND lightness unchanged (lightness deliberately preserved — `bgDeep`'s entire role is staying dark enough for `textOnAccent`'s fixed-white splash text to read clearly regardless of system appearance, and changing lightness would undermine that). Result: light `#5B2D9F → #5F418B`, dark `#382556 → #3A2E4D`. Verified contrast before writing: white/`textOnAccent` vs. new light value 8.03:1, vs. new dark value 12.52:1 — both far above the 4.5:1 minimum, no regression risk.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green. Installed to the simulator, forced both `simctl ui ... appearance light` and `dark`, and screenshotted the splash screen in each — confirmed the new muted tone reads as part of the same family as the rest of the app's purples in both modes, with title/subtitle/buttons still clearly legible.

- **2026-07-14** — **Fixed remote-pause-acts-like-skip (a genuine two-Task scheduling race) and party creation not carrying over the host's existing queue (build-green, 58 NodalongKit tests pass).** User reported both, entered Plan Mode; 2 parallel Explore agents confirmed root causes, then a Plan agent pressure-tested the initial fix designs and found both had a fatal flaw before any code was written — corrected designs below are what actually shipped.
  **Bug 1 — remote pause (Control Center/headphones) intermittently advances to the next track instead of pausing.** Root cause: MusicKit's `ApplicationMusicPlayer` has no end-of-track callback, so `startProgressTimer()` polls for a `.playing→.paused/.stopped` status edge, waits 200ms, and — if `isPlaying` is still `true` — calls `handleTrackEnd()` (advances). The only thing meant to stop this misfiring on a deliberate pause is `togglePlayPause()` setting `isPlaying = false`; both the in-app button and the remote `pauseCommand` target call the same `togglePlayPause()`, but the remote path wraps it in `Task { @MainActor in await self?.togglePlayPause() }` — an unbounded-latency hop from whatever thread `MPRemoteCommandCenter` invoked the target on. MusicKit's own internal Control Center integration can flip the underlying `playbackStatus` to `.paused` before that hop completes; if a progress-timer tick lands in that gap it sees stale `isPlaying == true` + real `.paused` and schedules the 200ms confirm, and if the remote command's own Task is *also* delayed past 200ms (plausible under system load or exactly when the device locks and process scheduling gets deprioritized), the confirm fires on stale state and wrongly advances. Genuine scheduling race between two independently-scheduled `Task`s — matches "intermittent" exactly.
  **First-draft fix rejected by the Plan agent's pressure-test:** stamping a disarm timestamp inside `togglePlayPause()` itself provides zero protection, because that stamp would be gated behind the *exact same* MainActor-hop delay as `isPlaying` — it fails in precisely the scenario that causes the bug (the Task hasn't run yet, so neither has the stamp).
  **Actual fix, `PlayerViewModel.swift`:** new thread-safe `lastExplicitPauseAt: Date?` (backed by a `nonisolated(unsafe)` field + `NSLock`, required since `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY = complete` and `MPRemoteCommandCenter` may invoke targets off-main) plus `nonisolated func markExplicitPause()`. `setupRemoteCommands()`'s `pauseCommand.addTarget` now calls `markExplicitPause()` **synchronously, before** the `Task { @MainActor in ... }` line — this is the real fix, since it runs on whatever thread the OS delivered the command on, not gated by MainActor queue congestion. The 200ms confirmation closure now checks `lastExplicitPauseAt` against a 2-second grace window before advancing — converting an unbounded "which of two Tasks wins" race into "was there a recently-known pause," far more tolerant of scheduling jitter (only fails if the remote command's own Task is delayed by the *entire* 2s window, not just 200ms). Cleared (`= nil`) on resume and at the top of `playCurrent()` (new-track start) to avoid a stale stamp suppressing a genuine auto-advance shortly after an unrelated pause/resume. `togglePlayPause()`'s pause branch also stamps it (harmless — in-app pause hits the same Task-body assignment as before, no new behavior there).
  **Bug 2 — starting a party silently drops the host's existing queue.** Root cause: `ListeningPartyViewModel.createAndEnter` creates the party doc then calls `enter()`, which wires `listenToPartyQueue` → a Combine sink → `playerVM.reconcileQueue(with:)` — but neither function ever reads the host's local `playerVM.currentItem`/`upNext` or writes anything to the party's Firestore `queue`. Firestore's very first (empty) snapshot fires `reconcileQueue(with: [])`; its anchor lookup (`songs.firstIndex(where: { $0.id == current.song.id })`) always returns `nil` on an empty array, so the function returns early and the OLD local queue keeps silently driving both "Now Playing" and `skipToNext()`.
  **First-draft fix rejected by the Plan agent's pressure-test:** naively seeding the party queue with the current track under an auto-generated Firestore doc id would still fail the SAME anchor check — `Song.from(partyQueueTrack:)` always sets a party-sourced `Song.id` to the real Firestore doc id once read back, and a fresh auto-id has no relationship to `current.song.id` (the host's pre-existing local track id, sourced from search/feed/library/wherever). A non-empty-but-wrong-id seed reproduces the identical bug.
  **Actual fix:** new `seedPartyQueue(partyId:songs:hostUid:hostUsername:)` in `Parties.swift` — mirrors `freezePlaylist`'s single-`WriteBatch` pattern (not a loop over `addToPartyQueue`, since the queue is known-empty at seed time, no need for its per-call `currentMaxQueueOrder` read). `songs[0]` (the currently-playing track) gets an **explicit doc id equal to `songs[0].id`** (`col.document(song.id)`, guarded non-empty/no-`/`, falling back to auto-id otherwise) — this is what makes the round-tripped `Song.id` equal `current.song.id`, so `reconcileQueue`'s anchor succeeds on the very first snapshot; every other track keeps a normal auto id. `songs[0]` is also seeded `played: true` (matching the invariant `hostBroadcast` establishes for every later track) since `enter()`'s `CombineLatest(nowPlaying, isPlaying)` sink fires immediately on subscribe, before the queue listener delivers anything locally, so there'd be no other chance to mark it played. `ListeningPartyViewModel.createAndEnter` now builds `[current] + upNext.map(\.song)` and calls this after `createParty` succeeds but before `enter()` wires the reconcile bridge — best-effort (log-and-continue on failure; a seed failure shouldn't block starting the party, same as a host who starts one with nothing loaded, which skips seeding entirely and falls through to `reconcileQueue`'s existing graceful no-op for an empty local queue).
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green after each fix; all 58 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit logic changed — both fixes are Nodalong-target-only). **Honest limitation, both flagged up front in the plan:** Bug 1 is an inherently racy timing bug — the fix is structurally sound by reading the code (correctly moves the disarm signal outside the losing race, per the Plan agent's independent verification), but "does it actually stop misfiring under real system load, on a real locked device" needs genuine on-device repeated testing (pause via Control Center / headphones many times, including right after locking), which this environment can't perform — absence of a repro after the fix is meaningful evidence, not deductive proof, for a race this inherently timing-dependent. Bug 2 needs an on-device check: build a local queue, start a party, confirm "Up Next" shows the same upcoming tracks and pressing Next advances through them (not a stale local queue). **Explicitly out of scope, per the user's own framing:** multi-device party audio (so guests not in the same room as the host can actually hear the party) is a deliberate follow-up conversation, not part of this fix — the DJ-model-vs-synced-multi-device tradeoff was already discussed and deferred when the party feature first shipped.

- **2026-07-13** — **Redesigned the light-mode palette to "muted dusty purple" (Option B) — user found the existing light mode too saturated/loud (build-green, verified structurally + via contrast math; live on-screen appearance NOT confirmed — see limitation below).** Direct follow-up to the splash-screen contrast fix below. Asked what specifically bothered the user (`AskUserQuestion`: multiSelect) — picked "too saturated/loud" + an unspecified "something else." Rendered 3 concrete directions as phone-mockup options (soft lavender / muted dusty purple / white-first-with-purple-as-accent-only) via the visualize tool. **The widget did not render in the user's client** (confirmed after 2 retries — user said "No you havent?" to a re-render) — loaded the `artifact-design` skill as a fallback path to publish a real hosted page instead, but the user made their decision before that was needed ("lets do B"), so no Artifact was ultimately published.
  **User-approved anchor values (Option B, light mode only):** `bgBase #C9BEE0`, `bgSurface #B9ABD8`, `purpleAccent #6B4FA0`. Confirmed via HSL these three sit in one coherent ~260°-hue, ~34–37%-saturation family, varying only in lightness (81% / 76% / 47%) — a genuine single "muted dusty purple," not three unrelated colors.
  **Derived the remaining light-mode purple-family tokens** (`bgElevated`, `purpleMid`, `purpleBold`, `purpleDark`) via a Python/`colorsys` script — **not** forced onto the same single hue (confirmed each of these already carries its own distinct hue in the current palette: `purpleMid`≈250° blue-violet, `purpleBold`≈282° magenta-violet, `purpleDark`/`purpleAccent`≈264°, matching their established distinct semantic roles per earlier palette entries — auth CTAs / NearMe's primary action / brand identity respectively). Instead: computed the saturation-reduction ratio implied by the user's own accent change (new `purpleAccent` S=33.9% vs old S=52.2% → ratio 0.649), applied that same ratio to each token's own original saturation while preserving its own hue; lightness derived by preserving each token's original point-offset *from* `purpleAccent`'s lightness (e.g. `purpleMid` was 10pts darker than `purpleAccent` before → still 10pts darker now), rather than reusing old absolute values or forcing a shared hue. `bgElevated` (a background-family token, not accent-family) anchored on the new `bgSurface`/`bgBase` hue (~259°) instead, preserving its original "further step past `bgSurface`" offset. Result: `bgElevated #B2A5CD`, `purpleMid #4A407D`, `purpleBold #663979`, `purpleDark #4E3871`.
  **Verified contrast computationally before writing any files** (same relative-luminance method as the splash-screen fix): white text (`textOnAccent`) on the four purple-family button/accent colors ranges 6.46–9.87:1 (all comfortably above the 4.5:1 body-text bar, even though these are typically larger button-label text); dark-ink text (`textPrimary`, unchanged) on the three background surfaces (`bgBase`/`bgSurface`/`bgElevated`) ranges 7.26–9.46:1 — both confirm no regressions from the swap.
  **Scope, both deliberate:** `bgDeep` (light) was **not** touched — it's the brand-always-dark background used for splash/auth screens specifically so they read as branded regardless of system appearance (established by the immediately-following fix); the user's complaint was about everyday light-mode surfaces (Feed/Profile/Search), not this one. Dark mode was **not** touched at all anywhere — confirmed by writing a script that only ever mutates each colorset's `light`-tagged entry, verified by reading 2 of the 7 rewritten files back in full afterward to confirm dark entries are byte-identical to before.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green. Installed to the simulator, forced `simctl ui ... appearance light`, and screenshotted — confirmed `SplashView`'s `bgDeep` background is visually unaffected by this change (as intended — it's a different token). **Honest limitation: could NOT visually confirm the actual redesigned tokens** (`bgBase`/`bgSurface`/the purple-family accents) **render as intended** — this simulator has no authenticated Firebase session, and every screen that uses those tokens (Feed, Profile, Search, Shelf) sits behind sign-in; `SplashView`/`LaunchSplash` (the only reachable unauthenticated screens) both use `bgDeep`, which this change deliberately doesn't touch. The derivation is verified mathematically (HSL math + WCAG contrast, both computed from the exact hex values actually written to the colorset files, confirmed via reading 2 files back) and the build is green, but the actual on-screen look of the redesigned palette is unconfirmed from this environment. **User should check Feed/Profile/Search on their own signed-in device** to confirm it actually reads as "muted dusty purple" rather than something unexpected. Also, this and the entry below noting `purpleAccent`'s low contrast against `bgDeep` (~1.4:1 light / ~3.4:1 dark) for `LaunchSplash`'s icon/spinner is now doubly relevant since `purpleAccent` itself just changed hex value — that finding still stands (still low-contrast against the untouched `bgDeep`) and is still unaddressed pending the user's decision on it.

- **2026-07-13** — **Fixed illegible splash/loading-screen text in light mode — `textPrimary` (mode-adaptive, assumes a light background) was being used over `bgDeep` (deliberately dark in BOTH modes), giving ~1.8:1 contrast in light mode instead of the intended readable white text (build-green, verified on-device with real screenshots in both appearances).** User asked to make the splash/loading screen colors match the theme. Investigation: computed actual WCAG contrast ratios from the live colorset hex values rather than guessing — `textPrimary` light (`#241542`, dark ink) against `bgDeep` light (`#5B2D9F`, a medium-dark purple, NOT actually light despite the mode) comes out to ~1.8:1 (need 4.5:1+ for body text) — nearly invisible. Dark mode was fine (white-on-dark-purple, ~13:1) since `textPrimary` is white there — the bug is specifically the light-mode half of `textPrimary`, which assumes whatever it's drawn on is a light surface, but `bgDeep` is a "brand always-dark" background by design (used for splash/auth screens specifically so they read as branded regardless of system appearance). The correct token for text drawn directly on `bgDeep` is `textOnAccent` — already defined as a fixed white in both modes, with its own doc comment stating exactly this intent ("tuned so textOnAccent (fixed white) stays legible on top in both light and dark mode") — it just wasn't used consistently everywhere `bgDeep` appears as a direct background.
  **Fixed every `textPrimary`/`textSecondary` usage sitting directly on a `bgDeep` background** (left `textPrimary` alone anywhere it sits on a *different*, genuinely mode-adaptive surface like `bgSurface` — confirmed via the same contrast math that `textPrimary` vs `bgSurface` light passes at ~4.5:1, so those pairings are correct and untouched): `SplashView.swift` (title, subtitle, and the "Create Account" button's text/background-tint/border — all three sat directly on the root `bgDeep` fill), `ContentView.swift`'s `LaunchSplash` (title + the "taking longer than usual" hint), and `MusicServiceLinkView.swift` (icon, title, subtitle, and the "You'll be prompted..." caption — its "Connect Spotify" button was correctly left on `textPrimary` since that button has its own nested `bgSurface` background, not `bgDeep` directly). `LoginView`/`RegisterView`/`ForgotPasswordView` were already correct (already used `textOnAccent`, confirmed via grep — not part of this bug).
  **Also re-themed the native `LaunchScreen.storyboard`** (the actual first-ever frame iOS shows, before any Swift code runs) — previously plain `systemBackgroundColor`/`labelColor` (white background, black text), completely untethered from the app's purple branding. Changed to reference the asset catalog's `bgDeep`/`textOnAccent` named colors directly (`<color key="..." name="bgDeep"/>` instead of `systemColor="..."`). **Hit a real gotcha**: removing the old `<resources>` block (which held `<systemColor>` fallback declarations) entirely caused `ibtool`/`CompileStoryboard` to crash (exit 255, no diagnostic text) — Interface Builder's asset-catalog named-color references need a companion `<namedColor>` fallback declaration under `<resources>` (mirroring the same role the old `<systemColor>` entries played for design-time preview), not just the inline reference. Fixed by adding `<namedColor name="bgDeep">`/`<namedColor name="textOnAccent">` stub declarations with each color's actual RGB values. Root-caused by running `ibtool` directly (same flags xcodebuild uses) outside the full build to get a fast, isolated repro loop.
  **Noted but NOT changed** (flagged to the user as a separate, smaller finding, not part of this fix): `LaunchSplash`'s radio-waves icon and `ProgressView` tint use `purpleAccent`, which — per the same contrast math — is only ~1.4:1 against `bgDeep` in light mode (both are similar-lightness purples) and a borderline ~3.4:1 in dark mode. This is a real but separate issue (a decorative/graphic element, not body text, and a brand-color choice rather than an obvious "wrong token" bug) — left alone pending an explicit decision on whether to swap it for something with more contrast (e.g. gold, or `textOnAccent`).
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green. Ran `ibtool` standalone first to isolate and fix the storyboard crash before the full build. Installed to a booted simulator and took real screenshots of `SplashView` in both forced light and dark appearance (`simctl ui ... appearance`) — confirmed title/subtitle/buttons are now clearly legible white text in both modes, matching the fix's intent exactly. Did not screenshot the native `LaunchScreen.storyboard` itself (it only renders for a fraction of a second before SwiftUI takes over — not reliably catchable via `simctl io screenshot`), but `ibtool`'s clean compile + using the exact same two already-verified tokens as the SwiftUI splash screens is strong indirect evidence it's correct.

- **2026-07-13** — **Spotlighted `TrackCard`s made visibly more prominent — bolder gold border, breathing glow, top gold artwork wash, pulsing badge (build-green).** User asked to make spotlighted tracks stand out more; discussed 4 directions (stronger card styling / pin-to-top-of-Feed / dedicated Spotlight rail / combination) — picked **stronger card styling only** (no Feed ordering or new UI section). Rendered 3 static mockups (current vs. bolder-border-and-glow vs. that-plus-a-top-gold-artwork-tint); user picked the top-tint option (**C**), then asked for "not taxing" animation — rendered a live CSS-animated preview of a slow breathing glow + badge scale (2.5s, GPU-only properties), user asked for it more prominent, re-rendered with a bigger swing (glow radius 14→32px, opacity 0.35→0.80; border opacity 0.65→1.0; badge scale 1.0→1.12), approved as final.
  **Implementation, `Nodalong/Shared/UI/TrackCard.swift`:** new `@State private var spotlightPulse: CGFloat = 0`, kicked off via `.onAppear { guard isSpotlit else { return }; withAnimation(.easeInOut(duration: 2.5).repeatForever(autoreverses: true)) { spotlightPulse = 1 } }` — a single declarative animation per spotlit card instance, no `Timer`/`TimelineView` needed since (unlike the mini-player disc fix above) there's no "freeze at current value on pause" requirement here, just a continuous ambient pulse that SwiftUI already suspends automatically once the card scrolls off-screen. Every visual property is a plain expression derived from `spotlightPulse` inside `body` (the correct SwiftUI idiom for animating computed values, not the state itself): border `strokeBorder` opacity `0.65 + 0.35 * spotlightPulse` (lineWidth bumped 1→2, non-spotlit cards unchanged at 0.08 opacity / 0.5pt), a second `.shadow(color: gold.opacity(0.35 + 0.45 * spotlightPulse), radius: 14 + 18 * spotlightPulse)` layered alongside the existing black drop shadow (two `.shadow()` modifiers compose additively in SwiftUI), and the existing read-only "Spotlight" badge (`onSpotlight == nil && isSpotlit`) gained `.scaleEffect(1 + 0.12 * spotlightPulse)`. The top gold artwork wash is a new conditional `overlay` (`isSpotlit` only) — a `LinearGradient` from `gold.opacity(0.28)` at the top fading to 0 by 40% down — inserted between the artwork/shimmer overlay and the existing black legibility scrim, so it tints the artwork without touching the bottom-anchored title/artist legibility; deliberately **not** part of the pulse (kept static, matching the last approved preview exactly) so it doesn't compete visually with the breathing glow/border. Build green (`xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`); no NodalongKit changes, no test impact. **On-device unverified** — SwiftUI Preview + build success confirm it compiles and the values are wired correctly, but the actual on-screen motion/timing (does 2.5s feel right, does the glow read as intended against real artwork colors) needs a device or simulator visual check, which wasn't done as part of this pass.

- **2026-07-13** — **Six independent bug reports fixed in one Plan-Mode pass — mini-player disc spin, Feed/Profile auto-scroll, NearMe radius/geohash, Follow-list avatar+nav, Feed attribution nav, Search People container (build-green, 58 NodalongKit tests pass — 6 new).** User listed six issues in one message and asked to investigate rather than blindly fix; entered Plan Mode, ran 3 parallel Explore agents (mini-player/scroll; NearMe geohash; follow-list/search-nav) plus direct follow-up reads, confirmed all six as real, independent bugs (no shared root cause), wrote a plan, asked one `AskUserQuestion` (auto-scroll fix: harden the detection vs. remove the feature — user chose **remove**), got the plan approved, implemented all six.
  **(1) Mini-player disc spin "snapped" instead of freezing** (`MiniPlayerBar.swift`) — root cause: `@State private var discSpinning: Bool` bound to `.rotationEffect(.degrees(discSpinning ? 360 : 0))` can only ever freeze at one of two literal angles; the freeze path (`disablesAnimations` + `discSpinning = false`) hard-snapped the disc back to 0° instead of holding wherever it visually was, on every pause/resume and every transient `scenePhase` blip away from `.active` (Control Center swipe, notification banner) — that's what read as "spins inconsistently." Confirmed via direct trace of `PlayerViewModel.swift` that normal track-to-track advance (`handleTrackEnd`/`skipToNext`/`skipToPrevious`/`playQueueItem`) never touches `isPlaying` when already `true`, so this wasn't a routine per-track glitch, specifically the pause/scene-phase paths. **Fix:** replaced the boolean+`repeatForever` approach with `TimelineView(.animation(minimumInterval: 1/30, paused: !(isPlaying && scenePhase == .active)))` — `TimelineSchedule.animation(paused:)` is the built-in API for exactly this, freezing `context.date` in place while paused and resuming from there, not from a reset origin. Rotation is now a pure function of `context.date` (`(seconds / 8.0 * 360).truncatingRemainder(dividingBy: 360)`); `applySpin()` and its three `.onAppear`/`.onChange` triggers are gone entirely — the `TimelineView`'s `paused:` condition reacts live. Checked `NowPlayingView.swift` for the same pattern — it has no spin logic of its own, so this bug was scoped to the mini player only.
  **(2) Auto-scroll-to-bottom fired even when nowhere near the bottom** (`FeedView.swift`, `ProfileView.swift`) — root cause: both screens gated `proxy.scrollTo(anchor: .bottom)` (on `playerVM.nowPlaying` nil→non-nil) behind a latch (`isNearFeedBottom`/`isNearProfileBottom`) set via `.onAppear`/`.onDisappear` on a zero-height sentinel — unreliable in a plain `ScrollView` (unlike `List`), so the flag could get stuck `true` once set, meaning a play tap from anywhere triggered the scroll. **Fix, per user's explicit choice — removed rather than hardened:** deleted the latch state, the sentinel + its appear/disappear handlers, the `.onChange(of: playerVM.nowPlaying != nil)` scroll block, and the now-unnecessary `ScrollViewReader` wrapper on both screens (confirmed via grep first that neither sentinel had any other purpose, e.g. pagination — safe to remove outright).
  **(3) NearMe radius slider silently capped at ~7km regardless of setting** (`Location.swift`, `Geohash.swift`) — investigated both requested questions. **Architecture confirmed sound**: `getNearbyUsers` already does the textbook-correct "geohash range query as an over-fetch superset (3×3 cell block), then client-side exact Haversine distance filter" pattern, not naive hash-prefix comparison — no change needed there. **Real bug found**: `let prefixLen = 5` was hardcoded regardless of the `radiusKm` argument, so the 3×3 query box was always a fixed ~15km regardless of the slider (1–50km range) — `radiusKm` only ever narrowed the post-fetch Haversine cutoff, never widened the actual Firestore query, so any radius past ~7km had zero effect on who could ever be fetched in the first place. **Fix:** new pure function `geohashPrecision(forRadiusKm:) -> Int` in `Geohash.swift` (cell-width brackets: <1.2km→precision 6, <4.9km→5, <39km→4, else→3 — the standard geohash-precision-to-cell-size table, chosen so the 3×3 block always covers the requested radius), used in `Location.swift`'s `getNearbyUsers` in place of the constant. Stored user geohashes are 9 characters (`geohashPrecision = 9` in `Limits.swift`), so truncating to any shorter prefix at query time is safe — no data migration. 6 new tests in `GeohashTests.swift` (bracket boundaries + monotonicity).
  **(4) Follow lists had no avatar and didn't navigate to a profile** (`FollowListView.swift`, `Follows.swift`, `Users.swift`) — root cause: `getFollowersList`/`getFollowingList` only ever returned bare `(uid, username)` tuples (no avatar emoji/color), and the row was a plain `HStack` with zero tap handling. **Fix — mirrors the existing batched-uid-fetch pattern** (`Parties.swift`'s `getLivePartiesHostedBy`, `Array.chunked(into:)`) rather than denormalizing avatar fields onto follow docs (avoids a backfill migration for existing follow relationships): new `getUserProfiles(uids:)` in `Users.swift` (chunked `whereField(FieldPath.documentID(), in:)`, ≤30), sharing a new private `decodeUserProfile(documentID:data:)` helper factored out of `getUserProfile`'s existing backfill+enrich logic (kept `getUserProfile` itself throwing on a genuine decode failure, unchanged behavior; `getUserProfiles` swallows a single bad row via `try?` so one malformed document doesn't fail the whole batch). `FollowListView` now fetches full `UserProfile`s (preserving the original follow-list order, dropping any uid whose fetch comes back empty — e.g. a deleted account), rows are `NavigationLink(value: profile)` wrapping an `AvatarView` + username + follow button (follow button uses `.buttonStyle(.borderless)`, the standard trick for a nested interactive control inside a `NavigationLink` row in a `List` that shouldn't also trigger the row's own navigation). Added `.navigationDestination(for: UserProfile.self) { UserProfileView(user: $0) }` to `ProfileView.swift`'s own `NavigationStack` (the one ancestor stack in the `FollowListView`-reachable set that didn't already have this handler — `UserProfileView`'s own presentations always arrive nested inside a stack that already does, e.g. Search/NearMe).
  **(5) Tapping a Feed card's username didn't navigate anywhere** (`TrackCard.swift`, `FeedView.swift`) — root cause: `TrackCard`'s attribution `userChip` had no tap target of its own; the whole card shares one `.onTapGesture { onPlay?() }`. **Fix:** new optional `onTapUser: (() -> Void)?` on `TrackCard`, wired with `.contentShape(...).onTapGesture { onTapUser?() }` directly on `userChip` (a child gesture intercepts before the parent's, so this doesn't also trigger `onPlay`) — confirmed via grep that `username:` is populated ONLY from `FeedView.swift`'s `post.username`, so no other screen's `TrackCard` usage is affected. `FeedView` wires `onTapUser: { Task { await openProfile(post.uid) } }` (`post.uid` already real and populated, confirmed via `Posts.swift`'s `mapRecentTrack`), fetching a full `UserProfile` via `getUserProfile(uid:)` then pushing. Needed a `NavigationStack(path: $path)` + `path.append(profile)` (not `.navigationDestination(item:)`, which is iOS 17+ only — deployment target here is iOS 16; mirrors the same `NavigationPath` pattern already used by Shelf's `AlbumDetailView` artist-name push) paired with `.navigationDestination(for: UserProfile.self)` on `FeedView`'s stack (previously absent).
  **(6) Search's People-mode results lacked the rounded container** Songs/Artists/Albums already have (`SearchView.swift`) — trivial, `peopleResults`' `VStack` gained the exact same wrapper as `SongsList` (`.padding(.horizontal, 12).padding(.vertical, 8)` per row, `.background(bgSurface).clipShape(cornerRadius: 12).padding(.horizontal, 16)` on the stack).
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green after every fix, built incrementally rather than as one diff. All 58 NodalongKit tests pass (52 existing + 6 new). **Honest limitations, flagged to the user up front in the plan:** items 1 and 3 are structural/mathematical fixes confirmed correct by reading the code and SwiftUI/geohash semantics directly, but "does the spin actually look right" and "do farther NearMe users now actually appear" (needs a second test account/device beyond ~7km) need an on-device pass this environment can't perform. Items 4 and 5 need an on-device tap-through (follow list row → correct profile; Feed username → correct profile). Item 2's removal is a behavior change (no more auto-follow-to-bottom) — worth confirming nothing else relied on the removed sentinels (confirmed via grep already that neither was used for pagination, but a real on-device pass is still the final word).

- **2026-07-13** — **Fixed SpotlightCarousel's bottom rounded corner going flat whenever a page's card count differs from the max; added the shared bgSurface container to Search's Artist/Album carousels and to Feed (build-green throughout).** Three small follow-ups after the container/nesting redesign session above. **(1) Spotlight corner bug**, reported with two screenshots: `SpotlightCarousel.swift`'s per-page `VStack` had `Spacer(minLength: 0)` *inside* the same `VStack` that carried `.background(bgSurface).clipShape(cornerRadius: 16)` — since the TabView's own frame is pinned to `maxPageHeight` (a deliberate constant, see the entry two below), the Spacer stretched that boxed VStack to fill the full pinned height on every page, and the separate animated `.frame(height: displayedHeight).clipped()` crop then sliced straight through the middle of the box before its bottom rounded corner ever got to render — a flat, square-cut edge on any page shorter than the max. Fixed by moving the `Spacer` *outside* the boxed VStack (wrapping both in an outer `VStack(spacing: 0)`), so the box itself always hugs its true content height and the outer crop lines up with its real (already-rounded) bottom edge. Same bug class as the trailing-list-spacer rounding bug fixed in the entry below — recorded in-code as a comment so it isn't reintroduced. **(2) Search container parity**: user pointed out Search's Songs list already had the `bgSurface` rounded-container treatment but Artists/Albums carousels didn't — added the identical wrapper (`.padding(12).background(bgSurface).clipShape(cornerRadius: 12)`) around both `ArtistsCarousel` and `AlbumsCarousel` in `SearchView.swift`. **(3) Feed container** — user asked to see a mockup first (visualize tool, dark + light mode, using the app's actual current hex values) comparing today's floating-shadow `TrackCard` list against wrapping the whole feed in one shared `bgSurface` box like Recent Tracks/Spotlight; flagged the caveat that Feed is an unbounded continuously-growing scroll (unlike the naturally-paged/day-capped carousels) before implementing, then shipped it on explicit user confirmation (`"do it!"`). `FeedView.swift`: the posts' `LazyVStack` (kept lazy — unlike Recent Tracks/Spotlight's bounded pages, Feed can hold up to 100 posts per `getFollowingFeed`, so eager `VStack` instantiation was deliberately avoided) gained `.padding(6).background(bgSurface).clipShape(cornerRadius: 16)` wrapping just the `ForEach`, sized correctly to its full logical height despite lazy child instantiation (confirmed this is standard, supported SwiftUI behavior — `.background`/`.clipShape` on a `LazyVStack` measure against the stack's total logical content size, not just currently-rendered children). Per-card `.padding(.horizontal, 16).padding(.vertical, 6)` was replaced by the `LazyVStack`'s own `spacing: 12` + the box's outer `.padding(.horizontal, 16)`, matching the Recent Tracks/Spotlight convention exactly. The pre-existing conditional mini-player-clearance spacer and the zero-height `feedBottom` scroll sentinel were both moved *out* of the boxed stack — the sentinel to a plain sibling position, the mini-player spacer to `.safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom)` on the `ScrollView` itself — specifically to avoid reintroducing bug (1)'s exact failure mode (an invisible structural row inside a rounded box stretching/misaligning the corner crop).
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green after each of the three changes. **Not yet re-verified on-device** — none of these three are reachable/confirmable from this environment (Spotlight needs a real multi-day spotlight history to see the corner render correctly; Search/Feed containers need visual confirmation the box renders as expected against real data, especially Feed's lazy-background sizing across a long real scroll). User should check all three on their device.

- **2026-07-13** — **Fixed cross-service track resolution picking the wrong release's artwork (compilation/deluxe/singles instead of the studio album) — build-green, 52 NodalongKit tests pass (15 new).** User reported wrong artwork when playing songs and asked how cross-service resolution works, assuming ISRC — planned via `EnterPlanMode`, investigated with 2 parallel Explore agents (resolution cascade + artwork/catalog storage), then implemented after user confirmed scope (`AskUserQuestion`: fix both root causes found, using a keyword + album-name match signal).
  **Two confirmed bugs, verified by reading the actual code, not just the agents' summaries:** (1) `upsertCatalogTrack`'s backfill branch (`Catalog.swift`) only ever added missing `appleMusicUri`/`spotifyUri` fields to an existing catalog row — `artwork`/`album` were never included, so whichever match happened to write the row *first* for a given ISRC (which could easily be a "Greatest Hits" compilation) had its artwork frozen there permanently, for every user, forever. (2) Every ISRC lookup on both platforms (`AppleMusicService.resolveCatalogArtwork`, `PlayerViewModel.resolve`, `SpotifyMusicService.searchByIsrc`/`searchByTitleArtist`) just took the API's first result with zero filtering — Apple's catalog routinely has multiple `Song` entries sharing one ISRC (original album, deluxe reissue, compilation) and nothing ever looked past `.items.first`. **Ruled out**: playback itself doesn't re-fetch artwork — `PlayerViewModel.playCurrent()` sets `nowPlaying.artwork` from the incoming `Song.artwork` before resolve ever runs, so the bug is entirely at catalog-write time, not a playback-time flip.
  **Shipped:** new pure-logic file `NodalongKit/Sources/NodalongKit/Utils/ReleaseMatching.swift` (matching the existing `PlaylistOrder`/`GroupByAlbum` pure-logic-plus-tests convention) — `containsCompilationKeyword(_:)` (keyword denylist: "greatest hits", "deluxe", "anniversary", "remaster", etc.), `bestReleaseIndex(albumNames:expectedAlbum:)` (scores candidates: exact album match > substring match > compilation-keyword penalty; ties keep the original/first-result order, so behavior is unchanged whenever the heuristic has nothing to go on), and `isBetterRelease(newAlbum:existingAlbum:)` (one-directional: true only when the existing album is keyword-flagged and the new one isn't — can upgrade a catalog row, never downgrade a good match). 15 new unit tests in `ReleaseMatchingTests.swift`.
  Wired into every candidate-selection point that previously took `.first`: `AppleMusicService.resolveCatalogArtwork` (both the private single-ISRC overload and the public id→ISRC→text-search cascade) gained an `album:` parameter, now fetches the full `.items`/`.songs` array instead of just the first, and scores against it — threaded from `LibrarySyncService.ingestToCatalogOnly`, which already had `song.album` in scope. `PlayerViewModel.resolve(_:)` (the *live* Apple Music playback cascade — `AppleMusicService.resolve(_:)` is separately dead code per the 2026-06-10 entry, not touched) — same treatment on its ISRC and text-search steps, scored against `song.album`. `SpotifyMusicService.searchByIsrc`/`searchByTitleArtist` — these are `MusicService` protocol requirements with fixed signatures, so added private `(_:preferAlbum:)`/`(title:artist:preferAlbum:)` overloads (bumped `limit` from `1` to `10`, score via `track.album?.name`) that the protocol-facing methods now delegate to with `preferAlbum: nil`; `resolve(_:)` calls the album-aware overloads directly with `song.album`.
  **Bug A fix**: `upsertCatalogTrack`'s backfill branch now also checks `isBetterRelease(newAlbum: input.album, existingAlbum: existing.album)` and, if true, corrects `artwork`/`album`/`albumLower`/`trackNumber`/`albumReleaseDate` alongside the existing service-id backfill — lets a catalog row self-heal toward a cleaner match on a later resolution, without any risk of regressing an already-good one.
  **Verification:** all 52 NodalongKit tests pass (37 existing + 15 new). `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green. **Honest limitation, stated up front in the plan**: confirming this fixes the real-world symptom needs a live device test against a real song known to have multiple ISRC-sharing releases (e.g. one that appears on both a studio album and a "Greatest Hits" compilation) — not verifiable from this environment without a real authenticated session and a specific known-ambiguous track. User should check on-device with a track they know exhibits the bug.

- **2026-07-13** — **Finished the "rounded container" list audit, fixed a real trailing-spacer rounding bug, widened bgBase/bgSurface, deleted 3 dead colorsets — build-green.** Direct follow-up to the container/nesting work below. User asked "did you tell me what colours are used for what and where" (answered with a full token/hex/usage table, read live from the colorset files rather than from memory, since the user had hand-edited several in Xcode by this point) and separately asked "did you do rounded corners for all lists?" — auditing found **no**: several lists (Shelf's Artists/Liked lists, `PlaylistDetailView`, `AddToPlaylistSheet`, Party's queue + add-sheet) got the `bgSurface` row-color treatment but were left on `.listStyle(.plain)`, which doesn't auto-round corners the way `.listStyle(.insetGrouped)` does. Switched all of them.
  **A deeper bug surfaced mid-fix, affecting the already-shipped `AlbumDetailSearchView` too:** several of these lists end with a *conditional* transparent spacer row (`if playerVM.nowPlaying != nil { Color.clear.frame(height: 70)... }`) to clear the floating mini-player pill. `.insetGrouped` rounds the list's true first/last row, not "the last row with a color" — so whenever a track is actually playing, that invisible spacer becomes the true last row and the rounded bottom corner silently lands on it instead of the last visible track, going square exactly when the mini player is showing. Never caught because every verification screenshot so far happened to be taken with nothing playing. **Fixed at the root**, not per-file: replaced the trailing-spacer-row idiom with `.safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom) { if nowPlaying != nil { Color.clear.frame(height: 70) } }` applied to the `List` itself, across `AlbumDetailSearchView`, both `ShelfView` lists, `PlaylistDetailView`, and `PartyView`'s queue — this reserves the same scroll clearance without it ever being a list row, so rounding is correct regardless of playback state.
  **`PartyView`'s queue also needed a real `Section` wrap**, not just the style switch: its `bgBase`-colored header content (now-playing hero, participants, notice, play-queue button, queue header) and its `bgSurface`-colored queue rows sat in one implicit section with no `Section { }` boundary — `.insetGrouped` would round based on the section's true first/last row, unrelated to which color group it's actually in. Wrapped the queue rows in their own `Section { } header: { queueHeader }`, mirroring `QueueView`'s existing two-`Section` ("Now Playing" / "Up Next") structure exactly rather than inventing a new pattern.
  **Two consistency findings the user explicitly agreed to fix:** (1) `bgBase`/`bgSurface` light-mode values (`#A08EE8` / `#A999F4`, both the user's own Xcode hand-edits) had drifted to be almost indistinguishable — undermining the whole container pattern. Proposed and confirmed with the user (`AskUserQuestion`) a new `bgSurface` light value, `#8A74E3` — same hue as their `bgBase`, ~6 points darker (contrast vs `bgBase` 1.05:1 → 1.32:1; ink-text-on-`bgSurface` contrast stays a healthy 4.51:1, meets WCAG AA) — before writing the file, since `bgBase` was their deliberate choice and `bgSurface` needed to complement it, not fight it. (2) Deleted `purplePale`/`purpleSoft`/`errorTint` colorsets and their `Color+Theme.swift` declarations — confirmed zero Swift references beforehand; the only other hits were the pre-existing, already-documented-dead `NodalongKit` `Theme.Colors` enum (unrelated raw-hex-string constants, not touched) and stale `.build/` artifacts.
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green after every step. Grepped for any remaining `.listStyle(.plain)` list with `bgSurface` rows — none left. Confirmed zero leftover references to the 3 deleted colorsets in real source. **Not yet re-verified on-device with the mini player actually showing** (the specific condition that exposes the bug this pass fixed) — this needs a real device check, since neither the simulator (unreliable for appearance/interaction this session) nor a static screenshot can confirm a playback-state-dependent rendering bug.

- **2026-07-12** — **Mixed palette (Option C for light mode, Option B for dark mode) + a new `bgElevated` token + container/nesting redesign across Queue/Album/Artist/Shelf/carousels — build-green, real-device-confirmed for the Feed + auth screens.** Follow-up to the same-day "Classic Purple" (Option C, both modes) entry below. User picked C for light and, separately, B for dark ("C light mode. B dark mode."), then asked to see mockups of Feed+mini-player/Queue/Search/Artist/Album/Shelf in both modes before implementing — delivered as one HTML mockup (`c_light_b_dark_all_screens`). No objection to the mix itself, but a **real device screenshot** of the actual Queue screen surfaced two separate, more important findings:
  **(1) A genuine pre-existing bug, unrelated to any palette choice:** `LoginView`/`RegisterView`/`ForgotPasswordView` never applied any themed background — they've always sat on the plain system default (black in dark mode), unlike `SplashView`/`MusicServiceLinkView` which correctly use `bgDeep`. Never caught before because prior verification passes covered Feed/Profile/Search/Shelf, never the pushed Login/Register/ForgotPassword screens. **Fixed:** added `.background(Color.nodalong.bgDeep.ignoresSafeArea())` to all three (matching `SplashView`'s existing tone, since they're all part of the same pre-auth `NavigationStack`).
  **(2) A structural design request:** the user pointed at Queue's "Up Next" section — its rows share one continuous `bgSurface`-colored rounded container (via `.listRowBackground(Color.nodalong.bgSurface)` on an `.insetGrouped` `List`, adjacent same-color rows visually merging with hairline separators) — and asked for this same "container" treatment everywhere a list of songs appears: an album's track list, an artist's Top Songs, and the box wrapping the Spotlight/Recent-Tracks carousels. Clarifying question asked first: where does "we need an extra colour in certain places" apply → user said **nested containers** (an element sitting inside another container needs a step lighter/darker so it doesn't blend into its parent).
  **Root cause of the nesting need, found by reading the actual code before touching it:** `ArtworkThumbnail`'s loading/failure placeholder (`Nodalong/Features/Shelf/ShelfView.swift`) and `TrackCard`'s own base fill + `noArtworkPlaceholder` (`Nodalong/Shared/UI/TrackCard.swift`) **already both used `bgSurface`** — so simply wrapping their parent rows/carousels in a new `bgSurface` container would make any not-yet-loaded or artwork-less thumbnail invisible against its own new backdrop. **Fix:** new `bgElevated` colorset (light `#9C7BCC`, dark `#7952AD`) — one step further from `bgBase` than `bgSurface` already is, in the *same direction* `bgSurface` already steps (darker in light mode — confirmed via HSL that `bgSurface` light, L≈74.9, is already slightly darker than `bgBase` light, L≈78.0, matching the light-mode Material convention of elevated-surfaces-get-darker; lighter in dark mode, matching dark-mode's opposite convention). `TrackCard`'s two `bgSurface` spots and `ArtworkThumbnail`'s placeholder now use `bgElevated` instead, so they read as a distinct nested layer against any `bgSurface` wrapper. Verified computationally before touching files: `bgElevated` vs `bgSurface` contrast is 2.19:1 (light) / 1.49:1 (dark) — deliberately subtle (this is a "nested layer" cue, not a text-contrast pairing), clearly perceptible without looking like a harsh block.
  **Container wrapping applied:** catalog `AlbumDetailSearchView`'s "Tracks" section rows (`.listRowBackground` `bgBase`→`bgSurface`, matching Queue's existing pattern exactly); `ArtistDetailSearchView`'s Top Songs row-stack (new `.background(bgSurface).clipShape(cornerRadius: 12)` wrapper, since this screen uses a plain `VStack`/`TabView`, not a `List`); `RecentTracksCarousel` (each page's `VStack` of `TrackCard`s gets a `bgSurface` wrapper, `carouselHeight` bumped +12pt for the added padding); `SpotlightCarousel` (same wrapper, but this view's height-pinning math is unusually fragile — documented in-code as having broken twice before from naive frame animation — so a new `containerPadding` constant was added to *both* `pageHeight`/`maxPageHeight` formulas alongside the existing `shadowInset`, rather than eyeballing a new magic number); Shelf's `ArtistsListView` and `RecentlyLikedListView` (`.listRowBackground` `bgBase`→`bgSurface`, since these are structurally identical `List`s of items, same as the catalog track list); Shelf's own `AlbumDetailView` (the personal-library album view, separate from the catalog one) — its track rows were plain `VStack` + `Divider()`s with zero shared background at all, now wrapped the same way as `ArtistDetailSearchView`, with the last track's trailing divider removed (it never made sense once the group has its own bottom edge). Left untouched, deliberately: Shelf's `AlbumsListView`/`PlaylistsShelfView`/`ArtistDetailView` album grids and `ArtistDetailSearchView`'s discography album-card rows — these are grids of self-contained artwork tiles (each already a distinct visual object), not lists of plain text rows, so they don't have the "floating with no group identity" problem this request was about.
  **Palette values:** light mode is byte-identical to the same-day Classic Purple (C) entry below (`bgBase C2ADE1` / `bgSurface D6C9E8` / `bgDeep 5B2D9F` / `purpleAccent 723DC2` / `purpleMid 443399` / `purpleBold 762C96` / `purpleDark 522C8C`). Dark mode switched to Option B's exact `bgBase 492F6A` / `bgSurface 5A3E7E` / `purpleAccent 9470C2`, with `bgDeep`/`purpleMid`/`purpleBold`/`purpleDark` re-derived via HSL anchored on B's own measured hue/saturation (not reused from C) — `purpleMid 6A5CB2` (hue≈250, blue-violet, auth CTAs + follow buttons), `purpleBold 9C5DB6` (hue≈282, magenta-violet, NearMe's primary action), `purpleDark 634592` (hue≈264, same hue as `purpleAccent`, brand identity: mini player pill + Shelf sync banner), `bgDeep 382556`.
