Contrast re-verified for the new dark-mode role tokens: white-text-on-background ranges 4.98–7.05:1 (unchanged `purpleAccent`) through the newly-derived 5.47–7.05:1 for Mid/Bold/Dark — all comfortably above the pre-existing bug's broken 1.19–2.51:1 (see the immediately-preceding session note about `textOnAccent` — unaffected by this palette swap, still fixed).
  **Verification:** `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` green. Removed a temporary `print()` diagnostic added mid-session to confirm `@AppStorage`/`.preferredColorScheme` resolution (root-caused a long simulator-only rendering wedge — this specific simulator instance stopped honoring `simctl ui ... appearance` entirely partway through the session, confirmed via `defaults read -g AppleInterfaceStyle` and a `shutdown`+`boot` cycle that didn't fully clear it; the debug print proved the app-side `AppearanceMode` resolution was correct throughout, isolating the problem to the simulator tool, not the code). **Real-device confirmation, not simulator**: the user's own physical-device screenshots (both the pre-fix black Login background and the Queue's container styling) are what actually grounded this entry's two fixes — the simulator was informative for the color-palette swap (did eventually render Feed correctly in light mode after a reboot) but unreliable for testing appearance toggling specifically. NodalongKit tests unaffected (no NodalongKit changes). **Not yet independently re-verified on-device by an agent**: the container/nesting changes to Search's Artist/Album pages and Shelf's four segments (reachable only via in-app navigation, which isn't automatable here without `idb`/a scratch XCUITest) — user should check these directly on their device.

- **2026-07-12** — **Replaced the "Deep Plum" palette with the user's exact hex values ("B185DB family") — build-green, verified on-device in both light and dark mode.** Direct follow-up to the Deep Plum entry below. User showed a horizontal purple gradient swatch image and asked to use it as the palette guide instead ("light mode would have more light hues, dark mode would be more darker"); when I started rendering a mockup from my own visual read of the image, the user interrupted and gave exact hex values instead: light mode `background #B185DB` / `secondary #C19EE0` / `button-action-tab-highlight-like #A06CD5`; dark mode `background #6247AA` / `secondary #7251B5` / `button-action-tab-highlight-like #815AC0`. Confirmed via HSL analysis these 6 values are a single monotonic lightness ramp (darkest→lightest: `6247AA→7251B5→815AC0→A06CD5→B185DB→C19EE0`), consistent with the swatch image now given as precise numbers. Rendered a mockup applying the exact values before implementing (`exact_hex_palette_check`), explicitly flagging one value the user hadn't specified — light-mode ink/text color, proposed as `#241542` since `#B185DB` is too saturated/medium-lightness for white text to read clearly against. Asked two clarifying questions before touching any files: (1) confirmed `#241542` for light-mode text, (2) since the user's 3-role guide (background/secondary/accent) is simpler than the app's existing 6-shade purple system (`purplePale/Soft/Accent/Bold/Mid/Dark`), asked whether all 6 should collapse to the one given accent or keep some variation — user chose **"keep some variation."**

The other 5 purple tokens + `bgDeep` are derived programmatically via HSL, anchored on each mode's accent hue (~270° light, ~263° dark) and each mode's own measured saturation (~55% light, ~45% dark), varying only lightness: light mode darkens for higher emphasis (`purplePale` L92 → `purpleDark` L28, since darker reads as "bolder" against a light background); dark mode brightens for higher emphasis (`purpleBold` L66 is the brightest, since lighter pops more against a dark background) — deliberately different derivation direction per mode, not a mirrored ramp, because "more emphasis" means moving toward opposite ends of the lightness scale depending which end the background sits at. `bgBase`/`bgSurface` are the user's `background`/`secondary` values verbatim in both modes. `textPrimary`/`textSecondary`/`textTertiary`: dark mode unchanged (white at 100/60/35%); light mode ink changed from the old Deep Plum `#2B123F` to the user-approved `#241542` at the same three opacities. `gold`/`error`/`errorTint` untouched (not part of this palette, still single universal values from before Deep Plum). Verified computationally before touching files: all 7 practical text/background pairings score between 3.75:1 and 13.17:1 contrast (WCAG) — the one low score, white text on the light-mode accent button (`A06CD5`, 3.75:1), is the user's own explicitly-specified value used as intended for icon/heart/tab-highlight contexts, not paragraph text, so left as specified rather than second-guessed.

Booted the simulator, forced `xrun simctl ui ... appearance light|dark`, launched the app fresh in each: **confirmed on the actual splash/auth screen** (uses `bgDeep`, `textPrimary`, `textSecondary`, and the purple button family) — light mode shows the new light-purple `bgDeep` background with fully legible dark-ink (`#241542`) title/subtitle/button text; dark mode shows the new darker-purple `bgDeep` with legible white text and a correctly-lighter "Sign In" button standing out against the background. **Partial verification, scope limited on purpose:** this device's Firebase Auth session was signed out (the auth/splash screen is the actual landing screen, not just a loading flash), and entering login credentials on the user's behalf is out of scope for this project (established in earlier Spotify-auth entries below) — so Feed/Profile/Search/Shelf could not be visually re-verified with the new exact-hex values specifically. That said, the colorset architecture, call sites, and every screen's usage of these same tokens is byte-for-byte identical to the already-fully-verified Deep Plum pass (only the underlying hex values changed) — so the residual risk is narrow (did the 9 files get the right values, not whether the wiring works), and that narrow risk is what today's screenshot pass + grep + contrast-ratio check directly covers. - **2026-07-12** — **Added an in-app System/Light/Dark appearance toggle (build-green, verified overriding a forced system appearance in-simulator).** Direct follow-up to the light-mode entry above — user asked "how can I swap to light mode," was told it currently just follows the OS setting, then asked for an in-app toggle instead. **Shipped:** new `AppearanceMode` enum (`Nodalong/Shared/Theme/AppearanceMode.swift`) — `.system`/`.light`/`.dark`, with a `colorScheme: ColorScheme?` (`nil` for `.system`, deferring to the OS) and a shared `appearanceModeKey` UserDefaults key. `NodalongApp.swift` reads it via `@AppStorage(appearanceModeKey)` and applies `.preferredColorScheme(appearanceMode.colorScheme)` on the root `WindowGroup` content. `ProfileView` gained a new `appearanceSection` (segmented `Picker`, placed right after "Music Services") bound to the *same* `@AppStorage` key — no extra plumbing needed for the picker to take effect immediately, since `@AppStorage` on a shared key auto-synchronizes across every reader in-process. Defaults to `.system` (today's only behavior) for anyone who never touches the picker. **Verified the override actually works, not just that it compiles:** forced the simulator's system-wide appearance to dark (`simctl ui ... appearance dark`), launched fresh, confirmed dark mode + "System" segment selected, then drove a scratch XCUITest tap on "Light" — the whole app switched to light mode immediately, with the simulator's system-wide setting *still* forced to dark the entire time, proving this is a genuine in-app override and not just reflecting the OS. Build green, 37 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). Scratch UI test target fully removed post-verification.

- **2026-07-12** — **Added a real light mode — "Deep Plum" palette, both light and dark now genuinely adaptive (build-green, 37 tests pass, verified in-simulator across both appearances).** User asked to "change the styling"; after narrowing scope (app-wide theme, new purple-family palette) and showing three mockup options via the visualize tool, picked "Deep Plum" and asked for the whole thing in one pass, not phased.
  **Why this was bigger than a color swap:** the app was dark-only in practice — all 37 asset-catalog colorsets (`Nodalong/Resources/Assets.xcassets/*.colorset/`) were "universal" (one RGB value, no Dark/Light appearance entries), and ~131 call sites across `Nodalong/` hardcoded `.foregroundStyle(.white)` / `Color.white.opacity(...)` / `Color.black.opacity(...)` directly instead of an adaptive token. Adding light colorset variants alone would have made text/icons invisible against a light background.
  **Shipped:** (1) New "Deep Plum" hex pairs (light/dark) written directly into `Contents.json` for the 10 core tokens (`bgBase`/`bgSurface`/`bgDeep`, the 6 `purple*` tokens, `AccentColor`) plus 3 brand-new adaptive tokens (`textPrimary`/`textSecondary`/`textTertiary`, replacing `Color+Theme.swift`'s old hardcoded `Color.white.opacity(...)`) — each colorset gets a `"appearances": [{"appearance": "luminosity", "value": "dark"}]` entry alongside the default (light) one. `gold`/`error`/`errorTint` deliberately left untouched (already render identically in both modes since a universal-only entry needs no explicit dark override — no edit needed). Audited the other ~22 colorsets (`gray*`, `primary*`, `gold100-300`, `accent500`, `error50/500`, `spotifyGreen`) via grep — **zero Swift references to any of them** (dead, same pattern as the already-known-dead `Theme.Colors` enum) — left untouched, not worth risking a blind edit to unused assets. (2) Swept all ~131 hardcoded white/black call sites: `.foregroundStyle(.white)` (91 exact + 6 more with trailing chained modifiers my first regex missed) → `Color.nodalong.textPrimary`; `.white.opacity(X)` (34, two sub-forms: `Color.white.opacity(` vs bare `.white.opacity(`) → `Color.nodalong.textPrimary.opacity(X)`; 6 ternary `? .white : ...` cases → `Color.nodalong.textPrimary`. `.black.opacity(...)`/`Color.black` (28 total) were individually reviewed and left as absolute — confirmed every one is either a `.shadow(...)` (standard cross-mode convention), a legibility scrim over unpredictable album-art/photo content (`TrackCard`, `ShelfView`'s artist hero, `AvatarView`), or inside a `#Preview` block — added a one-line comment at the two least-obvious spots (`TrackCard`'s title gradient, `AvatarPickerView`'s swatch selection ring) so a future pass doesn't "fix" them by mistake. (3) Fixed a related, deeper bug in `MainTabView.swift`'s one-time chrome-setup: `UIColor(Color.nodalong.purpleAccent)` bakes a **static snapshot** of the color at whatever appearance was active when the (once-per-process) `init()` ran — it would never follow a live system light/dark toggle. Switched every UIKit appearance-proxy assignment (tab bar tint, nav bar title/large-title colors, window background) plus `SystemVolumeSlider`'s `MPVolumeView` tint to `UIColor(named:)`, which stays genuinely trait-aware since it re-resolves the named asset on every query, not just at assignment time — `SystemVolumeSlider` specifically needed this since `NowPlayingView`'s background (blurred art + `bgBase`) is no longer always-dark now that light mode exists.
  **Verification — actual on-device appearance toggling, not just build success:** `xcrun simctl ui <device> appearance dark|light` + a scratch XCUITest tour (`NodalongUITestsScratch`, added to `project.yml`, fully removed after) capturing Feed/Profile (incl. scrolled Music Services section)/Search/Shelf in both modes. Confirmed: dark mode renders the new deeper aubergine tones correctly with no regressions from today's look; light mode shows fully legible dark-ink text on light lavender everywhere, including the gold "Apple Music connected" checkmark row and the destructive-red Sign Out button, which weren't touched and didn't need to be (their existing colors already had sufficient contrast against the new light background).
  **Incidental infrastructure fix, unrelated to theming but blocking verification:** `xcodebuild -scheme Nodalong` started failing with `does not contain a scheme named "Nodalong"` — the project had never had a persisted `.xcscheme` file (only `xcuserdata/.../xcschememanagement.plist`, which just orders/hides schemes Xcode.app autocreates on open; `xcodebuild` from a pure CLI session with no prior GUI open apparently can't materialize that implicit scheme). Fixed by adding `scheme: {}` under the `Nodalong` target in `project.yml`, which tells XcodeGen to explicitly emit a real, persisted `Nodalong.xcscheme` under `xcshareddata/xcschemes/` — this is a real, permanent fix (not reverted), since the same failure would otherwise recur for every future from-scratch `xcodegen generate` in a CLI-only environment.
  Build green (`xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), no new warnings, 37 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). Scratch UI test target fully removed post-verification.

- **2026-07-12** — **`ensureActiveDevice()` retries an empty Spotify Connect device list once, and prefers an `is_active` device over blindly picking the first entry (build-green, 37 tests pass).** Direct follow-up to the same-day error-surfacing fix above — once the real error was actually visible, the user hit a *different*, more specific failure: after the earlier 403 resolved itself (Search/Play now working — root cause of that 403 still not confirmed, but no longer reproducing), tapping Play showed "Nothing to play — Open Spotify on a device, then try again." (`SpotifyServiceError.noActiveDevice`). **Exact repro, narrated by the user:** tapped Play → got the no-device error → opened the real Spotify app → navigated back to Nodalong (Spotify now backgrounded) → tapped Play again → worked → a few moments later, tried a different track → same "Nothing to play" error again, with Spotify still running in the background the whole time (not force-quit). This "works once right after opening Spotify, then fails again shortly after while backgrounded" pattern is consistent with Spotify Connect's device-presence registration lapsing on an idle, backgrounded, non-playing Spotify instance — a timing gap, not a permanently absent device. **Fix, `Nodalong/Services/SpotifyMusicService.swift`:** `ensureActiveDevice()`'s `GET /me/player/devices` call now retries once (after a 1.5s delay) if the first response comes back with an empty device list, before finally throwing `.noActiveDevice` — cheap, safe, no behavior change on the already-working path. Also added `isActive` (`is_active`, auto-mapped via the existing `.convertFromSnakeCase` decoder) to `SpotifyDeviceDTO`, and `ensureActiveDevice()` now prefers a device where `isActive == true` over blindly picking `.first` — guards against a stale/ghost entry (e.g. an inactive "Web Player" placeholder Spotify sometimes lists alongside the real device) winning by list order. **Explicitly not fixed/out of scope:** this doesn't address Spotify being *fully* suspended by iOS while backgrounded for an extended period — that's inherent to the "pure Web API remote control, not embedded SDK" architecture already documented in Profile's copy ("Spotify playback needs the Spotify app open on this device"); the retry only papers over a momentary registration gap, not a genuinely absent device. Build green (`xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), 37 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). **On-device unverified** — no working Spotify session available in the local simulator to reproduce with (see the entry above); needs the user's own device to confirm the retry actually resolves the "works once then fails" pattern, or whether it still recurs after a longer background idle period (which the retry is not designed to fix).

- **2026-07-12** — **Surfaced the real Spotify API error instead of a misleading generic message; retracted the Feb-2026-Dev-Mode-migration theory after the user confirmed the Dashboard is clean (build-green, 37 tests pass).** User reported Search still returning "No results" for everything and every Spotify play attempt still showing "Track unavailable... Couldn't find this on Spotify" (screenshots), and asked directly: is the token being attached to Web API requests, are we using Spotify's SDK correctly, can we track down the actual reason. **Answered by rereading `SpotifyMusicService.swift` line by line:** token attachment is correct (`authenticatedURLRequest` sets `Authorization: Bearer <token>` on every request; 401 correctly triggers refresh+retry — not a wiring bug). There's also no "Spotify Web SDK" in this app to misuse — it's plain Web API (REST) by deliberate design (Stage B, 2026-07-11), not the browser-embedded Web Playback SDK, which was rejected for hijacking Control Center branding. The actual failure is a genuine `403 Forbidden` from Spotify's servers, captured live last session (`searchAll('Kanye') failed: forbidden`). **The user then checked the Spotify Developer Dashboard and confirmed everything is correct** — Premium active on the owning account, no pending migration banner, account within the 5-user cap — which retracts the previous entry's specific "unmigrated Dev Mode app" theory. The 403 has some other, not-yet-identified cause.
  **What this re-investigation did surface, independent of the still-unknown root cause: the real Spotify error is completely invisible everywhere it matters**, which is precisely why neither agent nor user could tell what was actually happening beyond "some catch block fired." Three fixes, all diagnostic/UX, no behavior change to the happy path: (1) `SpotifyMusicService.resolve(_:)` — its id→catalog→ISRC→title/artist cascade caught every step's error via `do/catch { print(...) }` and *unconditionally* ended with `throw SpotifyServiceError.notFound`, so even when every step failed with `.forbidden` (an already-well-written, already-existing error case — `"Spotify denied this request."`), the caller only ever saw the generic `.notFound` → `"Couldn't find this on Spotify."`, indistinguishable from a genuine catalog miss. Fixed by tracking `var lastRealError: SpotifyServiceError?` across the cascade (set only from genuine Spotify API call failures — id lookup, catalog-known-id lookup, ISRC search, text search — deliberately *not* the Firestore catalog-row lookup, which is unrelated to Spotify's own health) and throwing `lastRealError ?? .notFound` at the end instead of always `.notFound`. All existing prints kept as-is. (2) `SearchViewModel` — the same collapse existed in `fetchMusic`'s four scope branches (`.all`/`.artists`/`.albums`/`.songs`): any error became `results = .empty`, rendering as a bare "No results" with zero signal the search never ran. Added `@Published private(set) var lastSearchErrorDetail: String?` (cleared at the top of `search(via:)`, set via a new `errorDetail(_:)` helper — `(error as? LocalizedError)?.errorDescription ?? "\(error)"`, deliberately mirroring `PlayerViewModel.lastPlaybackErrorDetail`'s already-established convention rather than inventing a new one) in all four catch blocks. `SearchView.noResultsState` now shows it as a secondary red caption beneath "No results for '...'" when present. (3) `fetchDisplayName()` (`GET /v1/me`, used only for Profile's "Connected as {name}" label) was a bare `try?` with zero logging — a concrete new lead surfaced while investigating: Profile has only ever shown the generic "Connected to Spotify" fallback, never the named variant, meaning `/me` may *also* be failing, which would point to something account/token-wide rather than `/search`-specific. Added a `print("[Nodalong.SpotifyMe] fetchDisplayName failed: ...")` in the failure path (console-only, no UI change — `lastError` is scoped to the connect-button's own failure state and repurposing it for a background refresh would show a confusing message in the wrong place).
  **Verification note — this could NOT be completed end-to-end this session**: this simulator's Spotify session had its Keychain tokens genuinely cleared during the *previous* entry's on-device disconnect-regression test, and reconnecting requires a real Spotify login (`ASWebAuthenticationSession`) that the agent will not complete on the user's behalf (credential-entry is out of scope regardless of context). Confirmed build green + `xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild` + 37 NodalongKit tests, but the actual new diagnostic text (what `lastSearchErrorDetail`/`lastPlaybackErrorDetail` now show) is **unverified** — the user's own device already has a live Spotify session and hit this exact failure in their screenshots, so the next step is on them: reproduce the same Search/Play attempt on that device and report back the new, more specific message. Whatever it says becomes the next concrete lead (if still `.forbidden`, that's a different external cause than the retracted Dev-Mode theory and worth a fresh Spotify-side investigation; if something else entirely — e.g. `.unauthorized` — that's a token/session problem, likely fixable by disconnecting and reconnecting Spotify).

- **2026-07-12** — **Fixed Apple Music silently "reconnecting" after being disconnected — true single-linked-service enforcement (build-green, 37 tests pass, verified end-to-end via force-terminate + relaunch on-device, not just build success).** User report, with a screenshot showing both Apple Music AND Spotify listed as "connected" simultaneously: *"Closing and reopening the app logs me back in to apple music (not expected functionality I should only be connected to one service at a time)."* This is the deeper bug behind the previous entry's routing fix — Search/play were already routing correctly, but the *display and gating* of "which service is linked" were still broken.
  **Root cause, confirmed by reading `AppleMusicService.swift`/`SpotifyMusicService.swift`/`ContentView.swift`/`ProfileView.swift` in full plus an Explore-agent completeness sweep of every `isAuthenticated`/`?? .appleMusic` call site:** MusicKit's OS-level Apple Music permission has **no per-app revoke** (already documented in this codebase). `AppleMusicService.logout()` only flips an in-memory `authStatus` flag for the current process; `init()` unconditionally re-reads `MusicAuthorization.currentStatus` on every launch, which is still `.authorized` since the OS never actually revoked anything — so `musicService.isAuthenticated` silently resurrects to `true` on **every relaunch**, deterministically, for anyone who has ever granted Apple Music once. Every "is this service connected" surface in the app (Profile's status rows/buttons, `ContentView.shouldShowMusicLink`, the pull-to-refresh sync target, the confirm-before-switch triggers) read this raw flag directly instead of the persisted `UserProfile.activeService` (Firestore, already `MusicServiceType?` — the actual source of truth for playback routing since Stage B). Two compounding bugs made it worse: (1) `ContentView`'s `.onChange(of: musicService.isAuthenticated)` was a **passive** reactive listener that treated *any* transition to `true` as "the user just connected Apple Music" — including the false-positive transition from the ghost OS-grant resurrecting on launch — and reactively overwrote Firestore's `activeService` back to `.appleMusic`, silently discarding the user's real choice of Spotify. This is the exact mechanism behind the reported bug. (2) `setActiveService(uid:service:)` only accepted a non-optional `MusicServiceType` — there was no way to persist "no service linked" at all, so even a correctly-written disconnect handler had nowhere to record that choice.
  **Fix — make persisted `activeService` the sole "is this linked" signal everywhere; raw `isAuthenticated` becomes purely a technical capability check, consulted only right before actually invoking an SDK (unchanged — that's already how playback/search routing works):** (a) `setActiveService` (`Nodalong/API/Firestore/Users.swift`) now takes `MusicServiceType?`; `nil` writes `FieldValue.delete()` so a missing field unambiguously decodes as "unlinked" (`UserProfile`'s `decodeIfPresent` already handled this). (b) New `setActiveMusicService(_:uid:playerVM:authVM:)` in `Nodalong/Shared/ActiveMusicService.swift` (paired with last session's `activeMusicService()` reader) — the **only** place `activeService` should ever be written from: persists to Firestore, calls `authVM.refreshProfile()`, and updates `playerVM.activeService` directly. (c) `ContentView`'s two `.onChange(of: ...isAuthenticated)` blocks had their `setActiveService` calls removed entirely — they now only trigger `syncService.sync(...)` (harmless, kept as-is), since a passive observer on a flag that can silently flip due to OS state is fundamentally the wrong place to record a real user choice. `shouldShowMusicLink` changed from `!musicService.isAuthenticated && !spotifyService.isAuthenticated` to `authVM.isAuthenticated && authVM.profile?.activeService == nil` (no extra loading-guard needed — `authVM.isInitializing` already gates `MainTabView` until `profile` has loaded). (d) The real connect/disconnect writes moved to the actual user-initiated actions: `MusicServiceLinkView`'s two connect buttons (gained `authVM`/`playerVM` env objects) and `ProfileView`'s `connectAppleMusic()`/`connectSpotify()`/both "Disconnect X" buttons all now call `setActiveMusicService(...)` directly after a real `authorize()`/`logout()` succeeds. (e) `ProfileView` gained `isAppleLinked`/`isSpotifyLinked` computed properties (`authVM.profile?.activeService == .appleMusic` / `== .spotify`) and every display/gating call site that used to read raw `isAuthenticated` — `authStatusRow`, `authStatusLabel`, `actionButton`'s three-way branch, `subscriptionStatusRow`'s visibility, `spotifyStatusRow`, `spotifyStatusLabel`, `spotifyActionButton`, both confirm-before-switch triggers, and `.refreshable`'s sync target — now reads these instead. `isDenied` (a genuine MusicKit fact, unrelated to which service is linked) still gates the "Open Settings" recovery branch as before.
  **Verification — the actual regression test, not just build success:** same scratch-XCUITest-plus-`console-pty` methodology as the previous entry (target added to `project.yml`, fully removed after). Confirmed on-device: (1) before the fix, Profile genuinely showed both services as "Connected" simultaneously — reproduced first, then fixed. (2) **The reported bug itself**: connected Spotify, force-terminated the app (`simctl terminate`, not just backgrounding), relaunched — Profile now correctly shows *only* Spotify connected (previously this is exactly when Apple Music's ghost grant would silently reassert itself). (3) Tapped "Disconnect Spotify" → the `MusicServiceLinkView` chooser cover correctly reappeared. (4) Force-terminated and relaunched again → **the chooser cover still showed** — Apple Music's OS-level grant did not silently re-link it, which is precisely the scenario that was broken before this fix. Build green (`xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), 37 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). **Test account left mid-flow**: Spotify was disconnected as part of test step (3)/(4) above and its Keychain tokens were genuinely cleared — the user will see the "Connect a Music Service" chooser on next open and needs to tap "Connect Spotify" once (a normal OAuth re-consent, not a bug) to relink it.

- **2026-07-12** — **Fixed Search (and every other Apple-Music-hardcoded browse/play-gate surface) for Spotify-only accounts; separately discovered and partially worked around a Spotify platform-side API restriction (build-green, 37 tests pass, genuinely verified via a scratch on-device XCUITest — not just build success).** User report: *"search doesn't work when signed into spotify either"* (screenshot: "No results for 'Kanye'"). This was the deliberately-deferred Stage A scope gap ("Search/Shelf/Feed do NOT yet route through Spotify") finally surfacing as a hard blocker now that Spotify-only accounts are possible (see the two entries above). **Root cause, confirmed by grep across the app:** `SearchViewModel`/`SearchView`/`ArtistDetailSearchView`/`AlbumDetailSearchView`/`ShelfViewModel`/`ShelfView`/`NowPlayingView`/`FeedView`/`UserProfileView`/`NearMeUserSheet`/`PlaylistDetailView`/`PartyView`/`ProfileView` all either called catalog methods (`searchAll`/`searchTracks`/`getArtistAlbums`/`getArtistTopTracks`/`getAlbumTracks`) directly on the concrete `AppleMusicService`, or gated play actions on `musicService.isAuthenticated` (Apple-only) — so a Spotify-only account failed not just Search but **every play button across the app**, silently, since these guards return early with no error surfaced. **Fix:** new `Nodalong/Shared/ActiveMusicService.swift` — a tiny free function `activeMusicService(playerVM:apple:spotify:) -> any MusicService` that resolves to whichever backend `PlayerViewModel.activeService` (the established single source of truth since Stage B) actually points at. Every one of the ~13 files above gained a `spotifyService` `@EnvironmentObject` (where missing) + a private `activeService: any MusicService` computed property built on this function, and every hardcoded `musicService.X` call/guard was mechanically replaced with `activeService.X`. This works because `MusicService` is a plain protocol (not `ObservableObject`) — `any MusicService` is fine as a local computed value/function parameter, just not as an `@EnvironmentObject` property, so this is additive, not a rewrite of the injection pattern. `SearchViewModel.search(via:)`/`perform`/`fetchMusic` changed their parameter type from `AppleMusicService` to `any MusicService` (mechanical, since both conformers already implement every method used with identical signatures — confirmed via direct signature grep before making the change). `ProfileView`'s own-profile pull-to-refresh sync also gained a Spotify branch (`syncSpotifyAndWait`, pre-existing function with zero prior callers) alongside the existing Apple-only one, same bug class. **`SearchView.musicAccessNotice`** (the "Apple Music not connected" footer) made service-aware too (shows "Spotify not connected" + a Spotify-authorize button when Spotify is active — Apple's `.isDenied` state stays Apple-specific since Spotify's OAuth has no equivalent).
  **Verification methodology, worth recording since it caught a real bug a build-only check would have missed entirely:** built a scratch XCUITest target (`NodalongUITestsScratch`, added to `project.yml`, fully removed + `xcodegen generate`'d away afterward — zero trace left in the shipped project) that taps Search, types "Kanye", and asserts result sections render. Root-caused two failed runs by launching the app via `simctl launch --console-pty` (captures `print()` stdout — `log stream`/os_log does NOT catch plain `print()`, a real dead-end hit first) and having the XCUITest `app.activate()` an already-running, already-authenticated instance instead of `app.launch()`-relaunching (which would kill the very process being console-captured). This surfaced `[Nodalong.Search] searchAll('Kanye') failed: forbidden` — **the routing fix itself is confirmed correct** (the request now genuinely reaches Spotify's API instead of silently no-op'ing on a false Apple-Music gate), but Spotify's own `/v1/search` is rejecting it with a 403. Also incidentally surfaced, while poking at Profile mid-investigation: this test account has **both** Apple Music and Spotify connected simultaneously (pre-existing state from before the one-service-at-a-time change landed — the exclusivity gate only prevents *new* dual-connects, it doesn't retroactively clean up an account that was already dual-connected under Stage A's old "alongside" model; `activeService` is still correctly `.spotify` so this isn't itself blocking anything, just noted as an untouched pre-existing loose end).
  **Fixed the obvious hypothesis, confirmed insufficient:** removed the deprecated `market=from_token` literal (present on all 8 Spotify search/browse call sites) entirely — Spotify's current API docs no longer document `from_token` as a valid `market` value, and omitting `market` lets Spotify auto-infer the account's market from the token's `user-read-private` scope (already granted). Rebuilt, reran the exact same scratch test against the fixed build: **still 403.** Ruled out as the (sole) cause.
  **Actual cause, found via `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` against Spotify's own developer docs (dated within days of "today" in this session, 2026-07-12):** Spotify shipped a **"February 2026 Web API Dev Mode Changes"** migration — from **2026-03-09**, these restrictions apply to *all existing* Development Mode Client IDs (not just newly-created ones): Development Mode now requires the app-owning account to hold an active **Spotify Premium** subscription (if it lapses, the whole app stops working for every connected user — not just the owner); each Client ID is capped at **5** authorized users; and several endpoints are **removed outright in Development Mode**, including `GET /v1/artists/{id}/top-tracks` (used by `getArtistTopTracks`, artist-detail "Top Songs" — this app already degrades this gracefully via `(try? await ...) ?? []`, so no code change needed there, it'll just always render empty for Spotify). `GET /v1/search` itself is still officially listed as *available* in Development Mode (just with `limit` capped lower — max 10, default 5, down from 50/20), so the plain 403 we're seeing on `/search` specifically doesn't match a "removed endpoint" — it's much more consistent with the **Premium-lapse / unmigrated-Client-ID** failure mode the same migration introduced, which isn't a per-endpoint allowlist thing but an app-wide gate. This Client ID (`19444d59f4a146cabf5eb29269e5c491`) was registered well before this migration (reused from the sibling RN app's older, already-shipped integration per the Stage A entry) and has never been through any Spotify Dashboard "migrate/acknowledge" flow. **This is a Spotify Developer Dashboard / account-configuration issue, not a code bug** — no further code change can route around it. **Concrete next step for the user:** open the Spotify Developer Dashboard for this Client ID, (1) confirm the *owning* Spotify account (whoever registered the app — not the test/connected account) has an active Premium subscription, (2) look for and complete any pending "Development Mode migration" banner/notice, (3) confirm the connected test account is within the new 5-user cap. Once resolved dashboard-side, Search should work immediately with zero further code changes — the routing fix (this entry's main contribution) is the part that was actually broken in our code, and it's confirmed correct by the same test.
  Build green (`xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), no new warnings, 37 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). Scratch UI test target fully removed post-verification — `project.yml` and the repo tree are unchanged from this testing process, only the `Nodalong` target's source files carry real changes.

- **2026-07-12** — **Fixed Spotify playback never actually resolving a track, plus product change: only one music service can be connected at a time (build-green, 37 tests pass).** Plan: `~/.claude/plans/the-next-big-feature-quizzical-treasure.md`. On-device testing of Stage B found zero tracks played via Spotify — every tap threw "Track unavailable... Couldn't find this on Spotify," inconsistently (some tracks, not others). **Root cause, confirmed by full read of `SpotifyMusicService.resolve(_:)` and the Feed→`Song` conversion path:** `Song.from(post:)` (`SongConversions.swift`) never passes `isrc` through — it silently defaults to `nil` — so every Feed-tapped track skipped `resolve()`'s reliable ISRC-bridge step entirely and fell straight to fuzzy title+artist text search, which is why results were inconsistent rather than uniformly broken. Invisible until Stage B shipped: `AppleMusicService.resolve()` never needed the ISRC step for Apple-sourced Feed posts, since its direct-ID step (`song.uri` = a real Apple Music catalog ID) always matched first. **Fix — deliberately not adding `isrc` to `Post`:** the project already reversed exactly this kind of per-post metadata duplication once (see the 2026-07-05 "Track metadata NORMALIZED" entry below) in favor of the shared `tracks/{catalogId}` catalog being the single source of truth. So `resolve(_:)` now does a catalog lookup (`getCatalogTrack(catalogId:)`, existing, no new Firestore function) *before* the id/ISRC/text cascade: if the catalog row already has a `spotifyTrackId` (some other Spotify user resolved this exact ISRC before — Stage A's `upsertCatalogTrack` merges this in), use it directly, skipping search entirely; otherwise backfill `song.isrc` from the catalog's `isrc` so the existing ISRC-search step actually becomes reachable. Also fixed a diagnostic gap while in this code: all three cascade steps were swallowing errors via bare `try?`, so there was no way to tell "genuinely zero results" from a masked HTTP failure — added `print("[Nodalong.SpotifyResolve] ...")` at each step, matching `PlayerViewModel.resolve()`'s existing Apple-side diagnostic convention. Considered and set aside: replacing the `market=from_token` literal with the user's real `country` (available via `/me`, `user-read-private` scope already granted, but currently discarded) — the sibling RN app uses the same literal in its entire shipped Spotify integration with no documented issues, so this wasn't implicated; revisit only if the ISRC fix doesn't fully resolve real-world failures.
  **Second, related change — product decision, not a bug fix:** once Spotify playback was real, the user asked whether the app should enforce one connected service at a time instead of Stage A's "alongside, not exclusive" model, plus a way to disconnect Apple Music (had no UI trigger anywhere until now). Confirmed with the user: Apple Music's "mandatory first connection" was never a deliberate product rule — it's a leftover from when Apple Music was the app's only supported service, pre-Spotify. Shipped: (a) **`ContentView.shouldShowMusicLink`** changed from `musicService.isUndecided` (Apple-Music-specific) to `!musicService.isAuthenticated && !spotifyService.isAuthenticated` ("at least one service connected," not "Apple Music specifically") — this also means the cover reappears automatically if a user disconnects their only remaining service, no extra guard needed. (b) **`AppleMusicLinkView` renamed to `MusicServiceLinkView`** and extended with two buttons (Connect Apple Music / Connect Spotify), dismissing once either succeeds; shows a steering note ("connect Spotify instead") when `musicService.isDenied` is true, since a previously-denied user would otherwise be stuck on a dead-end retry (MusicKit silently re-reports `.denied` with no fresh system prompt). (c) **`ProfileView`** gained a "Disconnect Apple Music" button (mirrors the existing "Disconnect Spotify" one, calls the already-existing `musicService.logout()` — which only flips local state, since MusicKit has no per-app revoke, same documented limitation as before, just newly reachable from the UI). (d) **Confirm-before-switch, both directions:** tapping "Connect X" while the other service is connected shows an alert ("Connecting Spotify will disconnect Apple Music. Continue?") before proceeding; on confirm, the new service is connected **first**, and the old one is only disconnected *after* the new one actually succeeds (`isAuthenticated` flips true) — avoids stranding a user with zero connected services if they cancel the system auth prompt partway through. (e) **Removed `ProfileView.activeServiceSwitcher`** (the segmented Picker) — with exclusivity enforced, "connected service" and "active service" are always the same thing, so a manual switcher can never have two real options anymore. `ContentView`'s Spotify `.onChange` block now also auto-calls `setActiveService(uid:service:.spotify)` (mirroring the existing Apple Music auto-set), which was deliberately *not* done in Stage A — now correct and safe, since connecting Spotify always means Apple Music was just disconnected in the same action. Build green (`xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), no new warnings beyond the pre-existing `SpotifyMusicService`/`@MainActor` conformance pattern, 37 NodalongKit tests pass. **On-device unverified** — both the resolve fix and the exclusivity/disconnect flow need a real device pass.
  **Immediate on-device follow-up, same day:** a user who connected *only* Spotify (no Apple Music) still couldn't play anything at all. Root cause: the two `.onChange` blocks above (item e) only wrote the new `activeService` to Firestore via `setActiveService(...)` and never called `authVM.refreshProfile()` afterward, so the local `authVM.profile` object never actually picked up the change — meaning `ContentView`'s `.onChange(of: authVM.profile?.activeService)` (the *only* thing that ever set `playerVM.activeService`) never fired, and `playerVM.activeService` stayed silently stuck at its `.appleMusic` default forever. Every tap-to-play was therefore trying Apple Music — a service that specific user had never connected — instead of Spotify. Fixed by having both `.onChange` blocks set `playerVM.activeService` directly and immediately (no longer dependent on the Firestore-write → profile-refresh → onChange round-trip at all for the routing decision itself); `authVM.refreshProfile()` was added too so `authVM.profile?.activeService` stays correct as a secondary source of truth (e.g. what `.task` reads on next launch). Build green, no NodalongKit changes.

- **2026-07-11** — **Spotify integration Stage B shipped: real playback via pure Web API remote control (build-green, 37 tests pass).** Plan: `~/.claude/plans/the-next-big-feature-quizzical-treasure.md`. On-device testing of Stage A surfaced the gap Stage A always flagged as deferred: switching "Active Service" to Spotify in Profile did nothing to what actually played — every tap-to-play still went through `AppleMusicService`/`ApplicationMusicPlayer`. Root cause confirmed by full read of `PlayerViewModel.swift`: it's a monolithic Apple Music engine (every method calls `ApplicationMusicPlayer`/`AVPlayer` directly), and `MusicService.play/resume/pause` were dead code on **both** services — zero protocol dispatch existed anywhere. **The redirect-URI OAuth bug found during this on-device pass, fixed separately and worth remembering:** Spotify's Dashboard now rejects a bare `scheme://` redirect URI with nothing after it — the RN app had originally registered exactly `com.bigdeccyh.nodalong://`, which Spotify's current validation flags "not valid," even though it worked when first registered. Fixed by switching `SpotifyMusicService.redirectURI` to `com.bigdeccyh.nodalong://callback` (the other URI already on the same Client ID's Dashboard entry, which Spotify does accept). Also added a `GET /v1/me` call (`fetchDisplayName()`) so Profile shows "Connected as {real Spotify name}" instead of a generic "Connected to Spotify" — concrete proof of which account is linked, prompted by the user asking how to confirm Spotify (vs. Apple Music) was really what got connected.
  **The core architecture question (already locked before Stage A, re-confirmed rather than re-litigated here):** Spotify's Web API is remote control of an *already-active* Spotify Connect device — almost always the real Spotify app, running somewhere. There is no in-process Spotify audio. Staying with pure Web API remote control (not the Web Playback SDK, whose Control-Center-branding landmine was flagged when Stage A shipped) means **Nodalong does not own the lock screen for Spotify-active playback** — the real Spotify app does, since it's the one actually outputting audio. This directly conflicts with CLAUDE.md §2's "lock-screen + Control Center show Nodalong's brand" rule, and there is no fix under this architecture (writing our own `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` would be misleading, since iOS wouldn't treat Nodalong as the now-playing app anyway). Flagged loudly in the plan and accepted as an unavoidable, scoped trade-off — mitigated with an honest in-app "Playing via Spotify" indicator instead of pretending otherwise.
  **Shipped:** (a) `SpotifyMusicService` un-stubbed: real `play(_:)`/`resume()`/`pause()`, a private `resolve(_:)` id→ISRC→title/artist cascade (deliberately *not* built in Stage A since it had no caller then — now does), `seekSpotify(toPositionMs:)`, `fetchPlaybackState()` (wraps `GET /me/player` — the poll target, since Spotify's API has no push/callback mechanism), and `ensureActiveDevice()` (checks for an already-active device via `GET /me/player`, else lists `GET /me/player/devices` and auto-picks the first, transferring via `PUT /me/player` with `play: false`; throws new `.noActiveDevice` if the device list is empty — no picker UI for v1). `play(_:)` always sends a single-URI list (`PUT /me/player/play` body `{"uris": [...]}`), never a `context_uri`, mirroring `PlayerViewModel`'s existing "one song loaded at a time, our queue is authoritative" model exactly — so Spotify's device stops after the one track instead of auto-advancing into an unrelated context. Added `user-modify-playback-state`/`user-read-playback-state` scopes — **since Spotify scopes are locked at consent time, any already-linked account (including the one used for on-device testing) needs to disconnect + reconnect once this ships.** Refactored the retry engine (`performRequest` → `sendWithRetry` returning raw `Data`) so both JSON-decoding calls and 204-No-Content playback commands (`authenticatedCommand`) share the identical 401/429/502/503 retry contract instead of duplicating it.
  (b) `PlayerViewModel` gained `@Published var activeService: MusicServiceType` + `weak var spotifyService: SpotifyMusicService?` (set via a new `configure(spotifyService:)`, called once from `ContentView` — `PlayerViewModel` is constructed with no init params so this is the wiring seam) — **a branch, not a protocol-dispatch rewrite**: `playCurrent()` gains one top-of-function check (`if activeService == .spotify { await playCurrentViaSpotify(...); return }`), leaving the existing Apple Music path byte-for-byte unchanged for the default case. New `playCurrentViaSpotify`/`startSpotifyPollTimer`/`pollSpotifyPlaybackState` mirror the existing MusicKit poll's shape but at a coarser ~3s cadence (Spotify's Web API has no finer push mechanism — accepted v1 trade-off, the scrubber visibly steps rather than sweeps). **`.noActiveDevice` deliberately does NOT trigger `skipUnplayableAndAdvance`** — unlike a single bad track, "no device" applies to every remaining queued track equally, so skip-and-retry would just burn through the whole queue instantly with the same error; instead it stops cleanly and sets a new persistent `@Published spotifyPlaybackError` (distinct from the existing per-track `trackUnavailable`). `togglePlayPause()`/`seek(to:)`/`stopBackends()`/`dismiss()` all got the same `activeService == .spotify` branch calling through to `spotifyService` instead of `ApplicationMusicPlayer`. Deliberately does **not** call `MPNowPlayingInfoCenter` writes for Spotify (per the branding trade-off above) and does **not** build a Spotify-side `prefetchNext` cache (scope decision — v2 nice-to-have, not required for correctness).
  (c) UI: `MiniPlayerBar` gets a small green dot badge on the artwork when `activeService == .spotify`; `NowPlayingView` gets an explicit "● Playing via Spotify" label above the title (same rationale — the OS surface won't say it, so the in-app UI has to); `PlaybackErrorAlert` gained a second `.alert` bound to `spotifyPlaybackError` (distinct title "Nothing to play" vs. the existing "Track unavailable"/"Subscription required" alert). Fixed a small pre-existing bug noticed while in this code: `NowPlayingView`'s heart button hardcoded `sourceService: .appleMusic` regardless of which service was actually playing — now reads `playerVM.activeService`.
  (d) `ContentView` wires `playerVM.configure(spotifyService:)` once and syncs `playerVM.activeService` from `authVM.profile?.activeService` via both an initial `.task` read and an `.onChange` for later switcher taps. `ProfileView`'s active-service switcher gained a small caption ("Spotify playback needs the Spotify app open...") shown only when Spotify is selected, so the device requirement isn't a cold surprise.
  Build green (`xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), no new warnings beyond the pre-existing `@MainActor`/`MusicService` conformance pattern, 37 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit changes this pass). **On-device unverified for the actual playback path** — requires reconnecting Spotify (new scopes) and testing with a real active Spotify Connect device; the redirect-URI fix and display-name confirmation feature described above *were* verified on-device as part of diagnosing the original OAuth failure.

- **2026-07-11** — **Spotify integration Stage A shipped (auth + search/catalog; playback deferred to Stage B) — build-green, 37 tests pass.** Plan: `~/.claude/plans/the-next-big-feature-quizzical-treasure.md`. First raw HTTP+JSON+OAuth client in the app (`AppleMusicService` uses MusicKit, not URLSession). Mined a sibling, already-shipped React Native Spotify integration for provably-correct implementation details before writing any code (see plan file for the full list) — the two concrete, non-obvious facts worth remembering: (1) Spotify's token exchange/refresh body is `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`, not JSON; (2) a refresh response can legitimately **omit** `refresh_token` — the existing Keychain value must be kept, not overwritten with nothing, or the session gets stranded on the next refresh. **Shipped:** (a) `KeychainHelper.swift` (new, generic `Security`-framework wrapper, no dependency) — built earlier this session. (b) `CatalogTrack`/`CatalogTrackInput` gained additive `spotifyUri`/`spotifyTrackId` fields (`NodalongKit`), mirroring the existing Apple fields; `CatalogTrackInput`'s Apple fields became optional too so a Spotify-only ingest doesn't fabricate Apple identifiers. (c) **`Catalog.swift:upsertCatalogTrack` rewritten around a Firestore transaction** — the RN app's own code review flagged (and never fixed) a real race in its equivalent function: a plain `whereField("isrc",...)` query followed by a separate write means two concurrent upserts for a brand-new ISRC can both miss the lookup and both insert, orphaning a duplicate catalog row. Firestore transactions can only read/write *known document references*, not arbitrary queries, so closing this required a new deterministic secondary index collection, `isrc_index/{sanitizedIsrc} → {catalogId}` (added to `firestore.rules`, same trust level as `tracks`) — the transaction does `getDocument` on the index ref; if absent, it creates both the new catalog row AND the index entry atomically in the same transaction; Firestore's automatic retry-on-contention means the race loser sees the winner's index doc on retry and returns the same id instead of creating a second row. Existing catalog doc IDs are untouched (no migration) — this only closes the race for *future* first-inserts. (d) **`SpotifyMusicService.swift`** (new, `@MainActor final class ... ObservableObject, MusicService`, mirrors `AppleMusicService`'s structure/doc-comment conventions exactly): hand-rolled PKCE (`SecRandomCopyBytes`-backed 128-char `code_verifier`, `CryptoKit.SHA256` + base64url `code_challenge`, no library exists for this in plain Swift), `ASWebAuthenticationSession` (+ a separate tiny `NSObject` presentation-context-provider, not the whole service — both held as stored properties for the flow's duration, the standard "ARC deallocates before the completion handler fires" gotcha), a single `performRequest` retry chokepoint implementing the RN-confirmed exact contract (401→refresh+retry once; 429→parse `Retry-After`, wait, retry once; 502/503→2 retries at 1s/2s), a one-shot `Task`-based proactive-refresh timer (~60s before expiry, re-armed on relaunch from Keychain state, no in-flight-dedup mutex — matches the RN app's own accepted simplification), file-private `Decodable` DTOs + `.convertFromSnakeCase` for the JSON↔`Song`/`ArtistResult`/`AlbumResult` mapping, and stubbed playback (`throw .notImplemented` — inert since `MusicService.play/resume/pause` are already dead code for Apple Music too). Deliberately did NOT include a `resolve(_:)` id→ISRC→title/artist cascade (zero callers until Stage B builds real playback — dead code the project's own conventions warn against); a `// TODO(Stage B)` comment marks the spot. Client ID/redirect URI reused from the RN app's existing Spotify Dashboard registration (`com.bigdeccyh.nodalong://` already whitelisted, same bundle id) — scopes deliberately narrow (`user-read-private`/`user-library-read`/`user-read-recently-played`, no playback scopes yet). (e) **`LibrarySyncService`** gained a parallel `syncSpotify`/`syncSpotifyAndWait` path (NOT a generalization of the existing Apple `sync`/`runSync` — the Apple path calls an Apple-only artwork-repair step Spotify doesn't need, since Spotify's `images[].url` is always a fetchable `https` URL already); shares the `isSyncing`/`task` guard with the Apple path (sequential, not concurrent — matches "one active service at a time"). Wired from `ContentView`'s existing `.onChange(of: musicService.isAuthenticated)` pattern, mirrored for `spotifyService.isAuthenticated` — **deliberately does NOT** also auto-set `activeService` the way the Apple case does (a product judgment call: Spotify is optional/secondary, so linking it shouldn't silently switch away from whichever service the user already had active; the new Profile switcher is how the user makes that choice explicitly). (f) **`ProfileView`**'s Apple-only "Music" section renamed "Music Services" and extended with a parallel Spotify status row + connect/disconnect button (mirrors the Apple rows' visual style) + an `activeServiceSwitcher` (segmented picker, shown only once both services are linked) that's `setActiveService(uid:service:)`'s first real reader/writer — previously a fully wired but zero-call-site function. (g) `NodalongApp.swift` gained `@StateObject spotifyService` injected the same way as `musicService`; `Nodalong/Info.plist` + `project.yml`'s `info.properties` both gained `CFBundleURLTypes`/`CFBundleURLSchemes = com.bigdeccyh.nodalong` (hard prerequisite for `ASWebAuthenticationSession`'s redirect to be caught — without it `authorize()` compiles and runs but the callback never fires). `PreviewFixtures.withPreviewEnvironment()` gained `SpotifyMusicService.mockPreview`. **Explicit Stage B landmine, recorded now while fresh:** the RN app's Spotify playback used the Web Playback SDK inside a hidden WebView, and its own `BUGS.md` documents that iOS Control Center shows "Spotify Embedded Player" instead of the real track — a native-SDK behavior that bypasses the WebView's JS layer entirely and isn't fixable from the app's side, directly conflicting with this app's own branding rule (§2: "lock-screen + Control Center show Nodalong's brand"). Whoever plans Stage B should evaluate the Web Playback SDK route with this in mind rather than assuming it's a strict upgrade over pure Web API remote-control. Build green (`xcodegen generate` + isolated `-derivedDataPath` `xcodebuild`), one pre-existing-pattern warning (`SpotifyMusicService:20` MusicService/`@MainActor` conformance — identical warning `AppleMusicService` already has, not a regression), 37 NodalongKit tests pass. **On-device unverified** — requires confirming the Spotify Dashboard entry (Client ID `19444d59f4a146cabf5eb29269e5c491`) is still active and its redirect URI still whitelisted before auth can be tested end-to-end; `firestore.rules`' new `isrc_index` collection also needs deploying (not done as part of this session — rule deploys are an external, hard-to-reverse action). **Not done (deliberately, per Stage A's scope boundary):** Search/Shelf/Feed do not route through Spotify when it's active, and tapping "play" anywhere does not yet play via Spotify — that's Stage B (a `SpotifyPlayerViewModel` + facade), a separate future plan.

- **2026-07-11** — **Fixed party broadcast/reconcile silently stopping when the host minimizes the party screen, plus "Up Next" showing already-played tracks (build-green).** On-device repro: host's screen correctly showed "You Said" playing; the guest's screen was stuck on "Radio" (an earlier track), and both screens' "Up Next" showed already-played tracks (checkmarked) mixed in with genuinely upcoming ones. Root cause, confirmed by direct read of both files (both authored this session): the host→guest RTDB broadcast (`hostBroadcast`) and the local-queue reconcile (`reconcileQueue`) were BOTH wired as `.onChange` modifiers inside `PartyView`'s body — which only fire while that screen is actually mounted. But the party's own chevron-down explicitly lets the host minimize the screen and keep hosting in the background (the class doc on `ListeningPartyViewModel` says this outright: "a DJ host legitimately keeps hosting while backgrounded"). The moment a host does that, both mechanisms silently stopped: the RTDB `nowPlaying` pointer froze at its last value (explaining the guest's stale "Radio"), and — since `hostBroadcast` is also the ONLY place a queue track gets marked `played = true` — the currently-playing track never got flagged either, which is exactly what the screenshots showed. **Fix:** moved both triggers off the View and onto `ListeningPartyViewModel`'s own session lifecycle, using the identical Combine-subscription pattern already proven this session in `NowPlayingBroadcastService` — `enter(party:me:playerVM:)` now establishes (host-only, gated at subscription-*setup* time so a guest's `enter()` never touches their own unrelated local playback at all) `Publishers.CombineLatest(playerVM.$nowPlaying, playerVM.$isPlaying) → hostBroadcast` and `$queue → playerVM.reconcileQueue`, stored in a new `cancellables: Set<AnyCancellable>`, torn down in both `leave()` and `endPartyAndFreeze()`. `createAndEnter`/`joinAndEnter`/`enter` all gained a `playerVM: PlayerViewModel` parameter, threaded through the three call sites (`StartPartySheet` — needed a new `playerVM` env object — plus the existing `FeedView`/`NearMeUserSheet` join-banner calls, which already had it in scope). `PartyView` lost the three now-redundant `.onChange` modifiers and the dead `broadcastIfHost()`/`reconcileIfHost()` helpers; `startPlayback()`'s one-time post-"Play Queue" `reconcileQueue` hand-off call is unaffected (the new subscription only reacts to *future* Firestore queue changes, not the moment `playerVM.queue` is first populated). **Second, independent fix:** "Up Next" rendered `partyVM.queue` completely unfiltered (played + unplayed together) — new `ListeningPartyViewModel.upcomingQueue` computed property (`queue.filter { !$0.played }`) is what `PartyView`'s list/empty-state/`.onMove`/`.onDelete` now target instead. Since the currently-playing track is marked `played` the instant it starts, this also naturally excludes it from "Up Next" (correct — it's already shown in the "Now Playing" hero). This required redesigning `moveQueueTrack`: since "Up Next" now displays a FILTERED subsequence, `.onMove`'s offsets are relative to that subsequence, not the full `queue` — feeding them as if they were full-array indices (the old implementation) would move/reorder the wrong item. Rewrote it to perform the move within the `upcomingQueue` subsequence and compute the new `order` from the adjacent UNPLAYED neighbours only — still a globally-correct position, since a midpoint between two tracks' order values sorts correctly regardless of what other (played, earlier-order) tracks sit elsewhere in the space; `deleteHandler` similarly maps filtered offsets to `upcomingQueue[$0]` (a track object) rather than indexing into the full `queue`. Build green (isolated `-derivedDataPath`), no new warnings, 37 tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). **On-device unverified.**

- **2026-07-10** — **Fixed zombie live parties — a `status: live` party doc that could get stuck forever, un-endable, and kept surfacing to other users (build-green).** On-device repro: host started a new party ("LP Test 1"); a second account joining via NearMe/Feed got routed into a completely unrelated, older party ("LP 1") from an earlier abandoned test session. Root cause (traced directly, not guessed) — exactly the "orphan" risk flagged as an accepted-for-v1 risk when the party feature shipped, now actually manifesting: (1) `createParty`/`createAndEnter` never checked for an existing live party before creating a new one — nothing prevented accumulation; (2) `endParty` only wrote `status: .ended` as its LAST statement, after the playlist-freeze work — if `getPartyQueueOnce`/`freezePlaylist` threw for any reason, the whole function threw and the status write never ran, leaving the doc stuck `.live` forever; (3) `ListeningPartyViewModel` never persists/restores a party session across an app relaunch (unlike `PlayerViewModel`, which does restore its queue) — so ANY relaunch after a crash/force-quit mid-party left that party's doc orphaned with zero client anywhere still tracking it, hence genuinely un-endable through the UI; (4) `getLivePartyHostedBy(uid:)` (added earlier this session for NearMe) had no ordering, so with multiple live docs for one host, which one a joiner got routed to was arbitrary — this is precisely why the simulator landed in the wrong, older party. **Fix, all in `Nodalong/API/Firestore/Parties.swift`:** `endParty` now isolates just the freeze attempt (queue fetch + `freezePlaylist`) in its own `do/catch` — the `status: .ended`/`endedAt`/`resultPlaylistId` write now always runs regardless, falling back to `resultPlaylistId: nil` on a freeze failure instead of never running at all. Added a re-fetch-and-no-op guard at the top of `endParty` (bail early, returning the existing `resultPlaylistId`, if the doc's `status` is no longer `.live`) — needed once cleanup becomes automatic from multiple places, to prevent two near-simultaneous callers from double-freezing the same queue into two playlists (this race genuinely didn't exist before, when "End Party" was the only caller). New `endOrphanedLiveParties(hostUid:)` — finds all of a uid's own live parties (reuses the already-correct `getLivePartiesHostedBy`) and best-effort `endParty`s each. Wired at two points: `ContentView.swift`'s existing auth-ready `.task` block (this is what actually self-heals the currently-stuck data — the next time the host's app launches, any of their own still-`.live` docs get cleaned up, safe by construction since the party VM never restores a session across relaunch, so nothing here is ever a legitimately-resumable session), and the top of `ListeningPartyViewModel.createAndEnter` (defense in depth for same-session double-starts). `getLivePartyHostedBy` fixed to be deterministic WITHOUT a new Firestore composite index: dropped the unordered `.limit(to: 1)`, now fetches all matches for the same (hostUid, status) equality-only query shape `getLivePartiesHostedBy` already uses successfully, and picks the newest client-side via `max(by: createdAt)`. Build green (isolated `-derivedDataPath`), no new warnings, 37 tests pass (no NodalongKit changes). **On-device unverified** — the fix should self-resolve the user's exact stuck state on their next app launch/reopen, but that hasn't been confirmed yet.

- **2026-07-10** — **NearMe: shows a nearby user's live "Now Playing" + a "Join Party" banner if they're hosting (build-green).** Plan: `~/.claude/plans/the-next-big-feature-quizzical-treasure.md`. Root problem: no live "currently playing" signal exists anywhere for a regular user — `PlayerViewModel.nowPlaying` is purely local, and `recent_tracks` only syncs on app-launch/foreground-return (could be hours stale). **User's framing, adopted:** treat solo playback as "a live party of one" — reuse the party's proven RTDB-presence *pattern* as a lightweight per-user entry, not a full Firestore `ListeningParty` doc per casual play (would mean a Firestore write/lifecycle per track, for every user, constantly). **Decisions locked:** party detection scoped to **hosting only** (not also "joined someone else's as a guest" — no reverse presence index exists, and in the DJ model only the host's device makes sound anyway); join gating = **anyone nearby, no relationship check** (matches the party feature's actual — unenforced — reality today; a code comment claims "friend-gated" but nothing in `joinAndEnter()` checks it). **Shipped:** new `UserNowPlaying` model (NodalongKit — deliberately leaner than `PartyNowPlaying`: just `catalogId/isPlaying/updatedAt`, no `positionMs`; stores only `catalogId` per the established normalization pattern, enriched via `CatalogCache` on read). New `Nodalong/Services/UserPresenceService.swift` — RTDB `users_live/{uid}/nowPlaying`, same shape/guard style as `PartyLiveService` (`isAvailable` gate, `onDisconnectRemoveValue()` so a killed app doesn't leave a stale "still playing" ghost). New `Nodalong/Services/NowPlayingBroadcastService.swift` — the bridge: subscribes to `PlayerViewModel`'s already-public `$nowPlaying`/`$isPlaying` via Combine and mirrors to RTDB, **without adding any social/party awareness to `PlayerViewModel` itself** (same "keep the player generic" principle applied to the earlier party bridge). Injected app-wide in `NodalongApp`, started once from `ContentView`'s existing cross-cutting-side-effects `.task` (added `playerVM` + the new service as `ContentView` env objects — `ContentView` didn't have `playerVM` before). Because party playback flows through the same `PlayerViewModel`, a hosting user's device broadcasts to **both** `users_live/{uid}` (this feature) and the existing `parties_live/{partyId}` (party-specific) automatically, no extra wiring. New single-uid `getLivePartyHostedBy(uid:)` in `Parties.swift` (today's `getLivePartiesHostedBy` is batch-only). `NearMeUserSheet` — added `partyVM` env object, a sheet-scoped live RTDB observer (own `UserPresenceService` instance, `.onDisappear { presence.detachAll() }` — NOT app-wide, matches NearMe's "load while viewing" philosophy), a "NOW PLAYING" row (shown only while `isPlaying == true`; paused-but-loaded reads as "not playing" — simplest interpretation) placed right under identity/follow, and a "Join Party" banner beneath it when hosting (mirrors `FeedView.partyBannerButton` styling exactly, calls the existing unchanged `joinAndEnter`). New RTDB rule (`database.rules.json`, `users_live/{uid}/nowPlaying` self-write, any-signed-in-user read) — **must be re-published**, same as the party rules. Build green (isolated `-derivedDataPath`), no new warnings, 37 NodalongKit tests pass (no NodalongKit logic changes beyond the new model). **Known v1 trade-offs (flagged, accepted):** no opt-out for broadcasting to nearby strangers (matches NearMe's existing no-opt-out norm for bio/recents/spotlight); a new steady per-play RTDB write across the whole active user base, not just during parties; no live indicator on the NearMe map avatars themselves, sheet only. **On-device unverified** (RTDB + two-device test needed).

- **2026-07-10** — **Party feedback fixes: unified queue, lobby redesign, tappable rows, auto-advance bug, freeze surfacing (build-green).** Five on-device-feedback items after the first live-party run. Plan: `~/.claude/plans/the-next-big-feature-quizzical-treasure.md`; two Plan agents pressure-tested the `PlayerViewModel` changes. **Core decision (user-confirmed): ONE unified queue** — the party's Firestore queue is the single source of truth; the host's local `PlayerViewModel.queue` is kept **reconciled** to it live (not a one-time snapshot), and host reorder/remove write **directly to the party queue**. **(1) Queue button → Party screen** (`NowPlayingView`): when `partyVM.isActive`, the top-right queue button dismisses the player cover then presents the party cover (dismiss-then-present, not nested); else the normal `QueueView` sheet. **(2/3/reorder) `PartyView` rebuilt as a `List`** (mirrors `PlaylistDetailView`'s List + `EditButton`-style toggle + optional `moveHandler`/`deleteHandler` + `.environment(\.editMode,…)`): prominent **Now Playing hero** (large artwork `min(width−80,360)` + `.title3.bold()` title + tappable artist), participants/notice/host-controls as header rows, queue as rows with **host-only `.onMove`** (→ new `partyVM.moveQueueTrack`, reimplements `Array.move` with plain ops since the VM doesn't import SwiftUI, mirroring `PlayerViewModel.moveUpNext`) and **`.onDelete`** (→ existing `removeFromQueue`). **Tappable artist nav** reuses `NowPlayingView`'s `NavTarget`/`loadArtist(searchAll)`/`.sheet(item:)` verbatim. New `reorderPartyQueueTrack` in `Parties.swift` mirrors `reorderPlaylistTrack`. **(4) Auto-advance bug** — root cause: host "Play Queue" loaded a one-time snapshot into `playerVM.queue` with no bridge back from the ever-growing `partyVM.queue`, so a song added mid-playback never played. Fixed with **`PlayerViewModel.reconcileQueue(with:)`** — anchors on **`song.id`** (the Firestore queue-doc id, from `Song.from(partyQueueTrack:)`, so a track queued twice anchors on the right occurrence), preserves the currently-playing track, rebuilds only the upcoming portion (`currentIndex = 0`), equality short-circuits to avoid `@Published` churn on played-flag flips, sets **`@Published isPartyDriven`** (hides "Play Queue" once reconcile owns the queue; cleared on any explicit `play(_:)`/`play(_:startingAt:)`/`dismiss()`). Wired host-only via `.onChange(of: partyVM.queue)`; bootstrap = "Play Queue" (`play(_ songs:startingAt:)`) then an immediate `reconcileQueue` call. Race-safety: a **`pendingReconcile` stash + `drainPendingReconcile()`** (drained where `skipUnplayableAndAdvance` releases `isAdvancing`) so a Firestore snapshot arriving mid-retry-chain can't corrupt `currentIndex` and isn't dropped. **(4b) Resolve-skip resilience (app-wide, user opt-in):** `playCurrent()` gained `attempt: Int = 1`; both failure branches (resolve throws; both playback paths throw) now call `skipUnplayableAndAdvance` (bounded `attempt < queue.count`, reuses `isAdvancing`) which skips the bad track with a "Skipped (unavailable)" toast instead of hard-stopping — a fully-broken queue still terminates to `trackUnavailable`; single-track taps unchanged in outcome. New `queueToastKind (.added/.skipped)` on `PlayerViewModel` + `flashQueueToast(_:kind:)`; `BottomSection` picks label/icon from it. **(5) Freeze surfacing** — the playlist WAS created (host always in `contributorUids`, played tracks stay in queue) but never shown: `endPartyAndFreeze()` swallowed errors (`try?`), discarded the playlist id, and `ProfileView`'s `hasLoaded` guard left the rail stale. Now: `do/catch` (surfaces `errorMessage`), fetches the created playlist into **`@Published endedPlaylist`**, and tears down the live layer while KEEPING `party` set + `isActive=false` — which `PartyView` detects (`!isActive && party != nil`) to show a **"Party ended" summary** (playlist cover/title/count + "View Playlist" → `PlaylistDetailView` sheet + "Done" → `dismissEndedSummary`); `PartyView` also calls `playlistsVM.reload(uid:)` so the Profile rail refreshes without a manual pull. Empty-queue party → nil playlist → graceful "no playlist" summary. Reactivity bug caught in review: `isPartyDriven` had to be `@Published` (plain `private(set) var` wouldn't re-render the view). Build green in isolated `-derivedDataPath` (avoids the Xcode build-DB lock hit earlier); no new warnings. **Known v1 gaps (accepted):** "previous" degrades to "restart current" during a party (consequence of `currentIndex=0` on reconcile); guest doesn't auto-close when host ends (pre-existing Phase-2 gap); remote participants get no explicit skip signal. **On-device unverified** (RTDB + auth-gated).

- **2026-07-10** — **"Add to Party Queue" wired into the app-wide long-press menu — no longer confined to the in-party search sheet.** User feedback on the just-shipped party feature: you should be able to add a track to the party from anywhere (Feed, Search, Shelf, Profile, Playlists), not just via `PartyAddSheet`; and the existing long-press "Add to Queue" action should read **"Add to Party Queue"** and route there while a party is active. Implemented entirely inside the shared `queueContextMenu` modifier (`Nodalong/Shared/UI/QueueContextMenu.swift`) — **zero call-site changes needed**, because every call site already passes a `song:` (wired for Add-to-Playlist). `QueueLongPressMenu` now reads `@EnvironmentObject var partyVM: ListeningPartyViewModel` (already available everywhere via `MainTabView`'s app-wide injection) and computes `routeToParty = partyVM.isActive && partyVM.canAddToQueue && song != nil`; when true the dialog shows "Add to Party Queue" → `partyVM.addToQueue(song)` instead of the personal "Add to Queue" → `action()`. Falls back to the personal queue when no party is active, or when this device isn't allowed to add (guest in an `.ownerOnly` party) — never silently drops the tap. Added a `queueToast`/`flashQueueToast` pair to `ListeningPartyViewModel` mirroring `PlayerViewModel`'s existing toast, and `BottomSection` now renders whichever toast is set ("Added to party queue" with a radio-waves icon, vs. "Added to queue") — `queueToastPill` parametrized to take `label`/`icon` instead of hardcoding "Added to queue". Build green (verified in an isolated `-derivedDataPath` to avoid colliding with the user's live Xcode build DB — a real lock hit earlier this session), 37 tests pass.

- **2026-07-09** — **Live Party Phase 2 built & build-green (DJ model).** The listening party from the plan (`~/.claude/plans/the-next-big-feature-quizzical-treasure.md`). **⚠️ SETUP PREREQUISITE:** Realtime Database is NOT provisioned — `GoogleService-Info.plist` has no `DATABASE_URL`. The app builds & won't crash (`PartyLiveService` guards on it), but presence + now-playing won't function until the user **enables Realtime Database in the Firebase console and drops in the updated plist**. Rules files also need deploying. **What shipped:** **Models** (`NodalongKit/.../Models/`) `ListeningParty` (Firestore `parties/{id}`: host, title, `status .live/.ended`, `permission .anyoneCanAdd/.ownerOnly`, `resultPlaylistId`), `PartyQueueTrack` (slim auto-ID doc mirroring `PlaylistTrack` + `played` flag; overlay via `merged(withCatalog:)`), `PartyParticipant` + `PartyNowPlaying` (RTDB-parsed value types). **Firestore** `Nodalong/API/Firestore/Parties.swift` — `createParty`, `getParty`, `getLivePartiesHostedBy(uids:)` (discovery, chunked `in`), `addToPartyQueue` (ensures catalog via shared `ensureCatalogId(for:Song)` now hoisted to `Catalog.swift` — removed the private dupe from `Playlists.swift`), `markPartyQueueTrackPlayed`, `removeFromPartyQueue`, `endParty` → `freezePlaylist` (copies queue → a new `playlists/{id}` `source: .party`, `contributorUids` = host + distinct queue adders, sets cover/trackCount), and the app's **first Firestore snapshot listeners** `listenToParty`/`listenToPartyQueue` (return `ListenerRegistration`). **RTDB** `Nodalong/Services/PartyLiveService.swift` (`FirebaseDatabase`, added to `project.yml`) — `parties_live/{id}`: `presence/{uid}` with `onDisconnect().removeValue()` (fixes ghost members), host-written `nowPlaying` pointer (display-only), `hostUid` mirror (so RTDB rules can gate host-only writes — RTDB can't read Firestore), `detachAll()`/`leavePresence()`/`clearLive()`. `isAvailable` guards everything when no DATABASE_URL. **VM** `ListeningPartyViewModel` (app-wide, injected in `MainTabView` like `playlistsVM`) — owns the 2 Firestore listeners + RTDB handles; `enter`/`leave` (leave detaches everything + removes presence), `presenting` flag = cover shown (session outlives it → minimize, not leave), `createAndEnter`/`joinAndEnter`/`reopen`, `addToQueue` (permission-gated), `hostBroadcast`, `endPartyAndFreeze`. **Listener-lifecycle decision (deviation from plan, documented in-code):** detach on explicit leave / `.onDisappear` only, NOT on scenePhase background — these are event-driven listeners (cheap idle), unlike the NearMe 60fps *timer*, and a DJ **host must keep hosting while backgrounded** (`UIBackgroundModes: audio`). **UI** `Nodalong/Features/Party/PartyView.swift` — full-screen cover (2nd `fullScreenCover` on `MainTabView`, after the player one): participants row, now-playing card (host reads its own `PlayerViewModel`, remotes read the shared pointer), host "Play Queue" (feeds `playerVM.play(_ songs:startingAt:)` from first unplayed), permission-aware "Add", queue list (played dimmed, host remove), End→freeze / Leave; `StartPartySheet` (title + permission); `PartyAddSheet` (in-party `musicService.searchTracks` → add). **Host↔player bridge** kept additive: `.onChange(of: playerVM.nowPlaying?.catalogId / .isPlaying)` → `hostBroadcast` (writes RTDB nowPlaying + marks queue track played). **Discovery** in `FeedView` — `party.popper` toolbar button (start, or reopen if active) + a top `safeAreaInset` banner (return to your party, else a followed user's live party → join); fetches via `getFollowingList` + `getLivePartiesHostedBy`. **Security rules (NEW, must deploy)** — `firestore.rules` (covers EVERY collection since deploy replaces all: host-only party doc, permission+live-gated queue create, host-only queue update/delete, owner-only playlist + tracks, owner-scoped user subcollections, follower-self-write, shared `tracks`/`nod_records` open to signed-in; **v1 relaxation:** playlist reads are signed-in-only, visibility enforced client-side, because visibility-gated reads break the `contributorUids array-contains` queries), `database.rules.json` (presence self-write, host-only `nowPlaying` via `hostUid` mirror), `firebase.json`. `Song.from(partyQueueTrack:)` added to `SongConversions`. `xcodegen` + `xcodebuild` green; 37 tests pass. **NOT done / v1 gaps:** runtime unverified (RTDB not provisioned + auth-gated); host-leave reconciliation of an orphaned `.live` party is minimal; new tracks added mid-session don't auto-append to the host's in-memory player queue (host re-taps Play); no position/drift sync (deferred Phase 3 with Spotify); app-wide "Add to Party" long-press deferred (add via in-party sheet for now). **Phase 3 remains:** `SpotifyMusicService` + multi-device synced playback.
