- **2026-07-09** — **Shelf save bug fixed: tracks saved from Search/Album showed as "Singles" by "Unknown Artist" with blank art.** Root cause (traced with two Explore agents): the Shelf save path was the **only** ingestion path that never ensured a `tracks/{catalogId}` catalog row. `AppleMusicService.toSong()` never sets `catalogId` (it's a Firestore concept), so Search/Album `Song`s have `catalogId = nil`; the save sites then keyed the slim library doc by `track.catalogId ?? track.uri` — falling back to the **Apple Music id**. `batchUpsertLibraryTracks`/`upsertLibraryTrack` write slim docs and never call `upsertCatalogTrack`, so `tracks/{appleMusicId}` never exists → read-time `enrichLibrary` overlay finds nothing → `merged(withCatalog: nil)` no-op leaves title/artist/album/artwork `""` → `groupByAlbum`/`groupByArtist` bucket empty-album→"Singles", empty-artist→"Unknown Artist" (`GroupByAlbum.swift:38,67`). **Intermittent** because Feed/Profile saves carry a real `post.catalogId` (row exists) while Search/Album saves poison to the Apple Music id. The recents-sync (`ingestToCatalogOnly`) and Playlists (`ensureCatalogId`) paths both already call `upsertCatalogTrack`; Shelf just skipped it. **Fix (forward-only, user's call — no healing of existing broken docs; user re-saves affected albums):** (1) new `ensureCatalogId(forLibrary:)` in `Library.swift` — trusts a non-empty `catalogId` (real Nodalong id), else `upsertCatalogTrack`s from the doc's own (still-populated-at-save-time) metadata and returns the canonical id. (2) `LibraryViewModel.toggleLibrary`/`saveAlbum` resolve the canonical id first (album: concurrently via `withTaskGroup`), re-key the doc via new `LibraryTrackDoc.withCatalogId(_:)`, then optimistic-insert + write keyed by the canonical id; in-memory `catalogIds`/`uris`/`tracks` use it. Membership + remove now match by catalogId **OR** uri (search docs arrive with empty catalogId until canonicalized), and `removeAlbum` takes `[LibraryTrackDoc]` (not `[catalogId]`) to resolve stored canonical ids by uri. (3) removed the `?? track.uri` poison at all 5 save sites (`AlbumDetailSearchView` ×2, `SearchView` `TrackSearchRow` onLike, `ArtistDetailSearchView`, `NowPlayingView` heart) → now `?? ""`. Firestore `upsert*`/`batch*` writers stay pure slim-writers (ensure lives in the VM, the sole caller). Build green; 37 tests pass. On-device verify pending (save album from Search → correct grouping/art; existing "Singles" entries stay until manually deleted + re-saved).

- **2026-07-09** — **Live Playlists / Listening Parties — feature planned; Phase 1 (Playlists entity) built & build-green.** Nodalong's take on Spotify Jam. Product decisions locked with user before coding: **(a) Party audio = DJ model** — one audio source (the host's device is the only thing making sound); remote friends contribute to a shared queue + watch. Cross-service works because the host resolves *any* added track via the existing ISRC fallback. **No per-device sync** in v1 (that + Spotify are deferred Phase 3 bets). **(b) Live transport = Firebase RTDB** (presence + host-written now-playing pointer, `onDisconnect` cleanup) **for ephemeral state, Firestore for durable records** (party doc, queue, playlists). **(c) Cross-service = design-for, ship Apple-only v1** — data layer already service-neutral (ISRC), a `SpotifyMusicService` slots into the `MusicService` seam later, additive. **(d) Playlists = full feature now.** Senior-dev review before build caught and fixed: over-scoped synced-audio (→ DJ model), "party stuck live forever" hole (→ RTDB onDisconnect), and a **modeling bug** — keying playlist/queue track docs by `catalogId` (the library/recent_tracks *set* pattern) would forbid duplicates; playlists/queues are *lists that can repeat*, so they use **auto-ID docs with `catalogId` as a field**. Plan file: `~/.claude/plans/the-next-big-feature-quizzical-treasure.md`.
  **Phase 1 shipped (this session):** **Models** `Playlist` + `PlaylistTrack` (`NodalongKit/.../Models/`) — `PlaylistTrack` is a slim event doc mirroring `LibraryTrackDoc` (tolerant decode + `merged(withCatalog:)` overlay), `id` is the injected documentID (not in the payload). **Pure logic + unit tests** (`NodalongKit/.../Utils/`, 13 new tests, all 37 green): `PlaylistOrder` (sparse fractional `order: Double` — `appended`/`midpoint`/`needsRenormalize`/`renormalized`, single-write reorder) and `friendUids(following:followers:)` (mutual-follow intersection, reused later for party invites). **Firestore** `Nodalong/API/Firestore/Playlists.swift` — `playlists/{id}` + `playlists/{id}/tracks/{autoId}`; CRUD + `addTrackToPlaylist` (ensures catalog via `upsertCatalogTrack` so ISRC identity is captured, appends `order = maxOrder + step`, seeds cover from first track, `FieldValue.increment` trackCount), `removeTrackFromPlaylist`, `reorderPlaylistTrack` (midpoint, single write), `getUserPlaylists` (`whereField(contributorUids, arrayContains: uid)` — owner always in `contributorUids`, sorted client-side to avoid a composite index), `enrichPlaylistTracks` (reuses `CatalogCache`, mirrors `enrichLibrary`). **UI** `Nodalong/Features/Playlists/` — `PlaylistsViewModel` (app-wide, injected in `MainTabView` like `feedVM`), `PlaylistsProfileSection` (horizontal rail placed **between the Spotlight and Recent Tracks sections** on own `ProfileView`; a read-only rail in the same position on other-user `UserProfileView`, which fetches that user's playlists and hides `.private` ones client-side until rules land; value-based nav via `.navigationDestination(for: Playlist.self)`), **Shelf** gained two segments so it's now **Playlists / Albums / Artists / Liked** (`ShelfViewModel.Segment`) — Playlists is a 2-col grid with a "New Playlist" button; "Liked" (short label for Recently Liked, kept compact for the 4-way segmented control) is a flat `recentlyLiked` song list (library sorted `savedAt` desc) with tap-to-play + long-press menu. `PlaylistTile` refactored to be width-agnostic (GeometryReader square art) so it serves both fixed-width rails and flexible grid columns. `PlaylistDetailView` (List with owner-only `EditButton`/`.onMove`/`.onDelete`, rename via alert, delete via menu, play whole list via `PlayerViewModel.play(_ songs:startingAt:)`), `AddToPlaylistSheet` + `CreatePlaylistSheet`. **Add-to-playlist from anywhere:** extended `queueContextMenu` with an optional `song:` param (defaults nil → backward-compatible) that adds an "Add to Playlist…" action presenting `AddToPlaylistSheet`; wired at Feed, Profile recents + spotlight carousels (via a new optional `TrackCard.addToPlaylistSong`), Search results, Album/Artist detail, Shelf, and playlist rows. Added `Song.from(playlistTrack:)` to `SongConversions.swift`. `xcodegen generate` + `xcodebuild` green (one type-checker "failed to produce diagnostic" on `PlaylistDetailView`'s `onMove/onDelete` ternary — fixed by extracting explicit optional `moveHandler`/`deleteHandler` closures). **Not yet done:** Phase 2 (live party: `Parties.swift` Firestore, `PartyLiveService` RTDB, party UI + host↔player bridge, freeze-on-end) and **security rules** (`firestore.rules` + `database.rules.json` — CORE for Phase 2 since it's the app's first multi-writer surface; playlists currently have no rules enforcing `visibility`/ownership). Runtime/Firestore writes not yet verified on device — build-green only.

- **2026-07-08** — **Search filter row redesigned: two stacked segmented pickers → one self-collapsing underline-tab row.** User felt Search's top chrome (`People|Music` segmented picker + `All|Artists|Albums|Songs` segmented picker stacked underneath) was cluttered. Explored three directions with live mockups (visualize tool) before landing on the final design: a single horizontal row of plain underline tabs (no filled backgrounds) that changes its own contents based on mode — `People | Music` (2 tabs) when in People mode; tapping `Music` expands the *same row* in place to `People | All | Artists | Albums | Songs` (5 tabs, Music itself replaced by its four scopes rather than sitting alongside them); tapping `People` from any music scope collapses straight back to 2 tabs. Implemented in `SearchView.swift`: replaced `modePicker`/`scopePicker` (both `Picker(...).pickerStyle(.segmented)`) with `searchFilterRow` (horizontal `ScrollView` of `filterTab` buttons — text + animated purple underline indicator, transitions wrapped in `withAnimation(.easeInOut(duration: 0.2))` so the tab-count change animates instead of snapping) + `filterTab(_:isSelected:action:)` helper. No `SearchViewModel` changes — `mode`/`scope` published properties and the existing `.onChange` search triggers were untouched; this was purely a presentation-layer change. Build green.

- **2026-07-08** — **Recent Tracks ordering fixed: was inverted within every sync, and re-stamped stale tracks to "now" on every app open.** User noticed songs they'd just played sometimes didn't show up where expected. Root cause: `Song.listenedAt` is never populated anywhere from MusicKit (confirmed — `AppleMusicService.toSong` never sets it; `MusicRecentlyPlayedRequest` only exposes relative order, no per-item timestamp), so `syncRecentTracks`'s `song.listenedAt ?? Date().timeIntervalSince1970 * 1000` (`Posts.swift`) always fell back to "time of sync" — and worse, `Date()` was called **fresh inside the loop, once per track**, while iterating MusicKit's most-recent-first list. Since wall-clock time only moves forward, the track processed first (your actual most recent play) got the *earliest* timestamp of the batch, so sorting `desc` on read **inverted the true order within every sync**. Separately, because the write was a full `setData` overwrite keyed by trackId, a track's `listenedAt` got bumped to "now" on *every* resync it was still visible in Apple's top-25, even without being replayed — so Recent Tracks could reorder itself just from opening the app. Fixed in `syncRecentTracks`: capture one `anchor` timestamp per sync (not per track), derive each track's `listenedAt` from its **position** in the batch (`anchor - index`, preserving MusicKit's true relative order) — but only for genuinely new tracks or ones that have **moved to a better rank than last sync** (a real replay signal, computed by sorting `existing` docs' stored `listenedAt` desc and comparing today's index against that prior rank). A track that's merely still present, unreplayed, keeps its original stored `listenedAt` untouched. User explicitly chose this "preserve unless genuinely moved up" semantic over the simpler "always re-stamp, just without the inversion bug" alternative — confirmed correct by hand-tracing three cases (no change → no re-stamp; a replay → fresh timestamp + jumps to top; a new track appended → fresh timestamp, everything else unchanged). Also guards `song.listenedAt` first in case a future source ever populates it directly (unused today, but a real `Song` field). Build green.

- **2026-07-05** — **Feed never showed spotlight badges — ID-space mismatch, not a display bug.** User noticed a track spotlit on a followed user's profile showed no gold badge on the equivalent Feed card. Root cause: `getFollowingFeed`'s `spotlitIds` set (`Posts.swift`) is built from `spotlightTracks[].trackId` — which despite the field name is always set to the **catalogId** (`FirebaseAuthViewModel.addToSpotlight`: `SpotlightTrack(trackId: track.catalogId, ..., catalogId: track.catalogId)`, both fields deliberately identical). But `mapRecentTrack`'s `isSpotlit` check compared this against `doc.documentID` — the `recent_tracks` doc's own key, which is the **Apple Music track ID** (`syncRecentTracks` writes `colRef.document(song.trackId)`). Two different id spaces that essentially never coincide, so `isSpotlit` came out false for virtually every genuinely-spotlit post. Fixed by comparing against `catalogId` instead (already extracted from the doc's data a few lines above in the same function). Confirmed `ProfileView`'s own recents section already did this comparison correctly (`todaySpotlitIds.contains($0.catalogId)`), which is what made the bug easy to isolate to `getFollowingFeed` specifically. **Related gap fixed at the same time:** `UserProfileView`'s recent-tracks section had `isSpotlit: { _ in false }` — a hardcoded stub, so another user's profile page never showed spotlight badges on their recents regardless of the above bug. Added `todaySpotlitCatalogIds` (mirrors `FirebaseAuthViewModel.todaySpotlitIds` — filters `activeSpotlights` by today's `dateKey` then maps to a `Set<String>` of catalogIds) and wired it into the `isSpotlit` closure. Build green.

- **2026-07-05** — **Spotlight carousel `displayedHeight` bug: stuck at 1 card when spotlighting a 2nd/3rd track today.** User reported snapping instead of smooth resizing, the top card "cut off," and the carousel "getting stuck" past 1 spotlight — confirmed via screenshot (header said "3/3 today," carousel showed only 1 card). Root cause in `SpotlightCarousel.swift` (the animated-outer-crop fix from earlier this session, otherwise still intact/unmodified): `displayedHeight` was only recalculated in `.onChange(of: days.count)` (fires on a whole new *day* appearing) and `.onChange(of: safeIndex)` (fires on swiping to a *different page*) — neither fires when a 2nd/3rd spotlight is added to the day already being displayed, since that only changes `days[safeIndex].tracks.count`, not `days.count` or `safeIndex`. So `displayedHeight` stayed stuck at whatever it was sized for (1 card), and every additional card was silently cropped away by the `.clipped()` outer frame. Separately, the `days.count` path never wrapped its update in `withAnimation`, so whichever transitions *did* fire were hard snaps, not smooth. Fixed by computing `targetHeight = Self.pageHeight(days: days, index: safeIndex)` fresh in `body` (reflects both which page AND that page's current track count) and replacing both onChange handlers with one `.onChange(of: targetHeight)` wrapped in `withAnimation` — single, always-correct, always-animated path for every kind of height change. `.onChange(of: days.count)` still exists but now only handles jumping `currentIndex` to today; it no longer touches height directly. **On-device follow-up (same session):** after this fix, all 3 cards rendered correctly in the steady state, but the user reported the top card still looked cut off. Root cause: `maxPageHeight` (the TabView's supposedly-fixed internal pin) was still computed live as `days.map { $0.tracks.count }.max()` — which itself grows (1→2→3) as spotlights are added to today. A "pin" that keeps changing isn't actually pinned; the TabView's underlying UIKit paging view still had to re-layout every time the max grew, reproducing the exact clipping bug the pin was meant to eliminate in the first place. Fixed by hardcoding `maxPageHeight` to the product-level cap (`maxSpotlights` = 3, `NodalongKit/.../Constants/Limits.swift`) instead of deriving it from current data — since 3 is a hard cap that can never legitimately be exceeded, this makes the pin a true constant for the view's entire lifetime, so the TabView's internal layout truly never changes and can't trigger UIKit-level relayout again. **Third report, same symptom, different root cause:** user still saw the top card "cut off" on a screenshot showing "3/3 today" — meaning `displayedHeight == maxPageHeight` exactly (both 504pt), so `.clipped()` wasn't cropping any *content* at all in that case, ruling out both fixes above as the explanation. Real cause: `TrackCard`'s drop shadow (`radius: 14, y: 4`, `TrackCard.swift:44`) bleeds a few points outside its own bounds — fine everywhere else in the app since nothing else wraps `TrackCard` in a `.clipped()` frame — but the per-page `VStack` here has zero top padding before the first card, so its shadow sits flush against the outer clip boundary and gets sliced off (cards 2/3 are shielded by the `spacing: 12` between them; only card 1's top has no such allowance). Fixed by adding a `shadowInset` constant (12pt) as top padding on each page's content, folded into both `pageHeight`/`maxPageHeight` so the extra room doesn't just push the clipping problem onto the last card's bottom edge instead. Build green.

- **2026-07-05** — **Pull-to-refresh on own Profile now re-syncs from Apple Music, not just Firestore.** Previously `LibrarySyncService.syncAndWait(uid:service:)` existed (an awaitable wrapper around the fire-and-forget `sync()`, clearly built for exactly this use case) but had zero callers anywhere — pull-to-refresh on both Feed and Profile only ever re-read whatever was already in Firestore, so a track played moments ago wouldn't appear until the next foreground-return sync (`ContentView`'s three triggers — see prior entry). Wired `syncAndWait` into `ProfileView`'s `.refreshable` only (not `.task`, and not Feed) — scoped deliberately to "viewing your own page," since Feed shows *other* users' recents, not yours, so re-syncing your own Apple Music data on a Feed pull has no purpose. Guarded on `musicService.isAuthenticated` (skips the pointless round-trip if Apple Music isn't linked) before calling `refresh()` as before. No changes to `LibrarySyncService`/`AppleMusicService` — this was purely a missing call site.

- **2026-07-05** — **"Purple screen" root-caused further — reproducible via sign-out → sign-in, not just jetsam relaunch.** The 2026-06-14/06-27 entries only addressed the jetsam-kill-then-cold-relaunch path (via memory cuts) and never fixed the underlying visual gap; user found a reliable non-jetsam repro (sign out, sign back in, app never quits) that disproves jetsam as the sole cause. Real root cause: `MainTabView.init()` (`MainTabView.swift`) synchronously mutates **global, process-wide** UIKit state — `UITabBar`/`UINavigationBar` appearance proxies + every window's `backgroundColor` — and `ContentView`'s `Group` constructs a **fresh** `MainTabView` every time `isAuthenticated` flips false→true (cold launch AND every sign-in). The raw just-set window color is visible before `MainTabView`'s real content (TabView + 5 tabs' fresh `@StateObject`s) paints its first frame — worse on repeat sign-ins than cold launch because by then the window is *already* the right color, so redoing the whole block is 100% wasted main-thread work landing right at the transition. (Checked and ruled out: `FeedView`'s loading/empty states, which both have visible content, never truly blank.) **Fix:** guarded the whole `MainTabView.init()` chrome-setup block behind `private static var didConfigureChrome` so it runs exactly once per process instead of once per construction. **Secondary hardening:** `FeedViewModel.hasLoaded` made `@Published` (was private) and `FeedView`'s condition changed to `isLoading || (!hasLoaded && posts.isEmpty)` so the very first synchronous render (before `.task` fires, when `isLoading` is still its default `false`) shows the spinner instead of momentarily falling through to `emptyState`. Deliberately did NOT flip `isLoading`'s own default to `true` — that would've broken `load()`'s own re-entrancy guard (`guard !isLoading, !hasLoaded`), since `load()` would then never run at all. Not touched: `AppDelegate`/`NodalongApp` (window may not exist yet at `didFinishLaunchingWithOptions`, so the static-guard-in-init approach is simpler and equally effective), no bridging/overlay loading screen added (this fix should shrink the gap enough without one — revisit if it recurs). Build green. Verification is on-device (repeat sign-out/sign-in) — user's to confirm.

- **2026-07-05** — **Nod count capped to once-per-track-per-day.** Clarified with user: the rule is per-(nodder, track)-per-calendar-day, NOT per-(nodder, source-person)-per-day — a user can still nod every distinct track a followed person has, same day; the cap is only "you can't keep bumping the same card's count by tapping play repeatedly today," and it resets tomorrow (re-nodding the same track on a new day is a genuine repeat signal, not abuse). Implemented by appending the same `dateKey(epochMs:)` (added in the prior spotlight-history entry) to `tryIncrementNod`'s `nod_records` document key: `{nodderUid}_{sourceUid}_{trackId}_{dateKey}` (`Posts.swift`), replacing the old permanent `{nodderUid}_{sourceUid}_{trackId}` key that blocked a re-nod forever once given. Record now also stores `dateKey` for auditability, mirroring the spotlight_history record shape. No changes needed to `FeedViewModel.tryNod`'s optimistic-bump/session-dedup logic — the day-scoping is entirely server-side in the record's document ID; only touched its doc comment for accuracy. Build green.

- **2026-07-05** — **Support re-spotlighting a track on a later day ("remind people about this song again").** Root cause: `spotlight_history` docs were keyed by bare `trackId`, so spotlighting the same track again — even months later — silently overwrote the previous history entry, losing the earlier date. Fixed by keying each history doc `{trackId}_{dateKey}` (`addSpotlightTrackBatch`, `Users.swift`): a new calendar day → a new permanent entry (the "re-reminder" the user wants); re-adding/updating within the *same* day still overwrites in place, so no duplicate card appears for one day. User explicitly chose day-granularity over "one entry per action" to avoid same-day toggle noise. Added canonical `dateKey(epochMs:)` (`NodalongKit/.../Utils/DateKey.swift`) and removed three near-identical private reimplementations (`FirebaseAuthViewModel.toDateKey`, `ProfileView.dateKey`, `UserProfileView.dateKey`) — keeps the write-side "day" definition and the display-side grouping guaranteed identical. No model/read changes needed: `SpotlightTrack.trackId` is decoded from document *data*, not the document ID, so `getSpotlightHistory`/`enrichSpotlights` were unaffected. Build green.

- **2026-07-05** — **Track metadata NORMALIZED: `tracks/{catalogId}` is now the single source of truth (clean build).** Culmination of the artwork-bug thread: rather than keep patching denormalized copies, metadata (`title/artist/album/artwork/uri/duration/isrc/trackNumber/albumReleaseDate`) now lives ONLY in the shared catalog; per-user docs (`recent_tracks`, `spotlight_history`, `library`, embedded `spotlightTracks[]`) store only `catalogId` + event data (`playCount`/`listenedAt`/`spotlitAt`/`savedAt`). Every screen joins to the catalog via a new **`CatalogCache`** actor (`Nodalong/Services/CatalogCache.swift`, `[catalogId: CatalogTrack]`, batched `whereField(documentID, in:)` ≤30, session-scoped; mirrors `TrackCache`). **RN app is fully deprecated** (confirmed with user — it shared `nodalong-d87ad` and read the copies directly), so we could stop writing copies. **Dev-mode wipe:** user cleared existing Firestore track data (`tracks/*`, per-user `recent_tracks`/`spotlight_history`/`library`, and the `spotlightTracks[]` field), keeping profile docs — so everything is born normalized; no migration, no legacy fallbacks. **Changes:** (1) `CatalogTrack`/`CatalogTrackInput` gained `trackNumber`+`albumReleaseDate` (Shelf sort needs them); populated at `upsertCatalogTrack`. (2) `LibrarySyncService.ingestToCatalogOnly` resolves real artwork via `AppleMusicService.resolveCatalogArtwork(appleMusicTrackId:isrc:title:artist:)` before writing the catalog row when incoming art isn't `ArtworkURL.isFetchable` — the ONE place that keeps the source healthy (removed the now-redundant `getRecentlyPlayed` ISRC backfill). (3) Models `Post`/`SpotlightTrack`/`LibraryTrackDoc`: metadata fields → `var`, decode-tolerant `init(from:)` (slim docs omit metadata keys → `decodeIfPresent ?? ""`), + pure `merged(withCatalog:)` overlay. (4) Reads overlay catalog metadata: `overlayCatalog` in `Posts.swift` (Feed; replaced `patchArtworkFromCatalog`), `enrichSpotlights` in `Users.swift` (`getSpotlightHistory` + embedded array enriched inside `getUserProfile`; plus lazy enrich in `NearMeUserSheet.loadData` + `UserProfileView.loadData` since those get a passed-in `user`), `enrichLibrary` in `Library.swift` (Shelf). (5) Writes slimmed: `syncRecentTracks`, `upsert/batchUpsertLibraryTracks` (`slimLibraryData`), `updateSpotlightTracks`/`addSpotlightTrackBatch` (`slimSpotlightData`). (6) Deleted `CatalogArtworkRepair.swift` + the ProfileView `#if DEBUG` repair button (obsolete after wipe + write-time resolution). `ArtworkURL.isFetchable`/`sized` render guard kept as final safety net. Build green (`xcodegen generate` + `xcodebuild`); only pre-existing warnings. **Ops done by user: Firestore wiped. To repopulate: launch → recents auto-sync (catalog rows get healthy artwork at ingest), re-save Shelf tracks + re-add spotlights.** Playback for library-matched tracks still resolves via `resolve()`'s title/artist search fallback (catalog `appleMusicUri` is a library id for those — unchanged, pre-existing). Not yet done: `getNearbyUsers`/`searchUsers` don't enrich embedded spotlights (NearMe sheet + UserProfileView enrich lazily instead, which covers display).

- **2026-07-05** — **TrackCard artwork/visual fixes + Spotlight carousel gap fix.** Three linked issues from on-device Feed/Profile screenshots. **(1) Missing artwork.** Root cause: `AppleMusicService.artworkURL` returns `""` when a MusicKit catalog item genuinely has no `Artwork` (occasional catalog gap), and that empty string is written to Firestore with no validation anywhere (`LibrarySyncService.isValidTrack` only checks title/artist/uri). Separately, a real-but-dead artwork URL hits `AsyncImage`'s `.failure` case, which fell into `default: Color.nodalong.bgSurface` with zero logging. Both cases were visually identical to a genuine in-flight load, because `ShimmerView`'s 6%-opacity highlight sweep was nearly invisible under the old heavy black gradient — "loading," "no art exists," and "art failed to load" all just looked like a flat dark rectangle. Fix (`TrackCard.swift`): empty artwork now skips `AsyncImage` entirely and renders a dedicated `noArtworkPlaceholder` (dimmed music-note glyph on `bgSurface`) immediately, instead of sitting in `.empty` forever; `.failure` now logs `print("[Nodalong.TrackCard] artwork load failed for ...")` and falls back to the same placeholder rather than a bare, indistinguishable rectangle. Deliberately did **not** add an artwork check to `isValidTrack` — rejecting a track with valid metadata just because MusicKit didn't return art would throw away real listening data over a cosmetic gap. **On-device follow-up (same session):** the new console logging immediately paid off — real device logs showed the dominant failure wasn't a dead CDN link at all, it was `Artwork.url()` resolving to a `musicKit://artwork/library/{uuid}/600x600?...` scheme for recently-played items matched against the user's personal Apple Music library, instead of an `https://` mzstatic URL. That scheme is an on-device-only artwork-catalog reference (`MPArtworkResizeDomain`/`MPMediaLibraryArtworkRequest`) that plain `URLSession`/`AsyncImage` can never resolve — every load of it was failing instantly by design, not intermittently. Fixed at the source in `AppleMusicService.artworkURL` (line ~388): now only accepts `http`/`https` schemes, returning `""` for anything else (including `musicKit://`), so a doomed URL is never written to Firestore or handed to `AsyncImage` in the first place — it goes straight to the `noArtworkPlaceholder` with no failed network round-trip or console spam. **(2) Cards felt visually flat.** The old 5-stop gradient darkened up to 95% at the bottom and 42% at the top, muddying artwork color across the whole card. Replaced with a scrim that stays fully transparent until 55% down, then ramps to ~0.88 black by the bottom — text legibility preserved (existing per-text `.shadow()`s are backup), but artwork color reads clearly across the top two-thirds. Added a subtle hairline border (`strokeBorder`, matching the pattern already used in `MiniPlayerBar.swift`) so every card — including the no-artwork placeholder state — reads as a distinct object against the purple page background instead of blending in. Spotlit cards (`isSpotlit == true`) get a gold-tinted, slightly thicker version of the same border, so a friend's deliberate pick stands out while scrolling the Feed, not just via the existing small gold "Spotlight" pill badge in the corner (which is unchanged — the border is additive). **(3) Large dead space between the Spotlight carousel and Recent Tracks on Profile.** Root cause: `SpotlightCarousel.carouselHeight` sized the `TabView` frame off `days.map { $0.tracks.count }.max()` — the historical max across *every* day, not the currently-displayed page. Since the spotlight cap is 3/day, any day that ever hit the cap locked the whole carousel at 3-cards-tall forever; a day with only 1 spotlight (e.g. "Today") still reserved the full height, leaving a permanent ~340pt gap. Fixed by computing height per-page. **First attempt (superseded within the same session):** animated the TabView's own `.frame(height:)` directly via `.animation(.easeInOut, value: safeIndex)` — this regressed into two new bugs the user caught immediately on-device: a jarring snap on swipe, and the first card persistently clipped. Root cause: animating a paged `TabView`'s own frame height forces its underlying UIKit page-view machinery to re-layout mid-transition, which SwiftUI/UIKit doesn't bridge reliably. **Fix that stuck:** the TabView's *internal* frame is now pinned to `maxPageHeight` (the tallest page across all days) so its UIKit paging view never has to resize itself at all; a second, separate `@State private var displayedHeight` — updated via `.onChange(of: safeIndex)` wrapped in `withAnimation` — drives an *outer* `.frame(height: displayedHeight, alignment: .top).clipped()` that crops the visible window down to the current page's real height. That outer crop is an ordinary SwiftUI frame animation with no UIKit bridging involved, so it resizes smoothly and the Recent Tracks section below reflows in lockstep instead of snapping. Lesson for future sessions: don't animate a paged `TabView`'s own size — pin it to a fixed max and animate an outer crop instead. Not touched: `ArtworkURL.swift` (regex confirmed correct, not a contributing cause), the other `AsyncImage` call sites in `ArtistDetailSearchView`/`ShelfView`/`NowPlayingView` (same silent-failure pattern exists there — worth a follow-up pass), `ProfileView`'s separate fixed-height *loading* skeleton (188pt, from the 2026-06-09 entry — a different, correctly-scoped placeholder for the pre-load state). **(4) Real artwork backfill for library-matched recent plays (same session, planned then implemented).** The `musicKit://` scheme fix in (1) degrades gracefully but never shows real art for these tracks — and it's not a one-off, it's routine `MusicRecentlyPlayedRequest` behavior for any track matched against the user's personal library. Since these tracks' catalog counterpart almost always exists with a normal portable `https://` mzstatic artwork URL, `getRecentlyPlayed()` (`AppleMusicService.swift:157`) now backfills empty artwork via an ISRC catalog lookup (`MusicCatalogResourceRequest<Song>(matching: \.isrc, equalTo:)` — the same pattern already used in `resolve(_:)`) before the track is ever persisted. Runs the (at most 25) lookups concurrently via `withTaskGroup` so a handful of misses don't serialize and slow down sync; a miss (no ISRC, or no catalog match) just leaves the track at `artwork: ""`, falling through to the existing placeholder — no regression. Deliberately ISRC-only, no title/artist secondary fallback (user's call — precise, avoids matching the wrong recording e.g. a remix/re-release). Scoped to `getRecentlyPlayed()` only — `getSavedTracks()`/search/`getArtistTopTracks()`/`getAlbumTracks()` already only touch the catalog directly and never produce this scheme. **(6) Data-repair migration — the real fix for existing broken artwork (Feed included).** (1)/(4)/(5) only prevent/hide bad artwork going forward on *this* device; the Feed shows *other users'* denormalized data, which this device can never rewrite, and artwork is denormalized into `recent_tracks`/`spotlight_history`/`library` + the shared `tracks` catalog. Verified the project is 100% client-side Firebase (no Functions, no server-side Apple Music API, no admin scripts, no committed rules). Chosen approach (with user): **client-side dev-run one-time catalog repair + catalog-preferred reads — no backend, no Apple `.p8`, reuse on-device MusicKit.** Pieces: **(A)** `ArtworkURL.isFetchable(_:)` — shared pure predicate (`http(s)` scheme only), single source of truth for "bad artwork," used by repair + read patch (render-time `sized()` guard stays as final safety net). **(B)** public `AppleMusicService.resolveCatalogArtwork(appleMusicTrackId:isrc:)` — prefers direct catalog-id lookup, falls back to ISRC, reuses existing private `resolveCatalogArtwork(isrc:)`. **(C)** new `CatalogArtworkRepair` service (`Nodalong/Services/`) — paginates the whole `tracks` catalog (cursor, 200/page, bounded concurrency), re-resolves unusable artwork via (B), `updateData`s in place; idempotent/re-runnable; triggered by a **`#if DEBUG`-only** "Repair catalog artwork" button in `ProfileView` (not shipped). **(D)** `patchArtworkFromCatalog(_:)` in `Posts.swift` — after building `[Post]` in both `getFollowingFeed`/`getYourMusicFeed`, substitutes catalog artwork for any post whose own artwork isn't fetchable (batched `whereField(FieldPath.documentID(), in:)`, ≤30/query); **self-limiting** — clean data ⇒ empty needing-set ⇒ zero extra reads. This is what makes "repair the catalog ⇒ fix every feed" work *without* cross-writing other users' subcollections. **(E)** `Post.artwork` `let`→`var` (mirrors `Song.artwork`) so (D) can substitute in place. **(F)** new `Array.chunked(into:)` util for (D)'s batching. Out of scope (fast-follow, reuse (D)'s helper): Spotlight carousels / Shelf / NearMe read *other* denormalized copies — repairing the catalog is the prerequisite that makes them fixable, but only the two `[Post]` feed builders are wired now. **Ops: after shipping, run the dev-only Repair button once to heal existing catalog data.** **First on-device run diagnosed a gap:** scanned 4,010, **repaired 0**, unresolved 3,802 (208 already-good). Root cause: the 3,802 broken docs were all created from **library-matched** recently-played plays, where `toSong` stored `appleMusicTrackId = s.id.rawValue` = a **library-scoped id** (invalid for a `MusicCatalogResourceRequest(matching: \.id)` catalog lookup) and `isrc = nil` — so the id+ISRC-only resolver had no valid anchor for any of them. Fix: added a **title+artist `MusicCatalogSearchRequest` fallback** to `resolveCatalogArtwork(...)` (new optional `title`/`artist` params, passed from the catalog doc by the repair) — the same fallback playback's `resolve()` already uses, and the only device-independent anchor for library-matched tracks (a text catalog search doesn't require the item to be in the *running* device's library, which matters for a shared catalog spanning many users' plays). Artwork-only, so a wrong-release match at worst swaps art, never identity. User approved reversing the earlier "ISRC-only" preference **for the bulk repair path only** — the real-time `getRecentlyPlayed` backfill still calls the private `resolveCatalogArtwork(isrc:)` and stays ISRC-only as originally chosen. Re-run the Repair button after this fix to actually heal the 3,802. Build green; the surfaced warnings (`AppleMusicService:18` MusicService/main-actor conformance, `ArtworkURL:45` `UIScreen.main.scale` default, `Posts.swift` Firestore `Sendable`) are all pre-existing Swift-6-mode warnings unrelated to this work — a full recompile (from `xcodegen generate` after adding files) just surfaced them together. **(5) The (1)/(4) fixes alone weren't enough — the exact same `musicKit://` console failure kept recurring after rebuild.** Root cause: (1) and (4) only prevent *this device* from writing bad artwork *going forward* — they can't retroactively fix a `recent_tracks` doc already persisted before the fix, a doc for a track that's since aged out of the most-recent-25 sync window (so it's never revisited/overwritten by any future sync), or — critically, since Nodalong is a social feed — a post written by a *different user's device* still running an older build. `ArtworkURL.sized` (`ArtworkURL.swift`) is the one shared choke-point every `AsyncImage` call site (`TrackCard`, `ArtistDetailSearchView`, `ShelfView`, `NowPlayingView`) already routes artwork strings through, so the defensive fix belongs there instead: it now rejects any non-`http(s)` scheme outright (returns `""`), regardless of which device or app version wrote the string, or how long ago. This is the fix that actually closes the loop — (1)/(4) are still worth keeping since they mean *fewer* strings ever need this fallback and some tracks get real art back via the ISRC lookup, but the render-time guard in `ArtworkURL.sized` is what makes the bug unable to resurface no matter the data's origin. Lesson: for a bug that can originate from cross-device/cross-version persisted data, fix it at the shared render-time chokepoint, not just at this device's write path.
- **2026-06-27** — **Bug-fix + perf + UX session (build-green throughout).** Eight related pieces of work:
  **(1) Full-screen player artist/album nav (was dead).** `NowPlayingView`'s artist/album taps used to only store a result and spin forever — no destination (the `NavigationStack` was torn out in the 2026-06-10 layout fix). Re-wired as a `.sheet(item:)` presenting `ArtistDetailSearchView`/`AlbumDetailSearchView` inside their own `NavigationStack` (with `ArtistResult`/`AlbumResult` destinations so taps inside the sheet keep navigating). Chose a sheet over wrapping the body in a `NavigationStack` to avoid disturbing the hard-won `UIScreen.main.bounds`-pinned layout.
  **(2) Shelf artist→album "reloads the artist page" fixed.** The artist page reached via the album's artist-name tap used `navigationDestination(isPresented:)` (boolean), which breaks downstream `NavigationLink(value: AlbumResult)` resolution. Made the whole chain value-based: `ShelfView` root `NavigationStack(path: $path)` (`NavigationPath`), added `navigationDestination(for: ArtistResult.self)`, and `AlbumDetailView` now `path.append(...)`s the resolved catalog artist/album instead of the boolean destinations. (Lesson: mixing `navigationDestination(isPresented:)` with downstream value-based links breaks the value links — keep a stack uniformly value-based.)
  **(3) PlaybackClock — progress split off `PlayerViewModel` (scroll-hitch perf).** `progress`/`currentDuration` were `@Published` on the app-wide `PlayerViewModel`, so the 2–10 Hz progress timer fired `objectWillChange` on the whole tree every tick; `FeedView` reads `playerVM.nowPlaying` so its body re-evaluated 2×/s during playback → scroll hitches. New `PlaybackClock: ObservableObject` (in `PlayerViewModel.swift`) holds `progress`+`duration`; `PlayerViewModel` owns one as a plain `let clock` with computed forwarders. Injected separately (`NodalongApp` + `withPreviewEnvironment`); only `NowPlayingView`'s scrubber observes it. (Full `@Observable` migration is the cleaner fix but blocked — deployment target is iOS 16.)
  **(4) Mini-player spacing on pushed detail screens.** Same fix the Feed already had: a conditional `Color.clear.frame(height: 70)` at the bottom of scroll content `if playerVM.nowPlaying != nil` — the TabView `safeAreaInset` doesn't reliably clear the iOS 26 floating pill on pushed screens. Added to `ArtistDetailSearchView`, Shelf `AlbumDetailView`, `AlbumDetailSearchView`.
  **(5) Swipe-to-queue removed app-wide → long-press, then action sheet.** The custom right-swipe (`TrackCard` drag + the `swipeToRevealQueue` modifier) collided with the system interactive back-swipe and broke navigation on pushed screens. First moved everything to a single `queueContextMenu` (long-press), deleting `SwipeRevealQueue.swift` and `TrackCard`'s drag machinery. Then the `.contextMenu` flooded the console with `_UIGravityWellEffectAnchorView`/`_UIReparentingView` warnings (its UIKit lift effect, fires as rows recycle), so `queueContextMenu` was reimplemented as a long-press gesture + `confirmationDialog` (`QueueContextMenu.swift`) — same trigger, no context-menu machinery, no warnings. Tradeoff: queueing is now long-press → tap "Add to Queue" (a bottom sheet, not an inline menu). This supersedes the 2026-06-10 `AddToQueueContextMenu`/swipe affordances.
  **(6) Artwork memory cuts (the app grew to ~428 MB; the rest of the console — `ICUserIdentityStore` account-store errors, `MPIdentifierSet EMPTY (Album)`, `applicationQueuePlayer ping-pong` — is benign system/MusicKit noise, left as-is).** `ArtworkURL.sized` now caps at the 600 px source (`maxPixels`, default 600) — full-width art on a 3× device was resolving to ~1080 px and decoding to 4–5 MB bitmaps for no quality gain. Routed `TrackCard`, `NowPlayingView` main art, and both artist heroes through `sized`. `NowPlayingView`'s blurred background now requests a ~60 pt rendition (it's blurred at radius 48 — ~95 % cheaper, no visible difference). Collapsed two parallel 50 MB image caches to one: removed the unused Kingfisher `ImageCache.default` config + dead `import Kingfisher` (AppDelegate, AlbumDetailSearchView); `URLCache.shared` is the sole cache, trimmed 50→24 MB memory. (Kingfisher SPM dep still declared in `project.yml`, now fully unused — removable cleanup.)
  **(7) "Purple screen on reopen" — investigated, root-caused, partially mitigated by (6).** The plain-purple-no-spinner screen is the `UIWindow.backgroundColor` (`bgBase`, set in `MainTabView.init`) showing because the first SwiftUI frame never rendered after a cold relaunch — *not* `LaunchSplash` (which has a logo+spinner) nor the `LaunchScreen.storyboard` (systemBackground/white). Cold relaunches are frequent because the app is **jetsam-killed in the background**, and the high memory from (6) made it a prime target; `UIBackgroundModes: audio` + a stalled foreground frame (MusicKit XPC `_establishConnectionIfNeeded` timeout, or Firebase/audio under a locked device) leave the bare window visible. No `addSnapshotListener` leaks exist (all Firestore reads are one-shot), so it's not leaked listeners. The memory fixes are the primary mitigation; a launch-hardening pass (guarantee `LaunchSplash` renders on background launches, no main-thread blocking at launch) remains if it recurs. Confirm via `JetsamEvent-*.ips` device logs.
  **(8) Play-in-context for albums / Top Songs + near-gapless advance.** Revises the 2026-06-10 "tapping a song resets the queue to just that song" rule **for ordered-list contexts only**: new `PlayerViewModel.play(_ songs:[Song], startingAt:)` loads the whole list with the tapped track as `currentIndex` (⏮ reaches earlier, later tracks auto-play). Wired into Shelf `AlbumDetailView`, catalog `AlbumDetailSearchView`, and artist `popularTrackRow` (Top Songs). Single-track surfaces (Feed, profile carousels, Near Me, flat Search results) keep `play(_ song:)` (reset-to-one). Fixes "album stops after the first song." Inter-track gap reduced via a one-slot `prefetchedNext` cache: `prefetchNext()` resolves the next queue item in the background while the current plays; `resolveUsingCache()` consumes it on advance, skipping the network `resolve` round-trip (the dominant gap). Keyed by `uri`, re-validated before use, falls back to live resolve on reorder/skip. Residual buffering gap remains (would need MusicKit's native multi-entry queue, deliberately avoided).
- **2026-06-14** — **Performance + crash audit: killed the background-CPU jetsam loop.** On-device Instruments showed 152 % CPU / "Very High" energy / 333 MB while merely foregrounded, and recurring "purple screen on reopen" after locking the phone mid-queue. **Root cause (confirmed by tracing, not a Swift crash): a jetsam termination from sustained background CPU.** The app declares `UIBackgroundModes: audio`, so it keeps running while locked during playback — and three timers were **never gated by visibility or scene phase**: `NearMeCanvasView`'s `Timer.publish(every: 1/60).autoconnect()` (a *stored property* → subscribes for the life of the view, and in a TabView the view stays alive, so once NearMe was visited the 60 fps loop ran **forever, on every tab, in the background**), `MiniPlayerBar`'s 30 fps disc-spin timer, and `PlayerViewModel`'s 20 fps progress timer (which `@Published`-publishes to the whole tree — `playerVM` is injected at the root + read in 87 places). The 60 fps loop also did **dozens of separate `@State` writes per tick** (one per avatar + 4 per collision pair). Sustained 60 fps main-thread work in the background trips the iOS CPU watchdog → kill → cold relaunch shows `ContentView`'s `isInitializing` fill = the "purple screen." **Fixes, 3 phases, all build-green:** **Phase 1 (the crash fix):** (1) `NearMeCanvasView` — replaced the autoconnect publisher with a `tickTimer` started in `.onAppear`, invalidated in `.onDisappear` AND whenever `scenePhase != .active` (new `@Environment(\.scenePhase)`); the loop now only runs while NearMe is the visible, foreground tab. Dropped 60→**30 fps** (`tickInterval`). Refactored `tick()` so `tickLifecycle`/`tickPhysics`/`tickCollisions` mutate **`inout` local copies** and write back to `avatarStates`/`activePool`/`unusedQueue` **once per frame** (3 invalidations vs. dozens). (2) `PlayerViewModel.startProgressTimer` — interval is now `progressInterval = isExpanded ? 0.1 : 0.5`, i.e. 10 Hz only when the full scrubber is open, **2 Hz** when collapsed to the mini bar (which shows no progress UI); `isExpanded`'s `didSet` restarts the timer at the new cadence. **Deliberately did NOT stop the progress timer on background** — at 2 Hz it's negligible and keeping it alive preserves the background queue auto-advance the user previously asked for; the edge detector still catches end-of-track within 0.5 s. (3) `MiniPlayerBar` — deleted the 30 fps `spinTimer`; the disc is now a single declarative `.rotationEffect` + `.linear(8s).repeatForever` toggled by `isPlaying` via `updateSpin(isPlaying:)` (freeze uses a `disablesAnimations` transaction so it stops in place). Core Animation runs it off the main thread and auto-suspends when backgrounded. **Phase 2:** (4) new `ArtworkURL.sized(_:points:)` helper (`Nodalong/Shared/UI/ArtworkURL.swift`) rewrites the `{w}x{h}` token of Apple Music `mzstatic` URLs (baked at 600×600 by `AppleMusicService.artworkURL`) to the display pixel size; applied in `ArtworkThumbnail` (covers mini-player/queue/search rows/follow lists — the worst memory offenders, decoding 600² into 44–48 pt). Non-matching/legacy URLs pass through unchanged. `TrackCard`/`NowPlayingView` left at 600 (genuinely large on screen). Also used for the lock-screen art in `updateNowPlayingInfo` (300 pt). (5) Auto-advance hardening: new `isLoadingTrack` flag (set across `playCurrent()`) added to the edge-detector confirm guard so a track hand-off's brief `.paused` can't trigger a premature advance. **Did NOT** rewrite the poll to Combine `state` observation — the poll is already well-guarded (the progress timer is killed during establishment) and the lock-screen behaviour was hard-won. **Phase 3:** (6) `ContentView`'s bare `Color.nodalong.bgDeep` init screen → new branded `LaunchSplash` (logo + wordmark + spinner, plus an 8 s "taking longer than usual" hint). (7) Deleted dead `PlayerViewModel.isSubscribed()`. (8) `SearchViewModel` now suppresses the benign `-999 cancelled` debounce errors via `isCancellation(_:)`. **Verification still pending on-device** (Instruments/lock-phone CPU gauge): expect background CPU ≈0 while locked, foreground playing CPU in low double digits, no purple screens. Plan file: `~/.claude/plans/unified-meandering-pearl.md`.
- **2026-06-10** — **Playback queue ("Up Next") system added.** The player went from single-track to a real queue: add, view, reorder, remove, skip ⏭⏮. **Product decisions (confirmed with user):** tapping a song **plays it and resets the queue to just that song** (no auto-queueing a list); one explicit **Add to Queue** action that **appends** (no "Play Next"); queue is **ephemeral / in-memory** (no persistence). **Architecture:** the queue is a `[QueueItem]` array that is the *single source of truth*, living in `PlayerViewModel` (which already owns `ApplicationMusicPlayer.shared` directly — it does NOT go through `MusicService.play()`, that protocol method is an unused wrapper). **Only one track is ever loaded into the underlying player at a time**; the whole "Up Next" list lives only in our array, so reorder/remove are pure array mutations with zero player-queue sync risk — deliberately chosen over MusicKit's native multi-entry queue to avoid the kind of state-desync that caused the NowPlayingView layout saga. One uniform advance path serves both backends (subscriber `ApplicationMusicPlayer` + non-subscriber `AVPlayer` preview). New `QueueItem` model (`NodalongKit/.../Models/QueueItem.swift`: `UUID` + `Song`, so the same song can be queued twice). New `PlayerViewModel` API: `addToQueue`, `skipToNext`, `skipToPrevious` (restarts current if >3 s in, else steps back — Apple Music behaviour), `playQueueItem` (jump), `moveUpNext`/`removeUpNext` (offsets relative to `upNext`; move reimplemented with plain `Array` ops to avoid importing SwiftUI), plus derived `currentItem`/`upNext`/`hasNext`/`hasPrevious`. `play(_:)` keeps its signature (resets queue → `playCurrent()`), so all existing call sites are unchanged. **Auto-advance:** preview uses `AVPlayerItemDidPlayToEndTime`; subscriber uses a heuristic in the 0.05 s progress timer (`isPlaying && progress ≥ 0.985 && playbackStatus ∈ {.paused,.stopped}`) guarded by `isAdvancing` — MusicKit has no end-of-track callback for `ApplicationMusicPlayer`. **The subscriber auto-advance heuristic is the one bit to watch on-device; explicit ⏭ works regardless.** UI: new `QueueView.swift` (Up Next sheet — `List` with Now Playing + Up Next sections, `.onMove`/`.onDelete`, `EditButton`, tap-to-jump, dark themed) presented from a new queue button in `NowPlayingView`'s top bar; the dead ⏮/⏭ are now live (⏭ dims/disables when `!hasNext`). Entry point everywhere: `TrackCard` gained `onAddToQueue` + an `AddToQueueContextMenu` modifier (long-press), wired at Feed/Profile/UserProfile/Shelf/Search/Album/Artist + both shared carousels (`RecentTracksCarousel`, `SpotlightCarousel` got optional `onAddToQueue`). Custom rows (Search `TrackSearchRow`, Shelf/Album/Artist track rows) got `.contextMenu` directly. `Icons.queue` = `list.bullet`, `Icons.addToQueue` = `text.badge.plus`. Out of scope (noted): gapless via pre-resolving the next track, persistence, "Play Next", auto-queue-the-list on tap.
- **2026-06-10** — **NowPlayingView layout FIXED — root cause was the tab bar's `safeAreaInset` leaking into the `fullScreenCover`.** This closes out a multi-session saga (the recurring "separator band ~77% down" + title/artist/album clipped off the *left* + invisible scrubber). Every prior diagnosis was wrong: it was NOT the blurred-background gradient (the band survived a uniform gradient), NOT a leftover `NavigationStack`, NOT a `@ViewBuilder` collapse, NOT `fullScreenCover`-vs-`safeAreaInset` modifier *ordering*. **Real cause:** `NowPlayingView` is presented as a `.fullScreenCover` from `MainTabView`'s TabView, which carries `.safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom) { BottomSection() }`. On iOS 26 that inset *leaks into the presented cover* as a phantom ~190pt bottom inset, which oversized and horizontally shifted the cover's content frame — so leading-aligned text rendered with its head off the left edge (you see string *tails*: "N", "ingle"), a hard boundary line was drawn at the leaked-inset edge, and the controls were shoved into that band. **Fix (all in `NowPlayingView.swift`):** (1) pin the root `ZStack` to `UIScreen.main.bounds` (`.frame(width:height:)`) + `.ignoresSafeArea()` so no leaked/oversized environment inset can stretch or offset it; (2) re-apply ONLY the real device insets, read from the key window via a new `deviceSafeAreaInsets` computed prop (`connectedScenes → keyWindow.safeAreaInsets`) — the window's insets reflect physical notch/Dynamic Island/home-indicator and are immune to the SwiftUI `safeAreaInset` leak; (3) every text row gets its own `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, alignment: .leading)` so the worst case is tail-truncation ("…"), never a left clip; (4) `background` simplified to just fill (dropped its own `.ignoresSafeArea()` now that the parent owns sizing); (5) bigger artwork `min(width − 48, height × 0.42)` (~345pt iPhone 15, height-capped on SE); (6) uniform top→bottom `LinearGradient([black 0.40 → bgBase 0.90])` over the blur (kept — harmless and consistent across albums); (7) scrubber inherits the body's 24pt h-padding (removed its extra 16pt inset). **Lesson for the next agent: when a `fullScreenCover`/sheet presented near a `safeAreaInset` shows phantom insets or shifted/clipped content, don't chase the symptom — pin to `UIScreen.main.bounds` + `.ignoresSafeArea()` and read real insets from the key window.** Dead `NavigationLink(isActive:)`/`navigationDestination` artist-album push code was also removed (no `NavigationStack` wraps the cover); `loadArtist`/`loadAlbum` still store results for when nav is re-wired (out of scope).
- **2026-06-09** — **Full-screen player redesign (Spotify/Apple Music style).** `NowPlayingView` completely rewritten. Layout: ↓ chevron → full-width square artwork (drag-to-dismiss) → title + ♡ heart row → tappable artist + tappable album → scrubber → ⏮ (dead) ▶▶|| ⏭ (dead) → MPVolumeView system slider. `NowPlayingTrack` extended with `album: String` and `catalogId: String?`. `MiniPlayerBar.fullScreenCover` now wraps `NowPlayingView` in a `NavigationStack` so artist/album taps can push into `ArtistDetailSearchView` / `AlbumDetailSearchView`. Tapping artist/album fires `musicService.searchAll()` with an inline loading spinner, then pushes via hidden `NavigationLink(isActive:)` (iOS 16 compatible). Heart button uses `libraryVM.toggleLibrary` — fills purple when track is in shelf. New `SystemVolumeSlider.swift` wraps `MPVolumeView` with white tint; `showsRouteButton = false`.
- **2026-06-09** — **Artist page redesign + Save Album to Shelf.** (1) Added `getArtistTopTracks(artistId:)` to `MusicService` protocol and implemented in `AppleMusicService` via MusicKit's native `artist.with([.topSongs])` — replaces the fragile `searchTracks() + filter-by-name` hack. "Popular" section renamed "Top Songs". (2) `ArtistDetailSearchView` discography section: replaced the segmented `Picker + conditional ScrollView` with a custom two-button tab row (animated underline indicator) above a `TabView(.page)` — user swipes left/right between Albums and Singles & EPs. (3) **Save Album to Shelf** in `AlbumDetailSearchView`: added "Save Album" / "Saved to Shelf" pill button in the album header. Uses `batchUpsertLibraryTracks` / `batchRemoveLibraryTracks` (new Firestore `WriteBatch` functions in `Library.swift`) via `LibraryViewModel.saveAlbum` / `removeAlbum` — one network round-trip for the whole album. Optimistic UI with rollback on error.
- **2026-06-09** — **Kingfisher image caching + shimmer + shelf sort fix.** (1) Added **Kingfisher 8.x** (`project.yml` + dependency) — all 6 `AsyncImage` network-image sites replaced with `KFImage`: `ArtworkThumbnail`, `TrackCard`, `NowPlayingView` background blur + centre artwork, `ArtistDetailSearchView` hero, `ShelfView` artist detail hero. Kingfisher's in-memory cache (50 MB) means images fetched once in the Feed appear instantly in Profile/Shelf within the same session. Disk cache (200 MB) survives app restarts. (2) **`ShimmerView`** (`Nodalong/Shared/UI/ShimmerView.swift`) — moving-highlight-gradient placeholder used everywhere via `.placeholder { ShimmerView() }` on `KFImage`. Makes loading feel designed rather than broken. (3) **Shelf album stable sort** — `GroupByAlbum.swift` now uses album name A→Z as a tiebreaker when two albums share the same artist and no/same release date. Before this fix, nil-date albums sorted in dict-insertion order (random), causing the grid to flicker and reorder on every render.
- **2026-06-09** — **Kingfisher removed from all rendering paths; NowPlayingView layout fixed.** Kingfisher consistently failed to load specific Apple Music CDN URLs (verified: Mac Miller / The Spins, Lil Skag / regular young lad worked with AsyncImage but not KFImage). Root cause unknown — likely a header/response-handling difference in Kingfisher's custom `ImageDownloader` vs URLSession. All `KFImage` replaced with `AsyncImage` phase API everywhere: `TrackCard`, `ArtworkThumbnail`, `ShelfView` hero, `NowPlayingView` (artwork + blurred background). Shimmer via `.empty` phase placeholder. URLCache (50MB/200MB) still provides caching for all `AsyncImage` paths. Kingfisher package kept as a dependency (unused) — can be removed in a cleanup pass. NowPlayingView layout also fixed in same pass: scrubber given `padding(.horizontal, 16)` internal to the property; `MPVolumeView` given explicit `frame(width: screenWidth * 0.55)` because UIKit views do not respect SwiftUI `.padding()`; removed the broken per-element `.padding(.horizontal, 28)` from the VStack body that was causing scrubber/volume to render off-screen.
- **2026-06-09** — **NowPlayingView layout fix.** Artwork constrained from `maxWidth: .infinity` (~337pt) to `UIScreen.main.bounds.width * 0.72` (~283pt on iPhone 15), centred via `frame(maxWidth: .infinity)`. Replaced six greedy `Spacer(minLength:)` elements (which were distributing all flex space equally across every gap and ballooning the area below the artwork) with a single `Spacer(minLength: 8)` above the artwork only, and fixed `.padding()` between all other sections — controls now always sit compact at the bottom. Scrubber and volume slider given extra `.padding(.horizontal, 28)` on top of the VStack's 28pt = 56pt total per side, making them visibly narrower than the transport row.
- **2026-06-09** — **Kingfisher artwork loading regression fix.** `KFImage` inside `.overlay {}` in `TrackCard` had no explicit frame — without `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, maxHeight: .infinity)` on the `KFImage` itself, Kingfisher doesn't reliably fill the overlay bounds and valid images fail to render. Also changed `TrackCard` placeholder from `Color.clear` to `Color.nodalong.bgSurface` so the loading state isn't invisible. Added `url.isEmpty ? nil : URL(string: url)` guard to all `KFImage` call sites (`TrackCard`, `ArtworkThumbnail`, `ArtistDetailSearchView` hero, `NowPlayingView`) so an empty artwork string produces a nil URL (proper placeholder) rather than `URL(string: "")` which can behave unpredictably across Kingfisher versions. Root cause of persistent missing artwork on many cards: tracks synced before the Spotify→Apple Music port have old Spotify CDN URLs stored in Firestore — those are expired/inaccessible now. This self-corrects as users re-sync tracks. The code fix only addresses the layout/rendering regression; data quality improves organically.
- **2026-06-09** — **Loading/caching/profile UX fixes (6 changes).** (1) **ProfileView `hasLoaded` guard** — `.task` no longer re-fetches on every tab re-visit; pull-to-refresh and `syncService.lastSyncedAt` onChange still force a refresh. (2) **NearMeViewModel `hasLoaded` + `loadedGeohash` guard** — same pattern; `forceReload: true` passed from the radius slider `onChange` so changing radius still re-queries. (3) **ShelfViewModel `artistImages` persisted to UserDefaults** (`"nodalong.artistImages"`) — on first launch after the fix, images are cached; on subsequent launches they load instantly with no album-art flash. (4) **ProfileView `spotlightHistory` → Optional** — `nil` while loading shows a fixed-height 188pt skeleton placeholder, preventing the "recent tracks jump down" layout shift when the spotlight section loads. (5) **SpotlightCarousel crash fix + stale-index fix** — `safeIndex = min(currentIndex, days.count - 1)` before every subscript prevents the OOB crash on last-spotlight-removal; `.onChange(of: days.count)` jumps to today's page when history loads; `.id(days.count)` resets the paging scroll position. (6) **Artwork fade-in** — `ArtworkThumbnail` and `TrackCard` both use phase-based `AsyncImage` with `.transition(.opacity.animation(.easeIn(duration: 0.15)))` so artwork fades in instead of popping.
- **2026-06-08** — **Mini player / keyboard glitch resolved.** Several linked fixes: (1) Added `Color.clear.frame(height: 70)` spacer (when `nowPlaying != nil`) to every scrollable tab view — FeedView (LazyVStack), ProfileView (VStack), SearchView (resultsScroll VStack), ShelfView AlbumsListView (ScrollView), ShelfView ArtistsListView (List footer row). NearMe is canvas-based and skipped. (2) **`safeAreaInset spacing: 120 → 0`** in `MainTabView` — the 120pt value was a debug artifact creating an unpainted gap that showed as black behind the keyboard. The content spacers inside each scroll view are the correct mechanism for the gap. (3) **`BottomSection` keyboard-adaptive padding** — `@State var keyboardVisible` driven by `UIResponder.keyboardWillShow/Hide` notifications. When keyboard is up: `.padding(.bottom, 0)` so pill sits flush against keyboard top; when down: `.padding(.bottom, 40)`. Two separate `.animation` calls (spring for play/stop, easeInOut for keyboard swap). (4) **`BottomSection` keyboard background** — `.background(keyboardVisible ? Color.nodalong.bgBase : .clear)` fills the region when keyboard is up so the transparent glassGapBuffer doesn't expose the UIKit black layer. (5) **`UIWindow` background set to `bgBase`** in `MainTabView.init()` via `connectedScenes` — fixes any gap between SwiftUI views and the keyboard/UIKit layers showing as black globally. (6) **Keyboard push-up behaviour in Search accepted as standard iOS UX** — `.ignoresSafeArea(.keyboard)` on the NavigationStack has no effect because the avoidance is a UIKit-level frame resize on `UIHostingController`/`UINavigationController`, not a SwiftUI safe area issue. Attempting to fight it would require fragile UIKit spy-view hacks. The push-up is what every iOS user expects from a search field.

Append-only. Newest entries at the top. Each entry: date + one-sentence summary.

- **2026-06-07** — **MiniPlayerBar geometry tune + light-purple TabView base.** Two follow-ups on the floating-pill MainTabView from earlier today. (1) **`pillBottomOffset` 96 → 78** — the previous value left a visible 22pt gap between the pill's bottom edge and the iOS 26 floating tab bar's top edge. iOS 26's tab bar pill sits with its TOP at ≈74pt from screen bottom (≈50pt tall pill + ≈24pt home-indicator inset). 78pt puts our pill ~4pt above the tab bar — visually attached without touching. (2) **`scenePlayerBottomPadding` 76 → 64** — re-derived for the new offset. Pill TOP is at 78 + 62 = 140pt from screen bottom. We want scroll content to stop ~8pt above that (148pt). iOS 26 already reserves ≈85pt at every scroll view's bottom for the floating tab bar, so we only add 148 − 85 = 63 ≈ 64pt via the per-tab `Color.clear` spacer. (3) **Light-purple TabView background** (`Color.nodalong.primary200`) — design choice, kept permanent. iOS 26 enforces ≈85pt of bottom safe-area reservation around the floating tab bar pill on every scroll view; pre-26 builds had ~50pt. The reservation isn't a bug we can shrink without dropping the floating tab bar aesthetic (and the user wants to keep it). The dead-black space on the Feed bottom + the gap between the mini-player pill and the tab-bar pill both read as accidents; coloring the TabView background `primary200` makes those gaps look intentional, branded, and visually attached to the player. Each tab's own content paints its own `gray800` on top, so the light purple only shows through where there's no opaque content — exactly the "gap" zones.
- **2026-06-07** — **MiniPlayerBar rewritten as RN-pattern floating pill — definitive fix for the iOS 26 tab-bar layout fight.** Three earlier passes tried to use `.safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom) { MiniPlayerBar() }` in different configurations and all of them broke against iOS 26's floating "Liquid Glass" tab bar — per-tab inserted a visible gap above the inserted view, TabView-level caused the system tab bar to be replaced entirely (mini player ate the tab bar's position). After looking at the React Native sibling repo (`nodalong/src/app/App.tsx` + `Player.tsx`) — which solved exactly this layout problem — the pattern that actually works is: the mini player is rendered as a **floating absolutely-positioned pill overlay**, not as a real safe-area "bar". Concretely: (1) **`MainTabView` restructured** — TabView keeps the system tab bar untouched. Each tab gets `.safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom, spacing: 0) { Color.clear.frame(height: 76 if playing else 0) }` — the inset is a *transparent spacer*, not the real bar, so iOS 26 has nothing to mis-interpret as "your new bottom bar." The bar itself is an `.overlay(alignment: .bottom)` on the TabView with `.padding(.bottom, 96) + .padding(.horizontal, 12) + .frame(height: 62)` — explicit positioning that doesn't go through the safe-area pipeline at all. (2) **`MiniPlayerBar` restyled to pill** — `clipShape(RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: 32, .continuous))` + soft drop shadow + 0.5pt white-8% strokeBorder. Solid `primary700` background (still opaque, still no content bleeding through). Artwork bumped 36→44 since the pill is taller (62pt vs the previous 52pt slab). The top hairline that used to differentiate the bar from the tab bar is gone — irrelevant when the bar is rounded and shadow-floated. (3) **Animation** — `.transition(.move(edge: .bottom).combined(with: .opacity))` on the overlay + `.animation(.spring(response: 0.45, dampingFraction: 0.85), value: playerVM.nowPlaying?.uri)` on the TabView so the pill slides in/out smoothly. When `nowPlaying == nil`, both the overlay and the per-tab spacer return nothing, so the mini player takes zero vertical space. Geometry constants (`pillHeight=62`, `pillSideInset=12`, `pillBottomOffset=96`, `scenePlayerBottomPadding=76`) are pulled to named constants at the top of `MainTabView` so future iOS releases adjusting tab bar height can be retuned in one place.
- **2026-06-07** — **NearMe pan bounds bug + rubber-band + MiniPlayerBar layout fix.** Three linked fixes from on-device feedback that the park let you pan into a black void in one direction (but not the other), there was no bounce when you hit the edge, and the gap between the mini player and the tab bar / content was still there. (1) **Stale `containerSize` was the root cause of the out-of-bounds pan.** `clampedOffset(_:scale:)` reads from `@State containerSize`, set once in `.onAppear { initialize(...) }`. On iOS 26 the playfield can settle to a smaller size *after* the initial layout pass (the floating Liquid Glass tab bar asynchronously re-measures the safe area), so the cached `containerSize` was bigger than the real container — which made `overhangX/Y` bigger than they should be, which let pan accumulate past the actual visible bound. The fix splits the old `initialize(_:)` into `updateSizing(_:)` (runs every `geo.size` change, recomputes `canvasSize`/`containerSize`, re-clamps current offset against new bounds) and `spawnInitial(_:)` (runs once, gated by a new `@State hasSpawned`). Both go through `applyLayout(_:)` which filters zero-size passes. iOS 17's `.onChange(of:initial:_:)` shortcut would have been cleaner but deployment target is iOS 16 — pair of `.onAppear` + `.onChange(of: geo.size)` instead. (2) **Rubber-band pan + pinch** finally landed. New `rubberBand(_:limit:)` helper applies 30%-resistance past the bound (matching iOS's own scroll-view feel). `panGesture.onChanged` uses `rubberBandedOffset(...)` instead of the hard clamp; `.onEnded` springs back to `clampedOffset(...)` with `.spring(response: 0.35, dampingFraction: 0.75)`. Pinch uses the analogous `rubberBandedScale(_:)`. New `minScale`/`maxScale` properties replace the inline `0.7`/`2.5`. The hard `clampedOffset(_:scale:)` is still there — used as the spring-back target and as the post-resize re-clamp — but never called directly from gestures during drag/pinch. (3) **MiniPlayerBar moved to TabView-level `.safeAreaInset`.** Previously each of the five tab items had its own `.safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom) { MiniPlayerBar() }`. On iOS 26's floating tab bar that pattern reliably produced a ~20pt visible gap above the bar — the system was inserting padding between each tab's safe area edge and the inserted content. Pulling the inset up to the TabView itself (single `.safeAreaInset(edge: .bottom, spacing: 0) { MiniPlayerBar() }` after the `TabView { ... }` block) lets the system place the bar flush against the tab-bar top edge with no upper gap; the bar's safe-area extends across all five tabs uniformly. When `playerVM.nowPlaying == nil`, `MiniPlayerBar`'s body returns nothing → `safeAreaInset` adds zero space → mini player only takes vertical room when it has content to show. Side effect: Profile's Recent-Tracks carousel gets its full 516pt back (Cotton card etc. no longer truncated).
- **2026-06-07** — **Surface opacity + recents capacity fixes.** Four linked tweaks from on-device feedback that scroll content was bleeding through the mini player and tab bar, and that the Recent Tracks carousel was visually clipping its first/last cards. (1) **MiniPlayerBar now fully opaque** — dropped the `.ultraThinMaterial` + `Color.nodalong.primary700.opacity(0.72)` stack for a flat `Color.nodalong.primary700` background. The "floating glass" reading lost the visual debate against the cost of seeing track-card content blurred behind it on dense Profile/Shelf scrolls. The top hairline stays (so the bar still reads as its own surface). (2) **Tab bar opacity enforced for iOS 26** — `tabAppearance.backgroundEffect = nil` + `UITabBar.appearance().isTranslucent = false` in `MainTabView.init`. On iOS 26, `configureWithOpaqueBackground()` alone wasn't enough; the system was falling back to the new "Liquid Glass" floating style for the scroll-edge appearance. Explicitly clearing the effect + forcing non-translucent keeps it solid like pre-26 builds. (3) **`RecentTracksCarousel.pageSize` default 10 → 6.** The previous 10-per-page carousel was sized at 10 × 86pt = 860pt — wider than the available vertical space on most iPhones after navigation chrome + profile header + spotlight section + mini player + tab bar. The parent ScrollView clipped it, which is what the "first/last cards cut off" reports were really showing. 6 × 86pt = 516pt fits comfortably on every device. With ~100 accumulated recents that's now ~17 pages of swipeable history. (4) **`getYourMusicFeed` limit 50 → 100** in `Nodalong/API/Firestore/Posts.swift`. Worth noting: `syncRecentTracks` was already an upsert by `trackId` with no delete pass — recent_tracks accumulates over time even though Apple's `MusicRecentlyPlayedRequest` is capped at 25 per sync (server-enforced; no pagination on that endpoint). So the cap was always at the *read* side, not the sync side. 100 gives the carousel real headroom without pagination round trips and is harmless for older users (the query already orders by `listenedAt` desc so newer entries come first).
- **2026-06-07** — **Spline removed; AvatarView is now the sole avatar render path (procedural SwiftUI).** Rationale: Spline's binary SDK has no simulator slice, live scenes are expensive at scale (drove the snapshot-cache complexity), it's a closed asset ecosystem, and *every* `perEmojiSceneURLs` entry was still pointing at the demo URL anyway — so for the on-device cost we were getting zero per-animal personality. Rive was the alternative considered; rejected for the same reason the Spline scenes never landed (`.riv` files can only be authored in Rive's visual editor, and there's no designer in the loop). Picked **procedural SwiftUI enhancement of the existing `AvatarView`** instead — lightest possible runtime (rasterised shapes), zero new dependencies, every user already visually distinct via `emoji × colour`, identical call sites for a future swap to Rive / USDZ / AI-PNG when assets arrive. Changes: (1) **`AvatarView` extended** with two new optional params, `animState: AvatarAnimState = .wandering` and `lifecycle: AvatarLifecycle = .alive`. A new `ProceduralAvatarMotion` ViewModifier layers all the per-state motion: idle 3D tilt (kept from the previous version, randomised phase per instance), breathe loop (1.0 ↔ 1.03 scale with 2.6–3.4s jittered duration per instance), listening sway (held 6° rotationEffect + slowed bob), bumping squash-and-stretch (1.18×/0.92y anchored at bottom, spring-snapped), lifecycle scale (entering/leaving render at 0.7 with spring snap into/out of view). New `EnteringRipple` view draws an expanding stroke ring under the avatar during `.entering` so arrivals read as "popped in." All motion is *small* — a few degrees, a few percent — so 12 of these on the NearMe canvas read as alive, not busy. (2) **`NearMeCanvasView.avatarLayer` collapsed** from the snapshot-vs-fallback branch to a single `AvatarView` call passing `animState` and `lifecycle` from the per-avatar state. The view's internal motion handles bump/listening/lifecycle visuals; the canvas only sets `.opacity` (lifecycle fade) and `.position`. The `splineCache` `@ObservedObject`, `warmSnapshotsForActivePool()`, and the `splineCache.warm(...)` call in `tickLifecycle` are gone — there's nothing to pre-warm for procedural rendering. (3) **`NearMeUserSheet.identitySection`** replaced `Spline3DAvatarView(sceneURL:, fallback...)` + accent ring overlay with a single `AvatarView(size: 200, animated: true)` call. The procedural depth styling already includes a colour-tinted drop shadow so the explicit accent ring is redundant. (4) **Deleted `Spline3DAvatarView.swift` and `SplineSnapshotCache.swift`**; removed the `SplineRuntime` SPM package and target dependency from `project.yml`; xcodegen + xcodebuild green. Doc comment in `AvatarView.swift` records the future swap path: when richer assets land (Rive rigs, USDZ models, AI-rendered portraits, hand-drawn PNGs), replace the `foreground` ViewBuilder — call sites do not change.
- **2026-06-07** — **NearMe playfield + app-wide brightness lift.** Five linked tweaks based on on-device feedback that the park "was sparse, dark, jagged, and bled over the slider/tab bar." (1) **App-wide colour lift** — `gray800` `#1a1a1a → #2a2540` and `gray700` `#221c30 → #352f52` in `NodalongKit/Constants/Theme.swift`. Both shift into the soft lavender-dark family (desaturated `primary200` cousins) — every screen reads one step brighter while staying clearly dark mode and picking up the brand's purple personality. Card-vs-background contrast preserved because both tokens lifted in parallel. (2) **Park palette** — grass gradient `(0.13,0.21,0.16)→(0.09,0.15,0.12)` lifted to `(0.22,0.36,0.26)→(0.16,0.28,0.20)` (sage / dusk-meadow). Tree canopy `(0.07s,0.18s,0.10s)` → `(0.10s,0.26s,0.14s)` so trees are deeper-green than the grass instead of near-black silhouettes. Tree highlight 0.04→0.07, shadow 0.25→0.20. (3) **Rounded playfield container** — in `NearMeView.usersContent`, the canvas is now wrapped with `.padding(8)` + `.clipShape(RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: 20, style: .continuous))` + 1pt white-6% strokeBorder + soft black-35% shadow. The playfield never moves; the park lives entirely inside the clip so pan/zoom can no longer bleed over the slider or the tab bar. (4) **Park-inside-viewport sizing + pan clamp** in `NearMeCanvasView` — `canvasSize` multiplier 1.3→1.5 (park is 1.5× the container so there's real pan room); `avatarDisplaySize` 50→60 and `collisionRadius` 55→70 so avatars feel meaningfully bigger; new `containerSize` state caches the playfield size for the clamp; new `clampedOffset(_ candidate:scale:)` keeps the park's edges from sliding past the container's edges at the current zoom (no empty corners). Initial offset is `.zero` because the inner ZStack is centred by default — the previous `(screen - canvas)/2` offset was a confused leftover and is gone. Pinch re-clamps after every magnification step so zooming-out can't leave the park half-off-canvas. (5) **60fps physics tick** — the old `Timer.publish(every: 0.1)` was literally 10fps, which is why the wandering looked jagged (positions snapped by 2–5px every 100ms). Bumped to `every: 1.0 / 60.0` and `tick()`'s hardcoded `dt = 0.1` → `1.0 / 60.0`. Physics math already used `dt`, so no other changes needed. At 12 avatars the O(N²) collision pass is ~4k ops/sec — trivial.
- **2026-06-07** — **NearMe park redesign.** The canvas is now a stylised park rather than an abstract void — fixes the "you have to zoom out to find anyone" UX and matches the product vision ("Waze-style radar … on an animated map") without surfacing real-world coordinates (privacy). Three structural changes: (1) **New `ParkBackgroundView`** — SwiftUI `Canvas`-drawn green-tinged dark gradient + ~8 darker tree circles placed via a seeded splitmix64 RNG so the scenery is stable across re-renders. Pure decoration, `allowsHitTesting(false)`, no collision against avatars. Only trees this pass — paths, ponds, benches deliberately out of scope. (2) **Canvas right-sized** — dropped from 2000×2000 to `max(screenWidth, screenHeight) × 1.3` computed via `GeometryReader`. Default zoom 1.0 shows the whole park; pinch clamp tightened to 0.7…2.5 (was 0.3…3.0) so you can't lose the park off-screen. (3) **Avatar lifecycle pool** — `AvatarAnimationState` extended with `lifecycle: { .entering, .alive, .leaving }`, `opacity`, `lifeRemaining`, and `nextListenIn`/`listenRemaining` for spontaneous "sitting on the grass" `.listening` lulls (3–6s). New `AvatarAnimationState.spawning(canvasSize:)` factory drops avatars at a random park edge with `.entering` + opacity 0, then they fade in over 0.6s, live for a random 60–120s, then fade out and despawn. **Pool cap 12** — `activePool: [String]` holds the alive uids; if `users.count > 12`, the rest sit in `unusedQueue` and rotate in when a leaver despawns (every nearby user gets a turn over a session). If `≤ 12`, everyone is persistent but still cycles through alive/listening states for liveliness. `reconcileQueue()` handles `users` changes (radius bump / arrivals / departures) without disturbing alive avatars — vanished users get nudged into `.leaving`. (4) **Tick loop split** into `tickLifecycle` (fades, spawn-from-queue on despawn), `tickPhysics` (wander + listening, only for `.alive`), `tickCollisions` (only between `.alive` avatars — entering/leaving are partially transparent and pass through). `warmSnapshotsForActivePool` walks the pool not the full user array. No "you are here" marker — the canvas stays a window on others.
- **2026-06-06** — NowPlayingView dismiss fix v2 (v1 didn't actually work on-device — close button + drag handle were invisible against dark album art). Three structural changes: (1) **`.safeAreaInset(edge: .top)` for the topBar** — guarantees the controls render in a visible band below the Dynamic Island regardless of how the body's `.ignoresSafeArea` background extends. Previously the topBar was inside the body VStack and getting positioned wrong on devices with the island. (2) **High-contrast styling** — drag handle is now `Color.white.opacity(0.85)` (was 0.4), close button uses `.ultraThinMaterial` + 1pt white-25% border (was 25%-black-on-black, invisible against dark artwork). 50×50 hit area, well above the 44pt minimum. Added a subtle linear gradient under the topBar so the controls always have contrast even against pure-black album art.
